# PVE: professional video editor knowledge base

PVE is what the AdobeBridge edit system knows about **After Effects 2026** and **Premiere Pro 2026**. Every item below
was harvested from the installed apps (names, match names, every property with its type, range and default), written
up as a knowledge card, checked by a validator against that harvest, reviewed by an adversarial agent, and (for
effects and transitions) rendered on test footage and checked against the frame.

## Using it

```
python pve/pve.py find "film grain"          # search by look / use
python pve/pve.py card "Camera Shake" PR      # one full card: params, demo settings, recipe, scripting notes
python pve/pve.py check spec.json             # pre-flight an AdobeBridge spec (also runs inside every build):
                                              #   names, ranges, transitions, flash rate (see Photosensitivity)
python pve/pve.py scenes scenes.json          # pre-flight After Effects VFX scenes (also runs inside vfx.py):
                                              #   names, ranges, preset on the wrong layer type / shape origin,
                                              #   flash rate
```

`pve.py card` warns (on stderr) when more than one card matches: distinct effects can share a display name (a current
and an obsolete Transform), so ask by match name, by app, or as "Category/Name" (`"Text/Rotation/Spin In"`).

In code: `sys.path.insert(0, "pve"); import pve` then `pve.find(...)`, `pve.card(...)`, `pve.check_spec(spec)`.

Spec conventions (both apps): After Effects effects are `{"name": <match name>, "props": {"Group/Property": value}}`
(dropdowns 1-based, points in pixels); Premiere effects are `{"name": <display name>, "pr_props": {"Property": value}}`
(repeated names `Name#2`, dropdowns 0-based, points as frame fractions). Colours `"#RRGGBB"`, keyframes `[[s, v], ...]`.

Preset timing (render-verified): applying an animation preset puts its first keyframe at the current time, so any
lead-in hold stored in the .ffx is lost; and preset keyframes keep their 30 fps frame counts, so in a 25 fps comp every
preset runs about 1.2x longer than the seconds quoted on its card. Shape presets draw around the layer's origin (put the
layer where the graphic should appear); text-animator presets do nothing on footage.

Status legend: **current**, obsolete (use the equivalent), hidden (AE internal), needs_input (needs a layer, file,
mask, audio or camera), unusable.

## Render verdicts

Each card's `verified.verdict`, shown in brackets after its name. A verdict describes what the test render showed
about the CARD; **pass = matched its render, not "works"**. Blank = no render check (Premiere presets, LUTs,
templates, audio, tools, and items never rendered).

- **pass**: matched its render: the test frame showed what the card says. Not a promise that the effect suits a job (an unusable transition can pass by rendering its failure, a shake can pass on one still frame).
- **weak**: it showed, but too faintly at the tested values to judge.
- **wrong**: the card disagreed with its render and has not been rewritten yet.
- **broken**: the render showed nothing usable (no change, black, garbage or a refused export) and the test setup was not shown to be the cause.
- **not_rendered**: never rendered: no test frame exists.
- **corrected_unverified**: the card disagreed with its render, then its text or demo values were changed: neither the old verdict nor the corrected card has been checked against a render.
- **untested: gpu_only** (`untested:gpu_only`): the harness could not test it; it draws only with GPU acceleration; the CPU-only test render showed the 'requires GPU acceleration' slate.
- **untested: needs_analysis** (`untested:needs_analysis`): the harness could not test it; the test rendered before its background analysis (stabilise / camera solve) had finished.
- **untested: needs_second_layer** (`untested:needs_second_layer`): the harness could not test it; it needs a second layer, a stack or a second instance that the one-effect test does not build.
- **untested: needs_second_track** (`untested:needs_second_track`): the harness could not test it; it needs a matte or clip on another track (or a real edit) that the one-clip test does not build.
- **untested: no_suitable_footage** (`untested:no_suitable_footage`): the harness could not test it; the test footage lacks what it acts on (camera shake, rolling-shutter skew, a chroma screen, an alpha edge, interlacing, a frame-rate mismatch, a low-res or blocky source).
- **untested: needs_setup** (`untested:needs_setup`): the harness could not test it; it needs a setup the harness does not script (layer markers, a text or shape host, matching copy, a stroke, several lines, a modal-dialog choice, a loaded LUT, a linked expression, a smaller layer).
- **untested: audio_only** (`untested:audio_only`): the harness could not test it; it makes only sound, which a still frame cannot show.

When a verdict changes, the old one stays in `verified.previous` with the date and the reason; details are in each
card's `verified.note`; cards whose values were fixed and then rendered again carry `verified.recheck`. A recipe
that carries both stacks has a second verdict for its After Effects half in `verified.ae` (shown as "AE ..."); the
first one is then the Premiere half.

## Photosensitivity

`check` and `scenes` count flashes for every effect, transition and preset whose card has a `flash` field (Strobe
Light, the Flash / Dip to White / Glow / Light Leak transitions, the flicker and flash presets...). The rule is the
Harding test / ITU-R BT.1702 one: no more than 3 flashes in any one second where the flashing area is a quarter
of the screen or more, and a flash to or from saturated red counts even when it is not bright. A rate PVE can compute
(a strobe's Period and Duration, a preset's measured flash rate, flash transitions placed on the timeline) is an issue
when it passes 3. Strobe Light (both apps, measured): each Period starts in the strobe colour, which holds for
Period minus Duration, and the picture shows for the last Duration, on the layer's own clock (After Effects: from the
source's first frame; Premiere: from the clip's start). Random Strobe Probablity 0 flashes once per Period, 100 is the
exact inverse (again once per Period), and any value in between inverts about that share of frames in random bursts
(10 reached 6-7 flashes in one second), so it is always an issue. A flasher whose rate PVE cannot compute gives an
advisory line starting "check by eye". Advisory lines never fail `check` / `scenes`; the build treats every PVE line as
a warning anyway.

Files: `harvest/` raw ground truth; `cards/` knowledge cards (verdicts in each card's `verified`); `gallery/` render
test frames; `cards/recipe_*.json` combined looks/moves; `cards/zz_critic.json` known gaps.

## After Effects effects


### 3D Channel

- **3D Channel Extract** *(needs_input)*: Turns an auxiliary 3D channel stored in a multichannel render (RPF/EXR from a 3D app: Z-depth, object ID, normals, UVs) into a visible greyscale or colour image. On ordinary footage it shows nothing useful.
- **Cryptomatte** *(needs_input)*: Picks per-object or per-material mattes out of a Cryptomatte EXR render by clicking objects, giving clean anti-aliased isolation of any CG element. Without Cryptomatte layers in the EXR it produces nothing.
- **Depth Matte** *(needs_input)*: Reads the Z-depth channel of a 3D render (RPF/EXR) and keeps only the parts nearer or farther than a chosen depth, creating a depth-based transparency matte. Does nothing on normal footage.
- **Depth of Field** *(needs_input)*: Simulates camera focus on a 3D render by blurring pixels according to their distance from a focal plane read from the Z-depth channel. On footage without a depth channel nothing happens.
- **EXtractoR** *(needs_input)*: fnord ProEXR plug-in bundled with AE that pulls any channel set (render passes such as diffuse, specular, AO, depth) out of a multichannel EXR and maps it into RGB/alpha for compositing.
- **Fog 3D** *(needs_input)*: Adds atmospheric fog that thickens with distance, using the Z-depth channel of a 3D render (RPF/EXR) to decide how much fog each pixel gets.
- **ID Matte** *(needs_input)*: Creates a matte that isolates one object or material from a 3D render, using the Object ID or Material ID aux channel stored in an RPF/EXR file.
- **IDentifier** *(needs_input)*: fnord ProEXR plug-in that reads an object/material ID channel in a multichannel EXR and shows it as random colours or outputs a matte for a chosen ID number.

### Audio

- **Backwards** *(needs_input)*: Plays the layer's audio in reverse; optional left/right channel swap. Video is unaffected.
- **Bass & Treble** *(needs_input)*: Simple two-knob tone control that boosts or cuts low (bass) and high (treble) frequencies of the layer's audio.
- **Compressor** *(needs_input)*: Dynamic range compressor that turns down audio louder than a threshold, evening out loud and soft parts of voice or music, with makeup gain and an output limit.
- **Delay** *(needs_input)*: Echo effect that repeats the audio after a set time, with feedback for multiple repeats and separate dry/wet levels.
- **Distortion** *(needs_input)*: Amp-style audio distortion (drive, gain, wet/dry mix, output volume) plus a Bitcrusher that lowers bit depth and sample rate for crunchy lo-fi / 8-bit sound.
- **Flange & Chorus** *(needs_input)*: Mixes delayed, pitch-modulated copies of the audio with the original: short delays give a swooshing jet-like flange, longer delays with several voices give a thick chorus.
- **Gate** *(needs_input)*: Noise gate: silences the audio whenever its level falls below a threshold, with attack, hold and release times controlling how smoothly it opens and closes.
- **High-Low Pass** *(needs_input)*: Single filter that removes frequencies below (High Pass) or above (Low Pass) a cutoff: makes audio thin and telephone-like or muffled and distant.
- **Modulator** *(needs_input)*: Adds vibrato (pitch wobble) and tremolo (volume wobble) by modulating the audio with a sine or triangle LFO; high rates give a ring-mod robot tone.
- **Parametric EQ** *(needs_input)*: Three-band parametric equalizer: boosts or cuts a chosen frequency band per band (centre frequency, bandwidth, boost/cut) to shape the tone of voice or music.
- **Reverb** *(needs_input)*: Simulates a room or hall by adding diffuse reflections and a decaying tail to the audio, from a small room to a long cathedral echo.
- **Stereo Mixer** *(needs_input)*: Sets the volume and pan position of the left and right channels independently, for balancing, mono fixes and left-right pans.
- **Tone** *(needs_input)*: Synthesizes simple tones (up to five frequencies mixed as a chord) on the layer's audio: beeps, bleeps, hums and alarms.

### Blur & Sharpen

- **Bilateral Blur** [pass]: Edge-preserving blur: smooths areas of similar colour while keeping strong edges sharp, giving smoother skin, cleaner noise or a flat painterly look.
- **CC Cross Blur** [pass]: Blurs the image separately horizontally and vertically and combines the two passes with a chosen transfer mode, giving soft directional or cross-shaped streaky blur.
- **CC Radial Blur** [pass]: Radial blur around a centre point. Type picks the style: 1 Straight Zoom, 2 Fading Zoom and 3 Centered Zoom streak toward or away from the centre, 5 Rotate, 6 Scratch (the default, frame-verified: a rotational spin smear) and 7 Rotate Fading turn around it.
- **CC Radial Fast Blur** [pass]: Fast zoom-style radial blur that streaks the image outward from a centre point, like a crash zoom or light rays bursting from the middle.
- **CC Vector Blur** [pass]: Smears pixels along the brightness contours of the layer (or of a chosen vector-map layer), giving a flowing, painterly or liquid streak look rather than a uniform blur.
- **Camera Lens Blur** [pass]: Realistic optical defocus that imitates a real lens, with shaped bokeh (iris blades) and blooming highlights; optionally driven by a depth/blur-map layer.
- **Channel Blur** [pass]: Blurs the red, green, blue and alpha channels separately, so colours bleed and fringe at different amounts; also softens a layer's alpha edge.
- **Compound Blur** [pass]: Blurs the layer by varying amounts according to the brightness of a control (blur) layer: white areas of the map get the Maximum Blur, black areas stay sharp.
- **Directional Blur** [pass]: Blurs along a single angle, creating motion streaks as if the camera or subject moved quickly in that direction.
- **Fast Box Blur** [pass]: Fast general-purpose blur; with 1 iteration it gives a boxy, square-ish blur, with 3 or more it approaches a smooth Gaussian blur.
- **Gaussian Blur** [pass]: Smooth, even, high-quality blur of the whole layer; the standard blur for softening, background fills and blur transitions.
- **Radial Blur** [pass]: Blurs around a centre point either in circles (Spin) or outward (Zoom), giving a rotating or crash-zoom motion feel.
- **Sharpen** [pass]: Increases contrast at edges so the image looks crisper; simple one-slider sharpening.
- **Smart Blur** [pass]: Blurs flat areas while keeping strong edges sharp, smoothing skin and noise into a soft, painted look; Edge Only mode outputs just the edges as lines.
- **Unsharp Mask** [pass]: Sharpens by boosting contrast along edges (image minus a blurred copy). Small radius = fine detail sharpening; large radius with low amount = local-contrast 'clarity' punch.
- **Camera-Shake Deblur** *(needs_input)* [weak]: Repairs motion blur caused by handheld camera shake by rebuilding blurred frames from sharper neighbouring frames; it does nothing visible on steady or still footage.

### Boris FX Mocha

- **Mocha AE** *(needs_input)*: Boris FX planar tracker bundled with After Effects: you open the separate Mocha AE window to track a flat surface or roto a shape, then the effect applies that tracked shape as a matte or exports corner-pin / transform data to another layer.

### Channel

- **Arithmetic** [pass]: Runs a simple math operation (Xor, Add, Difference, Slice, Multiply...) between every pixel's red, green and blue values and constant values you set per channel, giving hard colour casts, channel inversions and solarised looks.
- **CC Composite** [pass]: Composites the layer's original, pre-effect image (or another layer picked in Top Layer) back over the result of the effects above it, using a transfer mode and opacity. Mixes a stylise effect with the clean image, or self-blends the layer for punchier contrast.
- **Calculations** [pass]: Combines one channel of this layer with a channel of a second layer through a blend mode. Used alone, it pulls a single channel (Gray, Red, Green, Blue, Alpha) out as a black-and-white image.
- **Channel Combiner** [pass]: Maps one channel or a colour-space conversion (e.g. Lightness, Hue, RGB to HLS, RGB to YUV) into a target channel, producing channel swaps, false colour or single-channel greyscale.
- **Compound Arithmetic** [pass]: Combines this layer with a second source layer by a math operator (Add, Multiply, Difference, Screen, Overlay...) on RGB, ARGB or alpha. With the layer itself as source it gives a quick self-blend.
- **Invert** [pass]: Inverts a chosen channel or colour space: full RGB photo negative, hue flip, lightness/luminance flip, or alpha inversion.
- **Minimax** [pass]: Replaces each pixel with the minimum or maximum value found within a radius, spreading bright or dark areas into blocky shapes; on alpha it grows or chokes a matte.
- **Set Channels** [pass]: Rebuilds the red, green, blue and alpha channels from chosen channels (or luminance, hue, lightness, saturation) of up to four source layers, defaulting to the layer itself; used for channel swaps and channel-based mattes.
- **Shift Channels** [pass]: Rebuilds the red, green, blue and alpha channels from other channels of the same layer (e.g. red from blue). Gives instant false-colour swaps or a channel extracted as greyscale.
- **Solid Composite** [pass]: Composites a solid colour behind the layer with its own opacity and blending mode. Lowering Source Opacity lets the colour wash through, giving a flat tint, or fills transparent areas with a colour.
- **Blend** *(needs_input)*: Blends the layer with another layer picked in the effect, using Crossfade, Color Only, Tint Only, Darken Only or Lighten Only.
- **Remove Color Matting** *(needs_input)*: Removes the dark or coloured fringe on semi-transparent edges of a layer whose alpha was premultiplied against a background colour (e.g. graphics rendered on black).
- **Set Matte** *(needs_input)*: Replaces the layer's alpha with a channel (alpha, luminance, a colour channel, etc.) taken from another layer, so that layer acts as a matte. An effect-based track matte: it travels with the layer when precomposed and the matte layer can sit anywhere in the comp (hidden).

### Cinema 4D

- **Cineware** *(unusable)*: Renders a Cinema 4D (.c4d) scene layer inside After Effects, with camera and multi-pass extraction. On this machine it fails because Cinema 4D 2025 is not installed.

### Color Correction

- **Auto Color** [pass]: Automatically balances colour and contrast by analysing shadows, midtones and highlights, removing casts. With Snap Neutral Midtones it pushes near-grey tones to neutral grey.
- **Auto Contrast** [pass]: Automatically stretches overall contrast so the darkest pixels go black and the brightest go white, without shifting colour balance.
- **Auto Levels** [pass]: Automatically sets black and white points for each colour channel separately, stretching contrast and often removing (or sometimes adding) colour casts.
- **Black & White** [pass]: Converts the image to monochrome, with sliders controlling how bright each original hue (reds, yellows, greens, cyans, blues, magentas) becomes in grey. Optional tint for sepia or cyanotype looks.
- **Brightness & Contrast** [pass]: Simple global brightness and contrast adjustment. The default (non-legacy) mode behaves like a gentle levels curve that protects highlights and shadows.
- **Broadcast Colors** [pass]: Limits luminance or saturation so the picture stays within legal broadcast (NTSC/PAL) signal levels, or keys out the unsafe areas to show where they are.
- **CC Color Neutralizer** [pass]: Removes or adds colour casts separately in shadows, midtones and highlights: pick the off-colour as an 'Unbalance' colour to neutralise it, or push red/green/blue per tonal range for split-tone grades.
- **CC Color Offset** [pass]: Rotates the red, green and blue channel values by a phase angle, cycling colours around; overflow handling wraps, solarizes or polarizes values for psychedelic, solarized false-colour looks.
- **CC Kernel** [pass]: Applies a custom 3x3 convolution kernel: every pixel becomes a weighted sum of itself and its eight neighbours. Depending on the nine weights it sharpens, softens, embosses or edge-detects the image.
- **CC Toner** [pass]: Remaps the image's luminance onto a ramp of user-chosen colours (duotone / tritone / pentone), like a darkroom toner. Shadows, midtones and highlights each take their own colour.
- **Change Color** [pass]: Shifts the hue, lightness and saturation of one selected colour range while leaving the rest of the image alone. Used to recolour or mute a single object such as a shirt, banner or flag stripe.
- **Change to Color** [pass]: Replaces one chosen colour ('From') with a specific target colour ('To'), with tolerance and softness controls for how wide the match is. Unlike Change Color, the output is an exact colour rather than a relative shift.
- **Channel Mixer** [pass]: Rebuilds each output colour channel as a percentage mix of the input red, green and blue plus a constant. Used for channel swaps, custom black-and-white conversions and strong stylised colour casts.
- **Color Balance** [pass]: Shifts the red, green and blue balance separately in the shadows, midtones and highlights, like three-way colour wheels driven by sliders. Used to warm, cool, split-tone or neutralise an image.
- **Color Balance (HLS)** [pass]: Rotates the hue of the whole image and adjusts overall lightness and saturation with three simple controls. A basic, older form of Hue/Saturation's master controls.
- **Color Link** [pass]: Fills the layer with a single colour sampled from a source layer (its average, median, brightest or darkest pixel and similar), blended over the original. Used to make graphics automatically pick up the colour of footage.
- **Colorama** [pass]: Turns an attribute of each pixel (intensity, a channel, hue and so on) into a phase and maps it through a colour wheel (the Output Cycle), giving gradient maps, rainbow cycles, thermal-camera looks or animated colour cycling.
- **Equalize** [pass]: Redistributes brightness or colour values so they spread evenly across the full range (histogram equalisation). Pulls detail out of flat or dark images and gives a harsh, high-detail look at full strength.
- **Exposure** [pass]: Photographic exposure change in f-stops, worked out in linear light, plus an Offset that shifts the black level and a Gamma Correction for midtones. Can act on the whole image (Master) or on the red, green and blue channels separately.
- **Gamma/Pedestal/Gain** [pass]: Per-channel video-style colour controls: Gamma bends the midtones, Pedestal adds an offset to the black level, Gain multiplies the brightness of each of the red, green and blue channels. Black Stretch brightens the darkest values for all channels.
- **Hue/Saturation** [pass]: Rotates hue and changes saturation and lightness of the whole image (or a chosen colour range), and with Colorize tints the whole frame a single hue, like sepia or a brand-colour monotone.
- **Leave Color** [pass]: Desaturates the whole image except pixels close to one chosen colour, giving a selective-colour (colour pop) look such as a red flag on a black-and-white frame.
- **Levels** [pass]: Classic levels: remaps the input black point, white point and midtone gamma to new output black and white levels, for contrast, brightness and fades. The per-channel histogram edits live in a custom property, so by script only the RGB master controls are reachable.
- **Levels (Individual Controls)** [pass]: The same levels remap as Levels, but every channel (RGB master, Red, Green, Blue, Alpha) has its own exposed Input Black/White, Gamma and Output Black/White, so each can be set and keyframed separately.
- **Lumetri Color** [pass]: Adobe's full grading panel inside one effect: white balance, exposure and tone, creative Looks/LUTs, fade and vibrance, curves, colour wheels, HSL secondary keying and a vignette. Gives everything from a clean correction to a stylised film grade.
- **Photo Filter** [pass]: Simulates a coloured lens filter: casts the image toward a warming, cooling or custom colour at a chosen density while keeping brightness.
- **Selective Color** [pass]: Photoshop-style selective colour: adds or removes cyan, magenta, yellow and black inside one colour range (reds, yellows, greens, cyans, blues, magentas, whites, neutrals, blacks) without touching the others.
- **Shadow/Highlight** [pass]: Brightens shadows and/or darkens highlights locally, based on surrounding pixels, without flattening the midtones; recovers backlit faces and blown skies.
- **Tint** [pass]: Turns the layer monochrome by mapping its darks to one colour and its lights to another (black-and-white by default); Amount to Tint blends with the original colour.
- **Tritone** [pass]: Maps the image's highlights, midtones and shadows to three chosen colours, giving a toned monochrome (sepia by default).
- **Vibrance** [pass]: Raises (or lowers) the saturation of the least saturated colours more than already saturated ones, protecting skin tones; Saturation changes all colours equally.
- **Video Limiter** [pass]: Clamps luma and chroma to a broadcast-legal level, optionally compressing values before the clip point; Gamut Warning paints out-of-range pixels in a warning colour.
- **Color Stabilizer** *(needs_input)*: Removes flicker and brightness or colour drift over time by locking the colour at one, two or three sampled points (black, mid, white) to their values on a reference frame. Only has a visible effect on footage whose exposure or colour changes over time.
- **Curves** *(needs_input)*: Adjusts tone and contrast through a drawn tone curve for the combined RGB or each colour channel (and alpha). The standard tool for S-curve contrast, lifted blacks and per-channel colour tweaks.
- **OCIO CDL Transform** *(needs_input)*: Applies an ASC CDL grade (slope, offset, power, saturation) read from a .cdl, .cc or .ccc file through OpenColorIO, so a colourist's or DIT's grade can be reproduced exactly. Without a CDL file it does nothing.
- **OCIO Color Space Transform** *(needs_input)*: Converts pixels from one OpenColorIO colour space to another (for example camera log such as S-Log3 or LogC into ACEScg or Rec.709), using the project's OCIO config.
- **OCIO Display Transform** *(needs_input)*: Applies an OpenColorIO display / view transform (for example ACES to sRGB or Rec.709 SDR video) to the layer, baking the look of the viewer into the pixels; Inverse brings display-referred images into scene-linear.
- **OCIO File Transform** *(needs_input)*: Applies a LUT or colour-transform file (for example .cube, .3dl, .csp, .clf, .spi3d) through OpenColorIO. Without a chosen file it does nothing.
- **OCIO Look Transform** *(needs_input)*: Applies a named 'look' defined in the OpenColorIO config (a show grade or creative LUT) between an input and an output colour space.
- **PS Arbitrary Map** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy effect that remaps the layer's brightness/colour through a Photoshop arbitrary map (.amp curve file) loaded in the effect's Options dialog; Phase cycles the map along the tone range. Curves does the same job today. -> AE replacement: Curves (ADBE CurvesCustom); Premiere: Lumetri Color RGB Curves

### Distort

- **Bezier Warp** [pass]: Warps the layer by moving the 4 corner vertices and 8 tangent handles of a Bezier border, curving the edges (flag wave, page curl, bowed card).
- **Bulge** [pass]: Magnifies (positive height) or pinches (negative height) an elliptical area around a centre point, like a fisheye lens bump.
- **CC Bend It** [pass]: Bends the layer along the line from a Start point to an End point, like flexing a rubber strip; Render Prestart decides what happens to the part before Start.
- **CC Bender** [pass]: Bends the layer around the axis from Base to Top so it leans or curves; Style picks the bend shape.
- **CC Blobbylize** [pass]: Turns a channel of a blob layer (by default the layer itself, default channel Lightness) into soft, lit, liquid-metal or plastic-looking blobs with specular highlights. Cut Away removes low values so shapes melt into droplets.
- **CC Flo Motion** [pass]: Pulls (positive Amount) or pushes (negative Amount) the image toward two knot points, creating liquid pinch and stretch distortion that can wrap around with tiled edges.
- **CC Griddler** [pass]: Cuts the image into a grid of square tiles and scales/rotates each tile around its own centre, giving a broken-screen / shrunk-tiles mosaic; at 100/100 scale and 0 rotation the image looks untouched.
- **CC Lens** [pass]: Bends the image through a lens that follows the layer's aspect (an oval on 16:9): positive Convergence bulges like a fisheye/magnifying glass, negative pinches; the area outside the lens drops out.
- **CC Page Turn** [pass]: Curls the layer like a turning book page from a corner, with a lit fold, a back side (paper colour or another layer) and transparency where the page has lifted away.
- **CC Power Pin** [pass]: Corner-pins the layer by its four corners with true perspective correction and optional edge expansion, so flat footage or graphics sit on a screen, sign or wall in the shot.
- **CC Slant** [pass]: Skews (shears) the layer sideways like italic type, optionally stretching it, setting its height and floor line, and filling it with a flat colour.
- **CC Smear** [pass]: Drags the pixels around a From point toward a To point, like smudging wet paint or a liquify push.
- **CC Split** [pass]: Tears the image open along the line between Point A and Point B, pushing the two sides apart into a lens-shaped transparent gap.
- **CC Split 2** [pass]: Like CC Split, but the two sides of the tear open by separate amounts, so the gap can be lopsided.
- **CC Tiler** [pass]: Shrinks the image and repeats it in a grid that fills the frame, like a wall of identical screens; can blend back with the original.
- **Corner Pin** [pass]: Moves the four corners of a layer independently, so a flat image is skewed into perspective. The standard tool for putting footage onto a screen, sign or document in the shot.
- **Detail-preserving Upscale** [pass]: Enlarges a layer while keeping edges sharper than a plain transform scale. Meant for blowing up low-res stills and archival footage to fill the frame.
- **Displacement Map** [pass]: Pushes pixels horizontally and vertically according to the colour or brightness of a map layer. Used for heat-haze, liquid, glitch and 'image wraps onto a surface' looks.
- **Magnify** [pass]: Shows an enlarged circle or square of the image, like a magnifying glass, over the rest of the frame.
- **Mirror** [pass]: Splits the image along a line and reflects one side onto the other, giving a symmetric, kaleidoscope-like picture.
- **Offset** [pass]: Slides the image inside the layer and wraps whatever leaves one edge back in on the opposite edge, like a tiled pattern.
- **Optics Compensation** [pass]: Adds or removes wide-angle lens distortion: bulges the image like a fisheye, or with Reverse on, straightens barrel-distorted action-cam footage.
- **Polar Coordinates** [pass]: Converts the image between rectangular and polar coordinates, curling a flat picture into a ring or 'tiny planet', or unrolling a circle into a strip.
- **Ripple** [pass]: Creates concentric water-like ripples spreading out from a centre point; the waves move on their own over time.
- **Spherize** [pass]: Wraps a circular area of the layer around a sphere so the centre bulges toward the viewer, like a fisheye or magnifying glass. Bulge only; it cannot pinch.
- **Transform** [pass]: Applies position, scale, skew, rotation and opacity inside the effect stack, with its own shutter angle so animated moves get motion blur without the layer motion-blur switch. The workhorse for punch-ins, shakes and whip moves.
- **Turbulent Displace** [pass]: Displaces pixels with fractal noise so the image ripples, melts or wobbles organically, from subtle heat haze to heavy liquid warping.
- **Twirl** [pass]: Rotates the image around a centre point, twisting more at the centre than at the edge, producing a whirlpool swirl.
- **Warp** [pass]: Bends the whole layer into one of Photoshop's warp-text shapes (arc, arch, bulge, flag, wave, fisheye, squeeze, twist and more).
- **Wave Warp** [pass]: Pushes the image into travelling waves (sine, square, triangle, noise, etc.) that animate on their own via Wave Speed, from gentle flag ripples to harsh glitch slicing.
- **CC Ripple Pulse** *(needs_input)*: Sends circular ripple rings out from a centre point; the ring profile is the history of Pulse Level over Time Span, so nothing appears unless Pulse Level is keyframed or driven by audio.
- **Liquify** *(needs_input)*: Brush-based push, twirl, pucker and bloat distortion painted by hand in the Composition panel. Nothing changes until strokes are painted into its distortion mesh.
- **Mesh Warp** *(needs_input)*: Overlays a grid of Bezier points that are dragged by hand to bend the image. It shows nothing until the mesh is edited in the Composition panel.
- **Reshape** *(needs_input)*: Morphs the area inside one mask into the shape of another mask on the same layer, optionally limited by a boundary mask.
- **Rolling Shutter Repair** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Analyses moving CMOS footage and removes rolling-shutter skew and wobble (leaning verticals, jello) caused by fast pans or vibration. Corrective only: on a static or still frame there is no visible change.
- **Smear** *(needs_input)*: Moves the image area inside a source mask to a new position and smears the surrounding pixels within a boundary mask, like pushing wet paint. Shows nothing until two masks exist on the layer.
- **Warp Stabilizer** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_analysis]: Analyses shaky footage and smooths or locks the camera move, warping and cropping the frame to hide the correction. Corrective; on a still or locked-off shot there is nothing to stabilise.

### Expression Controls

- **3D Point Control** *(needs_input)*: Adds an [x, y, z] point value to a layer that expressions on other properties can read. Renders nothing by itself.
- **Angle Control** *(needs_input)*: Adds an angle dial (degrees, with revolutions) to a layer for expressions to read. Renders nothing by itself.
- **Checkbox Control** *(needs_input)*: Adds an on/off checkbox (0 or 1) to a layer for expressions to read. Renders nothing by itself.
- **Color Control** *(needs_input)*: An expression control that holds one colour value and draws nothing by itself. Other properties read it through expressions, so one swatch can recolour a whole template.
- **Dropdown Menu Control** *(needs_input)*: An expression control that holds a menu choice (a 1-based index) and draws nothing by itself. Expressions read the index to switch layouts, colours or text in a template.
- **Layer Control** *(needs_input)*: An expression control that points at another layer in the comp and draws nothing by itself. Expressions use it to read that layer's position, opacity or other properties without hard-coding its name.
- **Point Control** *(needs_input)*: An expression control that holds one x,y position (with an on-screen handle) and draws nothing by itself. Expressions read it to place pointers, line ends or effect centres from one draggable point.
- **Slider Control** *(needs_input)*: An expression control that holds one keyframeable number and draws nothing by itself. It is the standard way to drive counting numbers, progress bars and master intensity knobs.

### Generate

- **4-Color Gradient** [pass]: Generates a smooth gradient blended from four coloured points. With Blending Mode None (default) it replaces the layer as a gradient plate and Opacity only fades it towards transparency; with Normal (or another mode) it lays a colour wash over the footage.
- **Advanced Lightning** [pass]: Draws a procedural electric lightning bolt with a bright core and coloured glow from an Origin towards a target or direction. Animating Conductivity State makes it flicker and re-branch.
- **Beam** [pass]: Draws a glowing laser/light beam between a start and an end point. Animating Time sends a bolt of light travelling along the line.
- **CC Glue Gun** [pass]: Paints a glossy, wet-looking 3D-shaded bead of 'glue' along the path of an animated Brush Position, lit and reflecting the layer. With a static position it draws a single shiny blob.
- **CC Light Burst 2.5** [pass]: Streaks the layer's own bright areas outward from a centre point into radial light rays, like a burst of volumetric light or a zoom-streak flash.
- **CC Light Rays** [pass]: Shoots volumetric light rays outward from a centre point, streaking the layer's own bright pixels (or a chosen colour) like god rays through a window.
- **CC Light Sweep** [pass]: Passes a bright band of light across the layer, like a glint sliding over chrome; on layers with alpha it also lights the edges.
- **CC Threads** [pass]: Re-renders the layer as a woven fabric of criss-crossing threads coloured by the underlying image.
- **Cell Pattern** [pass]: Generates an animated cellular texture (bubbles, crystals, plates, static) that replaces the layer.
- **Checkerboard** [pass]: Draws a checkerboard pattern over (or instead of) the layer.
- **Circle** [pass]: Draws a solid disc or ring (optionally feathered or inverted) on the layer.
- **Ellipse** [pass]: Draws a soft glowing elliptical ring with separate inside and outside colours, like a neon tube.
- **Eyedropper Fill** [pass]: Samples the colour at a point in the layer and floods the layer with that colour, optionally blended with the original.
- **Fill** [pass]: Replaces the layer's opaque area (or a chosen mask) with a solid colour; Opacity fades the filled layer itself rather than mixing the colour with the footage.
- **Fractal** [pass]: Renders a Mandelbrot or Julia set fractal with cycling colour palettes over the layer.
- **Gradient Ramp** [pass]: Draws a linear or radial colour gradient between two points, optionally blended with the original layer.
- **Grid** [pass]: Draws a grid of lines (tech/blueprint style) over or instead of the layer.
- **Lens Flare** [pass]: Renders a classic optical lens flare (hot spot, streaks and a chain of coloured ghosts) on top of the layer at a chosen point.
- **Paint Bucket** [pass]: Flood-fills a region of similar colour, starting from the Fill Point, with a solid colour (like Photoshop's paint bucket), with optional feathered, spread, choked or stroke-only edges.
- **Radio Waves** [pass]: Generates rings (circles, polygons or stars, or copies of a mask or image contour) that expand outward from a point over time, like sonar or broadcast signal pulses.
- **Vegas** [pass]: Traces glowing line segments along the outlines found in an image (or along a mask path) and can run them around the contour like chasing marquee lights.
- **Write-on** [pass]: Paints brush dabs at the keyframed Brush Position over time, so an animated position leaves a hand-written / drawn stroke trail on the layer.
- **Audio Spectrum** *(needs_input)*: Draws a frequency-spectrum visualiser (bars, lines or dots) from an audio layer along a straight line or a mask path (a circular mask gives a ring), moving with the music or voice.
- **Audio Waveform** *(needs_input)*: Draws the amplitude waveform of an audio layer as a moving line, bars or dots along a straight line or mask path, reacting to speech or music.
- **Scribble** *(needs_input)*: Fills or outlines a mask with animated, hand-drawn-looking zig-zag scribble strokes that can wiggle and draw on over time.
- **Stroke** *(needs_input)*: Paints a brush stroke along a layer mask path, with start/end controls that make it draw on or off like a pen tracing the path.

### Immersive Video

- **VR Blur** [pass]: Blurs 360-degree (equirectangular) footage without leaving a visible seam at the left/right edges or pinching at the poles; on flat footage it still blurs, but treats the frame as a sphere, so the top and bottom smear more sideways than the centre.
- **VR Chromatic Aberrations** [pass]: Offsets the red, green and blue channels outward from a point of interest with a falloff, giving colour fringing that is seam-aware for 360 footage.
- **VR Color Gradients** [pass]: Overlays a soft multi-point colour gradient (up to 8 coloured points) on the footage with a chosen blend mode and opacity, wrapped correctly for 360 footage.
- **VR Converter** [pass]: Re-projects footage between 360 formats (equirectangular, cube-map layouts, fisheye and a flat 2D source) and can re-orient the sphere with tilt, pan and roll.
- **VR De-Noise** [pass]: Reduces video noise and grain in 360 footage without creating a seam at the wrap edges; high levels smooth fine detail into a plastic look.
- **VR Digital Glitch** [pass]: Seam-aware digital glitch for 360 (equirectangular) footage: block displacement, RGB colour splitting and noise that wrap cleanly across the left/right seam. It also renders on flat footage, where it reads as a strong data-corruption glitch.
- **VR Fractal Noise** [pass]: Generates cloudy fractal noise mapped onto a sphere, so it tiles seamlessly across the 360 seam and the poles, and blends it over the layer. On flat footage it gives a smoky, cloudy texture overlay.
- **VR Glow** [pass]: Adds a soft glow to the bright areas of the image (above a luma threshold), computed on the sphere so the glow wraps across the 360 seam. Optional tint colour.
- **VR Plane to Sphere** [pass]: Projects a flat 2D layer (title, logo, graphic, video) onto an equirectangular 360 frame so it looks undistorted in a VR headset; on a flat comp the layer is bent into a curved, pinched patch surrounded by transparency.
- **VR Rotate Sphere** [pass]: Rotates equirectangular 360 footage around the tilt, pan and roll axes, re-aiming the view or levelling the horizon. On a flat frame pan slides the image sideways with wrap-around, and tilt/roll bend it into curved distortions.
- **VR Sharpen** [pass]: Sharpens 360 equirectangular footage with seam and pole awareness, so there is no visible edge line at the wrap. On flat footage it behaves like a simple sharpen.
- **VR Sphere To Plane** [pass]: Extracts a flat, rectilinear (normal-looking) view from 360 equirectangular footage, like pointing a virtual camera into the sphere. On flat footage it shows a magnified, lens-warped crop of the frame.

### Keying

- **CC Simple Wire Removal** [pass]: Paints out a thin straight line between two points (a wire, cable, boom pole, film scratch) by filling it with displaced neighbouring pixels, with pixels from another frame, or by making the band transparent so a clean plate on a lower layer shows through.
- **Color Difference Key** [weak]: Older two-matte keyer: Partial B makes the key colour transparent, Partial A makes transparent the areas that lack a second, different colour, and the two are combined into the final alpha matte. Each matte has its own levels, giving fine control over semi-transparent areas such as smoke, glass or hair on blue or green screens.
- **Color Range** [pass]: Makes a range of colours transparent, defined by min/max limits on each channel in Lab, YUV or RGB space. Useful for unevenly lit or multi-coloured backdrops that a single-colour key cannot catch.
- **Extract** [pass]: Luminance (or single-channel) key: pixels darker than Black Point or brighter than White Point become transparent, with softness ramps at both ends. Keys out black or white backgrounds and can punch bright or dark areas out of footage.
- **Keylight (1.2)** [pass]: Professional colour-difference keyer: removes a chosen screen colour (green or blue) and suppresses its spill on the foreground, with matte clipping, shrink/grow and edge colour controls. The standard tool for green-screen presenters.
- **Linear Color Key** [pass]: Simple colour key: removes (or keeps) pixels close to a chosen Key Color, matched by RGB, hue or chroma, with tolerance and softness. Quick and crude compared with Keylight.
- **Unmult** [pass]: Turns brightness into transparency: a black background becomes transparent while bright elements keep their full colour (White mode does the reverse, removing a white background and keeping dark elements), so flares, fire, sparks and light leaks shot on black composite cleanly without a blend mode.
- **Advanced Spill Suppressor** *(needs_input)* [corrected_unverified]: Removes green or blue colour spill (the tinted fringe and cast that a chroma screen throws onto a subject's edges, hair and skin) after keying. Use the Standard method (1) after the key: the Ultra method with Desaturate 100 greyed every hue on ordinary footage and on a green-screen plate alike (the subject too), whatever the Key Color, Tolerance and Spill Range.
- **Difference Matte** *(needs_input)*: Makes pixels transparent where the layer matches a reference 'clean plate' layer (the same shot without the subject), leaving only what changed, such as a person who walked in.
- **Inner/Outer Key** *(needs_input)*: Isolates a subject with fuzzy edges (hair, fur, smoke) from two rough masks: one drawn inside the subject and one outside it; the effect computes the soft edge in between. Without masks on the layer it does nothing.
- **Key Cleaner** *(needs_input)*: Repairs the alpha of an already keyed layer: restores fine edge detail (hair, motion blur) lost to the key and reduces matte chatter. On footage without a key (fully opaque) it shows no change.

### Matte

- **Simple Choker** [pass]: Shrinks (positive) or spreads (negative) a layer's alpha edge by a number of pixels. A one-slider fix for fringes on keys and a way to fatten or thin text and shapes.
- **Matte Choker** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Two-pass spread/choke of a layer's alpha with geometric and grey-level softness: fills small holes and tightens or softens the edges of a keyed or rotoscoped matte. Also used to merge shapes into gooey blobs.
- **Refine Hard Matte** *(needs_input)*: Roto Brush-style edge refinement for a hard-edged matte: feathers, contrasts, shifts the edge, reduces chatter, adds motion blur and decontaminates edge colours. Needs a layer that already has an alpha matte (key, mask or track matte).
- **Refine Soft Matte** *(needs_input)*: Edge refinement for soft, detailed mattes (hair, fur, smoke): recalculates edge detail inside an edge band, then smooths, feathers, shifts and decontaminates it. Needs a layer that already has an alpha matte.

### Noise & Grain

- **Add Grain** [pass]: Synthesises realistic film grain with control over intensity, size, softness, per-channel response, colour and animation, optionally from film-stock presets. Unifies CG, stock and phone footage into one filmic texture.
- **Dust & Scratches** [pass]: Median-style filter that replaces pixels differing from their neighbourhood (within Radius) by more than Threshold, removing specks, dust and noise; large radii give a smeared, painterly look.
- **Fractal Noise** [pass]: Generates procedural greyscale cloud / turbulence noise that replaces or blends over the layer. The workhorse for smoke, fog, light-leak textures, flicker mattes and organic displacement maps.
- **Median** [pass]: Replaces each pixel with the median of its neighbours within Radius: removes speckle noise at low radius and turns the image into a flat, painterly watercolour look at high radius.
- **Noise** [pass]: Adds random per-pixel noise that changes every frame, in colour or monochrome. A quick video-noise / dirty-signal texture rather than true film grain.
- **Noise Alpha** [pass]: Adds random noise to the layer's alpha channel, punching speckled transparent holes (or speckled opacity) so whatever is below shows through. Used for disintegration and dusty, eroded mattes.
- **Noise HLS** [pass]: Adds noise separately to hue, lightness and saturation, with an optional grain mode and grain size. Gives colour speckle, luminance grain or psychedelic hue noise; static unless Noise Phase is animated.
- **Noise HLS Auto** [pass]: Same hue / lightness / saturation noise as Noise HLS, but it animates automatically at a set speed, so it behaves like live grain without keyframes.
- **Remove Grain** [pass]: Analyses the grain/noise in sampled flat areas and removes it, with optional temporal filtering and sharpening. A denoiser for noisy low-light footage and the degrain step before compositing.
- **Turbulent Noise** [pass]: A faster, GPU-friendly version of Fractal Noise: procedural cloud/turbulence noise with an extra Turbulence Factor but fewer sub-settings. Same uses: fog, smoke, organic textures and mattes.
- **Match Grain** *(needs_input)*: Samples the film grain / sensor noise of another layer and adds matching grain to this layer, so CG, graphics or clean stock sit in the same plate.
- **Median (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The older Median filter: replaces each pixel with the median of its neighbours, removing speckle at low radius and painting the image at high radius. Kept for old projects. -> AE replacement: Median (ADBE PS Median); Premiere: none in v26

### Other

- **2D Text Box** *(hidden)*: Control panel (pseudo effect) for Adobe's '2D Text Box' presets: it holds the padding, scale, offset and corner-rounding values that the preset's expressions use to draw an auto-sizing rounded box behind a text layer. On its own it renders nothing.
- **Animated Shape Control** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect control used by Adobe's animated shape presets (Box Swarm, Kaleidoscopic, Alien Face, Mandala, SpiroMandala): its sliders feed wiggle() expressions that animate the shapes' size, position and rounding. Renders nothing on its own.
- **Animated Shape Control** *(hidden)*: Two-control pseudo effect (Speed, Random Seed) used by Adobe's animated shape presets such as Nerve Net, Ring Chart, Wireframe Worm, Circular - cellular division and Dancing Knot; it sets how fast and with which random pattern their wiggle() expressions move. Renders nothing on its own.
- **Autoscroll - horizontal** *(hidden)*: Control for the 'Autoscroll - horizontal' behaviour preset: one Speed slider drives a Motion Tile expression that scrolls the layer's content sideways endlessly, wrapping seamlessly. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Autoscroll - vertical** *(hidden)*: Control for the 'Autoscroll - vertical' behaviour preset: one Speed slider drives a Motion Tile expression that scrolls the layer's content up or down endlessly, wrapping seamlessly. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Bounce** *(hidden)*: Parameter-holder pseudo effect for a decaying bounce behaviour (amplitude, frequency, decay, delay, repeat cycle). No preset installed with AE 2026 uses it, so it does nothing unless an expression on the layer reads it.
- **Bounce At Marker** *(hidden)*: Parameter-holder pseudo effect for a decaying bounce that retriggers at each layer marker (amplitude, frequency, decay). No installed preset uses it, so it does nothing unless an expression reads it.
- **Bounce On Beat** *(hidden)*: Parameter-holder pseudo effect for a bounce triggered whenever an audio-amplitude layer crosses a threshold (amplitude, frequency, decay, audio layer, threshold). Needs an 'Audio Amplitude' keyframe layer and an expression; renders nothing on its own.
- **Bounce Random** *(hidden)*: Parameter-holder pseudo effect for a decaying bounce that retriggers at random intervals between Min Delay and Max Delay seconds. No installed preset uses it, so it does nothing unless an expression reads it.
- **Card Wipe Master Control** *(hidden)*: Master slider for Adobe's three Card Wipe transition presets (2D fractured, 3D pixelstorm, 3D swing): one Transition Completion percentage drives the Card Wipe effect's completion plus its card rotation / jitter settings through expressions, and a checkbox flips the flip direction. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Chaser Control** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Chasing Line - Boxes' shape preset: a group of rectangles that steps horizontally across the full comp width and wraps around, like marquee chaser lights.
- **Color Swirl** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo-effect control panel with a Transition Completion slider and a Final Color swatch, apparently meant to drive a swirl-to-colour transition; no shipped AE preset uses it.
- **Corner Reveal** *(hidden)*: Legacy control panel for a corner reveal: the layer appears as a rectangle growing out of one chosen corner (a horizontal and a vertical Linear Wipe moving together), with a feathered edge. No AE 2026 preset uses this panel any more.
- **Counter Controls** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Counter Countdown Monospaced' text preset: turns a text layer into a rolling number counter with padding, decimals, locale grouping and optional prefix/suffix text.
- **Cracked Tiles** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Cracked Tiles' image preset: cuts the layer into a grid of Card Wipe cards that shrink apart (thin gaps) and jitter slightly, like a cracked tiled wall.
- **Crop Edges** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Crop Edges' utility preset: crops the same percentage off all four edges of the layer, optionally feathered.
- **Currency Controls** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Currency Counter Monospaced' text preset: a rolling money counter with currency symbol or code, decimals and locale grouping.
- **Dissolve - unmelt** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Dissolve - unmelt' transition preset: the layer fades in from transparent while a turbulent liquid warp eases out to clean (Transition Completion 0 = invisible and fully warped, 100 = clean and opaque).
- **Dissolve Master Control** *(hidden)*: Shared single-slider control panel used by several dissolve transition presets (vapor, dither, ripple, block dissolves) to drive their progress.
- **Drift Over Time** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Drift Over Time' behavior preset: moves the layer continuously in one direction at a constant speed from its in-point.
- **Face Measurements** *(hidden)*: Data container written by the AE Face Tracker ('Extract Face Measurements'): per-frame head offset, scale, orientation, eyebrow, eyelid, gaze and mouth values. It draws nothing.
- **Face Track Points** *(hidden)*: Data container written by the AE Face Tracker (Detailed Features): keyframed 2D and 3D positions of about 68 facial landmarks plus head position, scale and orientation. It draws nothing.
- **Fade In+Out - frames** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect (a block of sliders defined in PresetEffects.xml) that renders nothing by itself. It is the control panel of the Behaviors preset 'Fade In+Out - frames', whose expression fades the layer up from transparent at its in point and back down at its out point over the set number of frames.
- **Fade In+Out - msec** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect that renders nothing by itself. It is the control panel of the Behaviors preset 'Fade In+Out - msec', whose expression fades the layer in from its in point and out to its out point over durations given in milliseconds.
- **Fade Master Control** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect with a single Transition Completion slider; it draws nothing itself. It is the master control of the 'Transitions - Dissolves' presets Fade - dip to black, Fade - flash to white and Fade - overexposed (and the Legacy Boxes/Ovals shape-wipe presets), whose Tint and Solid Composite expressions read the slider.
- **Fly to Inset** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect that is the control panel of the Legacy preset 'Fly to Inset': as Transition Completion rises, the full-frame layer shrinks and travels to a corner picture-in-picture box with a bevelled coloured frame and drop shadow. On its own it renders nothing.
- **Follow** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect (control sliders, a Leader layer menu and checkboxes) intended for a follow-the-leader behaviour where a layer copies another layer's position, rotation, scale and opacity with a time delay. It renders nothing and no preset shipped with AE 2026 uses it, so on its own it has no effect.
- **Getting Jiggy** *(hidden)*: Adobe's sample pseudo-effect in PresetEffects.xml that shows most pseudo-effect parameter types (slider, percent, pixel, layer, colour, point, angle, checkbox, group) with joke names. No preset uses it; it does nothing visually and has no editing use.
- **Grid Wipe** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect that is the control panel of the 'Transitions - Wipes/Grid Wipe' preset: two perpendicular Venetian Blinds driven by expressions so the layer is revealed or removed through a growing grid of square cells. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Inset Video - framed** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect that is the control panel of the Legacy preset 'Inset Video - framed': the layer is cropped on all four sides by Linear Wipes, given a bevelled coloured frame and a drop shadow, and scaled into a picture-in-picture box. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Inset Video - torn edges** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect that is the control panel of the Legacy preset 'Inset Video - torn edges': the layer is cropped by Linear Wipes, its border roughened into a torn-paper edge and given a drop shadow, then scaled into an inset. Alone it renders nothing.
- **Iris Wipe Master Controls** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect with Transition Completion, an Invert Alpha checkbox and Feather, meant as the master control of an iris (circle) wipe built from other effects. It renders nothing and no preset in AE 2026 references it, so applied alone it has no visible effect.
- **Jiggle** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect with Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Delay and Cycle Time sliders, meant to drive a decaying wobble (bounce/jiggle) expression on a layer property. It renders nothing and no preset in AE 2026 uses it, so on its own nothing moves.
- **Jiggle At Marker** *(hidden)*: Pseudo-effect with Amplitude, Frequency and Decay sliders, meant to drive an expression that fires a decaying wobble at each layer marker. It renders nothing and no preset in AE 2026 uses it, so alone nothing moves.
- **Jiggle On Beat** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: a panel of sliders (Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Audio Threshold) plus a layer picker for an audio-keyframe layer, meant to drive a decaying jiggle that fires when the music crosses a threshold. On its own it renders nothing; the motion only exists if an expression on Position/Scale/Rotation reads these controls.
- **Jiggle Random** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: sliders for a decaying jiggle that should fire at random intervals between Min Delay and Max Delay seconds. It renders nothing by itself; an expression must read the sliders to move the layer.
- **Light Leaks - layer markers** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Light Leaks - layer markers' (Presets/Image - Special Effects). The full preset adds Calculations (used only as a value holder, no second layer) and Solid Composite with a white solid in Add mode; an expression raises the solid's Opacity to 50% centred on each layer marker, so the layer gets a short additive white flash (flash-frame) at every marker. Flash Width (msec) sets the total flash length. Despite the name it is a flat white flash, not a coloured organic light leak. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Light Leaks - random** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Light Leaks - random' (Presets/Image - Special Effects). The preset adds Calculations (value holder) and Solid Composite with a white solid in Add mode whose Opacity follows a noise() expression, so the layer brightens toward white in random, smoothly varying bursts (up to 50% white added). Chance of Flashing sets how much of the time it is lit, Flash Nervousness how fast it changes. At the default Chance 100 it never fully switches off (a constant shimmering wash); lower it to get separate flashes. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Mask Fade Controls** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel used by the Legacy mask-transition presets (Presets/Legacy: 'Boxes - concentric', 'Boxes - random 1/2/3', 'Ovals - concentric', 'Ovals - random 1/2', each in NTSC and PAL). The preset adds 5 fixed masks and Transition Completion drives their Mask Opacity in sequence (mask 1 fades in over 0-10, mask 2 over 20-30, mask 3 40-50, mask 4 60-70, mask 5 eases in over 80-100), so the layer appears box by box or oval by oval (concentric = centre outward, random = scattered shapes). The masks do not grow or move. Feather drives the mask feather. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Mood Lighting - amorphous** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Mood Lighting - amorphous' (Presets/Image - Creative). The preset adds Fractal Noise (Basic, Soft Linear noise, Contrast 225, Brightness -25) blended in Overlay mode, whose Scale, Evolution and Opacity are driven by these sliders, laying soft drifting cloud-like patches of light and shadow over the layer. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Mood Lighting - digital** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Mood Lighting - digital' (Presets/Image - Creative). The preset adds Fractal Noise (Threads type, Block noise, Contrast 170) blended in Overlay mode, whose Scale, Evolution and Opacity follow these sliders, giving a shifting blocky digital pattern of light and dark over the layer. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Mood Lighting - streaks** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Mood Lighting - streaks' (Presets/Image - Creative). The preset adds Fractal Noise (Threads type, Soft Linear noise, Brightness 33) blended in Soft Light and stretched to 4x the layer height, so the layer gets soft vertical bands of light that shimmer and morph as Evolution animates (the bands change in place; they do not travel across the frame). Streak Width sets band width, Intensity the opacity. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Opacity Flash - layer markers** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Opacity Flash - layer markers' (Presets/Behaviors). The preset adds Solid Composite (solid Opacity 0, so no colour is added) whose Source Opacity expression shows the layer only around each layer marker: it peaks at 100% on the marker and is gone Flash Width/2 before and after, so the layer blinks on at every marker. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Opacity Flash - random** *(hidden)*: Hidden control panel of the AE preset 'Opacity Flash - random' (Presets/Behaviors). The preset adds Solid Composite (solid Opacity 0) whose Source Opacity follows linear(noise(...)), so the layer fades in and out at random, smoothly rather than as hard cuts; at high Nervousness it reads as flicker. The sliders set how much of the time it is visible and how fast it changes. Added alone it does nothing.
- **Opacity Pulse** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: sliders (Min/Max Opacity, Attack, Decay, Delay, Cycle Time) meant to drive an opacity pulse that rises to Max and falls back to Min, optionally repeating. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Opacity must read the sliders.
- **Opacity Pulse At Marker** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Min/Max Opacity, Attack and Decay sliders meant to fire one opacity pulse at each layer marker. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Opacity must read the sliders and the layer's markers.
- **Opacity Pulse On Beat** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Min/Max Opacity, Attack, Decay and Audio Threshold sliders plus an audio-layer picker, meant to drive an opacity flash each time the music's amplitude crosses the threshold. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Opacity must read it.
- **Opacity Pulse Random** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Min/Max Opacity, Attack, Decay and Min/Max Delay sliders, meant to drive opacity flashes at random intervals (an irregular flicker). It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Opacity must read it.
- **Orbit** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Radius, Frequency and Starting Phase controls plus a 'Layer To Orbit' picker, meant to move a layer in a flat circle around another layer. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it.
- **Orbit 3D** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Radius, Frequency, Starting Phase, X/Z Tilt and Elevation controls plus a 'Layer To Orbit' picker, meant to move a 3D layer around another layer on a tilted ring so it passes in front of and behind it. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it.
- **Oscillate** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Direction, Delay and Cycle Time controls, meant to drive a damped back-and-forth shake along one axis, optionally repeating. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it.
- **Oscillate At Marker** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay and Direction controls, meant to fire a damped shake at each of the layer's markers. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it and the markers.
- **Oscillate On Beat** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Direction and Audio Threshold controls plus an audio-layer picker, meant to fire a damped shake whenever the music's amplitude crosses the threshold. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it.
- **Oscillate Random** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Direction, Random Direction and Min/Max Delay controls, meant to fire damped shakes at random intervals, optionally each in a random direction. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Position must read it.
- **Pattern Template** *(hidden)*: A pseudo-effect that only holds controls (Color, Pattern Size, Repeat Width/Height as % of comp, Animation Speed) for an Adobe pattern template's expressions. It draws nothing on its own, and no preset shipped with AE 2026 uses it.
- **Pendulum** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay, Delay and Cycle Time controls, meant to swing a layer's rotation around its anchor point like a pendulum that comes to rest, optionally repeating. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Rotation must read it.
- **Pendulum At Marker** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency and Decay controls, meant to start a damped pendulum swing at each of the layer's markers. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Rotation must read it and the markers.
- **Pendulum On Beat** *(hidden)*: Hidden pseudo effect: Amplitude, Frequency, Decay and Audio Threshold controls plus an audio-layer picker, meant to kick a layer into a damped pendulum swing whenever the music's amplitude crosses the threshold. It renders nothing by itself; an expression on Rotation must read it.
- **Pendulum Random** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: sliders for a pendulum swing (rotation that oscillates and dies away) that re-triggers at random intervals. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Rotation reads it.
- **Percentage Controls** *(hidden)*: Control panel for AE's 'Percentage Counter Monospaced' text preset: an animatable number plus formatting options that the preset's Source Text expression turns into a counting percentage such as 0% to 72%. On its own it shows nothing.
- **Pulse** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: sliders for a scale 'pop' that jumps and settles back, optionally repeating every Cycle Time seconds. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Scale reads it.
- **Pulse At Marker** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: sliders for a scale pop that fires at each layer marker and settles back. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Scale reads it.
- **Pulse On Beat** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: sliders plus an audio-layer picker for a scale pop that fires whenever an audio amplitude track crosses a threshold. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Scale reads it.
- **Pulse Random** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: sliders for a scale pop that re-fires at random intervals between Min Delay and Max Delay. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Scale reads it.
- **Radial Wipe Master Controls** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect from AE's legacy transition presets: a Transition Completion slider, Feather and two direction checkboxes meant to drive a Radial Wipe through expressions. On its own it shows nothing.
- **Random Fill Color** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: a base colour plus hue/saturation/lightness variation and timing sliders for a fill colour that drifts randomly over time. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on a Fill colour reads it.
- **Random Motion** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: timing and horizontal/vertical range sliders for a layer that drifts to random positions. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Position reads it.
- **Random Motion 1D** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: like Random Motion but along one axis (horizontal, or vertical when Vertical Motion is on). Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Position reads it.
- **Random Opacity** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: min/max opacity and timing sliders for a layer whose opacity drifts randomly between the two. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Opacity reads it.
- **Random Rotation** *(hidden)*: Control-only pseudo effect: min/max rotation and timing sliders for a layer that turns to random angles. Nothing changes on screen until an expression on Rotation reads it.
- **Random Rotation 3D** *(hidden)*: Expression-control pseudo effect: a panel of sliders (travel time, min/max X, Y and Z rotation, random start toggles) meant to drive a random 3D tumble of a layer. It draws nothing itself, and no preset shipped with AE 2026 wires expressions to it.
- **Random Scale** *(hidden)*: Expression-control pseudo effect holding settings for random scale changes (travel time, min/max scale, lock X and Y, random start toggles). It draws nothing itself, and no preset shipped with AE 2026 wires expressions to it.
- **Rotate Over Time** *(hidden)*: Control for the Behaviors preset 'Rotate Over Time': the preset adds this slider plus a Transform effect whose Rotation expression adds (time - inPoint) x the rate, so the layer spins continuously at a set speed. On its own the control does nothing.
- **Sample Image** *(hidden)*: Control for the preset 'Image - Utilities/Sample Image Expression': its Sampled Color Output colour is driven by sampleImage(Center, [Radius, Radius]) on the layer, so it continuously reads the average colour of an area of the image. It changes no pixels; other properties link to the sampled colour.
- **Scale Bounce - layer markers** *(hidden)*: Control for the Behaviors preset 'Scale Bounce - layer markers': at every layer marker the layer swells to the target scale and eases back to 100%, a pop that peaks exactly on the marker. On its own the control does nothing.
- **Scale Bounce - random** *(hidden)*: Control for the Behaviors preset 'Scale Bounce - random': noise over time randomly pushes the layer's scale from 100% toward the target scale, giving irregular nervous pops. On its own the control does nothing.
- **Separate XYZ Position** *(obsolete)* [untested: needs_setup]: Legacy control from the preset 'Legacy/Separate XYZ Position': three sliders whose values are ADDED to the layer's Position by an expression, so X, Y and Z could be keyframed independently. It is superseded by the native Separate Dimensions on Position. -> Premiere: Motion > Position (AE native replacement: Position > Separate Dimensions)
- **Separate XYZ Scale** *(hidden)*: Control for the Behaviors preset 'Separate XYZ Scale': three sliders are added to the layer's Scale through an expression ([X, Y, Z] + value), so X, Y and Z scale offsets can be keyframed separately with different eases. AE still cannot natively separate Scale dimensions.
- **Slide - variable** *(hidden)*: Orphaned transition control: a Transition Completion slider with an Entrance Angle and Initial Scale, meant to slide a layer in from an angle while scaling up. No preset shipped with AE 2026 references it, so it does nothing unless a script writes the expressions.
- **Slide Master Control** *(hidden)*: Master control for the 'Transitions - Movement' presets Slide - straight, Slide - drop and Slide - swoop: keyframing Transition Completion from 0 to 100 moves the layer into its resting position. Straight slides in horizontally by one layer width, drop slides in vertically by one layer height, swoop arcs in from a top corner while scaling up from 0%.
- **Stereo 3D Controls** *(hidden)*: The control effect AE puts on the 'Stereo 3D Controls' layer when you run Layer > Camera > Create Stereo 3D Rig: it sets the left/right camera separation (scene depth) and convergence of a stereoscopic camera pair. It changes no pixels and needs a 3D camera rig.
- **Stretch Master Control** *(hidden)*: Master control for the 'Transitions - Movement' presets Stretch & Slide, Stretch & Blur and Stretch Over - horizontal / vertical: keyframing Transition Completion from 0 to 100 snaps the layer from a stretched state to normal size. Stretch & Slide: width 2000% -> 100% while sliding in from the left and fading in over the first third; Stretch & Blur: width 500% / height 125% -> 100% as a Fast Box Blur clears and it eases in; Stretch Over: one axis grows from 0% while the other settles from 150% to 100%.
- **Stretch Master Control (edge)** *(hidden)*: A pseudo-effect (expression control container from PresetEffects.xml) holding a Transition Completion % slider and a '...the other side' checkbox for a legacy edge-stretch transition preset. On its own it renders nothing; it only drives expressions that read it.
- **Stretch Master Control(corner)** *(hidden)*: A pseudo-effect (expression control container) holding a Transition Completion % slider and a '...the other corner' checkbox for a legacy corner-stretch transition preset. By itself it changes nothing on screen.
- **Swarm** *(hidden)*: A pseudo-effect (expression control container) with Amplitude, Frequency and a Leader layer picker, meant for a 'swarm' behavior where layers jitter around a leader layer. It renders nothing by itself; the motion only exists if expressions read these controls.
- **Timer Controls** *(hidden)*: The control panel of the 'Text/Number Counters/Timer Countdown Monospaced' preset: an Animate Value slider plus formatting options that the preset's text expressions turn into a clock readout (MM:SS, HH:MM:SS or DD:HH:MM:SS) with optional unit labels. Alone, on a layer without the preset's expressions, it shows nothing.
- **Trace Path** *(hidden)*: The control added by AE's 'Create Nulls From Paths' panel (Trace Path button): a Progress % slider and a Loop checkbox that drive a null along a mask or shape path (position via pointOnPath, rotation auto-oriented to the path tangent). Alone, without the generated expressions and a path, it does nothing.
- **Transition Master Control** *(hidden)*: A pseudo-effect holding a single Transition Completion % slider, used as the one-knob controller for legacy multi-effect transition presets. It renders nothing by itself.
- **Wiggle - gelatin** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wiggle - gelatin' preset: speed and amount sliders that the preset's Transform-effect expressions use to wiggle Skew (by Wiggle Amount) while Skew Axis also wanders randomly (+/-180 deg), so the layer wobbles like jelly in changing directions. Scale is NOT touched. Added alone (without the preset) it does nothing.
- **Wiggle - position** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wiggle - position' preset: speed and pixel-amount sliders that the preset's Transform-effect expressions use to jitter the layer's position randomly (a handheld / floating drift). Alone it does nothing.
- **Wiggle - rotation** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wiggle - rotation' preset: speed and angle sliders that the preset's Transform expressions use to rock the layer randomly back and forth. Alone it does nothing.
- **Wiggle - scale** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wiggle - scale' preset: speed and % amount sliders (with optional separate width wiggle) that the preset's Transform expressions use to pulse the layer's size randomly. Alone it does nothing.
- **Wiggle - shear** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wiggle - shear' preset: speed and amount sliders that the preset's Transform-effect expressions use to skew the layer back and forth randomly along one fixed axis, a wobbly, rubbery lean. Alone it does nothing.
- **Wigglerama** *(hidden)*: Control panel of the 'Behaviors/Wigglerama' preset: one panel that wiggles position, rotation and scale together (with a nervousness/octaves control) through the preset's Transform-effect expressions, giving an all-round random float or shake. Alone it does nothing.
- **Wipe Master Control** *(hidden)*: Master slider of AE's wipe transition presets Barn Doors, Clam Shell and Paint On (Transitions - Wipes): one 'Transition Completion' value drives the preset's two Linear Wipes (+ Invert) or its Scribble reveal through expressions. On its own it shows nothing.
- **Wipe Master Controls** *(hidden)*: Feathered variant of the wipe master control: a 'Transition Completion' slider plus a 'Feather' slider meant to drive a wipe's progress and edge softness through expressions. No installed preset uses it, so it does nothing visible on its own.
- **Wobble Bounce** *(hidden)*: Expression-control set (Dan Ebberts behaviour family) for a decaying bounce with a secondary wobble, starting after a delay and optionally repeating. It has no expressions attached, so alone it does nothing visible.
- **Wobble Bounce At Marker** *(hidden)*: Marker-triggered version of the Wobble Bounce controls: the bounce-plus-wobble is meant to restart at each layer marker. Only sliders; no expressions ship with it.
- **Wobble Bounce On Beat** *(hidden)*: Audio-triggered version of the Wobble Bounce controls: the bounce is meant to fire whenever an audio-amplitude layer crosses a threshold. Only sliders plus a layer picker; nothing animates on its own.
- **Wobble Bounce Random** *(hidden)*: Random-interval version of the Wobble Bounce controls: the bounce-plus-wobble is meant to re-fire after a random pause between Min and Max Delay seconds. Only sliders; no expressions ship with it.
- **Z Spring** *(hidden)*: Expression-control set (Dan Ebberts behaviour family) for a layer that springs in along the Z axis from an offset, overshoots and settles, with random drift and rotational wobble. No expressions ship with it, so alone it does nothing.
- **Z Spring At Marker** *(hidden)*: Marker-triggered version of the Z Spring controls: the Z-axis spring with drift and rotational wobble is meant to restart at each layer marker. Only sliders; no expressions ship with it.
- **Zoom - 2D spin** *(hidden)*: Master control of the 'Zoom - 2D spin' transition preset: as Transition Completion goes 0 -> 100 the layer scales up from 0 to 100 % while rotating from minus Spin Amount back to 0 (via a Transform effect).
- **Zoom - 3D tumble** *(hidden)*: Master control of the 'Zoom - 3D tumble' transition preset: the layer scales up from 0 while Basic 3D swivels and tilts it from the X/Y tumble angles back to flat, so it tumbles into frame.
- **Zoom - bubble** *(hidden)*: Master control of the 'Zoom - bubble' transition preset: the layer grows from 0 to full size (ease-out over the first two thirds) while a Spherize bulge that starts at half the layer diagonal flattens to 0 near the end, like a bubble popping into place.
- **Zoom - spiral** *(hidden)*: Master control of the 'Zoom - spiral' transition preset: as Transition Completion goes 0 -> 100 the layer scales up from 0 to 100 % while it circles inward from Spiral Size pixels off its centre (radius shrinking to 0) between the start and end angles, so it swirls into place.
- **Zoom - wobble** *(hidden)*: Master control of the 'Zoom - wobbling' transition preset (Transitions - Movement): as Transition Completion goes 0 -> 100 a Transform effect scales the layer up from 0 to 100 % while its Skew, Skew Axis and Rotation unwind from Wobble Amount-based angles to 0, so the layer grows in with a sheared, swirling wobble and lands flat. Linear mapping, no elastic overshoot or bounce.

### Perspective

- **Bevel Alpha** [pass]: Adds a chiselled, lit bevel along the alpha edges of a layer, giving text, logos and cut-outs a raised embossed look. On a full-frame clip it only bevels the outer frame edge.
- **Bevel Edges** [pass]: Gives the layer's rectangular bounds a hard, lit 3D bevel, like a tile or a raised picture frame. Unlike Bevel Alpha it uses the layer edges, not the alpha shape.
- **CC Cylinder** [pass]: Wraps the layer around a pseudo-3D cylinder with its own light and shading, so a flat image becomes a rotating drum or column. The layer's width runs around the cylinder's circumference.
- **CC Sphere** [pass]: Wraps the layer onto a shaded pseudo-3D sphere, turning a flat image into a ball or globe with its own light, specular highlight and optional reflection map.
