- **CC Spotlight** [pass]: Simulates a theatrical spotlight cone falling on the layer: a lit ellipse with soft edges while the rest of the frame drops into darkness; an optional gel layer can tint/pattern the light.
- **Drop Shadow** [pass]: Adds a shadow behind the layer's alpha shape, offset by direction and distance and softened by blur, so text, logos and cut-outs lift off the background.
- **Radial Shadow** [pass]: Casts a shadow from a point light source rather than a fixed direction, so the shadow stretches and changes angle depending on where the light sits; Glass Edge mode tints the shadow with the layer's colours.
- **3D Camera Tracker** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_analysis]: Analyses moving footage, solves the real camera's 3D motion and lets you create an AE camera plus null/solid/text anchors that stick to surfaces in the shot. It does not change the picture itself; only coloured track points show. Until the background solve finishes, renders carry AE's blue 'Analyzing in background' banner.
- **3D Glasses** *(needs_input)*: Combines a left-eye and a right-eye layer into a stereoscopic image: red/cyan style anaglyph, side-by-side, over-under, interlaced or difference views, with convergence and vertical alignment.
- **CC Environment** *(needs_input)*: Maps an environment image (spherical / light-probe / vertical-cross panorama) chosen from another layer onto the layer as a backdrop that turns with the active 3D camera, like a cheap sky dome.

### Simulation

- **CC Ball Action** [pass]: Rebuilds the layer as a grid of shaded 3D balls coloured from the image, which can be scattered, twisted and rotated in pseudo-3D for disintegrate / reassemble effects.
- **CC Bubbles** [pass]: Replaces the layer with a stream of shiny, wobbling bubbles that rise up the frame, each bubble textured with the colours of the source image.
- **CC Drizzle** [pass]: Simulates raindrops hitting a liquid surface: expanding, lit concentric ripples that displace and shade the layer as if it were a puddle.
- **CC Mr. Mercury** [pass]: Turns the layer into a stream of liquid-metal blobs that are emitted from a producer point, merge like mercury and fall under gravity, each blob textured by the source image and lit with specular shine.
- **CC Particle Systems II** [pass]: Built-in 2D particle generator: emits lines, stars or spheres from a producer with velocity, gravity and resistance, shifting from a birth colour to a death colour. It replaces the layer's pixels (the source image disappears), so it is applied on a solid and composited with Screen/Add.
- **CC Particle World** [pass]: 3D particle generator that emits lines, polygons, spheres or textured sprites from a producer point, with gravity, floor bounce and an internal effect camera. Particles fade from a birth colour to a death colour and can be composited over the layer.
- **CC Pixel Polly** [pass]: Breaks the layer into a grid of polygons or squares that fly apart from a force centre, spin and fall under gravity, like a shattered pane.
- **CC Rainfall** [pass]: Generates falling rain streaks with depth, wind and spread, optionally picking up colour from the background, composited over the layer.
- **CC Scatterize** [pass]: Scatters the layer's pixels into a cloud of dust particles, with optional twist on the left and right halves; animated it makes a layer disintegrate into dust.
- **CC Snowfall** [pass]: Generates 3D snowflakes falling with wind, wiggle and depth variation, lit by the background, composited over the layer.
- **CC Star Burst** [pass]: Turns the layer into a grid of spheres coloured by its pixels that fly towards the viewer, creating a warp-speed starfield.
- **Card Dance** [pass]: Splits the layer into a grid of cards that can move, rotate and scale individually in 3D, each card's amount driven by a gradient layer, with lighting and a back face.
- **Foam** [pass]: Particle simulation of bubbles: produces, floats, wobbles and pops bubbles with physics, wind, a flow map and optional reflection map.
- **Particle Playground** [pass]: Legacy particle system: a cannon, grid or exploded layer emits dot or text particles driven by gravity, repel, walls and layer-based property maps.
- **Shatter** [pass]: Breaks the layer into extruded 3D pieces (bricks, glass, hexagons, puzzle and more) that explode outward from force spheres, tumble and fall, with lighting and textured faces.
- **Wave World** [pass]: Simulates water waves on a grid from ring or line producers and outputs a greyscale height map (or a wireframe preview), mainly as a source map for Caustics or Displacement Map.
- **CC Hair** *(needs_input)* [weak]: Grows lit 2D hair / fur strands from every pixel of the layer, coloured from a fixed colour or inherited from the image, with length, weight, density and an optional map layer controlling growth. Needs a layer with alpha (text, logo, shape or cut-out) or a Map Layer to read as fur: on a full-frame opaque photo the strands inherit the photo's colours and only smear it into a washed-out painterly blur with jagged fringes along edges.
- **Caustics** *(needs_input)*: Renders the light patterns and refraction of water: a bottom layer seen through a rippling water surface with tinted, lit caustic highlights.

### Stylize

- **Brush Strokes** [weak]: Smears the layer into short directional brush strokes along a stroke angle; on a 1920x1080 frame even the harvested maxima (Brush Size 5, Stroke Length 40, Stroke Density 2) only give a subtle smeared, jagged-edged texture, so a clearly painted look needs the effect on a scaled-down precomp.
- **CC Block Load** [pass]: Simulates a progressive-JPEG style image load: the layer builds up as large blocks that refine pass by pass into the full-resolution picture as Completion rises to 100.
- **CC Burn Film** [pass]: Burns spreading holes through the layer like celluloid melting in a projector: dark scorched blotches grow out from around the Center and their cores become transparent.
- **CC Glass** [pass]: Turns the layer into a lit, refracting glass surface: a bump map (the layer itself by default) adds height, specular highlights and displacement so the image looks embossed under glass.
- **CC HexTile** [pass]: Rebuilds the frame as a honeycomb of hexagonal tiles, each tile repeating the image area around the centre, giving a hex-mosaic / insect-eye pattern.
- **CC Kaleida** [pass]: Kaleidoscope: mirrors a wedge of the image around a centre point in one of several mirror patterns, producing symmetrical trippy imagery.
- **CC Mr. Smoothie** [pass]: Replaces the image with smooth flowing colour bands: it samples the colours along a line between Sample A and Sample B and maps them onto the frame by a property (e.g. luminance) of the flow layer, giving a psychedelic liquid-colour look.
- **CC Plastic** [pass]: Lights the layer as a glossy plastic or metal surface using a bump layer (the layer itself by default), giving embossed relief with specular highlights and optional dust.
- **CC Threshold** [pass]: Converts the image to pure black and white at a chosen threshold level (based on a selected channel), giving a stark photocopy / stencil look.
- **CC Threshold RGB** [pass]: Thresholds the red, green and blue channels separately, reducing the image to the eight pure primary/secondary colours for a loud posterized pop-art look.
- **CC Vignette** [pass]: Darkens (or with negative Amount, brightens) the frame edges with a lens-like falloff around a movable centre, optionally protecting highlights.
- **Cartoon** [pass]: Turns live action into a flat cel-shaded cartoon: smooths detail into a few flat shading bands and draws dark ink outlines along strong edges.
- **Color Emboss** [pass]: Embosses the image like a raised relief while keeping its original colours, so edges get light/dark bevelled ridges.
- **Emboss** [pass]: Converts the image to a grey bas-relief: flat grey everywhere except light and dark ridges along edges, like a stamped metal plate.
- **Find Edges** [pass]: Replaces the image with its detected edges: coloured lines on white (or glowing lines on black when inverted), like a sketch or X-ray outline.
- **Glow** [pass]: Finds the bright parts of the image (or alpha edges) and blooms them outward into a soft light halo, for neon, highlight bloom and dreamy looks.
- **Mosaic** [pass]: Pixelates the layer into a grid of solid-colour blocks.
- **Motion Tile** [pass]: Repeats the layer as tiles beyond its own edges, optionally mirrored, so you can move, shake or zoom the image without exposing empty edges.
- **Posterize** [pass]: Reduces each colour channel to a few tonal levels, producing flat colour bands like a screen print or poster.
- **Roughen Edges** [pass]: Distorts the layer's alpha edges with fractal noise so they look torn, eroded, rusty or photocopied; on a full-frame clip it roughens the frame border.
- **Scatter** [pass]: Randomly displaces pixels within a radius, dissolving the image into a frosted, diffused or sand-like scatter.
- **Strobe Light** [pass]: Flashes the layer to a solid colour (or makes it transparent) on a fixed clock: each Period starts in the strobe colour, which holds for Period minus Duration, then the picture shows for the last Duration (Duration 0.1 / Period 0.5 = a 0.4 s flash twice a second). The clock is the layer's own, from its source's first frame. Measured under aerender frame for frame the same as Premiere's Strobe Light (same plug-in); Random Strobe Probablity between 0 and 100 turns it into random bursts.
- **Threshold** [pass]: Turns the image into pure black and white: every pixel brighter than the Level becomes white, every darker pixel becomes black.
- **CC RepeTile** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Extends the layer beyond its own edges by tiling copies of it (mirrored, repeated, brick etc.) to the right, left, top and bottom.
- **Texturize** *(needs_input)*: Embosses the luminance of another layer (canvas, paper, brick, concrete) into this layer, so the image looks printed or painted on that texture.

### Text

- **Timecode** [pass]: Burns a timecode or frame-number readout (from the layer's source, the comp, or a custom counter) onto the layer, optionally on a black box.
- **Numbers** *(unusable)*: Generates numeric text on the layer (random numbers, plain numbers, timecode, date/time or hex), with font and style picked in a modal options dialog.

### Time

- **CC Force Motion Blur** [pass]: Forces real motion blur onto a layer by rendering it at several sub-frame times and averaging them. It works on precomps, animated effects and text animators that the normal motion-blur switch misses.
- **CC Wide Time** [pass]: Averages a number of frames before and after the current one into each frame, so moving subjects leave ghostly, long-exposure-style smears.
- **Echo** [pass]: Composites copies of the layer from earlier (or later) frames onto the current frame, so moving subjects leave ghost trails or strobe-style echoes.
- **Pixel Motion Blur** [pass]: Adds natural motion blur to moving footage: it tracks pixel motion between frames with optical flow and smears along the motion vectors.
- **Posterize Time** [pass]: Locks the layer to a lower frame rate, so motion becomes choppy and stuttered like stop-motion or low-fps animation.
- **Time Difference** [pass]: Shows the colour difference between the layer and a target layer (by default itself) offset in time, so only change and motion stand out. Static areas go flat grey, or black with Absolute Difference.
- **Time Displacement** [pass]: Shifts each pixel in time according to the brightness of a map layer (by default the layer itself), so different parts of the frame show different moments. The result is a slit-scan / rolling time-warp smear.
- **Timewarp** [pass]: Retimes the layer (slow motion, speed-up or keyframed source-frame mapping), with optional optical-flow frame interpolation (Pixel Motion) and motion blur for smooth slow-mo.

### Transition

- **Block Dissolve** [pass]: Transition effect that makes the layer vanish in random rectangular blocks as Transition Completion goes from 0 to 100%, revealing whatever is underneath.
- **CC Grid Wipe** [pass]: Wipes the layer out through a grid of tiles that each open with a radial, door or rectangular pattern, revealing what is underneath.
- **CC Image Wipe** [pass]: Wipes the layer away in an order driven by a chosen channel (luminance, colour, alpha...) of a gradient layer, with a soft edge, like a configurable Gradient Wipe.
- **CC Jaws** [pass]: Splits the layer along a line into two halves with toothed, jagged edges that pull apart like opening jaws, revealing what is behind.
- **CC Light Wipe** [pass]: Wipes the layer away from a centre point in a round, square or door shape while flooding the remaining image with the light colour, so the picture burns toward white as the hole grows, like a burst of light eating the frame.
- **CC Line Sweep** [pass]: Wipes the layer off along one sweeping staircase edge: right-angled steps whose long faces lie square to the Direction angle and whose short faces are Thickness px tall, the whole staircase tilted a little off the Direction line by Slant. Reads as one stepped diagonal wipe, not a set of stripes.
- **CC Radial ScaleWipe** [pass]: Opens a circular hole from a centre point and pushes the image outward around it with a radial stretch, so the frame looks sucked outward as the hole grows.
- **CC Scale Wipe** [pass]: Stretches the image from a centre point in a chosen direction, smearing its pixels across the frame like a scan-line stretch; animating Stretch makes a smear-in/smear-out transition.
- **CC Twister** [pass]: Twists the layer around an axis like a ribbon or page being wrung, with shading, so it flips over to reveal its backside layer.
- **CC WarpoMatic** [pass]: Warps and dissolves the layer into a reveal layer, with the warp and blend order driven by image brightness or contrast, giving a liquid morph-like transition.
- **Card Wipe** [pass]: Breaks the layer into a grid of 3D cards that flip over in sequence to show a back layer, with its own camera, lighting and position/rotation jitter.
- **Gradient Wipe** [pass]: Wipes the layer away in the order of a gradient layer's luminance (dark pixels disappear first), with optional soft edge: the classic luma-matte transition.
- **Iris Wipe** [pass]: Cuts a growing polygon or star-shaped hole (6-32 points) out of the layer from a centre point, revealing whatever is underneath; a classic iris / shape-wipe transition.
- **Linear Wipe** [pass]: Wipes the layer to transparent along a straight edge at any angle, with an optional soft feathered edge; also widely used as a gradient fade mask.
- **Radial Wipe** [pass]: Sweeps the layer to transparent like a clock hand around a centre point, clockwise, counter-clockwise or both ways at once.
- **Venetian Blinds** [pass]: Wipes the layer away in parallel stripes like closing venetian blinds; stripe width, angle and edge softness are adjustable.
- **CC Glass Wipe** *(needs_input)* [weak]: Wipes the layer away through a refractive, melting-glass edge: a gradient layer's luminance decides which areas go first, and the edge displaces the image like liquid glass while the reveal layer shows through. Needs a Layer to Reveal (and ideally a textured Gradient Layer) set by script: both default to the clip itself, so a spec-only apply wipes nothing and only smears the picture slightly along the glassy edge.

### Utility

- **Cineon Converter** [pass]: Converts between Cineon/DPX logarithmic film encoding and linear video values, with control over black point, white point, gamma and highlight roll-off.
- **Color Profile Converter** [pass]: Converts the layer's colours from one ICC colour profile to another (e.g. sRGB to Rec.709 or a linear profile), with rendering intent and black-point compensation.
- **HDR Compander** [pass]: Compresses HDR (32-bpc) pixel values into the 0-1 range by a gain factor so 8/16-bit-only effects can process them, then expands them back with a second instance; on its own it darkens or brightens the image.
- **Apply Color LUT** *(needs_input)*: Applies a colour look-up table file (.cube, .3dl, .look or .csp) to the layer, e.g. a camera log-to-Rec.709 conversion or a creative film look.
- **CC Overbrights** *(needs_input)*: Diagnostic view that flags pixels whose values go outside the 0-1 range (overbrights) in a 32-bpc HDR project, painting them in a clip colour; channel views are selectable.
- **Grow Bounds** *(needs_input)*: Enlarges the layer's rendering bounds by a number of pixels so following effects (glow, shadow, distortions) on shape, text or vector layers are not cut off at the layer edge; invisible by itself.
- **HDR Highlight Compression** *(needs_input)*: Tone-maps the highlights of a high-dynamic-range (32-bpc) image down into the displayable range, keeping detail that would otherwise clip to white.

### _Obsolete

- **3D Glasses (Obsolete)** *(obsolete)*: Old version of 3D Glasses: combines a left-eye and a right-eye layer into a stereoscopic image (anaglyph red/blue or red/green, side-by-side, over-under, interlaced or a difference view). -> AE replacement: 3D Glasses (ADBE 3D Glasses2, Perspective category; its 3D View list has 12 entries in a different order, so indices do not transfer); Premiere: none
- **Alpha Levels** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Levels for the alpha channel only, on a 0-255 scale (probably the 8-bit version): remaps transparency with input/output black and white points and gamma. It tightens or softens a keyed or masked edge; on fully opaque footage only Output White Level changes anything (it fades the whole layer). Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: Levels (Individual Controls) (ADBE Pro Levels2) Alpha controls, or Simple Choker / Matte Choker for edges; Premiere: no alpha-levels effect (Alpha Adjust only scales opacity; Ultra Key Matte Cleanup chokes/softens a keyed edge)
- **Alpha Levels** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Levels for the alpha channel only, with values on a 0-1 scale (likely the higher-bit-depth version, unverified): remaps transparency with input/output black and white points and gamma. It tightens or softens keyed or masked edges; on opaque footage only Output White Level has an effect. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: Levels (Individual Controls) (ADBE Pro Levels2) Alpha controls, or Simple Choker / Matte Choker for edges; Premiere: no alpha-levels effect (Alpha Adjust only scales opacity; Ultra Key Matte Cleanup chokes/softens a keyed edge)
- **Apply Color LUT** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Legacy version of Apply Color LUT: grades the layer through a LUT file (.cube, .3dl and similar) chosen in a file dialog when the effect is applied in the UI. With no LUT loaded it leaves the image unchanged. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu.
- **Box Blur** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy box blur: averages neighbouring pixels in a square. More Iterations make it smooth and close to a Gaussian blur; 1 iteration gives a blocky, boxy blur. Same controls, ranges and dropdown as the current Fast Box Blur. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: Fast Box Blur (ADBE Box Blur2); Premiere: Fast Blur / Gaussian Blur
- **Brightness & Contrast** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy brightness and contrast: shifts every tone up or down and stretches or flattens the tonal range. The old maths clips highlights and shadows quickly at large values (same behaviour as 'Use Legacy' in the current effect). Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: Brightness & Contrast (ADBE Brightness & Contrast 2; its 'Use Legacy (supports HDR)' checkbox gives the old behaviour); Premiere: Brightness & Contrast
- **CC Composite (obsolete)** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_second_layer]: Legacy CC Composite: blends the layer's original, un-effected image back with the result of the effects above it in the effect stack, using a blend mode and opacity. The current CC Composite (CS Composite) can also take another layer as Top Layer. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu.
- **CC PS Classic (obsolete)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy CC Particle Systems: replaces the layer's image with animated particles on black that burst out, fade from a birth colour to a death colour and fall or drift with gravity and turbulence (the default Animation draws four-point star sparkles). Superseded by CC Particle Systems II. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: CC Particle Systems II (CC Particle Systems II) or CC Particle World; Premiere: none
- **CC PS LE Classic (obsolete)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy light edition of CC Particle Systems: emits particles from a Producer point spread over an area set by X/Y Radius, with gravity, turbulence and a birth-to-death colour fade. Superseded by CC Particle Systems II. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: CC Particle Systems II (CC Particle Systems II) or CC Particle World; Premiere: none
- **CC Rain (obsolete)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy rain generator: draws falling rain streaks over the layer with controls for amount, angle, drop size and opacity. Superseded by CC Rainfall, which adds scene depth, wind and background reflection. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: CC Rainfall (CSRainfall); Premiere: none native (overlay a rain clip in Screen mode)
- **CC Snow (obsolete)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy snow generator: draws drifting snowflakes over the layer, with amount, fall speed, sideways wobble and flake size. Superseded by CC Snowfall. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu. -> AE replacement: CC Snowfall (CSSnowfall); Premiere: none native (overlay a snow clip in Screen mode)
- **CC Time Blend** *(needs_input)* [broken]: Accumulates frames over time so anything moving leaves smeared, fading trails (an accumulating echo). The Transfer mode and Accumulation decide how bright and how long the trails are. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu.
- **CC Time Blend FX** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_second_layer]: Two-instance version of CC Time Blend: the Instance popup marks a start copy and an end copy, so only the effects placed between the two copies get accumulated into smeared motion trails. Listed in the hidden '_Obsolete' category (kept so old projects open), not in the normal Effects menu.
- **Cineon Converter** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Cineon Converter: converts 10-bit logarithmic Cineon/DPX film-scan footage to linear (or linear to log) using film black/white points, gamma and a highlight roll-off. On normal footage it shifts contrast and brightness heavily. -> AE replacement: Cineon Converter (ADBE Cineon Converter2, Utility); Premiere: Cineon Converter (Utility), which runs this same legacy engine (AE.ADBE Cineon Converter) with a 0-based Conversion Type (0 Linear to Log, 1 Log to Linear, 2 Log to Log)
- **Color Balance** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old three-slider Color Balance: shifts the whole image toward or away from red, green and blue (-100 to 100 each) to add or remove a global colour cast. It has no shadow/midtone/highlight split. -> AE replacement: Color Balance (ADBE Color Balance 2, Color Correction); Premiere: Color Balance (RGB) or Lumetri Color
- **Exposure** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Exposure: brightens or darkens the image in photographic stops, with Offset (lifts/crushes blacks) and Gamma, either for the master or per RGB channel, computed in linear light. -> AE replacement: Exposure (ADBE Exposure2, Color Correction); Premiere: Lumetri Color (Basic Correction > Exposure)
- **Fast Blur (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy fast blur: an approximated Gaussian-style blur of the whole layer, horizontal, vertical or both. Superseded by the GPU Gaussian Blur and Fast Box Blur. -> AE replacement: Gaussian Blur (ADBE Gaussian Blur 2) or Fast Box Blur (ADBE Box Blur2); Premiere: Fast Blur (AE.ADBE Fast Blur, this same engine, dropdown 0-based) or Gaussian Blur (Legacy) for the same Blurriness-style controls (Premiere's new 'Gaussian Blur' uses different property names)
- **Levels** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Levels: remaps input black/white points, midtone gamma and output black/white to set contrast and brightness. Values are normalised 0-1. -> AE replacement: Levels (ADBE Easy Levels2, Color Correction); Premiere: Levels (different names and units: '(RGB) Black Input Level' 0-255, '(RGB) Gamma' x100) or Lumetri Color
- **Levels (Individual Controls)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Levels (Individual Controls): the same black/white/gamma remapping as Levels but with every channel (RGB master, Red, Green, Blue, Alpha) exposed as separate properties, so it can tint and grade per channel. -> AE replacement: Levels (Individual Controls) (ADBE Pro Levels2, Color Correction); Premiere: Levels (has per-channel '(R)/(G)/(B) ...' controls, 0-255 units, gamma x100) or Lumetri Color (RGB Curves)
- **Noise** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Noise: adds random per-pixel noise (colour or monochrome) that changes every frame, for a grainy video-noise texture. -> AE replacement: Noise (ADBE Noise2, Noise & Grain); Premiere: Noise (AE.ADBE Noise2; there the checkboxes are named 'Use Color Noise' and 'Clip Result Values')
- **Noise Alpha** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Noise Alpha: adds random noise to the layer's alpha channel, so the layer becomes speckled or dissolves in noisy patches over whatever is underneath. -> AE replacement: Noise Alpha (ADBE Noise Alpha2, Noise & Grain); no Premiere equivalent in v26
- **Noise HLS** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Noise HLS: adds noise separately to hue, lightness and saturation, with an optional film-grain mode and grain size. The noise is static unless Noise Phase is animated. -> AE replacement: Noise HLS (ADBE Noise HLS2, Noise & Grain); Premiere: Noise (no HLS variant in v26)
- **Noise HLS Auto** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Noise HLS Auto: the same hue/lightness/saturation noise as Noise HLS, but it animates by itself at a set speed, giving moving grain with no keyframes. -> AE replacement: Noise HLS Auto (ADBE Noise HLS Auto2, Noise & Grain); Premiere: Noise (no HLS variant in v26)
- **Paint** *(hidden)*: Internal container effect AE adds when you paint on a layer with the Brush, Clone Stamp or Eraser tools; it holds the paint strokes. It is not in the Effects panel and with no strokes it shows nothing.
- **Photo Filter** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of Photo Filter: mimics a coloured lens filter (warming, cooling, coloured or custom) laid over the image at a set density, optionally keeping brightness unchanged. -> AE replacement: Photo Filter (ADBE PhotoFilterPS, Color Correction); Premiere: Lumetri Color (Temperature/Tint or Creative tints)
- **Photoshop Bevel And Emboss** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Photoshop-5-style bevel/emboss kept so pre-CS3 projects (where PSD layer effects were converted into these 'Photoshop' effects) still open. Applied in AE 2026 it renders only the bevel pass, a highlight edge on the lit side and a shadow edge opposite, traced along the layer's alpha, and drops the layer's own pixels: the opaque test photo turned black with a thin white highlight along the top and left frame edges. -> AE replacement: Layer > Layer Styles > Bevel and Emboss, or Bevel Alpha (ADBE Bevel Alpha); Premiere 2026: none (Bevel Alpha is not in the harvested Premiere effect list) - bake the bevel in AE/Photoshop or a MOGRT
- **Photoshop Drop Shadow** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Photoshop-5-style drop shadow kept only for old projects. Applied in AE 2026 it renders only the shadow (the layer's alpha offset by Angle/Distance, blurred and filled with Color) and drops the layer's own pixels: on the opaque test photo the whole frame went black, a full-frame black shadow over the black comp. The Drop Shadow effect or layer style replaces it. -> AE replacement: Drop Shadow (ADBE Drop Shadow) or Layer Styles > Drop Shadow; Premiere: Drop Shadow
- **Photoshop Inner Glow** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Photoshop-5-style glow painted inward from the edges (or from the centre) of the layer's alpha, kept only so old projects open. Applied in AE 2026 it renders only the glow and drops the layer's own pixels: the opaque test photo turned black with a faint pale-yellow line around the frame border. The Inner Glow layer style replaces it. -> AE replacement: Layer Styles > Inner Glow; Premiere: none direct (Alpha Glow glows outward only)
- **Photoshop Inner Shadow** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Photoshop-5-style shadow cast inside the layer's alpha edges, kept only for old projects. Applied in AE 2026 it renders only the inner shadow and drops the layer's own pixels: on the opaque test photo the whole frame went black (black shadow on the black comp). The Inner Shadow layer style replaces it. -> AE replacement: Layer Styles > Inner Shadow; Premiere: none direct
- **Photoshop Outer Glow** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Photoshop-5-style glow spreading from the layer's alpha in one colour, kept only for old projects. Applied in AE 2026 it renders only the glow and drops the layer's own pixels: an opaque full-frame layer turns into a flat field of the glow colour (pale yellow #FAFABE by default, slightly darker at the frame edge). The Outer Glow layer style or the Glow effect replaces it. -> AE replacement: Layer Styles > Outer Glow, or Glow (ADBE Glo2) with Glow Based On = 1 (Alpha Channel) and Glow Colors = 2 (A & B Colors); Premiere: Alpha Glow
- **Photoshop Solid Fill** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy translation of Photoshop's Color Overlay layer effect (stored in PSDs as 'Solid Fill'): replaces the layer's visible pixels with one flat colour while keeping its alpha. Kept only for old projects; Fill or the Color Overlay layer style replaces it. -> AE replacement: Fill (ADBE Fill) or Layer Styles > Color Overlay; Premiere: none direct (Tint with Map Black To = Map White To approximates it)
- **Puppet** *(needs_input)*: Mesh deformation driven by pins: the Puppet Position, Starch, Bend and Advanced Pin tools place pins on a layer and moving/keyframing them bends the image like a puppet. It is applied by the Puppet tools, not the Effects panel, and does nothing until pins exist.
- **Refine Matte** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy (CS5-era) matte cleanup that smooths, feathers, chokes and de-chatters an existing alpha matte, with motion blur for moving edges and edge-colour decontamination. Its direct successor is Refine Hard Matte (same control set with renamed edge controls); Refine Soft Matte is the newer edge-detail variant for hair and soft edges. -> AE replacement: Refine Hard Matte (ADBE RefineRBMatte), or Refine Soft Matte (ADBE RefineMatte2) for hair (Matte category); Premiere: Ultra Key > Matte Cleanup (Choke, Soften)
- **Roto Brush & Refine Edge** *(needs_input)*: AI-assisted rotoscoping: separates a subject (person or object) from its background using brush strokes painted in the Layer panel and propagated through the shot, with Refine Edge for hair and soft edges. Output is the subject on a transparent background.
- **Set Matte** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older version of Set Matte: replaces this layer's alpha with a channel (alpha, luminance, a colour channel and so on) taken from another layer, so any layer can matte another anywhere in the stack. The current Set Matte (ADBE Set Matte3) replaces it. -> AE replacement: Set Matte (ADBE Set Matte3) or the layer Track Matte menu; Premiere: Track Matte Key
- **Three-Way Color Corrector** *(obsolete)* [weak]: Legacy Premiere-era colour corrector: hue-balance wheels, saturation and levels for shadows, midtones and highlights separately, plus a secondary correction limited by hue/saturation/luma. Lumetri Color replaces it in both apps. -> AE replacement: Lumetri Color (ADBE Lumetri) Color Wheels; Premiere: Lumetri Color (Three-Way Color Corrector is not in the harvested Premiere 2026 effect list, so it cannot be applied there either)
- **Threshold** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older version of Threshold: turns the image into pure black and white, with pixels brighter than Level going white and darker ones black. The current Threshold (ADBE Threshold2, Stylize) replaces it. -> AE replacement: Threshold (ADBE Threshold2, Stylize; its Level is on a 0-1 scale, default 0.5); Premiere 2026: no Threshold in the harvested effect list - approximate with Black & White followed by Posterize at 2 levels, or a hard Lumetri contrast push
- **Transform** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy CPU version of the Transform effect: moves, scales, skews, rotates and fades the layer inside the effect stack, with its own shutter angle for motion blur. Superseded by the current GPU Transform ('ADBE Geometry2'), which has the same controls. -> Transform
- **Unsharp Mask** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Unsharp Mask: sharpens by raising contrast along edges (image minus a blurred copy). Superseded by the current Unsharp Mask ('ADBE Unsharp Mask2', Blur & Sharpen). -> Unsharp Mask

### Obsolete

- **Basic 3D** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Tilts and swivels a layer in pseudo-3D space and pushes it toward/away from the viewer, with an optional specular glint. Superseded by making the layer a real 3D layer with a camera. -> AE replacement: the layer's 3D switch plus a camera; Premiere: Basic 3D (same AE effect)
- **Basic Text** *(unusable)*: Legacy (Obsolete category) effect that renders text typed into a modal dialog onto the layer. Superseded by native text layers; unusable from scripts here because applying it opens a modal Edit Text dialog and the harvest could read no properties.
- **Color Key** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old one-colour keyer: makes every pixel close to the Key Color transparent, with a hard tolerance and simple edge thin/feather. Produces crunchy, aliased edges compared with modern keyers. -> Color Key
- **Gaussian Blur (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The older Gaussian Blur implementation, kept in Obsolete for old projects: softens the whole layer evenly in both directions or only horizontally/vertically. Same look as the current Gaussian Blur, but it has no Repeat Edge Pixels option and is slower at large radii. -> Gaussian Blur
- **Lightning** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old Lightning generator: draws an electric bolt between a Start point and an End point over the layer, with zig-zag, side branches, an outside glow colour and an inside core colour; the bolt shape changes over time (Speed, Stability). Superseded in AE by Advanced Lightning. -> Lightning
- **Luma Key** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old luminance keyer: removes all pixels brighter or darker than a threshold (or near/far from it), with edge thin and feather. Used to drop black or white backgrounds. -> Luma Key
- **Path Text** *(unusable)*: Legacy text generator that draws characters along a Bezier path, circle or line, with per-character jitter and fill/stroke options. Replaced by native text layers with Path Options.
- **Reduce Interlace Flicker** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Applies a slight vertical blur to stop thin horizontal lines and fine detail from flickering on interlaced broadcast displays. On progressive 1080p work it just softens the image vertically. -> Reduce Interlace Flicker
- **Spill Suppressor** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Removes green or blue colour spill (reflected screen colour) from the edges and skin of a keyed subject by desaturating the chosen colour. Suppression above 100 over-corrects: the chosen colour is pushed past neutral toward its complement (red turns green). -> Ultra Key
- **mocha shape** *(needs_input)*: Obsolete Imagineer Systems (mocha for AE) importer: renders a tracked spline shape pasted from mocha as a matte, cut-out or filled colour shape on the layer. Nothing happens until mocha shape data is pasted into it, which a script cannot do.

## Premiere Pro video effects


### Adjust

- **Extract** [pass]: Turns the clip into pure black and white: pixels whose brightness falls between Black Input Level and White Input Level become white and the rest become black, with Softness adding grey gradation at the boundary. The result is a stark stencil or threshold image.
- **Levels** [pass]: Classic input/output levels for the composite RGB and for each channel separately. It remaps the black point, white point and gamma to fix contrast, crush or lift blacks, or tint shadows and highlights per channel.
- **Lighting Effects** [pass]: Relights the clip with up to five virtual lights (spot, omni or directional) plus an ambient level, darkening everything the lights do not reach. Can also emboss a texture via a bump layer.
- **ProcAmp** [pass]: Broadcast-style processing amplifier: one-stop brightness, contrast, hue rotation and saturation, with an optional split-screen before/after.

### Blur & Sharpen

- **Bokeh Blur** [pass]: A lens-style defocus: bright points bloom into aperture-shaped bokeh discs, with a polygonal iris, diffraction fringe and lens distortion. It reads as a real camera going out of focus rather than a flat blur.
- **Camera Blur** [pass]: Simulates an out-of-focus camera lens with a single Percent Blur control.
- **Directional Blur** [pass]: Smears the clip along one angle, as if it moved fast in that direction, with optional chromatic fringing. It sells whips, speed and impact.
- **Fast Blur** [pass]: Quick box-style blur of the clip, horizontally, vertically or both. Used by the built-in 'Fast Blur In/Out' presets.
- **Focus Blur** [pass]: Keeps an elliptical (or linear) focus area sharp and lens-blurs everything outside it, with feathered falloff, bokeh highlight gain and chromatic fringing, to steer the eye to the subject.
- **Gaussian Blur** [pass]: Premiere 26's GPU blur (from Film Impact), which replaces the older Gaussian Blur. It softens the whole clip evenly and has optional chromatic-aberration fringing and edge handling.
- **Reduce Interlace Flicker** [pass]: Applies a small vertical-only blur to calm the line twitter / flicker of thin horizontal detail (fine text, stripes, venetian blinds) on interlaced or downscaled output. On progressive footage it simply softens vertically.
- **Sharpen** [pass]: Increases contrast at edges to make soft footage look crisper. One control: Sharpen Amount.
- **Unsharp Mask** [pass]: Sharpens by boosting contrast along edges, with a radius for how wide the edge boost is and a threshold so flat areas and noise are left alone.
- **Compound Blur** *(needs_input)*: Blurs the clip by different amounts across the frame, driven by the brightness of a blur map layer picked on another track. It is used for fake depth of field or selective blur.
- **Gaussian Blur (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The old Gaussian blur: smooth, even blur in both or one direction. Superseded by the newer Gaussian Blur effect; kept so old projects still open. -> Gaussian Blur

### Channel

- **Invert** [pass]: Inverts the colour information of the chosen channel (by default RGB, giving a photo-negative look). Other channel choices invert only hue, lightness, a single colour channel or alpha.

### Color Correction

- **ASC CDL** [pass]: Applies an ASC Color Decision List primary grade: per-channel Slope (gain), Offset (lift) and Power (gamma), plus overall Saturation. CDL is the standard numeric format for carrying a look between grading tools.
- **Brightness & Contrast** [pass]: Simple global brightness and contrast adjustment for the whole clip. Quick fix or stylised punch, without Lumetri's curves and wheels.
- **Broadcast Colors** [pass]: Clamps or keys out pixels whose luminance or saturation exceeds broadcast-legal limits (NTSC/PAL IRE), for TV delivery.
- **Channel Mix** [pass]: Scales and inverts individual channels in RGB, HSV or YCbCr space (for example red, hue or chroma), with a Master mix. It gives quick stylised colour shifts, channel inversions and hue rotations.
- **Color Balance (HLS)** [pass]: Shifts the hue of the whole image and adjusts overall lightness and saturation (Hue / Lightness / Saturation). A quick global colour twist or desaturation.
- **Lumetri Color** [pass]: Premiere's main colour-grading effect: white balance, exposure and tone, creative looks and LUTs, curves, colour wheels, HSL secondary and vignette in one GPU effect.
- **Tint** [pass]: Maps the image's dark tones to one colour and bright tones to another, turning it into a monochrome or two-tone (duotone) image. Defaults give plain black and white.
- **Video Limiter** [weak]: Limits luma and chroma to a chosen level so the output stays broadcast-safe, optionally compressing the top range before clipping and flagging out-of-gamut pixels.
- **Color Space Transform** *(needs_input)*: Converts the clip from one colour space / transfer function to another (e.g. camera log or HDR to Rec.709), with tone mapping and gamut compression options. Part of Premiere's newer colour-management toolset.

### Distort

- **Corner Pin** [pass]: Moves the four corners of the clip independently, distorting it into any quadrilateral for perspective placement.
- **Lens Distortion** [pass]: Simulates or corrects lens curvature: positive Curvature bulges the image outward (convex, barrel / fisheye) and negative Curvature pinches it (concave, pincushion), with optional off-centre and prism skew. Uncovered areas become transparent or are filled with Fill Color.
- **Mirror** [pass]: Reflects one side of the image onto the other across a line defined by a centre point and an angle, giving symmetrical or kaleidoscope-like frames.
- **Spherize** [pass]: Bulges a circular area of the image outward as if wrapped around a sphere, like a fisheye magnifier. Radius sets the size of the bulge and Center of Sphere where it sits.
- **Transform** [pass]: A second, keyframeable copy of position, anchor, scale, skew, rotation and opacity that renders inside the effect stack, with real per-clip motion blur via its own shutter angle.
- **Turbulent Displace** [pass]: Warps the image with fractal noise so it ripples, bulges or twists like heat haze, water or a waving flag; animating Evolution makes the warp move.
- **Twirl** [pass]: Spins the pixels around a centre point, strongest at the centre and fading to nothing at the twirl radius, like a whirlpool.
- **Wave Warp** [pass]: Bends the image with a travelling wave (sine, square, triangle, noise and so on) that animates on its own over time. Gives a flag, water, heat-haze or wobbly-cartoon distortion.
- **Wiggle** [pass]: Film Impact-derived procedural wiggle built into Premiere 2026 that randomly jitters a clip's or graphic's position, rotation and scale. It has built-in motion blur, an optional timing window with ease in/out, and per-object delay and variation for multi-object graphics. For realistic handheld camera movement on footage, Camera Shake is the dedicated effect.
- **Bend** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy Premiere distortion that runs travelling waves through the frame horizontally and vertically, so the picture ripples and bends like a flag or a reflection in water. The waves move on their own over time, without keyframes. -> Wave Warp
- **Magnify (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Draws a circular or square magnifying-glass zoom of part of the image, with feather, opacity and blend controls. Still renders but is the legacy version. -> No new-generation Magnify found in the harvest; this legacy effect still works. Alternative: duplicate the clip, scale it with Transform/Motion and cut it with a mask. AE: Magnify
- **Rolling Shutter Repair** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Corrects the jello / skew distortion that CMOS cameras and phones produce during fast pans or vibration, by analysing motion and un-warping each scanline.
- **Warp Stabilizer** *(needs_input)*: Analyses camera shake in the clip and smooths or removes it, cropping and scaling (or synthesising edges) to hide the moving borders. Analysis runs automatically in the background after it is applied.

### Generate

- **4-Color Gradient** [pass]: Generates a smooth four-colour gradient from four placeable colour points and blends it over the clip (or replaces it at full opacity).
- **Lens Flare** [pass]: Renders a simulated camera lens flare (bright hotspot plus coloured ghost rings along a line through frame centre) over the clip, using one of three lens types.
- **Lightning** [pass]: Draws an animated electric bolt between a start and end point on top of the clip, with branches, glow colours and random flicker.
- **Ramp** [pass]: Generates a linear or radial colour gradient between two points and colours, replacing the clip's image unless blended back with the original.

### Graphics (Essential Graphics component; not an Effects-panel effect)

- **Group** *(needs_input)*: The transform of a group of layers inside a Premiere graphic clip (layers grouped in the Essential Graphics panel): position, scale, rotation and anchor for just that group.
- **Vector Motion** *(needs_input)*: The built-in transform of a Premiere graphic clip (Essential Graphics text and shapes): position, scale, rotation and anchor for the whole graphic, applied as vectors so it stays sharp at any scale. It is not an effect you add from the Effects panel.

### Graphics (Essential Graphics shape-layer component; not an Effects-panel effect)

- **Shape** *(needs_input)*: The Shape layer inside a Premiere Graphic clip (rectangle, ellipse or pen path drawn with the shape tools). It carries the path, the fill/stroke appearance and a layer transform; applied on its own with no drawn path it shows nothing.

### Graphics (Essential Graphics text-layer component; not an Effects-panel effect)

- **Text** *(needs_input)*: The text layer inside a Premiere Graphic clip made with the Type tool or a .mogrt: it holds the Source Text (words, font, style) and a layer transform. It cannot show anything without text authored in Essential Graphics.

### Image Control

- **Black & White** [pass]: Converts the clip to greyscale with no controls. One-click monochrome.
- **Color Balance (RGB)** [pass]: Scales the red, green and blue channels independently to shift the overall colour cast. It is a fast, simple white-balance or tint correction.
- **Color Pass** [pass]: Keeps one chosen colour (and the shades near it) and turns everything else greyscale, the classic 'colour splash' or Sin City look. Reverse does the opposite: it greys out only the chosen colour.
- **Color Replace** [pass]: Replaces every pixel close to a chosen Target Color with a new Replace Color, keeping the original shading (or a flat fill with Solid Colors). Similarity sets how wide a range of shades is matched.
- **Gamma Correction** [pass]: Brightens or darkens the midtones while leaving the deepest blacks and brightest whites largely in place. It has one slider, and 10 means no change.

### Immersive Video

- **VR Blur** [pass]: Projection-aware blur for 360/VR footage that blurs evenly across the sphere and across the left/right seam.
- **VR Chromatic Aberrations** [pass]: Projection-aware RGB split for 360/VR footage: the red, green and blue channels are pushed apart, increasing with distance from a point of interest.
- **VR Color Gradients** [pass]: Paints a soft multi-point colour gradient (up to 8 coloured points) over the frame, seamless for 360 footage, blended onto the clip with its own opacity and blending mode.
- **VR De-Noise** [pass]: Projection-aware noise reduction for 360/VR footage that smooths grain and sensor noise without a visible seam at the frame edges; in this Premiere install it is also the only general-purpose video denoiser in the effect list, so it gets used on flat footage too.
- **VR Digital Glitch** [pass]: Procedural digital glitch: colour channel corruption, blocky geometric displacement and noise, concentrated around a point of interest and seamless for 360 footage.
- **VR Fractal Noise** [pass]: Generates seamless spherical fractal noise (clouds, smoke, turbulence) over the clip, with its own opacity and blending mode; Evolution animates it.
- **VR Glow** [pass]: Adds a soft bloom around the brightest parts of the image (above a luma threshold), with radius, brightness, saturation and an optional tint; seamless for 360 footage and often used as a glow on flat footage too.
- **VR Plane to Sphere** [pass]: Projects a flat 2D layer (title, logo, photo, inset clip) onto the inside of a 360 equirectangular sphere so it looks undistorted when the 360 video is viewed in a headset or 360 player. On a normal flat sequence it shows the clip curved and shrunk on a transparent background.
- **VR Projection** [pass]: Reprojects 360/VR footage: changes its layout (mono, over/under, side by side), field of view, and re-orients it with Pan, Tilt and Roll. Used to fit 180/360 media into a different VR sequence or to reframe where the viewer starts.
- **VR Rotate Sphere** [pass]: Rotates 360 equirectangular footage around the X (tilt), Y (pan) and Z (roll) axes without seams, to set the starting view or fix a tilted horizon.
- **VR Sharpen** [pass]: Sharpens 360 equirectangular footage while treating the left/right edges as one continuous seam, so no visible line appears where the sphere wraps.

### Internal (effect masks)

- **Mask** *(needs_input)*: Internal component that holds a mask drawn on a clip's effect or Opacity (ellipse, 4-point or pen mask), with feather, opacity, expansion and invert. It is created by Premiere's mask tools rather than picked from the Effects panel.
- **Mask2** *(needs_input)*: Newer internal mask component (Premiere 2025/2026 masking) with its own transform (position, scale, rotation, anchor), feather, opacity, expansion, invert, blend mode, mask type and tracker data. Created by the mask tools, not from the Effects panel.

### Intrinsic (fixed effect on every clip)

- **Motion** [pass]: Premiere's built-in transform present on every visual clip: position, scale (uniform or width/height), rotation, anchor point, anti-flicker and edge crops (Crop Left/Top/Right/Bottom, now part of Motion). It is the basis of reframing, picture-in-picture, Ken Burns moves and split screens.
- **Opacity** [pass]: Premiere's built-in opacity and blend-mode control present on every visual clip. It fades clips in and out, makes overlays semi-transparent and sets how a clip blends with the tracks below.

### Keying

- **Alpha Adjust** [pass]: Changes how a clip's alpha channel is used: scale its opacity, ignore the embedded alpha, invert it, or show only the matte. On an opaque live-action clip it simply makes the clip see-through.
- **Color Key** [pass]: Makes every pixel close to one chosen colour transparent, with tolerance, edge thin and edge feather controls. A simple hard-edged key for flat-colour backgrounds.
- **Eight-Point Garbage Matte** [pass]: An eight-point polygon cutout (four corner vertices plus four edge mid points); everything outside the shape becomes transparent.
- **Four-Point Garbage Matte** [pass]: Cuts the clip to a four-cornered polygon defined by four corner points; everything outside becomes transparent. Used to crop away rigs, edges or unwanted areas, often before keying.
- **Logo Cutout** [pass]: Removes the plain white or black background from a flat logo or graphic by keying on brightness. The result is a logo with real transparency that you can lay over footage. It works without a mask or a second clip.
- **Luma Key** [pass]: Makes the dark (low-luminance) parts of a clip transparent so the track below shows through. Used to drop elements shot on black over other footage.
- **Sixteen-Point Garbage Matte** [pass]: Same as the four-point garbage matte but with sixteen points (corner vertices plus intermediate edge points) for a more detailed polygon cutout; everything outside becomes transparent.
- **Ultra Key** [pass]: Premiere's main chroma keyer: picks a screen colour (usually green or blue) and makes it transparent, with matte cleanup, spill suppression and colour correction of the foreground.
- **Blue Screen Key** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy keyer that makes blue-screen pixels transparent using a threshold and cutoff. Superseded by Ultra Key. On footage without a blue screen it cuts no matte: lowering Threshold just fades the whole picture semi-transparent. -> Ultra Key
- **Chroma Key** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Legacy keyer that makes a chosen colour and similar colours transparent. Superseded by Ultra Key. -> Ultra Key
- **Track Matte Key** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Shows this clip only through the alpha or brightness of a clip on another video track (the matte), e.g. video playing inside text or a shape. Needs a matte clip on a track above.

### Lights & Glows

- **Glint** [pass]: Adds animated star / cross-shaped glints and shimmer to the brightest highlights of the clip, with control over star shape, size, colour and chromatic fringing.
- **Light Leaks** [pass]: Generates animated, film-style light leaks (coloured strikes and soft spots of light) over the clip, with spectrum, hue, diffraction and blend controls and a Master amount.
- **RGB Split** [pass]: Separates the red, green and blue channels horizontally, vertically or radially (depth), with vertigo twist, channel shift, softness and a feathered clean centre, for chromatic-aberration and glitch looks.

### Noise & Grain

- **Noise** [pass]: Adds random per-pixel noise to the clip, either coloured or monochrome. The noise re-randomises every frame on its own, so it moves without keyframes.

### Perspective

- **Basic 3D** [pass]: Rotates the clip in simple 3D space (swivel around the vertical axis, tilt around the horizontal axis) and moves it nearer or farther, like a flat card in perspective.
- **Drop Shadow** [pass]: Adds a coloured, offset, optionally soft shadow behind the clip's alpha edges. Only shows where the clip has transparency or is scaled down.
- **Long Shadow** *(needs_input)*: Casts a long, flat-design extruded shadow from the alpha edges of text, logos or cut-outs at a chosen angle and length, with colourise, two-colour gradient and alpha falloff.

### Source Settings (master clip, not in Effects panel)

- **ARRIRAW Development Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source settings that develop classic ARRIRAW (.ari sequence) files: colour space, exposure index, white balance and tint applied at decode.
- **Canon Cinema RAW Light Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source settings for Canon Cinema RAW Light (.crm) footage: colour temperature, tint, exposure, colour space and gamma chosen at decode.
- **CinemaDNG Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source settings for CinemaDNG raw image sequences (Blackmagic, DJI drones): temperature, tint and exposure applied at decode.
- **Cineon Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source settings that convert log Cineon/DPX scans to a viewable image: black/white points, gamma, highlight rolloff and log-to-linear conversion.
- **MPEG Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Source-settings component that Premiere attaches to MPEG-family media (e.g. H.264/HEVC phone or screen recordings). It controls how variable-frame-rate source is interpreted. It is not a creative effect and has no visible look.
- **MXF/ARRIRAW Development Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source settings that develop ARRIRAW recorded inside MXF (ALEXA Mini LF / ALEXA 35 style): colour space, exposure index, white balance and tint applied at decode.
- **PRORES RAW Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: RAW-development component that Premiere attaches to Apple ProRes RAW clips. It sets exposure (ISO-style offset) and the colour space the RAW data is decoded into before any grading.
- **RED Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: RAW-development component that Premiere attaches to RED .R3D clips. It exposes the RED SDK decode controls: image pipeline (IPP2/legacy), ISO, white balance, exposure, debayer quality, denoise, output tone map and highlight roll-off. It also has a legacy colour section with CDL, curves and lift/gamma/gain.
- **Sony RAW MXF Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source setting of Sony RAW / X-OCN MXF media (VENICE, VENICE 2, BURANO and similar). Its only control is the colour space the RAW data is decoded into. F65-family RAW may use the separate 'Sony Raw Source Settings' instead.
- **Sony Raw Source Settings** *(needs_input)*: Master-clip source setting of the Sony F65-family RAW decoder (match name SonyRawF65): picks the colour space the RAW is decoded into. VENICE / X-OCN MXF clips usually get the separate 'Sony RAW MXF Source Settings' component instead.

### Stylize

- **Brush Strokes** [pass]: Repaints the image as directional brush strokes, giving a rough painted look.
- **Clone** [pass]: Repeats the clip as a grid or row of copies (left/right/top/bottom clone counts) with spacing, shift, rotation and scale/alpha falloff. It has built-in eased animation toward target values, so the grid can spread, slide or rotate without keyframes.
- **Color Emboss** [pass]: Embosses the edges of the image like a raised metal or paper relief while keeping the original colours. A stylised texture effect, not a correction.
- **Echo Glow** [pass]: A glow built from stacked echoes of the highlights that radiate from an anchor point. It can be tinted, outlined, spun and animated over time, giving a dreamy or energetic halo.
- **Edge Glow** [pass]: A highlight glow with a width/height-shapeable bloom (round or stretched like an anamorphic streak) plus colour tint, vibrance and chromatic aberration. It gives bright, neon or cinematic bloom.
- **Find Edges** [pass]: Detects high-contrast edges and draws them as dark, colour-tinted lines on a white field (or bright coloured lines on black when inverted), giving a pencil-sketch or line-art version of the shot.
- **Mosaic** [pass]: Turns the image into a grid of square or hexagonal tiles, with optional bevel lighting, rounded tiles, distortion and coloured grout strokes; the modern replacement for Mosaic (Legacy).
- **Posterize** [pass]: Reduces the number of tonal levels per colour channel, turning smooth gradients into flat bands of colour like a screen print or poster.
- **Replicate** [pass]: Tiles the whole clip into a grid of identical copies (Count x Count) inside the frame.
- **Roughen Edges** [pass]: Eats into the clip's alpha edges with fractal noise to make torn, eroded, rusty or photocopied borders; on a full-frame opaque clip it roughens the frame border.
- **Strobe Light** [pass]: Flashes the clip with a solid colour (or makes it transparent) on a fixed clock: each Period starts in the strobe colour, which holds for Period minus Duration, then the picture shows for the last Duration (Duration 0.1 / Period 0.5 = a 0.4 s flash twice a second). The clock starts at the clip's start on the timeline; Random Strobe Probablity between 0 and 100 turns it into random bursts.
- **Vignette** [pass]: Film Impact-derived vignette built into Premiere 2026 that darkens or tints the frame edges with an adjustable elliptical shape. It has its own centre, size, roundness, feather and colour, plus chromatic fringing in the darkened edge that is on (20) by default.
- **Volumetric Rays** [pass]: Film Impact-derived light-ray effect built into Premiere 2026 that streaks god rays outward from a light position through the bright areas of the image. Optional drifting fog and caustics make the light look volumetric.
- **Wonder Glow** [pass]: Film Impact-derived glow built into Premiere 2026 that blooms the image's highlights with a tintable glow and an ambient colour wash. It also has volumetric spread, shape (width, height, angle) and chromatic fringing (10 by default).
- **Alpha Glow** *(needs_input)*: Adds a coloured glow that radiates outward from the edges of a clip's alpha channel, for example around text, a logo or a keyed subject. A full-frame opaque clip has no alpha edges, so nothing shows. In Premiere 26 it sits in the Legacy folder.
- **Mosaic (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Pixelates the clip into a grid of solid-colour blocks. Superseded in Premiere 2026 by the new Mosaic effect but still renders. -> Mosaic (new-generation, AE.Impact_Mosaic_FX); AE: Mosaic
- **Stroke** *(needs_input)*: Film Impact-derived outline effect built into Premiere 2026 that draws a coloured stroke along the clip's alpha edge, for cut-out subjects, logos, PNG stickers and text. It needs real transparency: on opaque full-frame footage it draws nothing at all, not even a border at the frame edge (verified with Size 80, Position 0).

### Time

- **Echo** [pass]: Composites copies of the clip from earlier (or later) frames on top of the current frame, creating motion trails and ghosting.
- **Posterize Time** [pass]: Locks the clip to a lower frame rate so motion becomes choppy and stepped, like stop-motion or a dropped-frame feed.
- **Timewarp** [pass]: Retimes the clip from inside an effect, with keyframable speed (or a direct source-frame map) and optical-flow frame interpolation plus optional synthetic motion blur, for smooth slow motion and speed ramps.
- **Pixel Motion Blur** *(unusable)* [broken]: Adds realistic motion blur to footage by tracking pixel movement between frames (optical flow) and smearing along it, for shots that were shot or rendered with too little blur.

### Transform

- **3D Rotate** [pass]: Self-animating 3D rotation: turns the clip in perspective around an axis from Initial Rotation to Target Rotation (or continuously at deg/s), with anchor, perspective, back-side colouring/flip and motion blur.
- **Auto Align** [pass]: Snaps a clip's visible content (the non-transparent pixels of a logo, lower third or cut-out) to a chosen edge or centre of the frame, with optional scale and offset. You don't have to set positions by hand.
- **Camera Shake** [pass]: Adds procedural handheld camera movement (sideways strafe, forward stride and roll) chosen from preset camera modes, with speed, variation, lean and motion blur. It turns a locked-off shot or still into something that feels handheld or hit by an impact.
- **Crop** [pass]: Trims the clip from the left, top, right and bottom by a percentage, optionally scaling the result up (Zoom) and feathering the cropped edge.
- **Edge Feather** [pass]: Feathers all four edges of the clip into transparency, giving a soft rectangular vignette-like border.
- **Grow** [pass]: Self-animating scale-up: the clip grows from Initial Scale to Target Scale (or keeps growing at a % per second rate) around an anchor, with easing curves and motion blur, i.e. a one-click Ken Burns push-in.
- **Horizontal Flip** [pass]: Mirrors the clip left to right. It has no controls.
- **Move** [pass]: Self-animating position move: slides the clip or graphic from Initial Position to Target Position (or continuously in a Direction at a Speed), with easing / bounce curves, edge handling and motion blur.
- **Offset** [pass]: Slides the image inside its frame and wraps whatever leaves one edge back in from the opposite edge, like a tiled texture. Blend With Original mixes the shifted copy over the untouched image.
- **Rounded Crop** [pass]: Crops the clip from each side with rounded (per-corner) corners, feather, and its own offset, angle and scale. In one effect it turns a clip into a modern rounded picture-in-picture card.
- **Shrink** [pass]: Self-animating scale-down: the clip shrinks from Initial Scale to Target Scale (or keeps shrinking at a % per second rate) around an anchor, with easing, edge handling and motion blur; the pull-out counterpart of Grow.
- **Spin** [pass]: Self-animating 2D rotation: spins the clip in the frame plane from Initial Spin to Target Spin (default one full turn) or continuously at deg/s, around an anchor, with easing, edge fill and motion blur.
- **Vertical Flip** [pass]: Flips the clip upside down (mirror across the horizontal axis). No settings.
- **Auto Reframe** *(needs_input)*: Adobe Sensei analyses the clip and pans / zooms it to keep the main action in frame when the sequence has a different aspect ratio from the footage (e.g. 16:9 footage in a 9:16 reel sequence). It writes the path it finds into generated keyframes, and the Reframe Offset / Scale / Rotation rows let you tweak it.
- **Spacer** *(needs_input)*: Self-animating spacing for text and multi-object graphics: detects the separate letters / objects in the layer's alpha and spreads or gathers them from Initial Spacing to Target Spacing, with range, variation and motion blur.

### Transition

- **Block Dissolve** [pass]: Makes the clip disappear in random rectangular blocks as Transition Completion rises, revealing what is on the track below (or black).
- **Gradient Wipe** [pass]: Makes the clip transparent pixel by pixel in order of brightness of a gradient source, as Transition Completion rises: dark areas of the gradient disappear first. With no gradient layer chosen it uses the clip's own luminance.
- **Linear Wipe (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: In-clip wipe effect that makes the clip transparent along a straight edge at a chosen angle, revealing the track below as Transition Completion rises. Still renders but is the legacy version. -> Linear Wipe (video transition) for wipes on a cut; Gradient Wipe effect for in-clip reveals; AE: Linear Wipe

### Utility

- **Cineon Converter** [pass]: Converts between logarithmic Cineon/DPX film-scan encoding and linear video, with black/white points, gamma and highlight rolloff.

### Video

- **Clip Name** [pass]: Burns the clip's name as text over the picture on a semi-transparent box, for review copies.
- **Metadata & Timecode Burn-in** [pass]: Burns up to five lines of timecode or clip metadata (source/sequence timecode, clip name, file name and similar) into the picture on a semi-transparent box.
- **SDR Conform** [pass]: Tone-maps HDR (HLG/PQ) footage down to standard dynamic range so it looks right in a Rec.709 sequence or SDR export. Brightness sets the overall SDR level, Contrast and Soft Knee shape how highlights roll off.
- **Simple Text** [pass]: Burns a single line of plain text onto the clip on a semi-transparent box, like a slate or review label. Position, size, justification and box opacity are adjustable; the text itself is typed via Edit Text in Effect Controls.
- **Timecode** [pass]: Burns a running timecode (or frame count / feet+frames) onto the clip on a translucent black box, optionally with a camera label. Used for review copies, not final delivery. The newer Metadata & Timecode Burn-in effect does the same with several lines (source/sequence timecode, clip or file name) and is the better choice for new work.

### Obsolete

- **Directional Blur (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Blurs the image along one direction to suggest fast motion. Superseded by the newer Directional Blur effect. -> Directional Blur
- **RGB Difference Key** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old simple colour keyer (lives in Premiere's Obsolete bin): makes pixels close to a chosen colour transparent, with a basic similarity threshold and None/Low/High edge smoothing. Ultra Key does the same job far better. -> Ultra Key

## Premiere Pro transitions


### 3D

- **3D Roll** [pass]: A 3D take on the roll transition: the image rolls around a curved, bulged surface with perspective and strong motion blur, carrying the next shot in from the side.
- **3D Spin** [pass]: The outgoing shot twirls away into depth, shrinking to a small spinning card over black, then the incoming shot spins back toward camera to full frame with zoom blur.
- **Flip Motion** [pass]: Flips the picture over like a 3D plane with perspective, a dolly, an easing curve and motion blur, revealing the incoming shot on the back (catalog: flip-over clock style). At defaults the gallery render turned around the vertical axis. It can also flip graphics in or out.
- **Fold Motion** [pass]: Flip-clock style 3D fold (catalog: 'Create a Flip Clock animation'): the picture flips over on a hinge like the leaf of a split-flap clock, with perspective and motion blur, revealing the incoming shot. In the default render the fold is horizontal and very fast, running from about cut-0.25 s to the cut, so the incoming shot is already full-frame at the cut point.
- **Louver** [pass]: Turns the outgoing clip into angled 3D venetian-blind slats that rotate with perspective and motion blur to reveal the next shot.
- **Page Peel** [pass]: Film Impact Page Peel (internal AE.AE_Impact_Page_Peel), the current replacement for 'Page Peel (Obsolete)'. The outgoing shot curls away like a sheet of paper, with lighting, a shadow and motion blur on the curl, and reveals the incoming shot underneath. The back of the page shows a desaturated grey, motion-blurred image rather than a plain colour. In the gallery render the curl runs diagonally from the left edge and exits off the right edge just after the cut.
- **Page Turn** [pass]: The outgoing clip folds away from the top-left corner along a straight diagonal crease, like a book page turned flat; the folded-over flap shows the clip mirrored, and the incoming clip is revealed underneath. No curl or shading (that is Page Peel).
- **Center Peel** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip A tears open from the centre as four page curls peeling toward the corners, showing grey shaded curl backs and clip B underneath. -> Premiere: Page Peel / Page Turn for a single curl. AE: CC Page Turn on four masked quarter copies of A, each peeling toward its own corner.
- **Cross Stretch** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B stretches in from one side while clip A squashes toward the other, like a flat fake cube turn. Old CS6-era transition. -> Premiere: the newer GPU Stretch / Stretch Wipe or a 3D Spin for a real cube. AE: anchor A on its right edge and keyframe Scale X 100 to 0 while B, anchored on its left edge, goes 0 to 100; or a real cube with two 3D layers rotating 90 degrees.
- **Curtain** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip A splits down the middle and both halves swing open in perspective like stage curtains, revealing clip B. Old CS6-era 3D transition. -> Premiere: Barn Doors for a flat version. AE: mask A into left and right halves as 3D layers, anchor each at its outer edge and keyframe Y Rotation 0 to about +/-90 degrees with a camera for perspective.
- **Doors** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip arrives as two door panels that swing in from the left and right edges in fake 3D and close over the outgoing clip. -> PR replacement: Barn Doors (Wipe) for a flat centre split, or Flip Motion / 3D Spin (new GPU 3D transitions in this build) for a modern 3D move. (Cube Spin and Flip Over are not in the v26 transition list.) AE: precomp clip B, duplicate it, mask left and right halves, make both 3D layers with anchor points on the outer frame edges and animate Y Rotation from +/-90 to 0 (add a camera for perspective).
- **Fold Up** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The outgoing clip folds in half like a sheet of paper (the right half folds onto the left, then the remaining panel folds again, showing its mirrored back), shrinking panel by panel until it disappears and reveals the incoming clip behind it. -> PR replacement: Page Peel or Flip Motion (new GPU transitions; Flip Over is not in the v26 list). AE: split clip A into halves as 3D precomps and rotate one half 180 deg on the crease, nesting the result and repeating per fold; or approximate with CC Page Turn (small Fold Radius) keyed in steps.
- **Page Peel (Obsolete)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old version of the page-curl transition: the outgoing clip curls away from the top-left corner like a sheet of paper and the curl travels diagonally off the bottom-right, revealing the incoming clip underneath. The back of the page is a plain grey-white shaded paper, not the image. Kept only for old projects. -> Premiere replacement: Page Peel or Page Turn (Page Peel folder). After Effects: CC Page Turn effect on the outgoing layer, keyframing Fold Position across the frame.
- **Peel Back** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The outgoing clip splits into four quarters at the centre and the quarters peel off one after another (top-left, top-right, bottom-right, then bottom-left in the harvest frames), each curling from the centre toward its outer corner, revealing the incoming clip. -> PR replacement: Page Peel (current version). AE: split clip A into four masked quadrant precomps and apply Distort > CC Page Turn to each, folding toward its outer corner.
- **Roll Away** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip A rolls up like a scroll from its left edge toward the right: the rolled part is a narrow squashed strip that travels across and off the right edge, uncovering clip B behind it. -> PR replacement: 'Page Peel' (new GPU transition). AE: CC Page Turn on the outgoing layer with a large Fold Radius and Fold Position animated across the frame.
- **Spin** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B grows from a thin vertical strip in the centre to full width over clip A, like a card turning on a vertical axis but drawn flat (a horizontal scale, no perspective). Clip A stays still behind. -> PR: the closest new GPU transition is 'Flip Motion' (real 3D flip with perspective and motion blur). Not '3D Spin' or 'Spin Motion': those twirl the image in the frame plane. AE: on the incoming layer keyframe Scale X 0 to 100% with the anchor at the centre (exact match), or make it 3D and keyframe Y Rotation 90 to 0 degrees with Easy Ease and motion blur for a nicer version.
- **Spin Away** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Like Spin, clip B turns into view from a thin strip in the centre on a vertical axis, but drawn with perspective: the panel is a trapezoid (one edge taller than the other) while it turns, then lands full frame over clip A. -> PR: the closest new GPU transition is 'Flip Motion' (3D flip with motion blur); '3D Spin' twirls into depth instead. AE: 3D incoming layer with Y Rotation keyframed 90 to 0 degrees, anchor at centre, a camera for perspective, motion blur on.
- **Swing In** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip swings into place like a door on a hinge at the left edge, rotating in perspective until it lies flat over the outgoing clip. -> PR: 3D Spin / Flip Motion (new 3D transitions) or Motion keyframes. AE: make the incoming layer 3D, move its Anchor Point to the left edge and keyframe Y Rotation from 90 to 0 with ease-out (use the sign that starts the free edge away from camera; flip it for the Swing Out look).
- **Swing Out** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Like Swing In, the incoming clip swings in on a hinge at the left edge, but its free edge comes in from in front of the screen (toward the viewer, overflowing the frame top and bottom) before settling flat over the outgoing clip. -> PR: 3D Spin / Flip Motion or Motion keyframes. AE: 3D incoming layer, Anchor Point on the edge, keyframe Y Rotation from 90 to 0 with ease-out, using the opposite sign to Swing In so the free edge starts toward the camera.
- **Tumble Away** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The outgoing clip shrinks and spins away into the distance, tumbling like a flipped card, revealing the incoming clip behind it. -> PR: Spin Motion or 3D Spin (new transitions), or Transform keyframes. AE: outgoing layer 3D, keyframe Scale 100 -> 0, Z Rotation 0 -> 720 and a little X Rotation, ease-in, incoming layer underneath.

### audio

- **Constant Gain**: Linear audio crossfade: gain changes at a constant rate on both clips. Loudness dips about 3 dB in the middle of a crossfade between different sounds, and the change can sound abrupt. Useful for very short click-removal fades and for correlated audio.
- **Constant Power**: Premiere's default audio crossfade, an equal-power curve. The outgoing sound drops slowly at first and fast at the end, while the incoming sound rises fast and then levels off, so loudness stays even through the overlap. The audio equivalent of a cross dissolve.
- **Exponential Fade**: Audio crossfade on a smooth logarithmic curve: the outgoing clip fades out while the incoming clip fades in. Used single-sided, it gives the most natural-sounding fade from or to silence.

### blur

- **Blur Dissolve** [pass]: Blurs the outgoing shot while crossfading into the incoming one, which comes back into focus. Optional chromatic aberration.
- **Directional Blur** [pass]: Smears the outgoing clip with a strong linear motion blur, switches at the blur peak and resolves the incoming clip out of the same blur. Default angle in the render is diagonal (streaks run upper-left to lower-right). The catalog lists angle, chromatic and slice options.
- **Lens Blur** [pass]: Film Impact Lens Blur (internal AE.AE_Impact_Lens_Blur). The outgoing shot defocuses like a real lens, with aperture-shaped bokeh, slight lens distortion and chromatic fringing. It crossfades to the incoming shot at peak blur, and the incoming shot pulls back into focus. In the gallery render the blur is moderate and confined to the middle: the frames 0.3 s either side are sharp, and the cut frame is a soft defocused mix of both shots.
- **Radial Blur** [pass]: Film Impact spin-blur transition: the frame smears into a rotational (spin) blur around the centre, peaks in an abstract swirl at the cut with a slight exposure lift, and unwinds into the next shot. With defaults the blur is heavy: 0.3 s either side of the cut both shots are already smeared. Chromatic aberration and slices are optional (0 by default).
- **VR Spherical Blur** [pass]: Blurs the outgoing 360/VR clip with a seam-aware spherical blur, crossfades at peak blur and sharpens into the incoming clip. Made for equirectangular footage.

### dip

- **Dip to Black** [pass]: Fades the outgoing clip down to black, then fades the incoming clip up from black, with eased (not linear) ramps. Replaces Dip to Black (Legacy). Single-sided it is the standard fade in from / fade out to black.
- **Dip to Color** [pass]: Film Impact's 'Blur To Color' under a new name: the outgoing clip softens slightly while washing into a solid colour, holds the colour at the cut, then the incoming clip resolves out of it. In the gallery render the default colour is a flat grey-lavender (#837F8A measured at the cut), with only light blur.
- **Dip to White** [pass]: Fades the outgoing clip up to full white, then the incoming clip fades down out of white, with eased ramps. Replaces Dip to White (Legacy). Gentler and longer than Flash; reads as a memory, dream or bright reveal.
- **Dip to Black (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older build of Dip to Black: the outgoing clip fades to black, then the incoming clip fades up from black, with linear ramps (gallery: steady darkening, full black at the cut). The current eased 'Dip to Black' replaces it. -> Premiere replacement: Dip to Black (non-legacy). After Effects: a black Solid above both layers with Opacity 0->100 into the cut and 100->0 after it (or fade each layer's Opacity over a black background).
- **Dip to White (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older build of Dip to White: the outgoing clip fades up to white, then the incoming clip fades down out of white, with linear ramps; short, it reads as a flash. The current eased 'Dip to White' replaces it. -> Premiere replacement: Dip to White (non-legacy). After Effects: a white Solid above the cut with Opacity 0->100->0 (peak on the cut); add a little Glow for a flash feel.

### dissolve
