- **Additive Dissolve** [pass]: Additive dissolve: the two images' light is added, so the mix blooms brighter mid-way before settling on the incoming shot. Has curve and gamma controls. Replaces Additive Dissolve (Legacy).
- **Cross Dissolve** [pass]: Standard crossfade from one shot into the next, rebuilt with curve and gamma-correction controls for a cleaner midpoint. Replaces Cross Dissolve (Legacy).
- **Film Dissolve** [pass]: A cross dissolve blended in linear light, the way film optically dissolves: bright areas of the incoming clip show through early and the mix looks richer than Cross Dissolve. Replaces Film Dissolve (Legacy) and adds curve / gamma-correction controls. In the render the incoming clip's pale highlights punch through the outgoing shot well before the midpoint.
- **Luma Fade** [pass]: Film Impact Luma Fade (internal AE.AE_Impact_Luma_Fade). The dissolve is keyed on brightness, so one tonal range of the picture changes before the rest with a soft feathered edge, which looks more organic than a flat crossfade. Invert Luma flips the order. In the gallery render most of the frame is already the incoming shot at the cut, with only scattered fragments of the outgoing shot left, and both frames 0.3 s away are clean.
- **Morph Cut** [pass]: Hides a jump cut inside a talking-head shot by face tracking and optical-flow interpolation between the last frame before the cut and the first frame after it, so the speaker appears to talk continuously.
- **Non-Additive Dissolve** [pass]: A luminance-comparison dissolve: per pixel it shows whichever clip is brighter, with hard edges instead of a soft mix. The bright areas of the incoming clip (sky, lights, pale surfaces) punch through the outgoing clip's darker areas from the first frames, the two images hold as a patchy, posterised double exposure for most of the duration, and the incoming clip's dark areas only take over at the end.
- **Additive Dissolve (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old dissolve that adds clip B's pixel values onto clip A before fading A out, so the middle of the transition blows out hot and bright. Replaced by the newer GPU 'Additive Dissolve'. -> Premiere replacement: Additive Dissolve (new GPU version). AE: put B above A with blend mode Add, keyframe B opacity 0 to 100 over the first half, then fade A's opacity out over the second half.
- **Cross Dissolve (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The classic crossfade: clip A fades out while clip B fades in, with a linear ramp. Replaced by the newer GPU 'Cross Dissolve', which eases (smoothstep). -> Premiere replacement: Cross Dissolve (eased). AE: opacity keyframes on the top layer 100 to 0 (or B 0 to 100) over the overlap, linear for this legacy version.
- **Dither Dissolve** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Dissolve where clip B replaces clip A through a fine, regular one-pixel dot (ordered dither) pattern, giving a grainy, screen-door blend instead of a smooth one. Old CS6-era transition. -> Premiere: no direct GPU replacement; Cross Dissolve for a clean blend, Mosaic or Grunge (new) for a blockier or textured break. AE: Block Dissolve on A with Block Width/Height 1-2 px and Soft Edges off, Transition Completion 0 to 100.
- **Film Dissolve (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older version of Film Dissolve: a cross-fade blended in linear light so bright areas bleed through first, like an optical film dissolve rather than a flat video mix. -> PR replacement: Film Dissolve (current version, same Dissolve folder). AE: overlap the layers and fade the top layer's Opacity 100->0 with Project Settings > Blend Colors Using 1.0 Gamma (or a linearized working space) for the same linear-light mix.

### glitch/damage

- **Chaos** [pass]: Camera-shake transition: both clips jitter in hard position jumps (about 10-30 px at 1080p, alternating horizontal and vertical, each held 2 frames) with a light blur, around a short ~0.2 s 50/50 mix on the cut. At defaults on a 1 s transition the shake runs from ~0.45 s before the cut to ~0.55 s after; frame edges stay filled. The catalog also lists noise distortion and chromatic aberration, but no colour split or warp showed in the default render.
- **Flicker** [pass]: Cuts back and forth irregularly between the outgoing clip, the incoming clip and blended frames for the duration, then settles on the incoming clip, like a faulty projector or unstable signal. The catalog lists strobe, grain, scanline and shake options.
- **Glitch** [pass]: Digital glitch: horizontal slice displacement, scanline tearing and RGB colour fringing mix the two clips and resolve to the incoming one.
- **Grunge** [pass]: Film Impact distressed-film transition (internal AE.AE_Impact_Grunge). Around the cut both shots get crushed into a dark, desaturated, high-contrast grungy mix. Dirt, scratches, a vignette, a grey colourise, a flash and a slight frame wiggle are added, and then it clears to the incoming shot. In the 1 s gallery render the frames 0.3 s either side of the cut are almost clean, so the damage sits in the middle.
- **VHS Damage** [pass]: Analog tape damage between shots: white tracking-noise bands, horizontal line displacement and vertical jumps, channel noise and slight desaturation, as if a VHS tape rolls over from one recording to the next.
- **Random Invert** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Random blocks of clip A flip to a colour negative until the whole frame is inverted, then clip B replaces the negative block by block. An old Dissolve-folder transition kept for old projects. -> PR replacement: 'Glitch' or 'Solarize' (new GPU transitions). AE: on the outgoing layer add Invert, then Block Dissolve animated 0 to 100% with the incoming clip below; or a 2-3 frame Invert flash on hold keyframes at the cut.
- **Three-D** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Puts the outgoing clip into the red channel and the incoming clip into the blue channel, giving a red/blue anaglyph-style frame for the whole transition instead of a gradual change. -> PR: Glitch or Chroma Leak (new transitions) for a modern colour hit. AE: 3D Glasses effect (Perspective > 3D Glasses: Left View = outgoing layer, Right View = incoming layer, 3D View = Balanced Colored Red Blue) held for the transition length.

### iris/shape

- **Iris Box** [pass]: The incoming clip opens from the centre in an expanding rectangle (keeping the frame's aspect) until it fills the frame.
- **Iris Cross** [pass]: The incoming clip appears as a plus-shaped cross whose arms run edge to edge and widen, squeezing the outgoing clip into four shrinking corner rectangles until the incoming clip fills the frame.
- **Iris Diamond** [pass]: The incoming clip opens from the centre in an expanding diamond-shaped iris until it covers the frame.
- **Iris Round** [pass]: The incoming clip opens from the centre in an expanding circle until it fills the frame: the classic iris-out.
- **Radial Wipe** [pass]: New Film Impact shape wipe: the incoming shot is revealed through a radial shape that grows from a centre point. Default is a plain hard-edged circle from frame centre (Roundness 100, Repeats 1, no stroke) that stays small until about the cut and then expands fast; Roundness, Repeats (concentric rings), Mirror, Perspective and coloured Stroke options turn it into diamonds, polygons or ringed graphic wipes. It is NOT the corner-pivot sweep of 'Radial Wipe (Legacy)'.
- **Star Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact multi-shape iris wipe: the incoming shot is revealed through a shape growing from a centre point. Default is a plain hard-edged 5-point star from frame centre with no stroke; Points (3-20), Pointiness, Roundness, Spin, Repeats, Mirror and Stroke turn it into flowers, polygons, diamonds or neon ringed shapes.
- **VR Iris Wipe** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) iris: a soft-edged circle of the incoming clip opens from about the frame centre and grows until it fills the frame. On flat 16:9 footage the circle stretches into a rounded rectangle as it grows.
- **Iris Points** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip pushes in from all four frame edges as four triangles (points), squeezing the outgoing clip into a shrinking diagonal X shape. -> PR replacement: Iris Cross (current Iris folder) for a similar geometric reveal. AE: four triangle shape layers animated in from the edges, merged and used as an Alpha Matte for clip B.
- **Iris Shapes** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip opens through several small shapes that grow until they merge and fill the frame; the default seen in the harvest is a row of three diamonds across the middle (shape type and rows/columns are Custom settings). -> PR replacement: Iris Diamond / Iris Box for a single shape. AE: Transition > CC Grid Wipe (Shape: Doors/Radial/Rectangular) or a repeated shape layer (Repeater) scaling up as an Alpha Matte.
- **Iris Star** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip opens from the centre in an expanding five-point star until it fills the frame: the notorious star wipe. -> PR replacement: Star Wipe (new transition in this build; harvest frames show the same centre star reveal). AE: a Star shape layer (Polystar, 5 points) scaling from 0 past the frame corners as an Alpha Matte for clip B; Transition > Iris Wipe with Use Inner Radius on can also make a star, but its Iris Points minimum is 6 and the setting for a 5-point star (likely 10, counting inner vertices) is unverified.

### light/flare

- **Burn Chroma** [pass]: Bright burn: the outgoing shot's highlights flare into an overexposed white (optionally colourised) burn that spreads and then reveals the incoming shot, like a film burn to light.
- **Chroma Leak** [pass]: Colourful light-leak streaks (warm orange/red with chroma split) sweep across the frame and wash out the cut between the two shots.
- **Flare** [pass]: A warm lens flare with rainbow/prismatic streaks sweeps across and blooms the frame to near-white at the cut, then fades away to reveal the incoming clip.
- **Flash** [pass]: A quick white-out flash: the outgoing clip blows to white and the incoming clip appears overexposed and settles. The event is front-loaded: in the 1 s render the frame is pure white at cut-0.3 s, the incoming shot is washed at cut-0.2 s and already normal by the cut, so the second half of the transition is plain incoming footage.
- **Glow** [pass]: A warm bloom builds on the outgoing clip and blows out to a soft warm-white glow right at the cut, then fades off the incoming clip. Softer than Flash, warmer than Dip to White.
- **Light Leak** [pass]: Film Impact Light Leaks (internal AE.AE_Impact_Light_Leaks). Organic film light-leak streaks and spots wash over the frame with a bright flash and chromatic fringing, cover the cut and fade off the incoming shot. With default settings the leak is multicolour, not warm orange: the gallery render shows yellow-green at top-left, magenta and violet across the centre and a white wash. A faint warm-violet tint is still on the incoming shot 0.3 s after the cut.
- **Ray** [pass]: Film Impact light wipe: a feathered wipe edge (default 45 degrees) throws volumetric light rays (god-rays) with a warm bloom, slight chromatic aberration and exposure lift across the frame, covering the cut and clearing onto the next shot.
- **VR Chroma Leaks** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) transition that blends the clips under a violent colour burn: the outgoing clip floods hot yellow-orange just before the cut, then the incoming clip emerges through magenta, blue and cyan fringes that fade away. It renders on flat footage too.
- **VR Light Leaks** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) transition that washes the whole frame in shifting saturated colour: the outgoing clip tints pink-magenta, then orange and a near-white yellow burn at the cut, and the incoming clip comes out green-cyan before clearing. At defaults it is a loud rainbow wash, not a subtle warm film leak.
- **VR Light Rays** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) transition: a cross-dissolve with a bright star-burst of light rays that flares up from a point near the frame centre around the cut and dies away. The burst covers roughly the middle third of the frame; the rest of the image is a normal mix of the two clips.

### motion

- **Block Motion** [pass]: 3D block animation: the frame moves like a solid block or cube that rotates and dollies with perspective and motion blur to bring in the next shot. It is also made to animate graphic layers in and out.
- **Earthquake** [pass]: Violently shakes both clips with motion-blurred ghosting through the transition and crossfades between them, settling on the incoming shot. Reads as an impact or tremor.
- **Motion Camera** [pass]: Film Impact virtual camera-move transition (internal AE.AE_Impact_Warp). A fast zoom with rotation, 3D tilt and a vertigo or dolly warp, plus heavy motion blur, joins the two shots as if one camera move carried through the cut. It has 12 motion presets and spring or bounce curve options. In the gallery render the cut frame is an unreadable zoom/rotation smear of the incoming shot, and the frames 0.3 s either side are sharp, so the move is concentrated at the cut.
- **Motion Tween** [pass]: Film Impact motion transition (internal AE.AE_Impact_Animate). It is believed to animate smoothly between the two clips' transform states, so a framed picture or graphic glides to its new size and position, and it also offers 27 motion presets, Move/Dolly/3D rotation controls and cropping. Verified: on two full-frame clips with default settings and identical transforms nothing moves, and it renders as a plain full-length crossfade (Dissolve Time 100).
- **Spin Motion** [pass]: Film Impact rotation transition built for motion graphics: the outgoing element spins away (default Angle 1799, about five turns, with a dolly zoom and motion blur) and the incoming one spins into place on an eased curve. On full-frame footage both shots tilt and shrink, exposing black corners, and the cut passes through a near-black swirl for a few frames.
- **Spring Motion** [pass]: Film Impact springy transition built for graphics and stickers with alpha: elements squash and bounce in and out with an elastic overshoot and a slight 3D perspective angle, with motion blur. On full-frame footage the gallery render squashes the outgoing shot into a thin strip, shows black for a couple of frames and springs the incoming shot open.
- **Travel Motion** [pass]: Travel/push animation: the outgoing shot travels out of frame and the incoming one travels in, with an adjustable angle, an easing (speed) curve and motion blur. At defaults the gallery render moved vertically (A up and out, B up from below). Also used to animate graphics in or out.
- **Multi-Spin** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip appears as a grid of small rotated tiles scattered over the outgoing clip; the tiles spin and grow until they join into the full frame. -> PR replacement: none direct; 3D Spin or Cross Zoom (new transitions) for energy. (Cube Spin is not in the v26 list.) AE: Transition > Card Wipe (Flip Axis and Flip Direction Random, about 4x4 rows/columns) or CC Grid Wipe; for a true match, a grid of precomp tiles with Rotation and Scale keyframes offset in time.

### slide/push

- **Band Slide** [pass]: Horizontal bands of clip B slide in from alternate sides of the frame and interlock to cover clip A. Classic old-style Premiere slide.
- **Center Split** [pass]: The outgoing clip is cut into four quarters that slide away to the corners, revealing the incoming clip through a widening cross in the middle.
- **Film Roll** [pass]: Shrinks the outgoing frame into a vertical film strip (rounded frames, black frame lines, sprocket edges), rolls the strip vertically to the incoming frame, then scales back up to full screen. Looks like a projector reel slipping; the catalog says gauge (8-70mm) and strip look are customisable.
- **Mirror** [pass]: Film Impact Mirror (internal AE.AE_Impact_Mirror). This is a push in which the frame is filled with mirrored (flipped) copies of the picture instead of empty edges. The shots slide along the push direction with motion blur, the reflected tiles form symmetrical seams, and the two shots dissolve into each other near the middle. In the gallery render the cut frame is split into mirrored panels showing both shots, and the frames 0.3 s either side are nearly clean.
- **Push** [pass]: GPU Film Impact version of the classic push: the incoming shot slides in and shoves the outgoing shot off-frame, with eased curves and directional motion blur. Default direction brings the incoming shot in from the right and pushes the old one off to the left; stretch, rolling-shutter and chromatic aberration are optional (all 0 by default). It replaces 'Push (Legacy)'.
- **Roll** [pass]: Film Impact directional roll (not a rotation): the picture scrolls through the frame like a slot-machine reel or an old TV vertical roll, wrapping around and travelling a couple of frame heights (Rolls 2) with heavy directional motion blur, then lands on the incoming shot. Default direction is vertical; zoom, stretch, skew and chromatic aberration are optional (0 by default).
- **Split** [pass]: Film Impact split: the outgoing frame breaks along a line (default nearly horizontal, tilted -7.5 degrees, through frame centre) into two halves that push apart with a zoom and motion blur, revealing the incoming shot in the widening gap; Mode can reverse it so the halves close in. It replaces 'Split (Legacy)'.
- **Whip** [pass]: Whip pan with heavy horizontal motion blur. By default the picture travels right to left: the outgoing shot is flung off to the left and the incoming shot arrives from the right, as if the camera swung fast to the right. A Direction control changes the travel and an optional Chromatic Aberration adds colour fringes. This is the current replacement for Whip (Legacy).
- **Center Merge** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip A splits into four quarters that slide and shrink into the centre of the frame, revealing clip B behind. Old CS6-era slide. -> Premiere: Iris Box with Reverse ticked (A closes to the centre as one box; Reverse is an Effect Controls setting, not settable from the spec) gives a cleaner result. AE: four masked quarter copies of A, each scaled to 0 toward the comp centre with Position/Scale keyframes over B.
- **Push (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B slides in from one edge and shoves clip A out of the opposite edge, both frames moving together at constant speed with no blur. Default direction (seen in the gallery render): B enters from the left and A exits right. This is the pre-2026 Push kept for old projects; the new Film Impact 'Push' replaces it. -> PR replacement: 'Push' (new GPU transition with easing and motion blur; exact name 'Push', a separate list entry). AE: keyframe Position of both layers by one frame width (A from 0 to +W, B from -W to 0 for the default left-to-right) with Easy Ease and motion blur on.
- **Slash Slide** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Diagonal strips of clip B fly in at staggered speeds and slide over clip A until they join into the full frame. -> PR replacement: 'Slice' (new GPU transition, cleaner diagonal cut). AE: CC Line Sweep or Venetian Blinds at about 45 degrees on the outgoing layer, or several masked diagonal strips of the incoming layer with staggered Position keyframes.
- **Slide (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B slides in from an edge and covers clip A, which stays still underneath. Default direction (gallery render): B enters from the left. This is the pre-2026 Slide kept for old projects. -> PR: no new GPU transition named 'Slide' appears in the 2026 list; use the new 'Push', or animate Position on a Transform effect with easing for a modern slide. AE: keyframe Position of the incoming layer from off-frame to centre with Easy Ease and motion blur.
- **Sliding Boxes** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Vertical bars made of clip B slide across the screen one after another until clip B fills the frame. -> PR replacement: 'Louver' or 'Slice' (new GPU transitions). AE: masked vertical strips of the incoming layer with staggered Position keyframes (Sequence Layers or offset keys), or Card Wipe with 1 row and several columns.
- **Split (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older build of Split: the outgoing clip splits along a vertical centre line and the two halves slide apart to the left and right like doors opening, revealing the incoming clip in a widening centre strip. Replaced by the current Split. -> Premiere replacement: Split (non-legacy). After Effects: duplicate the outgoing layer, mask left/right halves, and animate Position of each half off-frame (ease out), incoming layer below.
- **Swap** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clips A and B trade places like shuffled cards: A slides out to one side and behind while B slides out the other way and comes to the front. -> AE: two layers with Position keyframes moving out to opposite sides and back, swapping stacking order at the midpoint (or 3D layers with Z Position pushed back on A), motion blur on.
- **Whip (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Old native GPU whip: the incoming clip pushes the outgoing one off horizontally under heavy directional motion blur, like a fast whip-pan. Superseded by the new 'Whip' (Film Impact based). -> Replacement in Premiere: 'Whip' (Slide folder, Film Impact AE.Impact_Whip). AE: animate Position of A out and B in over 6-10 frames with layer Motion Blur (shutter 360) or Directional Blur (direction 90 deg) keyframed to peak at the cut.

### stylized

- **Burn Alpha** [pass]: Luma-driven burn dissolve: the outgoing shot burns away through its darker tones (dark blotches spread, near black at the midpoint) and the incoming shot burns in from the shadows. Colourise options.
- **Glass** [pass]: Defocuses the outgoing clip, then refracts it through a grid of glass blocks (reeded/tiled glass) that smear the image into squares, and resolves the incoming clip from blur. A textured, refractive dissolve.
- **Kaleidoscope** [pass]: Film Impact Kaleido (internal AE.AE_Impact_Kaleido). The picture is folded into rotating, mirrored kaleidoscope segments that zoom, with motion blur. At the cut it becomes a dense mandala, and the incoming shot unfolds out of the pattern. In the 1 s gallery render the frame is still fully abstract 0.3 s before and after the cut, so most of the duration is unrecognisable.
- **Liquid Distortion** [pass]: Film Impact Liquid Distortion (internal AE.AE_Impact_Liquid_Distortion). The outgoing shot melts into a turbulent liquid warp, combined with a dolly or vertigo zoom, defocus and heavy motion blur, and the incoming shot settles out of the swirl. It is strong at default settings. In the gallery render the outgoing shot is already smeared 0.3 s before the cut, the cut frame is an unrecognisable marbled liquid, and the incoming shot is still swirling 0.3 s after.
- **Mosaic** [pass]: Film Impact Mosaic (internal AE.AE_Impact_Mosaic). This is a tile wipe, not a whole-frame pixelation. The frame breaks into a grid of square tiles (Num. Tiles 8 by default) that switch from the outgoing to the incoming shot in a staggered, partly random sweep. Tiles in mid-switch show as lit, softened blocks. In the gallery render the cut frame is a patchwork of both shots, and the frames 0.3 s either side are almost clean.
- **Phosphor** [pass]: Film Impact Phosphore (internal AE.AE_Impact_Phosphore). This is a fiery burn-through, not a CRT glow. Holes driven by luminance open organically in the outgoing shot, edged with white-hot orange (#FF6400) incandescent rims like burning film or paper, and reveal the incoming shot. In the gallery render the cut frame shows the incoming shot through large burnt holes with glowing edges, and bright white-orange embers are still flaring on the incoming shot 0.3 s after the cut.
- **Random Blocks** [pass]: Clip B appears in small rectangular blocks that pop in at random positions until they cover clip A.
- **Shape Flow** [pass]: Film Impact 'smart' reveal: the transition spreads outward from start points (default one, at frame centre), delayed by detected objects, edges and luminance, so graphics with alpha are flowed off and drawn on shape by shape, with an optional coloured gradient trail. On plain full-frame footage with defaults it acts as a circle that shrinks the outgoing shot to a point on black and grows the incoming one back from the centre.
- **Slice** [pass]: Film Impact 'ninja' slice: the frame is cut into strips that slide along their length in a staggered cascade with motion blur, pushing the old shot out and the new one in. Default is 7 vertical slices sliding up/down, with thin purple-magenta burn lines on the slice edges (Burn Amount 100 is on by default); Angle turns it horizontal or diagonal.
- **Solarize** [pass]: Film Impact tonal-inversion transition: across the cut the image solarizes, with highlights and shadows flipping into a burnt, partly negative picture and glowing streaks, then it resolves into the next shot. Defaults (Red Gain +25, Green -50, Blue -100, Glow 48) give an orange-brown, fire-like negative at the cut and grey posterized patches either side; the teal look in the dashboard demo comes from that demo's footage.
- **Stripe** [pass]: Film Impact stripes transition. A pattern of thin diagonal stripes (45 degrees by default, tinted a pale cream colour) slides over the whole frame. The outgoing and incoming shots interleave through the stripes, and the pattern clears off the incoming shot. It reads as a graphic striped blend rather than a wipe with one moving edge.
- **TV Power** [pass]: Old CRT television switch-off and switch-on. The outgoing picture turns magenta-tinted with scanlines and shrinks into a keystoned (trapezoid) screen, then collapses to a bright horizontal line and a dot with an RGB flare. A few frames of black follow. The incoming picture then powers on in reverse: dot, line, trapezoid, full frame.
- **Text Animator** [pass]: Film Impact text animator. On a title or graphic clip with transparency, it finds the separate objects (letters, words or lines, detected from gaps in the alpha) and animates them on or off with a stagger. The objects offset, zoom and fade with an elastic overshoot curve, and a Preset menu offers other styles. On two full-frame video clips there is nothing to split: the gallery render is just a dip through black with a slight move.
- **Typewriter** [pass]: Film Impact typewriter. It reveals a title or graphic with transparency one character at a time, left to right and line by line (characters are detected from gaps in the alpha), and removes it the same way at the out point. An optional blinking caret is off by default. On two full-frame video clips the whole frame counts as one character: the gallery render shows the outgoing shot cutting to black for about 0.35 s and the incoming shot popping on.
- **Wave** [pass]: The outgoing shot ripples and warps in sine waves, dissolves into the incoming shot at the peak of the distortion, and the waves settle out. A liquid, dreamy distortion transition with motion blur on by default.
- **Channel Map** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Builds the transition frames from colour channels picked from clip A and clip B in a settings dialog. With the default mapping the harvest shows plain, unaltered clip A for the whole transition, then a hard cut to B at the end, so the cut lands about half the duration late.
- **Displace** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Old CS6-era transition meant to warp one clip using the colour channels of the other. With default settings the harvest shows clip B from the start of the transition with only a faint displacement (a few pixels, invisible at normal viewing size), so it reads as a hard cut.
- **Luminance Map** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Blends the two clips by brightness: during the transition the picture turns into a harsh, near-monochrome high-contrast mix where one clip's luminance drives the other, then resolves into the incoming clip. -> PR replacement: Luma Fade (new transition: a cleaner brightness-driven dissolve that keeps colour). Gradient Wipe exists in v26 only as a video effect, not a transition. AE: Transition > Gradient Wipe with Gradient Layer set to the outgoing clip itself (luma-driven reveal), optionally with Hue/Saturation desaturation and Levels contrast keyed over the overlap for the harsh mono look.
- **Stretch** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B starts squeezed thin against the left edge and stretches across the frame, covering clip A, which stays put. This is the old Stretch-folder version, not the new GPU 'Stretch'. -> PR: the new GPU 'Stretch' has the same name and cannot be reached by name (see scripting); 'Stretch Wipe' is the scriptable modern alternative. AE: keyframe Scale X of the incoming layer 0 to 100% with the anchor point on its left edge, or CC Scale Wipe.
- **Stretch** *(unusable)* [pass]: New Premiere 26 GPU 'Stretch' (Film Impact set, list index 144): from Film Impact's description, the outgoing shot is pulled and smeared out of frame in one direction like rubber and the incoming shot snaps back from a stretched state. NOT VERIFIED: the scripted route never reaches this transition. Applying 'Stretch' by name always gives the old CS-era Stretch (match 'ADBE Stretch'), where clip B squeezes in from the left edge and widens across the frame while A is squashed to the right.
- **Stretch In** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B appears over clip A stretched very wide, then un-stretches to its normal shape while fading in. -> PR: no modern by-name match (the new GPU 'Stretch' shares its name with the old one and is unreachable by name); 'Stretch Wipe' is the nearest scriptable modern elastic transition. AE: on the incoming layer keyframe Scale X from about 300% to 100% together with Opacity 0 to 100%, Easy Ease out.
- **Stretch Over** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip B appears as a thin full-width horizontal band across the middle, squashed vertically, and stretches up and down over clip A until it fills the frame. -> PR: no modern by-name match ('Stretch Wipe' is the nearest scriptable elastic transition). AE: on the incoming layer keyframe Scale Y 0 to 100% with the anchor point at the centre (Scale X stays 100%).
- **Swirl** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip arrives as a cluster of small rotating tiles that swirl out from the centre and grow until they assemble into the full frame. -> PR: Shape Flow or Kaleidoscope (new transitions) for a modern stylized hand-off. AE: Card Wipe on the outgoing layer (Back Layer = incoming, Flip Axis Random, keyframe Transition Completion 0 -> 100%) or CC Twister.
- **Texturize** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Maps the luminance of one clip onto the other as an embossed texture, so both images appear merged in a relief-like, colour-shifted blend for the length of the transition, then it cuts to the incoming clip. -> PR: Additive Dissolve or Luma Fade for a cleaner blend. AE: Texturize effect (Stylize > Texturize) on the incoming layer with Texture Layer = outgoing clip, plus an opacity crossfade.

### wipe

- **Band Wipe** [pass]: Clip B is revealed through horizontal bands that wipe in from alternate sides while both images stay still. Like Band Slide but the picture does not move, only the reveal does.
- **Barn Doors** [pass]: A hard-edged wipe that opens from a vertical seam in the centre: the incoming clip appears in a centre strip that widens toward the left and right edges like opening barn doors. The outgoing halves are masked in place, they do not slide.
- **Checker Wipe** [pass]: Clip B wipes across clip A in a checkerboard of squares, each square wiping in the same direction at staggered times.
- **CheckerBoard** [pass]: Clip B appears in a checkerboard pattern of squares that fill in over clip A, in two passes (alternate squares first, then the rest).
- **Clock Wipe** [pass]: Radial clock-hand wipe: the incoming shot is revealed by a hand sweeping clockwise from 12 o'clock around the centre (hard edge at defaults). Adds repeats, mirroring and curve controls. Replaces Clock Wipe (Legacy).
- **Inset** [pass]: The incoming clip is revealed in a hard-edged rectangle that starts in the top-left corner and grows diagonally until it fills the frame. It is a wipe: the incoming image is cropped, not scaled (gallery shows subjects at their full-frame positions inside the growing box).
- **Light Sweep** [pass]: Film Impact Light Sweep (internal AE.AE_Impact_Light_Sweep). This is a hard-edged angled wipe with a bright warm-gold light beam riding the edge, like a shine pass on a logo, plus a slight emboss or relief on the edge. In the gallery render the default angle is diagonal (Angle 60): the beam travels from the lower-left to the right, with the incoming shot on the left of the beam and the outgoing shot on the right.
- **Linear Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact Linear Wipe (internal AE.AE_Impact_Linear_Wipe). A straight-edged, eased wipe with a small feather replaces the outgoing shot. In the gallery render the default angle is diagonal (Angle 60), with the incoming shot sweeping in from the lower-left and a little motion smear on the edge. Repeats (several parallel bands), Mirror and an optional coloured or glowing stroke on the edge are available.
- **Neon Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact Copy Machine, renamed (internal AE.AE_Impact_Copy_Machine). A thin glowing neon line, magenta-pink #FF00AA by default with a purple glow trail, sweeps diagonally across the frame like a scanner bar and reveals the incoming shot behind it. In the gallery render the line enters from the left before the cut, sits about halfway across at the cut and exits bottom-right after it, so it is visible for most of the duration.
- **Paint Splatter** [pass]: The incoming clip is revealed through irregular paint-splash blobs that spread until they cover the frame.
- **Panel Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact Panel Wipe (internal AE.AE_Impact_PanelWipe). Angled panels (135 degrees by default) slide across the frame in a stagger to reveal the incoming shot. The panels behave like tinted glass, with slight refraction, texture, warm highlights and chromatic fringing at their edges. In the gallery render the cut frame is split into diagonal bands of both shots, with the first band visible top-left 0.3 s before the cut and the last leaving bottom-right 0.3 s after.
- **Pinwheel** [pass]: The incoming clip is revealed by many rotating wedge blades sweeping round the centre like a pinwheel until they fill the frame.
- **Plateau Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact 'transformer' wipe: a soft-edged angled wipe (default 30 degrees, Wipe Feather 50) in which both pictures are lifted toward camera as the edge passes (Lift Clip A 100, Lift Clip B 50), with motion blur. With defaults on footage it reads as a soft diagonal wipe with a slight push-in, spread over the whole duration rather than a hard line.
- **Random Wipe** [pass]: Clip B is revealed by small blocks that fill in at random along a moving front, giving a ragged pixel wipe. Default direction (gallery render): top to bottom.
- **Soft Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact wipe with a wide, blurred and feathered edge. By default it runs diagonally (45 degrees) from the lower-left corner to the upper-right, with a slight brightening along the edge. The incoming shot sweeps in behind a soft gradient border instead of a hard line.
- **Spiral Boxes** [pass]: A band of boxes travels round the frame edge and spirals in toward the centre, replacing clip A with clip B box by box.
- **Stretch Wipe** [pass]: Film Impact rubbery wipe: pixels along the wipe edge stretch into long streaks (jelly/rubber feel) on both clips as the incoming shot is pulled across, with a slight exposure lift and strong motion blur. Default angle wipes vertically, bottom to top, with vertical streaks; Angle sets any other direction.
- **VR Random Blocks** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) block dissolve: small rectangular blocks of the incoming clip pop in at random positions until they cover the frame; at the cut the image is a jumble of both clips, and a few stray blocks of the outgoing clip linger briefly after it. On flat footage it looks like a digital blocky dissolve.
- **Venetian Blinds** [pass]: The incoming clip is revealed through horizontal bands that open together like venetian blinds.
- **Wedge Wipe** [pass]: A pie-shaped wedge opens from the centre of the frame and sweeps both ways around it until the incoming clip fills the screen.
- **Wipe** [pass]: A straight hard-edged line moves across the frame (left to right by default), revealing the incoming clip behind it.
- **Zig-Zag Blocks** [pass]: The incoming clip fills in as rows of blocks that run back and forth in a zig-zag, row by row from the top to the bottom of the frame.
- **Clock Wipe (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The old Clock Wipe: a radius line sweeps clockwise around the centre from 12 o'clock like a clock hand, revealing the incoming clip. -> PR: Clock Wipe (the new v26 transition). AE: Radial Wipe on the outgoing layer, Start Angle 0, Wipe = Clockwise, keyframe Transition Completion 0 -> 100%.
- **Radial Wipe (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Older build of Radial Wipe: a hard straight edge pivots on the top-left corner and sweeps clockwise from the top edge down to the left edge, like a clock hand, revealing the incoming clip (the outgoing clip's last sliver sits in the bottom-left). Replaced by the current Radial Wipe. -> Premiere replacement: Radial Wipe (current). After Effects: Radial Wipe effect on the outgoing layer above the incoming one, Wipe Center at the top-left corner [0, 0], Start Angle 90 degrees, Wipe Clockwise, Transition Completion 0->25% over the duration (only one quadrant of the sweep is in frame); add a little Feather to soften.
- **Sliding Bands** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Thin vertical bands of clip B open up in clip A and widen until they join into the full frame, like vertical blinds opening. The opening is staggered from left to right: the left side is already solid B while the bands on the right are still thin. -> PR: closest new GPU transitions are 'Stripe' (flat bands) or 'Louver' (3D slats). AE: Venetian Blinds on the outgoing layer (Direction 90 degrees for vertical bands) animating Transition Completion 0 to 100%.
- **VR Gradient Wipe** *(unusable)* [pass]: Immersive Video (360) wipe meant to reveal the incoming clip along a soft gradient. With the defaults a script gets on flat footage it does not read as a wipe: the outgoing clip vanishes within about two frames of the transition start, and a few of its brightest fragments (flowers, a small gold detail) linger over the incoming clip until the end, which looks like a hard cut with leftover artefacts.

### zoom

- **Cross Zoom** [pass]: Outgoing clip zooms in hard with a radial zoom blur toward the centre, the switch happens at the blur peak, and the incoming clip zooms back out of the same blur to rest. New GPU version that replaces Cross Zoom (Legacy), which had no blur. Gallery render confirms strong radial streaking at the midpoint.
- **Frame** [pass]: The incoming clip appears as a small rounded-corner card with a white border in the centre and grows over the outgoing clip (which pushes in slightly) until it fills the screen. A picture-in-picture 'card zoom' reveal.
- **Pop Motion** [pass]: Film Impact scale transition built for motion graphics: the outgoing element shrinks away and the incoming one pops up with a bounce/overshoot curve and motion blur. On a graphic or PNG with alpha it animates the element in or out over the track below; on full-frame footage the gallery render shrinks the outgoing shot into a small zoom-blurred box on black for 2-3 frames around the cut.
- **Pull Motion** [pass]: Film Impact zoom-through transition: the outgoing picture is pulled toward the viewer (scales up hugely, default Zoom 800) and fades, and the incoming one arrives out of depth with a zoom blur that settles on an eased curve. Works on graphics with alpha and on full frames; on full-frame footage the gallery render passes through black for a few frames at the cut (Fade 60).
- **VR Mobius Zoom** [pass]: Immersive Video (360) zoom: the outgoing clip bends into a spherical, fish-eye wrap while the incoming clip opens from a small circle at the frame centre and grows outward, pushing the wrapped remains of the outgoing clip off the left and right edges as spheres.
- **Zoom Blur** [pass]: The outgoing shot rushes forward into a radial zoom blur and the incoming shot resolves out of the same blur, like a fast punch through the centre of the frame. The zoom centre is movable and a slight exposure lift is on by default.
- **Cross Zoom (Legacy)** *(obsolete)* [pass]: Clip A zooms hard into its centre, the cut happens at maximum zoom, then clip B zooms back out from close-up. No motion blur, so the midpoint is a flat blow-up of a few pixels. -> Premiere replacement: Cross Zoom (new GPU version, adds blur). AE: keyframe Scale of A up to ~400-1000% into the cut and B down from the same to 100%, with CC Radial Fast Blur or Transform shutter angle for motion blur.
- **Zoom** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip starts as a small rectangle in the centre and scales up until it fills the frame over the outgoing clip. -> PR: Pop Motion / Motion Tween (new transitions) or Motion Scale keyframes with easing. AE: keyframe the incoming layer Scale 0 -> 100% with an ease-out (optionally add motion blur).
- **Zoom Boxes** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The incoming clip appears as a grid of small boxes that each scale up from their own centre until they merge into the full frame. -> PR: Mosaic or Shape Flow (new transitions) for a modern tiled hand-off. AE: a grid of square shape layers scaling 0 -> 100% used as a track matte for the incoming layer; CC Grid Wipe is the nearest single effect.
- **Zoom Trails** *(obsolete)* [pass]: The outgoing clip shrinks toward the centre leaving a tunnel of trailing copies behind it, revealing the incoming clip as the trails vanish. -> PR: Zoom Blur or Cross Zoom (new transitions) for a modern zoom hand-off. AE: keyframe the outgoing layer Scale 100 -> 0% and add Echo (Number of Echoes 6-10, Decay 0.7) or CC Force Motion Blur.

## Premiere Pro audio effects


### Amplitude and Compression

- **Amplify**: Boosts or cuts the level of the clip by a fixed amount, with a gain slider per channel (linked by default).
- **Balance**: Shifts a stereo clip's level between the left and right channels. Works only on stereo clips; on a mono clip it cannot be applied.
- **Channel Mixer**: Routes and mixes the input channels into each output channel (L to L, R to L, L to R ... up to 8 channels): swap, fold to mono, invert one side or blend left and right on a stereo or multichannel clip.
- **Channel Volume**: Sets the level of each channel of a stereo or 5.1 clip separately (for example left and right), in dB.
- **DeEsser**: Frequency-targeted compressor that turns down harsh sibilance ('s', 'sh', 'ch') in voices when it crosses a threshold.
- **Dynamics**: All-in-one dynamics processor (the current Audition-derived Dynamics) with AutoGate (silences low-level noise between words), Compressor, Expander and Limiter sections, each switched on separately.
- **Dynamics Processing**: Audition's graph-based dynamics processor: a drawn input-to-output level curve makes it a compressor, limiter, expander or noise gate, with separate level-detector and gain-processor timing.
- **Hard Limiter**: Brick-wall limiter: keeps audio from going above a set ceiling and can push the input into that ceiling to make it louder. The usual last effect on a mix.
- **Multiband Compressor**: Splits audio into up to four frequency bands (default crossovers about 120 Hz, 2 kHz and 10 kHz) and compresses each separately, with a peak limiter on the output. Tames boomy lows or harsh highs without pumping the whole mix.
- **Single-band Compressor**: Classic full-band compressor: reduces the level of anything above the threshold by the ratio, then makes it back up with output gain. Evens out a voice so quiet words and loud words sit closer together.
- **Tube-modeled Compressor**: Same controls as Single-band Compressor but emulates a vintage tube compressor, adding subtle harmonic warmth and a softer, rounder compression character.
- **Volume**: Stackable gain effect that raises or lowers loudness and can be keyframed for fades and ducking. Same control as the fixed Volume every clip already has, but placed wherever you want in the effect chain.

### Delay and Echo

- **Analog Delay**: Echo modelled on tape, tube and analog delay units: repeats of the sound with warm, slightly dirty colouring. Delay time, feedback, wet/dry mix and saturation are adjustable.
- **Delay**: Single echo with feedback: replays the sound after a set time, repeating and fading according to Feedback.
- **Multitap Delay**: Up to four separate echoes, each with its own delay time, feedback and level, mixed with the dry sound. Builds rhythmic or layered echoes.

### Filter and EQ

- **Bandpass**: Keeps a band of frequencies around a centre frequency and removes everything above and below it; Q sets how narrow the band is.
- **Bass**: Boosts or cuts the low frequencies (roughly 200 Hz and below) with a single Boost control.
- **FFT Filter**: Draw-a-curve filter: an FFT-based gain-vs-frequency graph built from up to 128 X/Y points, good for very steep notches, band-passes and telephone or radio shapes.
- **Graphic Equalizer (10 Bands)**: Fixed-frequency equalizer with 10 octave-spaced sliders (about 31 Hz to 16 kHz) that boost or cut each band. Fast broad tone shaping without choosing frequencies.
- **Graphic Equalizer (20 Bands)**: Fixed-frequency equalizer with 20 half-octave sliders from about 31 Hz up. Finer than the 10-band version for broad tone shaping.
- **Graphic Equalizer (30 Bands)**: Fixed-frequency equalizer with 30 third-octave sliders, the classic live-sound graphic EQ. Fine enough to dip a resonant room tone or mild hum band without a parametric EQ.
- **Highpass**: Removes everything below a cutoff frequency and passes the highs. The standard rumble, wind and handling-noise cleanup for voice.
- **Lowpass**: Removes frequencies above the Cutoff and lets the lows through, so sound becomes muffled and distant. Keyframing the Cutoff gives the classic muffled-to-full sweep.
- **Notch Filter**: Cuts up to six very narrow frequency bands (notches), each with its own frequency, depth and on/off switch, plus DTMF options that remove phone keypad tones.
- **Parametric Equalizer**: Audition's full parametric EQ: low and high shelves, five peaking bands, plus switchable high-pass and low-pass filters and an output gain. The workhorse tone-shaping tool for VO, music and SFX.
- **Scientific Filter**: Precise mathematical low-pass, high-pass, band-pass or band-stop filter (Bessel, Butterworth, Chebyshev, Elliptic designs) with set order, ripple and attenuation. A technical tool for surgical cuts rather than a musical EQ.
- **Simple Notch Filter**: Removes a narrow band around one frequency and leaves the rest untouched. Used to kill a single steady tone such as hum or a whine.
- **Simple Parametric EQ**: A single-band bell EQ: boosts or cuts a band of frequencies around Center, with Q setting how narrow the band is.
- **Treble**: High-shelf EQ with one Boost control that raises or lowers the high frequencies (roughly 4 kHz and up).

### Modulation

- **Chorus/Flanger**: Modulated short delays in one effect: chorus thickens a sound into several slightly detuned voices, flanger sweeps a hollow jet-plane comb filter. Mode picks which.
- **Flanger**: Audition's flanger: mixes the sound with a copy whose short delay sweeps between an initial and a final time, giving the classic jet-plane whoosh and metallic comb sweep.
- **Phaser**: Sweeping phase-shift filter like the classic guitar pedal: moving notches give a swirling, whooshing, spacey motion to the sound.

### Noise Reduction/Restoration

- **Adaptive Noise Reduction**: Learns the steady background noise (hiss, fan, AC, traffic wash) on the fly and reduces it, without a noise print. Strong settings cause watery, bubbly artefacts.
- **Automatic Click Remover**: Finds and repairs short clicks, pops and crackle automatically (Adobe Audition's restoration algorithm). It treats brief impulses only, not steady hiss or hum.
- **DeHummer**: Removes electrical hum (mains frequency and its harmonics) with a comb of narrow notch filters. Frequency, notch width, depth and number of harmonics are adjustable.
- **DeNoise**: Real-time adaptive noise reduction that learns and removes steady background noise (fan, AC, hiss, traffic hum) without a noise print. One main Amount control.
- **DeReverb**: Reduces room echo and reverb tail on recorded speech, making a voice recorded in a hall, office or tiled room sound closer and drier.

### Reverb

- **Convolution Reverb**: Places audio in a real space by convolving it with an impulse response (rooms, halls, churches, plates); the space is chosen from a built-in Impulse list.
- **Studio Reverb**: Algorithmic room reverb with controls for room size, decay, damping, diffusion and wet/dry levels. Light on CPU and the usual choice for adding space in Premiere.
- **Surround Reverb**: Convolution reverb built for 5.1 surround: places sound in a real sampled space (chosen by impulse) with separate front/surround width, balance and centre/LFE input controls. Mainly for 5.1 mixes.

### Special

- **Distortion**: Waveshaping distortion: an amplitude transfer curve (drawn on positive and negative graphs in the UI) clips and bends the waveform for grit, fuzz or a broken-speaker sound.
- **Fill Left with Right**: Copies the right channel into the left and discards the original left, so both speakers play the right channel.
- **Fill Right with Left**: Copies the left channel into the right and discards the original right, so both speakers play the left channel.
- **GuitarSuite**: Guitar amp chain (from Audition): compressor, filter, distortion and speaker-cabinet modeler with a dry/wet Mix. Made for guitar, but roughens any sound.
- **Invert**: Flips the polarity (phase) of the clip's audio by 180 degrees. On its own it sounds identical; it only matters when the clip is mixed with a second mic or another copy of the same sound.
- **Loudness Meter**: Meters loudness (momentary, short-term and integrated LUFS plus true peak) against a target level. Analysis only: it does not change the sound.
- **Loudness Radar**: TC Electronic loudness meter shown as a radar display (short-term, momentary and integrated loudness against a target such as EBU R128 -23 LUFS). It measures only and does not change the audio.
- **Mastering**: All-in-one mastering chain (from Audition): 3-band EQ (low shelf, peak, high shelf), reverb, exciter, stereo widener and loudness maximizer with output gain.
- **Mute**: Silences the clip's audio, either all channels at once or individual channels, and can be keyframed to silence just a section.
- **Swap Channels**: Swaps the left and right channels of a stereo clip, so what was on the left plays on the right and vice versa.
- **Vocal Enhancer**: One-knob voice sweetener: applies preset EQ and compression tuned for a low (male) voice, high (female) voice or music, reducing sibilance and plosives and adding presence.

### Stereo Imagery

- **Stereo Expander**: Re-centres (Center Channel Pan) and widens (Stereo Expand) the stereo image of a stereo clip. Stereo clips only: it does not attach to mono clips.

### Time and Pitch

- **Pitch Shifter**: Raises or lowers the pitch of a clip without changing its speed. The UI shows Semi-tones (-12..+12), Cents and Ratio (0.5..2.0); the API exposes only the normalised Transpose Ratio. Works on voice, music and SFX.

## After Effects animation presets


### Adobe Express/In

- **Bungee In** [pass]: Springs into place from a 200 px offset (not from off-screen) in the chosen Direction, overshooting and settling in 1-4 decaying bounces like on a bungee cord; opacity fades up over the first quarter of the Duration.
- **Fade In** [pass]: Plain opacity fade from 0 to 100%, finishing at half the Duration, with the ease curve picked by Personality.
- **Flicker In** [pass]: Strobes the layer's opacity (3 dips toward 100 - Intensity %) over the first half of the Duration, then holds at full opacity. Its curve starts at 100%, so the layer is already visible on its first frame: it reads as a flicker-on, not a fade from black.
- **Grow In** [pass]: Scales up from 0% to full size around the anchor point (comp-space anchor for text/shape layers) while fading in over the first half, eased by Personality.
- **Pop In** [pass]: Grows from 0% with an overshoot bump past full size, then settles at 100% (Scale = Base Animation 0->100 + Bump 0->50->0 x Intensity); opacity fades up over the first half.
- **Shrink In** [pass]: Starts at 150% and shrinks down to 100% while fading in over the first half, like a slam or zoom-down landing, eased by Personality.
- **Slide In** [pass]: Slides 200 px into its resting position along the chosen Direction (a short slide, not from off-screen) while fading in over the first half, eased by Personality.
- **Spin In** [pass]: Starts rotated (30-360 deg, set by Intensity) and 200 px off along the Movement Direction, then unwinds to upright and slides into place while fading up; Rotation Direction picks the turning direction (1 Clockwise really turns clockwise here).
- **Tumble In** [pass]: Bounces in sideways like a tossed object: it travels 250 px in the chosen Direction while dropping from 150 px up and landing in shrinking hops (75, 25, 12, 5 px), unrotating from the Rotation tilt to upright in the first ~45% of the move; opacity fades up over the first quarter.

### Adobe Express/Looping

- **Blinking Loop** [pass]: Continuously blinks the layer's opacity between a lower value and 100%, with the blink shape set by Personality.
- **Bob Loop** [pass]: Floats the layer up and down (always vertical) with a gentle rocking rotation, like something bobbing on water; a 2 s cycle at Speed 0.
- **Breathe Loop** [pass]: Slowly scales the layer up and down around 100% in a continuous breathing rhythm, shape set by Personality.
- **Flicker Loop** [pass]: Keeps the layer's opacity flickering in an irregular pattern (5 dips per 2.3 s at 1x), like a failing light or a neon tube; Intensity sets how dark the dips go.
- **Jitter Loop** [pass]: Adds a nervous, choppy jitter: a wiggle(50 Hz) whose sample rate is stepped down by Speed, giving a stop-motion shake.
- **Pulse Loop** [pass]: Repeatedly pops the layer up in scale and back to 100% like a heartbeat, shape set by Personality.
- **Spin Loop** [pass]: Spins the layer continuously, one eased turn after another (ease set by Personality), clockwise or counter-clockwise.
- **Wiggle Loop** [pass]: Wobbles the layer's rotation randomly (5-45 degrees by Intensity) with a stepped, hand-made feel from posterized time.
- **Yoyo Loop** [pass]: Moves the layer back and forth along the chosen direction in a smooth yo-yo oscillation.

### Adobe Express/Out

- **Bungee Out** [pass]: Winds up with 1-4 growing wobbles around its position, then is flung 200 px in the chosen Direction (not off-screen) while fading out over the last quarter of the Duration.
- **Fade Out** [pass]: Plain opacity fade from 100 to 0%, over the last half of the Duration, ease curve set by Personality.
- **Flicker Out** [pass]: Strobes the layer's opacity (3 dips toward 100 - Intensity %) over the last half of the Duration, and the third dip lands on the last frames before the out-point, so at the default Intensity 100 the layer is already dark when it ends: it reads as a flicker-off, not a fade.
- **Grow Out** [pass]: Scales the layer from 100% up to 200% around its anchor while fading it out over the last half, as if it blows toward the camera and vanishes.
- **Pop Out** [pass]: Pops away like a bubble: Scale = Base Animation (100->200) minus Bump (0->50->0) x Intensity, so it first holds or squeezes slightly, then swells to 200% while fading out over the last half. It does not shrink to nothing.
- **Shrink Out** [pass]: Shrinks the layer from 100% to 50% around its anchor while fading it out over the last half; it fades away rather than collapsing to a point.
- **Slide Out** [pass]: Slides the layer 200 px away from its position in the chosen Direction (a short slide, not off-screen) while fading out over the last half, eased by Personality.
- **Spin Out** [pass]: Rotates the layer away (30-360 deg by Intensity) while sliding 200 px in the Movement Direction and fading out over the last half. The Rotation Direction labels are reversed here (2 'Counter Clockwise', the default, actually turns clockwise).
- **Tumble Out** [pass]: Hops away sideways: it travels 250 px in the chosen Direction with growing hops (5 px up to 150 px), tipping over by the Rotation angle in the second half and fading out over the last quarter. It does not leave the frame; the fade hides it.

### Backgrounds

- **Anime Radial** [pass]: Black-on-white manga focus lines: jagged black streaks radiating from the Center toward the frame edges around a clean white central circle, re-randomised on stepped frames (Posterize Time). Fully opaque.
- **Anime Speed Lines** [pass]: Anime speed lines: magenta / violet streaks with orange-white hot cores on near-black, scrolling sideways, tapered by CC Power Pin so they converge toward the left, coloured by CC Toner and stepped with Posterize Time. Horizontal by default.
- **Apparition** [pass]: Vertical streaks of sepia / cream light on black (Fractal Noise stretched tall, coloured by Tritone) that flicker erratically: keyframed Fast Box Blur and Dust & Scratches jump between sharp and heavily blurred every few frames, like a haunted, failing projector beam.
- **Blocks** [pass]: Large flat colour blocks (navy, indigo, purple and pink) made by squaring Fractal Noise off with Minimax and colouring it with Tritone; over about 5 s the blocks morph from tall to wide as the noise scale keys swap.
- **Cinders** [pass]: Glowing orange-yellow ember specks and clusters on black: two Fractal Noise layers evolving in opposite directions, a Glow pass and warm Tritone colouring, like drifting cinders.
- **Circuit** [pass]: Thin green rectangular outlines on black (Fractal Noise traced by Find Edges, coloured green by Tritone), overlapping like a circuit-board or tech grid, slowly zooming in and evolving.
- **Cosmic Power** [pass]: Electric blue-and-white plasma: contrasty Fractal Noise shaped by Levels and coloured by Tritone into glowing energy veins on black, brightening and gaining contrast over its run.
- **Creepy** [pass]: Sparse amber / sepia specks and clumps on black that churn fast (Evolution 0 to 1800 deg) and get randomly smeared by Directional Blur whose direction and length are re-keyed about every 0.1 s, like crawling bugs or flickering film dirt.
- **Curtain** [pass]: A deep crimson velvet stage curtain: Fractal Noise stretched into vertical folds and coloured red by Tritone, slowly rippling.
- **Deep Tissue** [pass]: Magenta / plum cell-like blobs separated by white glowing veins (Fractal Noise with Glow and Tritone), slowly zooming and morphing, like living tissue under a microscope.
- **Fog Lights** [pass]: Slate blue-grey drifting fog / cloud texture with lighter patches (Fractal Noise shaped by Levels and coloured by Tritone): a soft, moody haze.
- **Green Crystals** [pass]: Glowing green block-like shards streaking outward from a dark centre (Fractal Noise blasted by CC Radial Fast Blur, softened, glowed and coloured green by Tritone), like flying through a tunnel of green crystal cubes.
- **Indigestion** [pass]: Dusty-red / pink bubbly cells with dark membranes (Fractal Noise shaped by Levels and coloured by Tritone), slowly churning, like stomach lining or blood cells.
- **Infection** [pass]: Turns a solid into a dark-red organic texture: stretched Fractal Noise smeared by Directional Blur (90 deg, length 10), pushed by Levels (input black 4, white 168, gamma 1.15), embossed by Color Emboss (50% blend) and toned cream / blood red / near-black by Tritone. Over about 5 s it brightens, gains contrast and churns like spreading tissue.
- **Lightning Bend** [pass]: Thin, very high-contrast white-gold filaments on black (Fractal Noise at about 388 contrast, zoomed to 1000%, toned by Tritone) that writhe like bending electric arcs as rotation swings and complexity rises over about 5 s.
- **Magma** [pass]: Molten-lava texture: Basic Fractal Noise toned cream / orange / dark brown-red by Tritone with Levels contrast. It slowly zooms in (Scale 100->300), turns and churns (Evolution 720 deg) over about 5 s.
- **Orb** [pass]: Dark cosmic background with a glowing rim: wide-stretched Fractal Noise is pushed through a huge Bulge lens (Horizontal / Vertical Radius 1600 x 900 px, Bulge Height 4 = maximum) and toned by CC Toner (near-black, indigo, periwinkle, lilac-white). In the 960x540 test render the whole frame sat deep inside that bulge, so 91-93% of it stayed near-black and only a thin periwinkle-to-lilac crescent glowed along the top-left edge (plus a sliver at the bottom), like a planet limb or eclipse, dimming to deep purple by 1.4 s; it did not read as a visible glowing sphere. The radii are fixed pixels sized for a 1920x1080 or larger frame, so a bigger comp should show more of the rim (not verified). Rotation, sub-rotation and evolution animate over about 5 s while the Bulge centre drifts sideways.
- **Racing Rectangles** [pass]: Green digital bars: block-type Fractal Noise stretched 12x wide into rectangles that race sideways (Offset Turbulence animates), with Glow and a lime / green / black Tritone. Looks like a data stream or a matrix screen.
- **Red Speed** [pass]: Red horizontal speed streaks: Fractal Noise stretched 1000->2000% wide, smeared sideways by a 200 px horizontal Fast Box Blur, crushed by Levels (input black 151, white 224) and toned white / deep red / black. Fast evolution (1440 deg in about 5 s) with counter-rotating detail gives a rushing, urgent motion.
- **River** [pass]: Calm blue flowing streaks, like light on moving water: wide-stretched Fractal Noise toned pale blue / ocean blue / navy with Levels, slowly rotating and evolving 360 deg over about 5 s.
- **Rose Light** [pass]: Soft rose-and-crimson blurred texture: Fractal Noise zooming 200->400% is smeared by CC Radial Fast Blur (Amount 97, Zoom = Brightest, centre in the upper-left area), softened by Fast Box Blur and toned rose / crimson / near-black by Tritone. In the 960x540 test render it read as large out-of-focus pink and crimson blotches with only a faint radial smear, no distinct light rays and no near-black areas (the darkest patches are deep crimson, about #4C0C13); the blotches shift and the crimson deepens between 0.5 s and 1.4 s. Rotation and evolution animate over about 5 s.
- **Silk** [pass]: Smooth flowing silk-fabric folds: wide-stretched Fractal Noise run through Find Edges and Curves into soft creased ridges, toned blush / mauve / plum by Tritone, gently swaying (rotation +-5 deg, evolution 360 deg) over about 5 s.
- **Smoke Rising** [pass]: Grey rising smoke: Fractal Noise stretched 10x tall into wisps, bent by Wave Warp whose phase cycles 360 deg, and toned white / slate grey / black. The wisps waver and churn over about 5 s (Fractal Noise Cycle Evolution is on, 1 revolution).
- **Sweeping Curves** [pass]: Abstract teal-green flowing bands: wide-stretched Fractal Noise bent into sweeping curves by Turbulent Displace, with Glow and a mint / teal / dark-green Tritone. The bands drift and evolve over about 5 s.

### Behaviors

- **Autoscroll - horizontal** [pass]: Makes the layer scroll sideways forever: Motion Tile repeats the layer and an expression moves 'Tile Center' at 'Speed (pixels/second)' (default 100), while 'Output Width' is set so the tiles always fill the comp width.
- **Autoscroll - vertical** [pass]: Makes the layer scroll vertically forever: Motion Tile repeats the layer and 'Tile Center' moves at 'Speed (pixels/second)' (default -25, a slow credit-roll-like move), with 'Output Height' set so the tiles fill the comp height.
- **Blinking Opacity** [pass]: Pulses the layer's Opacity smoothly (sine wave) between 'Min Opacity' (0) and 'Max Opacity' (100) at 'Frequency (per Second)' (1 per second), with an 'Offset' slider (-0.25) to shift where the blink starts; at the default the layer starts at Min Opacity at its in point.
- **Drift Over Time** [pass]: Moves the layer in a straight line at a constant speed: a Transform effect's 'Position' is driven by 'Direction' (degrees, 0 = up, 90 = right; default 135 = down-right) and 'Speed (pixels/second)' (default 50).
- **Fade In+Out - frames** [pass]: Fades the layer in from 0 at its in point and out to 0 at its out point, using Solid Composite 'Source Opacity'. Durations are in frames: 'Fade In Duration (frames)' and 'Fade Out Duration (frames)', 15 each by default; 0 turns that fade off.
- **Fade In+Out - msec** [pass]: Fades the layer in from 0 at its in point and out to 0 at its out point, using Solid Composite 'Source Opacity'. Durations are in milliseconds: 'Fade In Duration (msec)' and 'Fade Out Duration (msec)', 500 each by default; 0 turns that fade off.
- **Lock Anchor Point** [pass]: Utility rig with no look of its own: it pins the layer's Anchor Point to one of 9 spots of its content box (sourceRectAtTime), so scale and rotation pivot from there even when the text changes. Choose the spot with the 'Lock Anchor Point To...' menu.
- **Opacity Flash - layer markers** [pass]: The layer is invisible except for a quick flash to full opacity around each layer marker (an eased bump 'Flash Width (msec)' wide, default 500). Without markers it simply stays visible.
- **Opacity Flash - random** [pass]: Makes the layer flicker in and out irregularly: noise drives Solid Composite 'Source Opacity'. 'Chance of Flashing' (50) sets how often it is visible and 'Flash Nervousness' (50) sets how fast it changes.
- **Random Colors** [pass]: Fills the whole layer (keeping its alpha) with a colour that jumps randomly around a 'Base Color', with 'Hue Variance', 'Saturation Variance' and 'Lightness Variance' (30 each) and a new colour 'Update FPS' (4) times a second.
- **Random Whole Numbers** [pass]: Turns a text layer into a random integer that changes 'Update FPS' (8) times a second, between 'Minimum Value' (0) and 'Maximum Value' (100); an 'Output' slider carries the same number for linking.
- **Rotate Over Time** [pass]: Spins the layer continuously: a Transform effect's 'Rotation' = (time - inPoint) x 'Rotation (degrees/second)' (default 10). It pivots around the layer's own Anchor Point (converted to comp space for text and shape layers).
- **Scale Bounce - random** [pass]: Noise-driven random pulsing: a Transform effect's scale moves between 100% and 'Target Scale Change' (200) as 'Chance of Bouncing' (50) and 'Bounce Nervousness' (50) allow.
- **Separate XYZ Scale** [pass]: Rig with no visible change until used: adds 'X Scale', 'Y Scale' and 'Z Scale' sliders (default 0) that are added to the layer's own Scale, so each axis can be animated separately (squash and stretch).
- **Trim Paths - at layer in** [pass]: Shape layers only: adds 'Trim Paths 1' so the stroke draws on from nothing to complete over 'Trim In Duration (seconds)' (default 1) starting at the layer in point (eased). The 'Trim In Reverse Path Direction' checkbox flips the direction. Needs a shape layer with a stroked path; on a text or footage layer there are no shape contents to trim, so it does nothing.
- **Trim Paths - at layer out** [pass]: Shape layers only: adds 'Trim Paths 1' so the stroke wipes itself off over the last 'Trim Out Duration (seconds)' (default 1) before the layer out point (eased). 'Trim Out Reverse Path Direction' flips the direction. Needs a shape layer with a stroked path; on a text or footage layer there are no shape contents to trim, so it does nothing.
- **Wave Value Generator** [pass]: Rhythmic oscillation: by default it pulses the layer's scale (through a Transform effect, Uniform Scale) as a sine between about 40% and 100% with a 2 s period, starting at 70% at the in point, peaking at 0.5 s and bottoming at 1.5 s. The Transform effect's anchor/position are left at the layer centre, so on text (and shape) layers the pivot lands far from the content (near the comp's bottom-right corner in the test) and the text slides toward that corner as it shrinks. An 'Output' slider exposes the same wave so other properties can be linked to it.
- **Wiggle - gelatin** [pass]: Jelly wobble: a Transform effect's 'Skew' wiggles by 'Wiggle Amount' (20) and its 'Skew Axis' wanders randomly, at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1). The layer looks rubbery.
- **Wiggle - position** [pass]: Random wandering motion: a Transform effect's 'Position' wiggles up to 'Wiggle Amount (pixels)' (50) at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1).
- **Wiggle - rotation** [pass]: Random rocking: a Transform effect's 'Rotation' wiggles up to 'Wiggle Amount (degrees)' (30) at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1).
- **Wiggle - scale** [pass]: Random breathing: a Transform effect's scale wiggles by 'Wiggle Amount' (10) at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1); with 'Wiggle Width Separately?' on (default) the width wiggles on its own by 'Wiggle Width' (10), giving organic squish.
- **Wiggle - shear** [pass]: Random slanting: a Transform effect's 'Skew' wiggles up to 'Wiggle Amount' (20) at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1).
- **Wigglerama** [pass]: All-in-one random float: a Transform effect wiggles position ('Wiggle Position (pixels)' 25), rotation ('Wiggle Rotation (degrees)' 30) and scale ('Wiggle Scale' 15) together at 'Wiggle Speed (wigs/sec)' (1) with 'Wiggle Nervousness' (1, the wiggle octaves: higher adds jittery detail, not speed).
- **Fade In Over Layer Below** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Automatic crossfade: Solid Composite 'Source Opacity' ramps 0->100 from this layer's in point to the out point of the layer directly beneath it (index + 1). If nothing overlaps below, it stays at 100 (no fade).
- **Fade Out Over Layer Below** *(needs_input)* [pass]: Automatic crossfade out: Solid Composite 'Source Opacity' ramps 100->0 from the in point of the layer directly beneath (index + 1) to this layer's out point, revealing that layer. Without an overlapping layer below it stays at 100.
- **Random On-Off** *(needs_input)* [broken]: Meant as a hard random flicker: a Transform effect ('Random on/off') whose Opacity expression switches the layer between 0 and 100 'Update FPS' (6) times a second, 'Probability' (50) being the chance of staying visible and 'Random Seed' changing the pattern. In the AE 2026 test render it did nothing: the text stayed at 100% in all 40 frames (1.6 s). The cause is not pinned down. The missing ';' after 'var r = random(0,100)' is not enough to explain it, because Wave Value Generator's expression also relies on the same automatic semicolon at a line break and ran in the same render. The same posterizeTime / seedRandom / random header also works in Random Whole Numbers. Test a rewritten expression (see scripting) or use Opacity Flash - random, which did flicker in the same render.
- **Scale Bounce - layer markers** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Meant to pop the layer's scale (via a Transform effect that is re-centred with toComp(anchorPoint) on text and shape layers) to 'Target Scale Change' (default 200%) around each layer marker, over a window of 'Bounce Duration (msec)' (500) centred on the marker. The shape of the pop has never been rendered: the expression is easeIn(|time - nearest marker|, Bounce Duration / 2000, 0, Target, 100), a reversed time range, so it may be a hard jump to Target for 250 ms either side of the marker instead of a smooth swell, or even the inverse (Target between markers, 100% on the marker). Render-check with markers. Needs layer markers: with none (as in the test render) the expression's try/catch returns 100% and the layer does not change at all.

### Image - Creative

- **Bloom - brights** [pass]: Highlight bloom: Box Blur (radius 2) softens the layer, then one Calculations pass adds the layer's own sharp original back in Add mode at 25% Second Layer Opacity, so bright areas brighten and glow softly.
- **Bloom - brights+darks** [pass]: Soft diffusion bloom: Box Blur (radius 3) softens the layer, then two Calculations passes blend the layer's own sharp original back over it at 50% Second Layer Opacity: 'Calculations/brighten' in Screen (lifts and glows highlights) and 'Calculations/darken' in Overlay (restores contrast in the darks). Reads like a Pro-Mist filter.
- **Bloom - crystallize 1** [weak]: Minimax (Operation Maximum, Radius 2, Channel Color) spreads bright pixels by 2 layer pixels, then Calculations lays the layer's original pixels back on top (Blending Mode Overlay, Second Layer Opacity 0.5). On footage it reads mainly as an Overlay-style contrast and saturation lift with slightly fattened, glowing highlights; the square 'crystal' facets are only a couple of pixels wide and are not visible when footage is scaled down to fit the comp (Radius is measured in layer pixels).
- **Bloom - crystallize 2** [weak]: Minimax (Operation Maximum Then Minimum, Radius 6) closes small dark gaps between bright areas, then Calculations lays the original back on top (Overlay, Second Layer Opacity 0.5). On footage it reads as a slight painterly smoothing of fine detail plus an Overlay contrast and saturation lift; Radius is in layer pixels, so on 1080p footage scaled to fit a smaller comp no blocks show, and the blocky look only appears near 100% layer scale or with a larger Radius.
- **Colorize - blue wash** [pass]: Cool blue cast: Color Balance pulls Red and Green down by 20 and pushes Blue up by 20 in shadows, midtones and highlights, and Color Balance (HLS) lowers Saturation by 25, so the picture stays readable but reads cold and slightly muted.
- **Colorize - gold dip** [pass]: Solid Composite combines the layer with a gold solid (#E0B116) in Color Burn mode at 60% Opacity. On footage it removes blue almost entirely and maps tones to a hot, very saturated duotone: shadows turn deep red, midtones orange and highlights gold-yellow. Dark areas are lifted to red rather than crushed to black, so contrast drops a little.
- **Colorize - infrared** [pass]: False-colour 'infrared': Solid Composite blends pure red (#FF0000) at 100%, which inverts the red channel, and the Levels / Hue/Saturation stored in the preset are not neutral, so the result comes out very dark and low-key. Tones split three ways: the darkest areas (black clothing, hair, dark red wood) turn deep red, mid-tones such as skin fall to near-black, and light areas (cream walls) turn dark green, with bright highlights (lamps) glowing green. Overall brightness drops to roughly a quarter.
- **Colorize - moonshadows** [pass]: Night look: Solid Composite puts deep navy (#0B0080) under the layer in Color Burn mode at 50%, which darkens the picture and strips red and green so what is left reads as dim blue moonlight. Hue/Saturation is included at neutral values (no change) as a tweak point.
- **Colorize - red hand tint** [pass]: Leave Color (Color To Leave red, Amount to Decolor 100, Tolerance 15, Edge Softness 5, Match colors Using Hue) greys out hues outside a band around red, and Hue/Saturation on the Reds channel clearly boosts what is kept. At Tolerance 15 the band covers reds, oranges, skin tones and warm yellow/cream, so on warm footage almost everything keeps its colour (noticeably more saturated, with skin pushed toward orange) and only greens, blues and other cool colours go grey; it only becomes a grey picture with red accents after Tolerance is lowered.
- **Colorize - royal purple** [pass]: Solid Composite puts a purple solid (#5900B2) under the layer in Hard Light mode at 67% Opacity, giving a strong, contrasty purple tint. Levels is included at neutral values (no change) as a tweak point.
- **Colorize - sepia** [pass]: Old-photo sepia: Fill turns the layer solid brown (#AB6E1A), Calculations puts the original layer's luminosity back onto that brown (Blending Mode Luminosity, Second Layer Opacity 1), and Tint (Map White To #FFD599, Amount to Tint 33) warms the highlights.
- **Colorize - sky blue** [pass]: Graduated blue sky: Gradient Ramp fills the layer with a linear ramp from blue-violet (#8066FF) at the top to neutral 50% grey (#808080) at the bottom (an expression sets 'End of Ramp' to [0, thisLayer.height]), and Calculations blends the original layer back in Hard Light, so the top of frame takes the tint and the bottom stays unchanged.
- **Colorize - sky orange** [pass]: Graduated orange sky: Gradient Ramp fills the layer with a linear ramp from orange (#FF6640) at the top to neutral 50% grey (#808080) at the bottom (an expression sets 'End of Ramp' to [0, thisLayer.height]), and Calculations blends the original layer back in Hard Light, so the top of frame takes the tint and the bottom stays unchanged.
- **Colorize - sunset gradient** [pass]: Sunset grade: Gradient Ramp fills the layer with a linear ramp from coral (#FF8566) at the top to blue-violet (#7765FC) at the bottom (an expression sets 'End of Ramp' to [0, thisLayer.height]), and Calculations blends the original back in Hard Light, so the top warms and the bottom cools. Unlike sky blue/orange, no part of the frame stays neutral.
- **Contrast - luminance** [pass]: Auto Contrast (Black Clip 0.1, White Clip 0.1, Temporal Smoothing 0, Scene Detect off) stretches each frame's darkest and brightest pixels to full black and white without shifting hue; a Curves effect is added with a straight (neutral) curve as a tweak point.
- **Contrast - saturation** [pass]: Calculations overlays the layer's original pixels onto itself (Blending Mode Overlay, Second Layer Opacity 0.67), which deepens shadows, lifts highlights and boosts saturation in one pass.
- **Dimension - bevel+shadow** [pass]: Bevel Alpha (Edge Thickness 2.5, Light Angle -45, Light Intensity 0.4) adds a lit, embossed edge to the layer's alpha, and a Drop Shadow (black, Opacity 153 on the 0-255 scale = 60%, Distance 8, Softness 5, Direction linked by expression to the bevel's light angle + 180) lifts it off the background; all of it appears only along alpha edges, so on full-frame footage none of it is visible. The preset also sets the layer's Scale to an absolute 90%: a footage layer that was scaled down to fit the comp (e.g. 1080p at 50% in a 960x540 comp) jumps to 90% and looks zoomed in about 1.8x.
- **Dimension - glow+shadow** [pass]: Three Drop Shadows with Distance 0 and Softness 20: 'Drop Shadow - white glow' (white, full opacity) wraps the alpha in a soft white glow, then 'Drop Shadow - dark halo 1' and '- dark halo 2' (black, full opacity) add a dark halo outside it; all of it appears only along alpha edges, so on full-frame footage none of it is visible. The preset also sets the layer's Scale to an absolute 90%: a footage layer that was scaled down to fit the comp (e.g. 1080p at 50% in a 960x540 comp) jumps to 90% and looks zoomed in about 1.8x.
- **Grayscale 1** [pass]: Black and white by Color Balance (HLS) Saturation -100: a straight desaturation (HLS lightness) with no channel weighting.
- **Grayscale 2** [pass]: Set Channels sets Red, Green and Blue all to the source's Luminance (alpha kept), giving a luminance-weighted black and white that keeps natural brightness relationships between colours.
- **Grayscale 3** [pass]: Channel Mixer with Monochrome on and Red-Red 100 (Red-Green and Red-Blue 0), so the picture becomes the red channel alone: a red-filter black and white with bright skin and lips, darker blue skies and darker greenery.
