- **Stretch Out Each Line** [pass]: Characters shrink to 0% scale toward their line's anchor (Anchor Point Grouping = Line, aligned toward the line's left end), so each line collapses into a point as a soft ramp sweeps left to right, comp.time +0 s to +2.47 s (starts at once). No fade and no tracking change.
- **Stretch Out Each Word** [pass]: Characters shrink to 0% scale toward their word's anchor (Anchor Point Grouping = Word, aligned toward the word's left end), so each word collapses into a point as a soft ramp sweeps left to right, comp.time +0 s to +2.47 s (starts at once). No fade and no tracking change.
- **Twirl Off Each Line** [pass]: Characters spin 2 full turns around their line's right end (Anchor Point Grouping = Line) while shrinking to 0% and fading out, in a soft left-to-right ramp from comp.time +0 s to +3 s (starts at once).
- **Twirl Off Each Word** [pass]: Characters spin 2 full turns around their word's right end (Anchor Point Grouping = Word) while shrinking to 0% and fading out, in a soft left-to-right ramp from comp.time +0 s to +3 s (starts at once).

### Text/Blurs

- **Blur In By Word** [pass]: Words appear left to right, one after another, each fading up from a heavy blur (184 x 277 px) until sharp.
- **Blur and Fade In** [pass]: Characters grow from 0% scale while un-blurring (Blur Amount 100), rotating from 53 deg and moving in from the Position offset, in a soft left-to-right ramp over 2 s. Despite the name the animator's Opacity is disabled: the 'fade' comes from the scale-up and blur.
- **Blur and Fade Out** [pass]: Characters blur (Blur Amount 100), rotate toward 53 deg, drift by the Position offset and shrink to 0% scale, in a soft left-to-right ramp over 2 s. Despite the name the animator's Opacity is disabled: the 'fade' comes from the shrink and blur.
- **Bullet Train In** [pass]: Characters rush in from the left (starting 302 px left of place) with a strong horizontal-only blur (416 px) while fading up, snapping into place left to right in about half a second, like a passing train.
- **Evaporate Out** [pass]: Characters vanish in random order: each swells to 263% while blurring (113 px) and fading out, as if evaporating.
- **Foggy In** [pass]: Characters fade in left to right out of a wide horizontal smear (stretched to 1204% width, blur 200 x 50 px), so the line clears like fog lifting.
- **Harsh Blur In By Character** [pass]: Characters snap in one at a time in random order out of a heavy 350 px blur while fading up; a wiggly selector makes not-yet-landed characters flicker partly into view.
- **Harsh Blur Out By Character** [pass]: Characters blur hard (350 px) and fade out one by one in random order, flickering via a wiggly selector: the reverse of Harsh Blur In By Character.
- **Transporter In** [pass]: Characters materialise in random order from thin, tall blurred streaks (13% wide x 3000% tall, blur 275 x 25 px) that collapse into the letters while fading up, like a sci-fi teleporter beam.
- **Blur Glitch** *(needs_input)* [broken]: Meant to smear the whole line in and out of blur: a wiggle(7, 100) expression on the animator's Blur drives horizontal and vertical blur independently about 7 times a second. In the gallery render (applied at 0 s) both sampled frames, 0.5 s and 1.4 s, were pixel-identical to the plain text with no blur at all, so the blur is intermittent at best (negative wiggle values clamp to 0) or not evaluating: scrub it before relying on it.

### Text/Curves and Spins

- **Bloom Flower In** [pass]: Characters spin very fast (Rotation keyed -36000 to 0 deg, 100 turns) about an anchor 100 px off each glyph while fading in, in random order, opening out like petals.
- **Clockwise Entry** [pass]: Characters grow in from zero size left to right while circling into place: one animator turns them two times clockwise (Rotation -720 to 0) about an anchor 100 px off the glyph at 600% scale, a second rides a looping circular Position path (9 keys, two loops about 490 px across) at 0% scale; settles in 2 s.
- **Counter Rotate** [pass]: A wave passes left to right: each character makes one full clockwise turn while it swells (up to 770%) and fades to transparent at the wave's peak, then returns; the line is readable before and after.
- **Dust Devil Out** [pass]: Characters whirl away from the line left to right: each spins two turns (720 deg) about a pivot about 280 px off the baseline and slides 130 px sideways, odd and even characters going opposite ways like a whirlwind. It does not fade: the characters end scattered but still visible.
- **Lasso In** [pass]: Characters swing in along a looping path driven by an animated anchor point (11 keys swinging side to side about 500 px, narrowing as they go) and land left to right, like a lasso being reeled in. No fade: before landing the characters are visible but displaced.
- **Lasso Tumble In** [pass]: Like Lasso In but tumbling: characters swing on an animated anchor while their scale flips between about +257% and -257% (mirror flips), and an opacity animator reveals them left to right; they settle after 3 s.
- **Musical Chairs** [pass]: In random order, each character swings one full turn around a pivot offset about 60 px right and 60 px down, so letters loop past their neighbours and land back in place; readable before and after the 4 s move.
- **Newton** [pass]: As a wave passes left to right, characters swing out on an animated anchor point (11 keys alternating roughly 400-500 px left and right, on looping arcs that also carry them a few hundred px above and below the line), then settle back in place; readable before and after.
- **Radial Flare In** [pass]: Characters spin in (Rotation 10800 deg, 30 turns) stretched 5x wide around a pivot that travels from 1000 px below to 500 px above the line, fading up left to right, like a radial flare collapsing into the text.
- **Retrograde** [pass]: Opposite orbits: every character except the second makes two clockwise turns about a pivot near the line's left side while the second character makes two counter-clockwise turns about the same pivot, at a constant speed (linear keys) starting at comp.time and landing back in place 2 s later; the line passes upside-down at about 0.5 s and 1.5 s and flashes upright for an instant at the 1.0 s midpoint.
- **Rotate** [pass]: In random order, each character spins one full turn (360 deg) about a pivot at the top of the glyph (not its centre, so a turning letter swings up above the line) and lands back upright; readable before and after the 3 s move.
- **Somersault In** [pass]: Characters roll in from about 600 px to the left, each doing three somersaults (three staggered 360-degree turns, each carrying it about 200 px right) before settling. No fade: the line is visible, shifted left, before the move.
- **Spin Fast In** [pass]: Characters spin extremely fast (50 turns) while fading in and settle into the line.
- **Spiral In** [pass]: Characters spiral into place left to right: each makes two turns (720 deg) about a pivot about 360 px away that shrinks to nothing, with tracking tightened (-50) until it lands. No fade: before the move the characters are visible but displaced.
- **Spiral Out** [pass]: Characters spiral away left to right and vanish: each turns 2.5 times (900 deg) about a pivot 300 px off the baseline, drifts 130 px sideways and shrinks to 0%.
- **Swoop In** [pass]: Characters swoop in from zero size along a long horizontal sweep (Position keyed about +1280 to -1570 px) while spinning 540 deg at 600% scale about offset anchors, landing left to right in about 2.5 s.
- **Tea Leaves In** [pass]: Characters drift and rotate around five offset anchor points, swirling slowly like tea leaves before settling into the line.
- **Tire Rim In** [pass]: Characters wheel into place: each spins 6.5 turns (2340 deg) about a pivot 460 px to its left while stretched 6x tall, unwinding left to right over 2 s. No fade: before the move they are visible, upside-down and swung around the pivot.
- **Whirl In** [pass]: Characters grow in from 0% scale left to right while a wave throws them along an outward spiral Position path (7 keys widening to several hundred px), landing in about 1.3 s.

### Text/Expressions

- **2D Text Box (Mask & Effects)** [pass]: Adds a box behind the text that auto-sizes to it (an expression-driven mask path measured with sourceRectAtTime), filled 50% grey by Fill, outlined 3 px white by Stroke, with the text kept in front by CC Composite ('In front'); controls for padding (35 px), roundness (20), scale and offset live on a '2D Text Box' pseudo effect.
- **Cycle Word List** [pass]: A Source Text expression swaps the text through a word list (default One|Two|...|Ten), stepping at the rate of a 'Cycle Frame Rate' slider (default 5 words per second).
- **Display Comp Time** [pass]: Replaces the text with a live readout of the current comp time via timeToCurrentFormat(), in the project's display format (timecode by default).
- **Frame Number** [pass]: Replaces the text with the current frame number via timeToFrames() in a Source Text expression.
- **Inch Worm** [pass]: The line crawls left like an inchworm: a hump of characters lifts (up to 120 px) and travels along the word while the rear catches up, and the whole line steps 60 px left each cycle (about 1.4 s), looping for the rest of the layer.
- **Text Bounce** [pass]: Characters drop from about 320 px above and bounce to rest one after another, each bounce half the height of the last, driven by an expression selector on a Position [0,-320] animator with sliders for frequency, per-character delay, decay and start phase.
- **Word List Highlight** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: An expression selector (based on words) turns every word found in a word dictionary (default: and, for, to, from, of, Highlight) red via 'Animator 1' Fill Color, leaving other words unchanged. It needs text that contains one of those words (or an edited dictionary): on 'ARCHIVE 1947' it did nothing.

### Text/Fill and Stroke

- **Chasing Stroke** [pass]: Sets the text's stroke colour to red ('Base Stroke') and sends a triangle-shaped wave of stroke (width up to +25 px) left to right and back over 3.2 s, so a red outline pulses along the characters.
- **Chill Hue Shift** [pass]: Turns the text blue (#0055FE via 'Animator 1') and lets a wiggly selector drift each character's fill hue by up to +/-30 deg about once a second: a gentle blue-violet-cyan shimmer.
- **Color Wash Behavior** [pass]: Patches of neighbouring characters drift in and out of pale pastel tints: 'Animator 1' Fill Color #F2BA41 (gold) is driven by a wiggly selector (2 wiggles/s, 60% character correlation, -50% to 75%, Lock Dimensions off) intersected with a full-line by-word range selector. Because R, G and B are wiggled separately, each character wanders between pale gold, pale yellow, cream, peach, pink and lavender rather than one gold, and the tint slides across characters rather than switching whole words. Subtle on white text.
- **Cycle Fill Hue** [pass]: Turns the text red ('Animator 1') and spins its fill hue through the spectrum on 'Animator 2' (985 deg in the first 2 s, then forever via loopOut('continue')): a fast rainbow cycle.
- **Fill Color Wipe** [pass]: The text turns red ('Animator 1'), and from comp.time a white fill ('Animator 2') wipes across it left to right, character by character, finishing 1.5 s later and holding white.
- **Flicker Color** [pass]: Gives the text a purple stroke ('Base Stroke', #603CB0) and, 20 times a second, randomly flickers each character's stroke width (up to 30 px) and shifts fill and stroke hues (+/-50 deg), like a faulty neon sign.
- **Pulse Orange** [pass]: Gives the text an orange (#FF5500) outline through a 'Base Stroke' animator, then a wiggly-selector wave rolls along the word: where it passes the stroke grows by up to 20 px and fill/stroke hues shift by about 20 degrees, so the orange outline pulses fat and thin across the letters (7 smooth cycles in about 5 s).
- **Pulsing Strokes** [pass]: Sets a white outline (through a 'Base Stroke' animator) and pumps its width between 2 and 10 px on every character in two quick double-pulses (0-0.5 s and 1.0-1.5 s, a key every 0.125 s), so the outline throbs in beats.
- **Sliding Color Flicker** [pass]: A soft-edged band (Range Selector, 50% wide, Smooth shape) slides left to right across the text over 3 s; letters inside it turn red-pink (Fill Color red plus a 335 degree Fill Hue) and at the band's soft edges the partial hue rotation runs them through a rainbow of hues, then they return to their own fill.
- **Stroke Ease Down** [pass]: An entrance: letters drop into place from far above (about 1270 px up, fading in from transparent) in a left-to-right cascade, while a narrow highlight (10% of the characters) with a thick green 8 px stroke runs across the word; it settles in about 2 s. A 'Base Stroke' animator also gives the text a black outline.
- **Wiggly Stroke Width By Line** *(needs_input)* [untested: needs_setup]: Meant to flicker each LINE of text fast (Wiggly Selector based on Lines, Mode Intersect, 10 wiggles/s) between a solid fill and a hollow, semi-transparent yellow outline up to 20 px (fill toward 20% opacity), over a black 'Base Stroke' outline. In the gallery render on a single-line white text layer with no stroke nothing changed at 0.5 s or 1.4 s, so it needs a re-test on stroked, multi-line text before use.

### Text/Graphical

- **Blue Note** [pass]: Random characters (re-picked about once a second) are replaced by hyphens stretched into tall, translucent blue (#337EDA, 30% opacity) blocks; a second animator scales them up to ~7x and lifts them about 290 px above the line at random sizes, leaving gaps in the word.
- **Cut Shapes** [pass]: About a quarter of the characters (re-picked slowly, ~0.3 wiggles/s) are replaced by a '2' glyph blown up to ~70x, mirrored and spun to random angles, in a very faint (5% opacity) green tint, so huge translucent curved shapes sit behind the remaining letters and shift over time.
- **Exchange** [pass]: About a fifth of the characters (random, re-picked slowly) turn into '+' signs that rise about 330 px above the line with soft arrive/leave moves and flip 180 degrees, while their glyphs keep cycling (Character Offset wiggle 3/s), like a ticking exchange board.
- **Hazard** [pass]: About a quarter of the characters (random, re-picked about twice a second) are replaced by '|' bars scaled ~7.5x wide (mirrored) and 18x tall and rotated 45 degrees, so they slash across the frame as bold diagonal bands in the text colour, like hazard stripes, while tracking opens up around them.
- **Red Circles** [pass]: About 40% of the characters (random, re-picked about once a second) are replaced by a degree sign (a small ring), tinted red at 34% opacity and blown up to random sizes (up to ~30x) with random fades by a wiggly selector, so translucent red rings of different sizes sit behind the remaining letters.
- **Scratchy Film** [weak]: The whole line jitters by about 1 px at high speed (like film gate weave). A 5% range selector (randomized order) is meant to turn the characters it covers into very tall, thin, faint '|' lines (30% opacity, 40x tall) that wander up to ~200 px sideways like film scratches, but its Offset = wiggle(2.5, 200) slides that window past the ends of the line much of the time, and on a 12-character line 5% is under one character: on 'ARCHIVE 1947' no scratch showed at 0.5 s or 1.4 s, leaving only the barely visible jitter.
- **Snowflakes** [pass]: About 40% of the characters (random, re-picked about once a second) become pale blue asterisk 'snowflakes' at 50% opacity, scattered around the remaining letters at random sizes (up to ~6x) and angles.
- **Yellow Boxes** [pass]: About 40% of the characters (random, re-picked about once a second) are replaced by hyphens blown up into huge translucent yellow boxes (25% opacity, up to 50x wide and 100x tall at random sizes) that sit behind the remaining letters.

### Text/Lights and Optical

- **Broadway** [pass]: Letters dim to 30% opacity in a repeating four-step chase (an expression selector stepping 20 times a second), so a pattern of lit and dimmed letters runs along the word like marquee bulbs.
- **Bubble Pulse** [pass]: A wave travels through the word: as it passes, one or two letters balloon to ~5x and fade to near-transparent (10% opacity) before shrinking back, repeating about every 0.8 s, like a ghostly magnifier passing over the text.
- **Flash** [pass]: Characters appear in random order over 0.6 s: each starts invisible at 8x size and shrinks into place while fading up (with a 200 px white stroke flare if the text has a stroke), so the word bursts into view.
- **Flicker Exposure** [pass]: Every letter flickers on its own and very fast (Wiggly Selector, 20 wiggles/s, no correlation) toward a black fill (and a black stroke up to 30 px if the text has a stroke), like text under a failing, stuttering exposure.
- **Fluorescent Light** [pass]: The word flickers like a fluorescent tube: an Add-mode Wiggly Selector (15 wiggles/s, 95% correlation) randomly dips the letters' opacity together, and in the first ~2.5 s a narrow band (8% of the characters, random order) blinks letters out as it passes. The flicker is not keyed, so it continues for the whole layer.
- **Office Light** [weak]: Meant to make random letters misbehave like faulty office lights: about 5% strobe between off and half brightness (Math.sin(time*100)*100-50, clamped to 0-50%) and about 20% switch fully on or off a few times a second (wiggle). In the gallery render it was far subtler: on 'ARCHIVE 1947' only one letter sat dimmed to roughly 70%, unchanged between 0.5 s and 1.4 s, and no letter switched off.
- **Overlay** [pass]: About 40% of the characters (random, re-picked about once a second) drop out of the word to 3% opacity and show as huge (up to ~50x), very faint ghost letters behind it, their sizes varying slowly, layering a large typographic texture under the word.

### Text/Mechanical

- **Algorithm In** [pass]: Most characters start as faint (20% opacity), tightly tracked, constantly scrambling letters (Character Offset wiggle 30/s, case and digits preserved) and resolve to the real text one at a time in random order over ~3.4 s, like a code decryption.
- **Algorithm Loop** [pass]: A few characters (a 5% slice of the line, re-picked about once a second) keep scrambling through random Unicode glyphs at 20% opacity with tightened tracking, giving a constant computing / glitch shimmer while the rest of the word stays readable.
- **Automation** [pass]: A small rounded bump (5% of the characters) travels left to right over 2 s: each letter in turn lifts about 100 px out of the line while tracking opens a gap around it, then drops back, like parts stepping along an assembly line.
- **Bad Reception** [pass]: The text spikes over and over: Math.tan(time*10) expressions shoot its skew, rotation and vertical scale to extremes about every 0.3 s (amount varied by a slow wiggly selector). Animator 2's Anchor Point keys also hold the whole line about 500 px right of its place (more than half off a 960 px frame) from the start until ~1.3 s, then jolt it sideways between about +500 and -500 px until ~2.7 s, like a TV with bad reception.
- **Doppler** [pass]: Letters buzz up and down every frame (Anchor Point Y flips by roughly +/-50 px each frame, the amplitude swelling and fading) inside a soft selection that sweeps back and forth across the word about once a second for ~4 s, like a vibrating sound wave; with motion blur on, the buzzing letters smear vertically.
- **Electro Magnet In** [pass]: Characters start scattered up to about 600 px sideways and 400 px vertically and spun to random angles (a per-character Wiggly Selector), drifting slowly; from ~1 s to ~2.3 s the words snap back into the line one word at a time in random order.
- **Helicopter In** [pass]: Characters fade on left to right over 2 s while a band of wildly flipping X-scale (Math.tan(time*30)*500, clamped to +/-5000%) whirls through them like rotor blades, then the word settles.
- **Insert Text In** [weak]: In 0.33 s the whole line slides about 347 px to the right into place while characters fade on from the right end back toward the start (opacity selector Offset 0 -> -100), as if text is pushed out of an insertion point.
- **Kinematic In** [pass]: Letters swing in on rotating arms from different directions, arcing around their anchors like a mechanical linkage before locking into the word.
- **Mechanical In** [pass]: The word starts scrambled: groups of letters are thrown sideways or down (up to ~600 px) and rotated; every ~0.17 s one of 14 offsets switches off, so the letters snap back into place in jerky mechanical steps over ~2.3 s.
- **Pistons** [pass]: Alternate letters pump up and down in opposite directions (+/-25 px) like engine pistons, and the pumping keeps accelerating (sin(time^4/2)).
- **Rack & Pinion** [pass]: A soft band covering half the characters passes through the word in random order over 4 s: letters inside it flip up to 180 degrees (upside down) and spread apart (tracking +75), and their hue jitters (wiggle 30/s, visible only on coloured text), then they settle back.
- **Roadtrip** [pass]: The whole line whips sideways: about every 0.67 s its anchor jumps ~1000 px in one frame (-500 -> +500 px) and glides back into place over ~0.3 s, six times in 4 s, like a car speeding past; with motion blur on, each whip becomes a horizontal streak.
- **Roadtrip 2** [pass]: Letters jolt vertically: every ~1.33 s the anchor whips about 500 px in one frame and eases back over ~0.6 s, each letter by a different amount (Wiggly Selector 0-100%, slowly changing), like text bouncing over a bumpy road; with motion blur on, the jolts become vertical streaks.
- **Scale Bounce** [pass]: A narrow band (10% of the characters) travels left to right over 3 s; the letters in it pump between +500% and -500% scale every 0.375 s, passing through zero so they flip upside down and mirrored as they bounce, then return to normal.
- **Screen Roll** [pass]: The text repeatedly rolls vertically off and back onto its line in sharp jumps, like a TV with a broken vertical hold.
- **Stairstep** [pass]: A soft band (23% of the characters) sweeps left to right over 2.5 s, pushing alternate letters about 50 px up and down as it passes, so a staircase ripple runs through the word and leaves it straight.
- **Submarine** [pass]: The whole line slides from about 540 px right of its place to about 560 px left of it over 4 s (right to left, like a submarine passing), while the last ~12% of the characters (the last letter of a short word) flips vertically every 0.17 s like a spinning propeller.
- **Underscore In** [pass]: A terminal-style type-on (0.1-1.1 s): pending characters show as underscores ('_') shifted ~580 px left; left to right, each character lands in its slot as '_' and turns into its real letter about 0.1 s later, so an underscore cursor leads the typing.
- **Warp 9.8** [weak]: About every 0.52 s the letters surge in scale on a Math.tan(time*6) spike and snap back, each by its own random amount and direction (per-character wiggly selector), like jumping to warp speed; enable motion blur for a smeared stretch. The surge lasts only a frame or two: between spikes the letters stay within roughly 15-20% of normal size, so most frames look almost untouched.

### Text/Miscellaneous

- **Angle Fly-In** [pass]: Characters grow from zero scale one after another, left to right, swooping in on an angled arc because the scale pivots around an offset anchor point (1000, 500); a 30 %-wide Ramp Up selector sweeps across in 2 s.
- **Back Flip** [pass]: A flip wave rolls through the text left to right: each character stretches wide (X-scale 200 %), squeezes to zero width and turns upside down (Y-scale to -100 %) as the wave passes, lifting into an arch above the line mid-flip, then returns to normal. The text is readable before and after, so it is an in-place emphasis, not an entrance.
- **Blow Away Out** [pass]: In random order, characters are blown away mostly toward the lower right (each slides about 1230 px right and 360 px down while spinning five turns); a static wiggly selector gives every character a different amount, some even going the opposite way, until the text is gone.
- **Bounce In** [pass]: Characters bounce in from the left like bouncing balls: each starts about 700 px left of its place, hopping up to 150 px high, and travels into position, left to right, over 2 s.
- **Bungee** [pass]: The whole line swings side to side (up to 280 px) while its letter spacing pumps between 0 and 50 every third of a second, like a bungee cord; from 1.35 s the characters settle into place left to right, finishing at 3 s.
- **Chaotic In** [weak]: Characters start jittering with random position (up to 50 px), scale (including mirror flips) and 70-degree skew, and the chaos calms down within half a second to leave clean text. The 'Wiggle Rotation' animator's Rotation is 0, so there is no visible rotation.
- **Clay Pigeons Out** [pass]: In random order, each character pops up on a short arc and shrinks to nothing (Scale 0 % pivoting on an anchor 400 px below) while tracking tightens by 25, until the text is gone after about 2 s.
- **Explode Out** [pass]: Text bursts apart: characters scatter outward with rotation, shrink and fade until nothing is left.
- **Glitchy Text Decoder** [pass]: Types the text on behind a cursor (a Source Text expression reveals it left to right as the 'Animation' slider goes 0 to 100); each newly typed character arrives glitched (random symbol, heavy blur, pushed 100 px down) and resolves about 5 frames later. Venetian Blinds stripes the layer and Glow adds bloom.
- **Hop Skip And A Jump** [pass]: A smooth wave travels through the text left to right over 3 s: each character dips down (up to 320 px) and rises back into place as the wave passes. The text is readable at the start and end (in-place emphasis).
- **Pendulum In** [pass]: All characters hang from a pivot 900 px above them and swing like long pendulums: they start rotated 50 degrees (displaced far to the side), oscillate with decaying amplitude and hang straight at 3 s.
- **Question** [weak]: The text first appears as small question marks (each character's value replaced by '?', scaled to 55 % with +35 tracking) and the real characters replace them from left to right at full size within about half a second of the in-point.
- **Rattle** [pass]: A smooth wave passes through the text word by word over 3 s, skewing each word 30 degrees while the skew axis spins ten turns, so words wobble and rattle; the text is readable at start and end.
- **Roll Bounce In** [pass]: In random order, characters roll in from about 550 px to the left, spinning three turns while growing from 10 % scale, then do two small hops with a slight tilt (50 px, then 10 px) before settling.
- **Sequential Jump** [pass]: A wave runs through the text: characters hop up 25 px one after another, tilting 15 degrees counter-clockwise then 15 degrees clockwise, and land back in place. The text is visible before and after (emphasis, not an entrance).
- **Six-Shooter in** [pass]: Characters shoot up into place from 400 px below, left to right in about 0.7 s, starting stretched tall and mirrored narrow (scale -50 %, 200 %) and snapping back to normal, while a fast 5 px vertical rattle passes through them in random order until about 1 s.
- **Slide Bounce In** [pass]: In random order, characters slide in horizontally, then hop up 50 px leaning 45 degrees over their neighbour and do a smaller second bounce before settling upright.
- **Slip In** [pass]: In random order, characters slip up into place from far below as tall vertical streaks (Y-scale up to about 3500 %), snapping to normal size within 1.5 s.
- **Smokey** [pass]: A wave of wispy distortion passes through the text character by character over 3 s: each character is skewed 30 degrees while the skew axis spins ten turns, giving a smoke-like wobble. The text is fully readable before and after; it does not fade, blur or dissolve.
- **Squeeze** [pass]: A soft selection wanders back and forth through the text in random character order, ballooning the selected letters to 500 % scale at 10 % opacity with +50 tracking, so ghostly giant letters swell and shrink over the words.
- **Superhero In** [pass]: Characters swoop in along a looping flight path (swinging up to about 250 px around their spot) while growing from zero scale, landing left to right in about 1 s.
- **Vanishing Point In** [pass]: Characters start enormous (500 %) and far off to the lower right, sweep across the frame and shrink into place left to right over 1.5 s, jittered by a fast 15 Hz wiggly selector.
- **Wiggly Lines In** [pass]: Text starts displaced and wobbling (up to 200 px; character correlation 95 % so whole lines bend like wavy strands) and straightens into readable lines left to right in about 1.2 s.
- **Yo-Yo** [pass]: In random order, characters drop 208 px spinning two full turns and spring back up into place, like yo-yos, as a soft selection passes through the text over 4 s. The text is readable at start and end.

### Text/Multi-Line

- **Alphabet Soup** [pass]: Characters start scattered, enlarged (up to 400 %), rotated and partly faded across and beyond the right of the frame, then, word by word in random order, fly in from the right spinning and shrinking into place; in the test render the first word had assembled by +1.4 s and the line by about +2 s.
- **Alternating Stack In** [pass]: Lines slide in horizontally (626 px) from alternating sides, odd lines from one side and even lines from the other, and stack into a block within 1 s.
- **Currents In** [pass]: Text fades up while flowing into place like a current: lines start squeezed together (line spacing -50), shifted 210 px left and 100 px down with tracking tightened by 141, and a line-correlated wiggly phase ripples them as a soft selection releases the text left to right over 3 s.
- **Data Packet** [pass]: An in-and-out text burst: characters scale up from zero while tracking collapses (0-0.33 s), resolve from a randomised, scattered state into legible lines as a legibility window sweeps through, then scale back to zero and spread out from 2.5 s, so the text is gone by +2.97 s.
- **Data Stream In** [pass]: Characters stream in horizontally on a travelling cosine wave: neighbouring characters start up to about 3940 px off to opposite sides and converge into place over 2 s while tracking tightens from 80 to 0, like a data feed locking in.
- **Deal In** [weak]: Characters are dealt in one at a time, left to right, one every 0.24 s (a 12-character line takes about 2.9 s): each letter swoops down into its slot from off-frame at the upper left, lands skewed about 70 degrees and straightens within a couple of frames, then holds about two frames before the next one comes. Unlanded letters stay off-frame, so it reads as a left-to-right build with a quick swoop per letter.
- **Encryption In** [pass]: Characters cycle through random characters with dimmed flickers, then decrypt into the real text from left to right.
- **Fly In By Characters** [pass]: In random order, characters zoom down into place from a huge 3000 % scale, as if flying in from in front of the camera, building the text over about 3 s.
- **Fly In By Words** [pass]: In random order, words zoom down into place from a huge 3000 % scale, as if flying in from in front of the camera, building the sentence over about 3 s.
- **Ideas** [pass]: A floating letter cloud: characters are scattered individually a few hundred px around the line, smaller than normal, rotated at different angles and partly faded, drifting slowly. As a soft selection sweeps through, each word in turn (random order) flies together into its normal readable form for a moment and then scatters back into the cloud; in the test render '1947' read cleanly at about +1.1 to +1.25 s. Keys run to +4 s.
- **Jetstream In** [pass]: Characters rush in vertically on a travelling cosine wave: neighbouring characters start up to 750 px above or below their line and converge over 3 s to a small 5 px ripple that keeps running.
- **Pneumatic In** [pass]: Characters are revealed left to right (opacity, 0-1.33 s) while a soft wave pumps through them like a piston: each character shrinks to 50 %, nudges 58 px right and pushes the following text apart with +300 tracking, settling by 2 s.
- **Word Processor In** [weak]: Text types on character by character behind a blinking cursor, like typing in a word processor; the default speed is fast (about 27 characters per second), so a short line is typed almost instantly.
- **Zippy In** [pass]: Lines zip in from about 800 px to the left one after another (top line first), skewing hard as they arrive and overshooting with a small bounce before stopping, all within about 1.1 s.

### Text/Number Counters

- **Counter Countdown Monospaced** [pass]: Turns the text layer into an animated number counter driven by the 'Counter Countdown Controls' pseudo effect; by default it counts down ('Animate Value' keyed 100 to 0 over 1.5 s) with monospaced digit spacing so the number does not jitter.
- **Currency Counter Monospaced** [pass]: Turns the text layer into an animated currency counter driven by the 'Currency Counter Controls' pseudo effect: it rolls up ('Animate Value' keyed 0 to 100 over 1.5 s) with a $, EUR, JPY or GBP symbol, group separators, 2 decimals and monospaced digits.
- **Percentage Counter Monospaced** [pass]: Turns the text layer into an animated percentage readout that counts from 0% to 100%; every digit sits in a fixed-width slot, so the number does not jitter as it counts.
- **Timer Countdown Monospaced** [pass]: Turns the text layer into a digital countdown clock (HH : MM : SS by default, with 'Hour Mins Secs' labels on a second line); digits sit in fixed-width slots so they do not jump as they tick. Animate Value is in the largest unit of the format (hours for HH:MM:SS), so the default keys race from 09 : 59 : 59 to 00 : 00 : 00 in 1.6 s.

### Text/Organic

- **Autumn** [pass]: The whole line falls about 300 px down into place from above, swaying side to side and skewing like a falling leaf, settling after 2.4 s (about 2.9 s in a 25 fps comp) with a fixed 10-degree skew (faux italic).
- **Boiling** [pass]: Letters bubble up into place in random order while jittering constantly, then from about 1.9 s they swell to 300% and fade out one by one like bursting bubbles; the text is gone by about 3.5 s.
- **Boomerang In** [pass]: Characters appear left to right over 2.4 s; each one flips and swings around an off-centre pivot at up to 300% size, like a boomerang, before landing in place.
- **Chewing Gum In** [pass]: Characters are released in a random sweep from extreme vertical streaks (Scale Y up to -10000%, i.e. flipped and stretched, and displaced up to 2000 px vertically) that snap back to normal like strands of chewing gum, finishing at 1.6 s.
- **Climb In** [pass]: Letters climb upward in eight staggered waves; each wave lifts the characters about 40 px with a half-turn flip, until the line settles near its own position at about 3.2 s. Each wave also wiggles the fill hue.
- **Diagonal Bounce In** [pass]: Characters bounce in one by one along a diagonal, flipping between -200% and 200% scale around a far-offset pivot, and land in place by 2.4 s.
- **Dip-Bounce** [pass]: A wave travels across text that is already visible: letters dip down, then rebound upward in decaying bounces, and the line is back at rest by about 2.9 s.
- **Double-Helix** [pass]: A smooth selection swings back and forth across the text, flipping characters vertically as it passes, so the line twists like a DNA double helix forever.
- **Fish Bait** [pass]: Characters bob and twitch continuously: tan() expressions jerk the vertical scale and rotation, with sharp periodic spikes like a fishing float being nibbled.
- **Flying Formation** [pass]: Characters start as a scattered jumble (offset up to 200 px, sized 0-200%), gather into a roughly readable line at 2.4 s, then scatter again by 4.8 s, like birds in formation; a highly correlated drift of up to 150 px and 30 degrees is never keyed down, so even the assembled line floats and tilts.
- **Horsefly In** [pass]: Characters buzz in one at a time in random order along an erratic darting path, spinning down from several turns and shrinking from 500% to normal size, landing by 1.6 s.
- **Hummingbird In** [pass]: Characters flutter in randomly from 200% size with a fast-spinning skew that flickers like wing beats, fading up and settling into place by 2.4 s.
- **Insect Wipe** [pass]: A buzzing disturbance sweeps across visible text in 1.2 s: characters inside the moving window shrink toward nothing and jitter on an erratic path, then return to normal behind it.
- **Insects In** [pass]: Characters emerge from zero size in random order, each buzzing in along an erratic path like an insect, all landed by 1.2 s.
- **Labrador In** [weak]: After a long blank lead-in, characters bound in left to right with springy hops, like a playful dog jumping up, growing from zero; in a 25 fps comp the first letter only appeared at about 1.5 s, and the full hop-in runs past 2 s.
- **Leapfrog** [pass]: A rounded bulge sweeps right to left across visible text three times (snapping back quickly between passes); letters under it inflate to 300%, rise 50 px and spread apart, as if leaping over each other.
- **Loose Line** [pass]: Characters drift loosely around the line: wiggly selectors push them up to 300 px sideways and 150 px vertically, tilt them up to 30 degrees and scale them between 0% and 200% in a continuously looping wave, while a 'Wiggle Correlation' slider (75 -> 100 -> 75, cycling) changes how unified the motion is.
- **Ocean Tide** [pass]: A continuous sine wave rolls through the characters, scaling, dimming, nudging and re-spacing them; at the default Magnitude (100) the spacing swing is so strong that part of the line crushes into an overlapping, unreadable clump while the rest spreads apart and dims, and the clump travels along the line forever.
- **Quiver** [weak]: Characters shiver very slightly: a wiggly selector (2 wiggles/s, -100% to 100%) varies each letter's skew by up to 5 degrees while spinning its skew axis (up to 3600 degrees), so the lean flickers in random directions; in a still the text looks almost untouched and the tremble reads only in motion.
- **Ripple In** [pass]: Characters appear left to right out of heavily skewed, invisible shapes (a ramp sweep over about 1.4 s) while a second, softer skew ripple trails behind until about 2.1 s.
- **Rubber** [pass]: A wave sweeps across the line, making each character inside it flip and stretch violently between +1000% and -1000% vertical scale in quick alternations, like snapping rubber, returning to normal by 2.4 s.
- **Rubber Floor** [pass]: A wave travels across the line: characters inside it squash and stretch vertically between 0% and up to 1000% in rapid alternations (selector strength keyed 50 -> 100 -> 50%), like stepping on a rubber floor, then return to normal by 2.4 s.
- **Sea-Sick** [pass]: The whole line sways continuously: characters slide sideways and rock with rotation in a slow, highly correlated wave, plus a second delayed sideways slide (Slide Delay slider), giving a queasy rocking-boat motion.
- **Shuffle** [pass]: A smooth selection slides back and forth across the text, mirroring characters horizontally as it passes, so the letters seem to shuffle like flipping cards forever.
- **Simmer** [pass]: Random characters pulse larger in a boiling wobble set by a Magnitude slider while a fast sine flickers their opacity, so the text simmers and flickers continuously.
- **Slice and Dice In** [pass]: Characters snap in fast in random order from thin, tall slivers (zero width, 2000% height) with a jittery shake, all in place by 0.8 s.
- **Sprouts In** [pass]: Characters grow up from their base in random order like sprouting plants, swaying and rotating as they rise from zero size over about 4.8 s, ending with a slight uneven tilt.
- **Tag Team** [pass]: A small group of characters (a 20% window) shrinks to almost nothing and chases back and forth through the word while being bounced vertically, as if letters tag each other in and out; keys end at 3.2 s.
- **Wheatfield** [pass]: Characters sway continuously like wheat in the wind: a looping wiggly selector waves a skew through the line while tan() expressions add twitchy stretch and rotation.
- **Wind Current** [pass]: Characters are blown around as a gust travels through the line: wiggly selectors shift them up to 150 px up or down, tilt them up to 30 degrees and scale them between 0% and 200%; after 3.2 s the motion stops and the text stays scattered.
- **Wobble In** [pass]: Characters pop in in random order (opacity reveal by 1.6 s) while wobbling: their width flips between about +200% and -200% and they rock up to 50 degrees, settling by about 2.1 s.

### Text/Paths

- **360 Loop** [pass]: Puts the text on a new mask path and whips it around a full loop along that path, with a quick tracking squeeze passing through the letters, before it settles at about 1.9 s. Also replaces the layer's text with '360 loop'.
- **Antelope** [pass]: Adds a long animated mask path and runs the text along it from beyond one end to past the other over 2.4 s, with tracking pulses at each bound, so the line leaps across the frame like an antelope. Also replaces the layer's text with 'antelope'.
- **Ants** [pass]: Replaces the text with 'ants ants ants ...', puts it on a new mask path and slowly crawls it along for 4 s; part of the line is shrunk to 30% and spread out with a small fast jitter (3 px, 5 wiggles/s), like a column of marching ants.
- **Balance** [pass]: Replaces the text with 'Balance' on a small mask path; the per-word Grouping Alignment is keyed left and right (0 -> -40 -> 80 -> -80 -> 40 -> 0%) over about 3.9 s, so the word teeters back and forth on the curve as if balancing on a beam, then settles.
- **Balloon** [pass]: Replaces the text with 'balloon' on a mask path; letters inflate from nothing in three puffs by about 1.9 s, then from about 2.1 s a randomized lift sends them floating off like a released balloon (ends around 3.7 s).
- **Bouncing** [pass]: Replaces the text with 'bouncing', adds a mask path and moves the text along it with an eased First Margin animation (0 -> 2720 px), so the word bounces across the frame over 2.4 s.
- **Bubble Pop Out** [pass]: Puts the text on a mask path whose shape grows over about 1 s; then letters spin and jump randomly and pop out (vanish) in random order by about 1.9 s. Exit animation: the text ends invisible.
- **Bump And Slide On** [pass]: Puts the text on a curved path with a hump and slides the characters on along it, so the line rides over a bump before settling.
- **Circuit Board Out** [pass]: Races the text along an angular, circuit-trace style path while shrinking it from 100% to 5%, so the line zips away through sharp corners.
- **Conveyor Belt** [pass]: Wraps the text around a closed loop path and moves it along like items on a conveyor belt.
- **Double Spiral** [pass]: Runs the text along a double spiral path: it grows from 0 to full size as it arrives at the middle, then spirals on and shrinks to nothing.
- **Down And Out** [pass]: Slides the text along a path that drops downward, so the characters run down and out.
- **Downhill Slide Off** [pass]: The characters slide along a sloping downhill path, like sliding down a ramp and off.
- **Hurdles** [pass]: Runs the text along a flat path with three humps while the humps themselves slide along (mask shape keyed), so the characters hop over 'hurdles' as they travel.
- **Karate Chop** [pass]: The text sits on a straight path that snaps into a sharp V-shaped kink in a tenth of a second, so the line looks chopped and bent by a hit.
- **Long Spiral Out** [pass]: Sends the text away along a long inward spiral while it shrinks to nothing and its tracking tightens; a Ramp Down selector also tapers the line so the first characters are up to 2.6x larger than the last.
- **Loop In and Out** [pass]: Moves the text along a path with a loop: it travels in, reaches its rest point at the middle of the move and carries straight on out.
- **Pipes** [pass]: Routes the text through an S-shaped run of pipes with rounded U-bends; the characters are shrunk to 25% at the start and end of the trip and full size in the middle.
- **Rat Nest** [pass]: Threads the text along a straight path with a tangled knot in the middle; mid-move the characters shrink to 35%, spread apart and jitter wildly in size (even flipping) and opacity, then return to normal as the move slows to a stop at 3 s.
- **Reel To Reel** [pass]: Runs the text slowly along a path between two circles like tape winding from one reel to the other.
- **Reel-To-Reel 2** [pass]: A second tape-reel preset on a smaller, flatter reel path: the text travels backwards between the two reels in 4 s instead of 8 s, so it moves noticeably faster than Reel To Reel.
- **Rope Bridge** [pass]: Hangs the text on a rope-like curve whose shape is animated, so the line sways like a rope bridge.
- **Serpent** [pass]: The text slithers along a winding path, growing from 5% to full size as it arrives and shrinking again as it slithers away, with a wiggle.
- **Springy** [pass]: Sends the text backwards along a long path of swooping arcs with sharp cusps while its letter spacing pumps in and out rapidly, so the line bounces like a spring.
- **Symmetry** [pass]: Splits the line into left and right halves that slide in along a mirror-symmetric looping path from opposite directions and meet in place after 4 s, while the characters' sizes jitter and then calm back to normal.
- **Tchotchke** [pass]: A whimsical toy swing: the line swings back and forth along a shallow arc (+-280 px along the path) while its letter spacing pumps every third of a second, both dying away to rest over 3 s.
- **Zig-Zag** [pass]: Races the text along a long zig-zag path of straight segments, then eases it to a stop.

### Text/Rotation

- **Drip Down** [pass]: Character by character, from the right end of the line to the left, letters rotate 90 degrees, skew and drop 126 px, so the text ends slumped below its baseline.
- **Flip Up** [pass]: Characters start upside down (rotated 180 degrees) and flip upright one after another from left to right.
- **Full Rotation** [pass]: A wave travels along the line in which each character spins one full turn; the text is upright before and after.
- **Loopy Loop** [pass]: Characters spin a full turn around an offset anchor in a travelling wave, so each letter loops around a small circle and lands back in place.
- **Random Rotation** [pass]: In random order, characters rotate to nearly upside down (179 degrees) and back as a selection sweeps through the line.
- **Rotate Per Character** [pass]: Every character spins one full turn around its own anchor at the same time, ending upright.
- **Rotate Per Word** [pass]: Every word spins one full turn around its own anchor (word baseline centre) at the same time, ending upright.
- **Spin In** [pass]: Characters start displaced up and to the right and rotated 180 degrees, then spin down into place one after another from left to right.
- **Swirly Rotation** [pass]: A soft wave sweeps the line in which characters swing a full turn around an offset anchor, curling through a swirl and landing back in place.
- **Whirlwind** [pass]: A tight wave whips across the line in which each character whirls a full turn around a pivot far to its right, swinging in a wide arc before landing back in place.

### Text/Scale

- **Lets Dance** [pass]: All characters start jittering wildly in size (0-200%) about a shared pivot so they bob and drift as if dancing, then in random order each one settles to normal size.
- **Random Scale In** [pass]: Characters grow from zero size in random order until the whole line is visible.
- **Scale In** [pass]: All characters grow together from zero to full size, each around its own anchor.
- **Scale In By Word** [pass]: Words grow from zero size one after another, left to right, each around its own centre.
- **Scale In From Point** [pass]: Characters grow from zero size starting at a chosen point along the line and spreading outward, by default from the middle (Start Point 50); direction (outward, or from both ends inward), width, easing and a Y offset are controls on the layer.
- **Scale Out** [pass]: All characters shrink together from full size to zero, each around its own anchor, starting the moment the preset is applied (comp.time).
- **Scale Out By Word** [pass]: Words shrink to zero one after another, left to right, each toward its own bottom centre (on the baseline), starting at comp.time.
- **Scale Out From Point** [pass]: Characters shrink to zero starting at a chosen point along the line and spreading outward, by default from the middle (Start Point 50); direction (outward, or from both ends inward), width, easing and a Y offset are controls on the layer.
- **Wiggly Scale Wipe** [pass]: Characters start jittering in size (about 34-166%), then a narrow wipe passes left to right and settles each character to normal size.
- **Zoom Away** [pass]: Characters shrink toward the centre of the line and fade out in a quick sweep from left to right, starting at comp.time, so the line collapses away in about 0.7 s.
- **Zoom Forward** [pass]: Text EXIT, starting at once: characters blow up to 800 % and fade out in a quick left-to-right sweep, as if flying toward the camera; the line is gone after about 0.7 s.

### Text/Tracking

- **Contract** [pass]: Letters start spread (+31 tracking) and the spacing closes up to normal over about 1 s, sweeping from the end of the line back to the start.
- **Decrease Tracking** [pass]: Letters start at wide spacing (+40 tracking) and the whole line's letter spacing tightens evenly to normal over 1 s.
- **Extend** [pass]: Letters spread apart (+54 tracking) in a sweep from the start of the line to the end over about 0.9 s, and stay extended.
- **Increase Tracking** [pass]: The whole line's letter spacing widens evenly from normal to +40 tracking over 1 s and stays there.
- **Magnify** [pass]: A narrow window (about 1/7 of the characters) of 300 %-scaled, +50-tracked letters travels left to right across the text like a magnifying glass; the text is normal before and after.
- **Shift In By Tracking** [pass]: Text ENTRANCE: it starts very widely spaced (+97 tracking) and transparent, then the letters snap back to normal spacing from left to right while the layer fades up.
- **Shift Out By Tracking** [pass]: Text EXIT: letters spread to very wide spacing (+97 tracking) from the end of the line back to the start while the layer fades out.
- **Spasm** [pass]: Letter spacing jitters randomly (a Wiggly Selector driving up to +/-102 tracking, about 6 wiggles per second), so the word twitches and shudders sideways for about 2 s, then calms back to normal.
- **Stretchy** [pass]: A one-third-wide band of extra letter spacing (+62 tracking) travels left to right through the text, letters spreading as it arrives and closing after it passes; the text is normal before and after.

### Transitions - Dissolves

- **Block Dissolve - digital** [pass]: The layer appears through random digital blocks: thin horizontal slivers (layer width/10 wide, 1 px tall) that grow taller as it resolves, like a corrupted video signal locking in.
- **Block Dissolve - random** [pass]: The layer appears as random rectangular blocks (layer width/20 x height/16, about 96 x 68 px on 1080p) pop in until it is fully visible.
- **Block Dissolve - scanlines** [pass]: The layer resolves through 1 px tall horizontal line segments whose width grows from layer width/5 to full width, like the scan lines of a TV signal.
- **Dissolve - blobs** [pass]: The layer appears through large soft-edged blobs (Block Dissolve with layer width/4 x height/4 blocks and Soft Edges on) instead of a flat crossfade.
- **Dissolve - dither** [pass]: The layer appears through a fine dithered noise pattern, like an old computer screen fading in.
- **Dissolve - ripple** [pass]: The layer fades in while rippling like water, the ripple calming as it finishes.
- **Dissolve - sand** [pass]: The layer appears through single-pixel random grains (Block Dissolve with 1 x 1 px blocks, Soft Edges on), like sand settling.
- **Dissolve - unmelt** [pass]: The layer forms out of a melting, turbulent liquid distortion that settles into the clean image.
- **Dissolve - vapor** [pass]: The layer fades up out of a heavy blur (Fast Box Blur radius from layer height/3 to 0), like vapour condensing into the image.
- **Fade - dip to black** [pass]: The layer comes in through black: over the first half it fades up as a black silhouette, over the second half the black lifts to reveal the image (a dip to black when the layer is full frame over the outgoing shot).
- **Fade - flash to white** [pass]: The layer comes in through white: over the first half it fades up as a white silhouette, over the second half the white clears to reveal the image (a dip to white when the layer is full frame over the outgoing shot).
- **Fade - overexposed** [pass]: The layer fades in from a blown-out, overexposed look that settles to normal exposure.
- **Flickering Opacity - at layer in** [pass]: The layer flickers on like a faulty light as it appears, starting at the layer's in point.
- **Flickering Opacity - at layer out** [pass]: The layer flickers randomly in its last 'Animation Out Duration' seconds and is gone at its out point.
- **Inky Dissolve** [pass]: The layer appears through spreading ink-like fractal noise shapes, like ink bleeding into paper.
- **Pixelated Dissolve** [pass]: The layer appears through blocky (60 px) pixelated noise patches, and the picture itself starts pixelated and sharpens over the last 60 % of the transition.

### Transitions - Movement

- **Card Wipe - 2D fractured** [pass]: The layer flips on as a 12 x 9 grid of cards in random order (roughly bottom to top), the cards starting 3x oversized and jittering in depth, then settling into the clean image.
- **Card Wipe - 3D pixelstorm** [pass]: The layer builds from a 20 x 20 storm of small 3D cards that flip on from the bottom-left to the top-right while the virtual camera swings from a 75 degree side angle to face-on and the cards' jitter dies out.
- **Card Wipe - 3D swing** [pass]: The layer is cut into a 5 x 5 grid of large cards that flip over in 3D (random axis, corner-to-corner order) while the virtual camera pushes in and swings from a 60 degree angle to face-on.
- **Pop and Wobble In** [pass]: The layer pops up from zero scale and wobbles in rotation from -45 degrees before settling upright.
- **Scale - Cardinal** [pass]: The layer scales up from zero to full size from a chosen anchor: a corner, an edge midpoint or the centre.
- **Slide - drop** [pass]: The layer slides vertically into place, dropping in from one full layer-height above.
- **Slide - straight** [pass]: The layer slides horizontally into place from one full layer-width to the side.
- **Slide - swoop** [pass]: The layer swoops in from the top-left corner (top-right with 'reverse direction'), growing from 0 to 100 % scale along a curved path that dips a little below centre before settling.
- **Stretch & Blur** [pass]: A fast horizontal whip-in: the layer fades up while squashing from 500 % width (125 % height) back to normal, with a horizontal blur (layer width/10) clearing.
- **Stretch & Slide** [pass]: The layer fades up (in the first third) and contracts from a 20x horizontal smear: its right edge starts at frame centre and travels to the right edge as the width settles to 100 %, so it reads as a fast stretch-in from the left.
- **Stretch - Edge** [pass]: The layer stretches out from one edge (left, right, top or bottom) to its full size.
- **Stretch Over - horizontal** [pass]: The layer unfolds horizontally from a vertical line at its centre (width 0 -> 100 %) while its height eases from 150 % back to 100 %.
- **Stretch Over - vertical** [pass]: The layer unfolds vertically from a horizontal line at its centre (height 0 -> 100 %) while its width eases from 150 % back to 100 %.
- **Zoom - 2D spin** [pass]: The layer scales up from 0 to 100 % while rotating from -180 degrees (half a turn, set by Spin Amount) to upright, landing at full size.
- **Zoom - 3D tumble** [pass]: Transition-in: the layer starts at 0% scale and tumbles in 3D (Basic 3D swivel 360 deg on Y, tilt 60 deg on X) while zooming up to fill the frame, landing flat and square at 100%.
- **Zoom - bubble** [pass]: Transition-in: the layer grows from 0% to 100% scale (Transform effect, ease-out, full size at 67% completion) while a Spherize bulge with radius = half the layer diagonal holds until 55% completion and then eases flat by 100%, like a bubble inflating into the picture.
- **Zoom - spiral** [pass]: Transition-in: the layer scales 0% -> 100% while its Transform-effect position makes one full orbit around the layer centre (start angle -360 -> end angle 0, starting from the top) with the orbit radius shrinking 200 px -> 0, so it spirals in and settles centred.
- **Zoom - wobbling** [pass]: Transition-in: the layer scales from 0% to 100% while skew, skew axis and rotation (Wobble Amount 60) unwind to zero, giving a jelly-like wobbly pop into place.

### Transitions - Wipes

- **Band Wipe - Build HD** [pass]: Mask-based reveal: seven horizontal bands (each ~1/7 of the 1080 px height) wipe in one after another, each growing from the LEFT edge to full width, top band first, so the image builds top to bottom over ~2 s.
- **Band Wipe - Zig Zag HD** [pass]: Mask-based reveal: seven horizontal bands wipe in one after another, top to bottom, alternating sides (band 1 from the left edge, band 2 from the right, ...), so the image builds down the frame in a zig-zag over ~2 s.
- **Band Wipe - crossing HD** [pass]: Mask-based reveal: seven horizontal bands grow at the same time from alternating sides (bands 1, 3, 5, 7 from the left edge, bands 2, 4, 6 from the right) and interlock to fill the frame in ~1 s.
- **Barn Doors** [pass]: Reveal: the incoming layer appears from the left and right edges as two hard-edged 'doors' that close toward the vertical centre line until the shot below is covered. Two Linear Wipes (90 / -90 deg, each driven 0 -> 51%) followed by Invert (Alpha), so the wiped-away areas are what shows; driven by a 'Wipe Master Control 1' slider.
- **Checker Wipe HD** [pass]: Mask-based reveal in a staggered checker (brick) layout: 3 rows of 960 px wide cells - top and bottom rows 2 cells, middle row offset by half a cell (3 cells) - and every cell wipes in from its left edge at the same time, filling the frame in ~1 s.
- **Clam Shell** [pass]: Reveal: the incoming layer appears from the top and bottom edges as two hard-edged halves that close toward the horizontal centre line, like a clam shell shutting over the shot below. Two Linear Wipes (0 / 180 deg) plus Invert on the alpha channel, driven by 'Wipe Master Control'.
- **Clock Wipe** [pass]: Reveal: a hard-edged radial sweep pivoting at the layer centre and starting at 12 o'clock uncovers the layer like a clock hand going round (standard Radial Wipe, Wipe = Counterclockwise, keyed 100 -> 0).
- **Corner Reveal** [pass]: Reveal: the image grows as a hard-edged rectangle from a chosen corner until it fills the frame, using two Linear Wipes (horizontal + vertical) driven by a 'Transition Completion' slider; a 'Corner' dropdown picks Left Top / Left Bottom / Right Top / Right Bottom and a 'Feather' slider softens the edges.
- **Grid Wipe** [pass]: Reveal: two crossed Venetian Blinds driven by a 'Grid Wipe' pseudo effect make a grid of small square cells that grow until they join into the full image (Cell Size 20 px, Grid Angle 0, Feather 0).
- **Inky Iris Wipe** [pass]: Organic iris transition: an ink-bleed circle with a boiling fractal-noise edge grows from 'Center' (frame centre by default) to uncover the layer. Built from Solid Composite, Circle, Fractal Noise, Shift Channels, Curves, CC Composite and Set Matte on the layer itself.
- **Inky Linear Wipe** [pass]: Organic straight wipe: an ink-bleed edge with fractal-noise breakup travels across the frame along the 'Direction' angle to uncover the layer (Linear Wipe + Fractal Noise + Shift Channels + Invert + Curves + CC Composite + Set Matte).
- **Linear Wipe** [pass]: Reveal: a single hard-edged straight wipe (Wipe Angle -90 deg, Feather 0) uncovers the layer, sweeping from left to right over about a second.
- **Paint On** [pass]: Scribble reveal: a Scribble effect (Composite = Reveal Original Image, Fill Type Inside, Angle 60, Spacing 47.5) draws zig-zag strokes across a full-frame mask and the layer shows only through the strokes; the strokes draw on over ~1 s and thicken 25 -> 50 px over ~1.5 s until they overlap into a solid fill, as if the picture were scribbled in by hand.
- **Pixelated Iris Wipe** [pass]: Digital iris transition: a circle with a blocky Mosaic-pixelated, noise-broken edge grows from 'Center' to uncover the layer (Circle + Fractal Noise + Mosaic + Shift Channels + Curves + CC Composite + Set Matte; Block Size 60).
- **Pixelated Linear Wipe** [pass]: Digital straight wipe: a blocky Mosaic-pixelated, noise-broken edge travels across the frame along the 'Direction' angle to uncover the layer (Linear Wipe + Fractal Noise + Mosaic + Invert + Curves + CC Composite + Set Matte).
- **Radial Wipe - Cardinal** [pass]: Wipe-OFF by default: a hard-edged radial sweep pivoting at one of nine cardinal points ('Center' dropdown: Left Top default ... Right Bottom), turning Clockwise / Counterclockwise / Both ('Direction' dropdown), erases the layer to reveal what is below. The sweep amount auto-scales (25% of a turn from corners, 50% from edge midpoints, 100% from the centre) so it just clears the frame. The preset also carries an Invert (Alpha) effect that is switched OFF; turning it on flips the rig into a reveal.
- **Venetian Blinds** [pass]: Reveal: thin hard-edged stripes (Width 24 px, Direction -90 deg, Feather 0) open like window blinds to uncover the layer over about a second (standard Venetian Blinds keyed 100 -> 0).

## Premiere Pro effect presets (incl. Lumetri looks)


### Presets/Bevel Edges

- **Bevel Edges Thick** *(unusable)*: Gives the clip's rectangular frame a lit, raised 3D bevel (thick: Edge Thickness 0.15 of the layer, white light from -45 degrees, intensity 0.3), like a chunky tile or button.
- **Bevel Edges Thin** *(unusable)*: Gives the clip's rectangular frame a lit, raised 3D bevel (thin: Edge Thickness 0.05 of the layer, white light from -45 degrees, intensity 0.3), like a slim raised picture frame.

### Presets/Blurs

- **Fast Blur In**: The clip starts heavily blurred (Fast Blur Blurriness 127) and sharpens to clear over its first second.
- **Fast Blur Out**: The clip stays sharp until its last second, then blurs out to heavy softness (Fast Blur Blurriness 0 to 127).

### Presets/Convolution Kernel

- **Convolution Kernel Blur** *(unusable)*: Very light 3x3 blur (cross-shaped kernel 0 1 0 / 1 2 1 / 0 1 0, divided by 6): softens edges by roughly one pixel.
- **Convolution Kernel Blur More** *(unusable)*: Stronger 3x3 box blur (all weights 4, centre 5, divided by 37): an even, slightly heavier softening than Convolution Kernel Blur, still only a pixel or two wide.
- **Convolution Kernel Emboss** *(unusable)*: Diagonal emboss (top-left weight 2, bottom-right -2, Offset 128): flat areas turn mid-grey and edges become light/dark ridges along the top-left/bottom-right diagonal, like a stamped metal relief with slight colour fringes (which side reads as lit depends on how Premiere applied the kernel; not verified).
- **Convolution Kernel Find Edges** *(unusable)*: Laplacian edge detector (all neighbours 1, centre -8, Offset 0): flat areas go black and only the edges remain as thin bright lines, an outline / X-ray look.
- **Convolution Kernel Gaussian Blur** *(unusable)*: Small Gaussian-weighted 3x3 blur (1 2 1 / 2 5 2 / 1 2 1, divided by 17): a smooth, very slight softening of about one pixel.
- **Convolution Kernel Gaussian Sharpen** *(unusable)*: Very strong 3x3 sharpen (centre 13, Gaussian-weighted negative surround summing to -12, divided by 1): mathematically about a 1600% unsharp mask at ~1 px radius, so edges get hard halos and fine texture, noise and compression blocks jump out.
- **Convolution Kernel Light Emboss** *(unusable)*: Diagonal (top-left to bottom-right) relief filter: output = pixel + (bottom-right minus top-left neighbour) + 128, so edges get embossed ridges and the +128 offset pushes the whole picture up by half the range: everything above mid-grey clips to white, leaving a bright, washed-out relief.
- **Convolution Kernel Sharpen** *(unusable)*: Strong 3x3 sharpen (centre 16 against -2 edge / -1 corner neighbours, divided by 4): each pixel is pushed away from a blur of its neighbourhood with about 4x gain, roughly Unsharp Mask 400% at a 1 px radius; crisp edges and fine texture with visible halos.
- **Convolution Kernel Sharpen Edges** *(unusable)*: Harsh edge filter, not a real sharpen: each pixel becomes the sum of its 8 neighbours minus 7x itself (= pixel - 8 x (pixel - neighbour average)), so flat areas stay put but edge and texture detail is inverted and multiplied about 7x: loud, inverted halos and gritty noise.
- **Convolution Kernel Sharpen More** *(unusable)*: Strongest sharpen of the set: classic 4-neighbour Laplacian (centre 5, up/down/left/right -1), i.e. pixel + 5 x (pixel - local blur), roughly Unsharp Mask 500% at a sub-pixel radius; a little harder and crunchier than 'Convolution Kernel Sharpen', with visible halos.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/Cinematic

- **2 Strip**: Two-strip Technicolor emulation: colour is rebuilt from red-orange and cyan-green only, so skin goes coral, skies and blues turn teal-cyan and yellows/purples largely vanish; 1920s-30s vintage colour.
- **Cinespace 100**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at full strength.
- **Cinespace 100 Faded Film**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at full strength, plus Faded Film 100 which lifts the blacks into a milky, matte grey for a faded-print feel.
- **Cinespace 25**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at a subtle quarter strength.
- **Cinespace 25 Faded Film**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at a subtle quarter strength, plus Faded Film 100 which lifts the blacks into a milky, matte grey for a faded-print feel.
- **Cinespace 50**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at half strength.
- **Cinespace 50 Faded Film**: Kodak 2383 print-film emulation (CineSpace2383sRGB6bit LUT): denser contrast and deeper blacks, warm yellowish highlights, slightly teal shadows and richer reds; a classic cinema-print look at half strength, plus Faded Film 100 which lifts the blacks into a milky, matte grey for a faded-print feel.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/Filmstocks

- **Fuji Eterna 250D Fuji 3510 (by Adobe)**: Film-stock emulation (Fuji Eterna 250D negative printed on Fuji 3510): deep blacks and a firm S-curve, cool cyan-tinted shadows against warm yellow-orange highlights, dense saturated reds and greens. Measured on Rec.709 input: 10% grey -> 3%, 85% grey -> warm 0.92/0.86/0.82.
- **Fuji Eterna 250d Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Film-stock emulation (Fuji Eterna 250D on Kodak 2395 print): low-contrast matte look with lifted, warm milky blacks (pure black lands near 13-15% grey, 10% grey near 17%), neutral mids, softened colour and slightly magenta highlights.
- **Fuji F125 Kodak 2393 (by Adobe)**: Film-stock emulation (Fuji F-125 negative on Kodak 2393 print): the most contrasty of the set, crushed blacks, warm yellowish highlights, greens pushed dark and deep (almost no blue added), reds leaning magenta; dramatic and dense.
- **Fuji F125 Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Film-stock emulation (Fuji F-125 on Kodak 2395 print): low-contrast matte grade with lifted warm blacks, neutral mids and vivid but soft greens; a gentle faded-print look.
- **Fuji Reala 500D Kodak 2393 (by Adobe)**: Film-stock emulation (Fuji Reala 500D on Kodak 2393 print): deep blacks and solid contrast, slightly green-cyan shadows, warm highlights and natural-looking skin; a balanced cinematic print look, less extreme than F125/2393.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/Monochrome

- **Monochrome Faded**: Black-and-white built from the Fuji Eterna / Kodak 2395 film LUT: soft contrast with lifted, milky grey blacks and gentle highlights; a faded old-print monochrome.
- **Monochrome Faded Film 100**: Plain black-and-white (Creative Saturation 0) with Faded Film 100 lifting the blacks into a matte grey; no film LUT, so the tonal curve is otherwise untouched.
- **Monochrome Faded Film 150**: Black-and-white with Faded Film 100 lifting the blacks to matte grey. The stored Intensity 150 only scales a creative Look and none is set, so it most likely renders the same as Monochrome Faded Film 100.
- **Monochrome Faded Film 50**: Black-and-white with a milder Faded Film 50: blacks lifted to a soft dark grey, more contrast left than the 100 version.
- **Monochrome Normal Contrast**: Black-and-white through the Kodak 5205 / Fuji 3510 film curve: normal, filmic contrast with near-true blacks and clean highlights; a neutral, versatile mono.
- **Monochrome Punch**: High-impact black-and-white: the Cinespace (Kodak 2383 print) curve at about 64% adds dense contrast and deep blacks, then Creative Saturation 0 removes colour.
- **Monochrome Strong Faded **: Black-and-white on the Kodak 5205 / Fuji 3510 curve with a strong Faded Film (about 113): blacks lifted well into grey, very matte, old-photograph feel.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/ARRI Alexa

- **SL Blue Cold (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Cold' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Cold cyan-blue cast through shadows and mid-tones (mid-grey lands cyan, red well below green/blue), bright near-white highlights, reds and skin muted toward neutral; wintry and clinical.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Day4Nite' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Day-for-night: mid-tones drop roughly 1.5-2 stops, everything pushes strongly blue and skin loses most saturation, so daylight shots read as moonlit night.
- **SL Blue Ice (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Ice' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Icy cyan-blue mid-tones with very saturated greens and blues while reds stay strong; crisp, cold and vivid rather than muted.
- **SL Blue Intense (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Intense' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Darker, contrasty grade with a strong blue-cyan cast in shadows and mids (mid-grey falls to about 25% red / 44% blue), saturated blues and greens; moody and heavy.
- **SL Blue Moon (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Moon' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Gentle blue-teal cast with slightly lifted, milky shadows; colours stay fairly natural, a soft moonlit coolness.
- **SL Blue Steel (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Steel' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Bright steely look: cyan-blue mid-tones, highlights roll to clean white early, warm tones muted; cold metallic sheen.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch HDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Clean, punchy grade: extra saturation and contrast with mildly lifted blacks and a slight green-cyan bias in the mids; the darkest of the three Clean Punch variants (HDR = tuned for high-contrast, wide-dynamic-range scenes: the gentlest curve of the three, it holds the most highlight range).
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch LDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Clean, punchy grade, brightest of the Clean Punch set: lifted mids, hot highlights that clip early, the most saturated skin and colours (LDR = tuned for low-contrast, flat-lit scenes, which it stretches: the steepest curve of the three).
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch NDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Clean, punchy grade between the HDR and LDR variants: added saturation and contrast, slight green-cyan bias, normal brightness (NDR = tuned for scenes of normal contrast).
- **SL Gold Heat (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Heat' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Hot golden-orange grade: highlights shift strongly yellow-orange, skin becomes deep saturated orange-tan, greens muted, blacks stay deep; desert-heat feel.
- **SL Gold Orange (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Orange' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Teal-and-orange blockbuster split: greys stay neutral but skin and reds go saturated orange while blues stay strongly blue; bright, glossy contrast.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Rush HDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Golden skin and warm reds against slightly cool neutral greys and teal-blue blues, with bright highlights; warm commercial gloss (SpeedLooks HDR/LDR variants are tuned for high- vs low-dynamic-range scenes; here HDR has the brighter mids).
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Rush LDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Same golden direction as Gold Rush HDR but warmer, more saturated skin and slightly darker mids (LDR = tuned for low-dynamic-range, flatter scenes).
- **SL Gold Sport (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Sport' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Punchy, saturated, near-neutral grade (greys very slightly cool) with deep shadows and vivid greens and blues; energetic sports / promo look.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Tobacco' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Brown-yellow nicotine warmth in mids and highlights, slightly lifted milky blacks, greens desaturated; gritty, aged, smoky-interior feel.
- **SL Gold Western (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Gold Western' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Dusty sepia-gold: warm yellow-brown highlights, lifted red-brown blacks, greens muted; sun-baked Western look.
- **SL Neutral Start (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Neutral Start' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Clean neutral starting grade: the SpeedLooks camera conversion with a gentle filmic contrast curve, faithful saturated colour and only a hint of cool in the shadows; meant as a base to grade on top of.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir 1965' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Black-and-white with strong contrast (deep blacks, highlights clip early) and a yellow/green-filter response: greens and skin render light, blues dark; classic 1960s newsreel / film-noir mono.
- **SL Noir HDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir HDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Black-and-white, bright and low-contrast: shadows lifted to a soft grey, gentle highlights; open, airy monochrome.
- **SL Noir LDR (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir LDR' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Black-and-white, very high contrast: blacks crushed, mids bright and highlights clipping; skin and greens render bright, blues dark; graphic and harsh.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir Nouvelle' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Soft black-and-white with lifted grey blacks and capped highlights (no pure white), neutral filter response; gentle French New Wave texture.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir Nouvelle Red' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Noir Nouvelle with a red-filter response: reds and skin render brighter, greens and blues darker, with the same lifted blacks and soft capped highlights.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir Red Wave' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Black-and-white with a strong red filter: reds and skin glow bright while greens and blues fall almost to black (dark skies and foliage); dramatic and graphic.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Noir Tri-X' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Kodak Tri-X style black-and-white: neutral filter response, moderate contrast and good shadow detail; classic photojournalism mono.
- **SL Retro Brown (Arri Alexa)**: SpeedLooks 'Retro Brown' for Arri Alexa footage (camera profile + look): Vintage brown grade: warm brown cast across greys, blues collapse to near-black, greens darkened and muted; 1970s faded-print / old-photo feel.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Blackmagic Cinema Camera

- **SL Blue Cold (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Cold' for BMC ProRes footage (camera profile + look): Cold cyan-blue cast through shadows and mid-tones (mid-grey lands cyan, red well below green/blue), bright near-white highlights, reds and skin muted toward neutral; wintry and clinical.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks 'Blue Day4Nite' for BMC ProRes footage (camera profile + look): Day-for-night: mid-tones drop roughly 1.5-2 stops, everything pushes strongly blue and skin loses most saturation, so daylight shots read as moonlit night.
- **SL Blue Ice (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a cool cyan-blue cast through the shadows and mids with slightly brighter mids and solid contrast; reds stay vivid, greens turn a saturated teal-green and skies go strongly cyan, while skin turns paler and less saturated; crisp and chilly but still colourful.
- **SL Blue Intense (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a heavier version of Blue Ice: deeper shadows, more contrast and a stronger cyan cast through the mids (highlights only slightly cyan); reds stay rich, greens go teal and skin turns pale and cool; moody and stylised.
- **SL Blue Moon (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a gentle cool look: slightly lifted blacks, a little brighter mids and a mild cyan tint in the mids and highlights; colours stay close to natural, so it reads as a subtle cool mood rather than a strong blue.
