- **SL Blue Steel (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a very bright, high-key grade close in tone to Blue Cold: a steel-teal cast that sits mainly in the shadows, near-neutral mids and highlights that clip early; reds turn lighter and pinker, greens are muted and skies go bright cyan; cool and clinical.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with saturated reds and greens; the HDR version is the flattest of the three, with slightly lifted blacks and rolled-off highlights that keep detail in high-contrast scenes.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with strong saturation; the LDR version has the steepest curve, bright mids and highlights, for flat low-contrast scenes.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with saturated reds and greens and a contrast curve between the HDR and LDR versions, for normally exposed scenes.
- **SL Gold Heat (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a hot, contrasty grade: crushed blacks and near-neutral shadows, then a strong yellow-orange warmth through the mids and bright areas (skin turns golden-orange) while the very top whites stay clean; greens turn yellow-olive and skies go pale and washed out.
- **SL Gold Orange (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a bright, high-key warm grade with orange-and-blue separation: shadows and mids only slightly warm, highlights clipping early, reds and oranges pushed toward orange-yellow and skin toward light gold, while blues and skies stay strongly saturated and greens turn muted olive.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a bright, high-key grade with warm golden skin, slightly cool shadows and greens and blues shifted toward teal; a polished warm-and-teal look.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies warm golden skin with greens and blues shifted toward teal, like the HDR version but darker in the shadows with more contrast, for flat low-contrast scenes.
- **SL Gold Sport (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a punchy commercial grade with crushed, deep blacks, solid contrast, mostly neutral balance and clean saturated colours.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a lifted-black grade with an amber 'tobacco' warmth that grows toward the highlights (mids a little brighter than normal); skies turn grey-blue and greens and blues are muted, while reds and oranges hold their colour.
- **SL Gold Western (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a dusty, low-contrast warm grade: lifted blacks, a warm cast through shadows, mids and highlights, dulled skies and dark greens, like a classic western.
- **SL Neutral Start (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a neutral, cast-free base conversion: normal colour and saturation, gentle contrast and a soft highlight roll-off, with no creative colour; with log exposed at the maker's grey point it can come out about a stop dim and flat (in a Canon Log simulation diffuse white landed near 70%), so it is a starting point for further grading rather than a finished look.
- **SL Noir 1965 (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a bright, contrasty black and white with a 1960s print feel: whites clip early, and reds, yellows and skin render lighter while blues render darker (a mild yellow-filter, panchromatic response).
- **SL Noir HDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a soft, washed black and white with lifted blacks (around 15%) and low contrast, keeping a wide tonal range.
- **SL Noir LDR (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a high-contrast black and white with near-black shadows and near-white highlights, for flat low-contrast footage.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a soft, lower-contrast black and white in a French New Wave spirit, with slightly lifted blacks and a mild red-filter mix (reds lighter than greens).
- **SL Noir Red Wave (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a darker black and white with a heavy red-filter mix and greyed-out whites (the top end stops near 80%): reds glow light and skin stays bright, greens and foliage go near-black and blue skies darken noticeably; pure grey output, no tint.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a dense black and white inspired by Kodak Tri-X: near-normal contrast with slightly deeper shadows, highlights that stop just short of pure white, and a clear red/orange-filter mix (reds and skin lighter, greens darker); gritty in tone but adds no grain.
- **SL Nouvelle Red (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies almost the same soft New Wave black and white as Noir Nouvelle with a slightly stronger red-filter mix: reds and skin render a touch lighter and greens darker; pure grey output, no tint.
- **SL Retro Brown (BMC ProRes)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Blackmagic Cinema Camera ProRes 'Film' (log) footage: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a warm red-brown cast in the shadows and mids with moderate contrast and lifted, brown blacks, while the highlights stay clean and slightly cool; skies go dull grey-blue, saturated blues drop almost to black and greens darken: a 1970s print look.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Canon 1D

- **SL Blue Cold (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a very bright, high-key cold grade: mids lifted well above normal and highlights clipping early, with a cyan-teal cast in the shadows and mids while the top end stays near neutral; skin keeps its hue but goes pale, reds turn lighter and pinker, greens are muted and skies go bright cyan.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a day-for-night grade: mids drop roughly 1.5 stops (shadows most, highlights less), everything takes a blue cast that is strongest in the highlights, whites turn pale blue and never reach pure white, and skin goes grey-purple and desaturated, so daylight footage reads as moonlit night.
- **SL Blue Ice (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a cool cyan-blue cast through the shadows and mids with slightly brighter mids and solid contrast; reds stay vivid, greens turn a saturated teal-green and skies go strongly cyan, while skin turns paler and less saturated; crisp and chilly but still colourful.
- **SL Blue Intense (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a heavier version of Blue Ice: deeper shadows, more contrast and a stronger cyan cast through the mids (highlights only slightly cyan); reds stay rich, greens go teal and skin turns pale and cool; moody and stylised.
- **SL Blue Moon (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a gentle cool look: slightly lifted blacks, a little brighter mids and a mild cyan tint in the mids and highlights; colours stay close to natural, so it reads as a subtle cool mood rather than a strong blue.
- **SL Blue Steel (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a very bright, high-key grade close in tone to Blue Cold: a steel-teal cast that sits mainly in the shadows, near-neutral mids and highlights that clip early; reds turn lighter and pinker, greens are muted and skies go bright cyan; cool and clinical.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with saturated reds and greens; the HDR version is the flattest of the three, with slightly lifted blacks and rolled-off highlights that keep detail in high-contrast scenes.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with strong saturation; the LDR version has the steepest curve, bright mids and highlights, for flat low-contrast scenes.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies clean, near-neutral colour with saturated reds and greens and a contrast curve between the HDR and LDR versions, for normally exposed scenes.
- **SL Gold Heat (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a hot, contrasty grade: crushed blacks and near-neutral shadows, then a strong yellow-orange warmth through the mids and bright areas (skin turns golden-orange) while the very top whites stay clean; greens turn yellow-olive and skies go pale and washed out.
- **SL Gold Orange (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a bright, high-key warm grade with orange-and-blue separation: shadows and mids only slightly warm, highlights clipping early, reds and oranges pushed toward orange-yellow and skin toward light gold, while blues and skies stay strongly saturated and greens turn muted olive.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a bright, high-key grade with warm golden skin, slightly cool shadows and greens and blues shifted toward teal; a polished warm-and-teal look.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies warm golden skin with greens and blues shifted toward teal, like the HDR version but darker in the shadows with more contrast, for flat low-contrast scenes.
- **SL Gold Sport (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a punchy commercial grade with crushed, deep blacks, solid contrast, mostly neutral balance and clean saturated colours.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a lifted-black grade with an amber 'tobacco' warmth that grows toward the highlights (mids a little brighter than normal); skies turn grey-blue and greens and blues are muted, while reds and oranges hold their colour.

- **SL Noir HDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a soft, washed black and white with lifted blacks (around 15%) and low contrast, keeping a wide tonal range.
- **SL Noir LDR (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a high-contrast black and white with near-black shadows and near-white highlights, for flat low-contrast footage.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a soft, lower-contrast black and white in a French New Wave spirit, with slightly lifted blacks and a mild red-filter mix (reds lighter than greens).
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a darker black and white with a heavy red-filter mix and greyed-out whites (the top end stops near 80%): reds glow light and skin stays bright, greens and foliage go near-black and blue skies darken noticeably; pure grey output, no tint.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a dense black and white inspired by Kodak Tri-X: near-normal contrast with slightly deeper shadows, highlights that stop just short of pure white, and a clear red/orange-filter mix (reds and skin lighter, greens darker); gritty in tone but adds no grain.
- **SL Nouvelle Red (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies almost the same soft New Wave black and white as Noir Nouvelle with a slightly stronger red-filter mix: reds and skin render a touch lighter and greens darker; pure grey output, no tint.
- **SL Retro Brown (Canon 1D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon EOS-1D C footage shot in Canon Log: converts the camera image to video contrast and applies a warm red-brown cast in the shadows and mids with moderate contrast and lifted, brown blacks, while the highlights stay clean and slightly cool; skies go dull grey-blue, saturated blues drop almost to black and greens darken: a 1970s print look.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Canon 5D

- **SL Blue Cold (Canon 5D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon 5D Mk II/III H.264 footage in a flat/neutral picture style: applies a mild camera-matching patch (close to neutral on ordinary Rec.709) and then a very bright, high-key cold grade: mids lifted well above normal and highlights clipping early, with a cyan-teal cast in the shadows and mids while the top end stays near neutral; skin keeps its hue but goes pale, reds turn lighter and pinker, greens are muted and skies go bright cyan.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (Canon 5D)**: SpeedLooks (LookLabs) camera version for Canon 5D Mk II/III H.264 footage in a flat/neutral picture style: applies a mild camera-matching patch (close to neutral on ordinary Rec.709) and then a day-for-night grade: mids drop roughly 1.5 stops (shadows most, highlights less), everything takes a blue cast that is strongest in the highlights, whites turn pale blue and never reach pure white, and skin goes grey-purple and desaturated, so daylight footage reads as moonlit night.
- **SL Blue Ice (Canon 5D)**: Icy teal grade: shadows and mids get a cyan-teal cast while highlights stay near neutral; skin and yellows are desaturated, and greens and blues hold or gain saturation, giving a cool modern thriller look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Blue Intense (Canon 5D)**: A heavier, higher-contrast version of Blue Ice: deeper blacks, a strong cyan-teal cast through shadows and mids, desaturated skin and warm tones, with blues and greens kept rich. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Blue Moon (Canon 5D)**: The mildest of the Blue looks: a light cool cast through shadows and mids, slightly brighter mids (a 45% grey lands near 50%), blacks lifted only a touch, normal contrast, and skin and yellows partly desaturated; a gentle cool tone rather than an obvious tint. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Blue Steel (Canon 5D)**: Steely, bright cool grade: mids strongly lifted, highlights clip early, the deepest blacks pick up a teal-green tint, and every colour except blue (skin, sky, yellows, greens) is strongly muted toward a metallic blue-grey. Its tone curve is almost the same as Blue Cold. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Canon 5D)**: Clean, near-neutral grade for high-dynamic-range (contrasty) scenes: lifts the blacks slightly, rolls highlights off below clipping, brightens mids a little and adds a touch of colour to greens and blues; no strong colour cast (highlights very slightly warm). This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Canon 5D)**: Clean grade for low-dynamic-range (flat) scenes: strongly expands contrast and brightens mids (a 45% grey lands near 68%), clips highlights early and makes greens and foliage much more saturated, while greys stay neutral and bright colours such as skin and sky pale as they brighten. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Canon 5D)**: Clean grade for normal-dynamic-range scenes: neutral greys with added contrast, noticeably brighter mids (a 45% grey lands near 57%) and richer greens and blues, while skin and yellows stay close to natural; a general-purpose 'make it pop' look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Heat (Canon 5D)**: Hot, sun-baked grade: crushed blacks, high contrast and a strong orange-yellow cast in mids, with warm skin tones boosted and blue skies largely drained of colour. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Orange (Canon 5D)**: Bright warm grade with deep blacks: greys stay near neutral but slightly warm, reds shift strongly toward orange, highlights clip early, and skin and skies are partly desaturated. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Canon 5D)**: Bleached golden grade: crushed blacks, very bright mids (a 45% grey lands near 69%) and clipped highlights, cool blue-tinted shadows, pale cream-gold skin, reds pushed to orange and most other colours desaturated (cool shadows against warm gold mids). Despite the 'HDR' label it is a punchy, high-key look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Canon 5D)**: Gold Rush for low-dynamic-range (flat) scenes: more contrast and deeper shadows than the HDR version, slightly cool shadows, warm-yellow mids, reds pushed to orange and skin desaturated toward gold. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Sport (Canon 5D)**: Clean commercial or sports grade: fully crushed blacks, added contrast, neutral greys and slightly warm highlights, with colour saturation left close to natural (blues a little richer) and skin kept natural; punchy but not tinted. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Canon 5D)**: Warm amber-brown 'tobacco' grade: an amber cast through the mids and highlights (shadows stay near neutral), blue skies heavily desaturated toward grey-lavender and blues muted, while skin keeps (even slightly gains) its colour; a gritty, nostalgic feel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Gold Western (Canon 5D)**: Dusty western grade: a strong orange-brown cast, warm slightly lifted blacks, blue skies almost fully desaturated and skin warmed and boosted, like a sun-bleached spaghetti western. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Neutral Start (Canon 5D)**: Near-neutral starting point: it only maps the camera footage into a clean display image with a gentle filmic S-curve and a slightly darker midtone; blues come out a little richer (shifting slightly toward violet) while yellows and skin come out a little calmer. No creative colour cast. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Canon 5D)**: Classic 1960s black-and-white: full desaturation with fairly strong contrast and bright, early-clipping highlights; the channel mix is close to a plain luminance conversion, with reds slightly lighter and greens and yellows slightly darker. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir HDR (Canon 5D)**: Bright, shadow-lifted black-and-white for high-dynamic-range scenes: the darkest tones are lifted (an 18% grey lands near 37%), mids are pushed up and highlights roll into white, so there are almost no deep blacks; reds and orange render lighter than a plain desaturate. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir LDR (Canon 5D)**: Very high-contrast black-and-white for flat, low-dynamic-range scenes: crushed blacks, mids pushed bright and highlights clipping early, a hard graphic monochrome; reds render much lighter and greens darker than a plain desaturate. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Canon 5D)**: Soft French New Wave black-and-white: near-normal tones with slightly lifted blacks and a gentle highlight roll-off; reds render lighter and greens darker than a plain desaturate, for a soft, filmic monochrome. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (Canon 5D)**: Noir Nouvelle with a stronger red-filter mix: the same soft, slightly lifted black-and-white, but reds, orange and skin render a little brighter and greens a little darker than in Noir Nouvelle. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Canon 5D)**: Dramatic red-filter black-and-white: reds, orange and skin render bright, greens and foliage very dark and blue skies darker; highlights are capped (pure white lands near 91%), for a moody, glowing-skin monochrome. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Canon 5D)**: Black-and-white modelled on Kodak Tri-X film: near-normal contrast with slightly deeper shadows, highlights capped just below white (about 97%), and a red-leaning channel mix (reds, orange and skin render lighter, greens and foliage darker); a gritty photojournalism look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.
- **SL Retro Brown (Canon 5D)**: 1970s retro grade: a brown-orange cast through shadows and mids with near-neutral (very slightly cool) highlights; blues and greens are darkened heavily (blue objects go near-black navy), skies and yellows are muted, and reds and skin keep their colour; a warm, dated-print feel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 5D Mk II/III picture-style footage; measured on plain Rec.709 input, the 5D patch plus Neutral Start keeps tones close to the input (a slight S-curve) with blues a little richer and yellows a little muted, so the look reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 DSLR or phone clips.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Canon 7D

- **SL Blue Cold (Canon 7D)**: Bright, high-key cold grade: mids strongly lifted (a 45% grey lands near 65%), highlights clip early, and shadows and mids take a cyan-blue cast; every colour except blue is paled and desaturated (yellows, skies and foliage most, skin moderately), giving a washed, wintry look. Its tone curve is almost the same as Blue Steel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.

This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Canon 7D)**: Clean grade for low-dynamic-range (flat) scenes: strongly expands contrast and brightens mids (a 45% grey lands near 68%), clips highlights early and makes greens and foliage much more saturated, while greys stay neutral and bright colours such as skin and sky pale as they brighten. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Canon 7D)**: Clean grade for normal-dynamic-range scenes: neutral greys with added contrast, noticeably brighter mids (a 45% grey lands near 57%) and richer greens and blues, while skin and yellows stay close to natural; a general-purpose 'make it pop' look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Heat (Canon 7D)**: Hot, sun-baked grade: crushed blacks, high contrast and a strong orange-yellow cast in mids, with warm skin tones boosted and blue skies largely drained of colour. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Orange (Canon 7D)**: Bright warm grade with deep blacks: greys stay near neutral but slightly warm, reds shift strongly toward orange, highlights clip early, and skin and skies are partly desaturated. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Canon 7D)**: Bleached golden grade: crushed blacks, very bright mids (a 45% grey lands near 69%) and clipped highlights, cool blue-tinted shadows, pale cream-gold skin, reds pushed to orange and most other colours desaturated (cool shadows against warm gold mids). Despite the 'HDR' label it is a punchy, high-key look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Canon 7D)**: Gold Rush for low-dynamic-range (flat) scenes: more contrast and deeper shadows than the HDR version, slightly cool shadows, warm-yellow mids, reds pushed to orange and skin desaturated toward gold. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Sport (Canon 7D)**: Clean commercial or sports grade: fully crushed blacks, added contrast, neutral greys and slightly warm highlights, with colour saturation left close to natural (blues a little richer) and skin kept natural; punchy but not tinted. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Canon 7D)**: Warm amber-brown 'tobacco' grade: an amber cast through the mids and highlights (shadows stay near neutral), blue skies heavily desaturated toward grey-lavender and blues muted, while skin keeps (even slightly gains) its colour; a gritty, nostalgic feel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Gold Western (Canon 7D)**: Dusty western grade: a strong orange-brown cast, warm slightly lifted blacks, blue skies almost fully desaturated and skin warmed and boosted, like a sun-bleached spaghetti western. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Neutral Start (Canon 7D)**: Near-neutral starting point: it only maps the camera footage into a clean display image with a gentle filmic S-curve and a slightly darker midtone; blues come out a little richer (shifting slightly toward violet) while yellows and skin come out a little calmer. No creative colour cast. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Canon 7D)**: Classic 1960s black-and-white: full desaturation with fairly strong contrast and bright, early-clipping highlights; the channel mix is close to a plain luminance conversion, with reds slightly lighter and greens and yellows slightly darker. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir HDR (Canon 7D)**: Bright, shadow-lifted black-and-white for high-dynamic-range scenes: the darkest tones are lifted (an 18% grey lands near 37%), mids are pushed up and highlights roll into white, so there are almost no deep blacks; reds and orange render lighter than a plain desaturate. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir LDR (Canon 7D)**: Very high-contrast black-and-white for flat, low-dynamic-range scenes: crushed blacks, mids pushed bright and highlights clipping early, a hard graphic monochrome; reds render much lighter and greens darker than a plain desaturate. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Canon 7D)**: Soft French New Wave black-and-white: near-normal tones with slightly lifted blacks and a gentle highlight roll-off; reds render lighter and greens darker than a plain desaturate, for a soft, filmic monochrome. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (Canon 7D)**: Noir Nouvelle with a stronger red-filter mix: the same soft, slightly lifted black-and-white, but reds, orange and skin render a little brighter and greens a little darker than in Noir Nouvelle. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Canon 7D)**: Dramatic red-filter black-and-white: reds, orange and skin render bright, greens and foliage very dark and blue skies darker; highlights are capped (pure white lands near 91%), for a moody, glowing-skin monochrome. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Canon 7D)**: Black-and-white modelled on Kodak Tri-X film: near-normal contrast with slightly deeper shadows, highlights capped just below white (about 97%), and a red-leaning channel mix (reds, orange and skin render lighter, greens and foliage darker); a gritty photojournalism look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.
- **SL Retro Brown (Canon 7D)**: 1970s retro grade: a brown-orange cast through shadows and mids with near-neutral (very slightly cool) highlights; blues and greens are darkened heavily (blue objects go near-black navy), skies and yellows are muted, and reds and skin keep their colour; a warm, dated-print feel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon 7D picture-style footage; that patch is byte-for-byte the same LUT as the 5D patch, so it behaves identically and reads as designed on most ordinary Rec.709 clips.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Canon C300

- **SL Blue Cold (Canon C300)**: Bright, high-key cold grade: mids strongly lifted, highlights clip early, and shadows and mids take a cyan-blue cast; every colour except blue is paled and desaturated (yellows, skies and foliage most, skin moderately), giving a washed, wintry look. Its tone curve is almost the same as Blue Steel. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon C300 Canon Log (C-Log) footage. On ordinary Rec.709 footage the C-Log patch over-brightens (a 45% grey lands about 10-15 points brighter than with the 5D/7D variant: near 77% with Blue Cold, 68% with Blue Ice), crushes the deepest shadows and over-saturates, so use it only on C-Log clips and pick the 5D or 7D variant for normal footage.
- **SL Blue Ice (Canon C300)**: Icy teal grade: shadows and mids get a cyan-teal cast while highlights stay near neutral; skin and yellows are desaturated, and greens and blues hold or gain saturation, giving a cool modern thriller look. This variant includes a camera patch for Canon C300 Canon Log (C-Log) footage. On ordinary Rec.709 footage the C-Log patch over-brightens (a 45% grey lands about 10-15 points brighter than with the 5D/7D variant: near 77% with Blue Cold, 68% with Blue Ice), crushes the deepest shadows and over-saturates, so use it only on C-Log clips and pick the 5D or 7D variant for normal footage.
- **SL Blue Intense (Canon C300)**: Moody cool grade: normal mids but highlights held down (whites near 80%), a steady cyan-blue cast from shadows into highlights, cool hues saturated, skin cooled down. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Blue Intense' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Blue Moon (Canon C300)**: Subtle cool grade: a light blue-cyan tint over a gentle, slightly flat tone curve (blacks a touch lifted, whites held near 77%) with near-normal saturation. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Blue Moon' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Blue Steel (Canon C300)**: Bright, desaturated steely grade: teal-cyan shadows, neutral clean highlights, greens and reds muted, giving a metallic, corporate feel. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Blue Steel' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Canon C300)**: Clean, saturated but low-contrast grade for high dynamic range scenes: blacks lifted, highlights rolled off softly, colours vivid with a slight warm lean in the highlights. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch HDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Canon C300)**: Punchy, high-contrast clean grade for flat, low dynamic range scenes: deep blacks, bright mids and highlights, strongly saturated colours with a slight warm cast. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch LDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Canon C300)**: Middle version of Clean Punch for normal dynamic range: natural contrast, vivid saturated colour, slight warm lean, clean neutral tones. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Clean Punch NDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Day4Nite (Canon C300)**: Day-for-night: the image is pulled down hard (whites land near 50%) and washed in deep blue, skin goes grey-blue and colours are suppressed except blues, so daylight footage reads as moonlit night. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Blue Day4Nite' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Heat (Canon C300)**: Hot, sun-baked grade: deep blacks, warm orange-yellow cast that grows into the highlights, greens turn yellow-olive, skies are muted. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Heat' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Orange (Canon C300)**: Bright warm grade: orange cast in shadows and mids, reds pushed toward orange, while skies stay blue, giving an orange-teal split. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Orange' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Canon C300)**: Bright, soft golden glow: high midtones, gentle warm-yellow tint in the highlights, moderate saturation, made for high dynamic range scenes. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Rush HDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Canon C300)**: Contrasty golden grade for low dynamic range scenes: deeper blacks, strong mid contrast, warm yellow highlights and saturated reds. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Rush LDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Sport (Canon C300)**: Clean, contrasty broadcast look: deep blacks, near-neutral colour balance with a slight warm lean in the highlights, saturated colours and moderate brightness. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Sport' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Canon C300)**: Warm brown vintage grade: soft contrast with lifted blacks and rolled-off whites, amber-brown cast growing into the highlights, skies and greens muted. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Tobacco' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Gold Western (Canon C300)**: Dusty western grade: lifted blacks, strong yellow-orange cast in mids and highlights, skies drained toward grey, greens turned olive. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Gold Western' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Neutral Start (Canon C300)**: Neutral log-to-video conversion: natural colour with no cast, gentle contrast and darker-than-normal mids and highlights that leave headroom, meant as a starting point for further grading. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Neutral Start' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Canon C300)**: Classic black-and-white with normal contrast and clean whites; warm tones (skin, orange, yellow) render a little lighter and blues, skies and cyan darker, like B&W shot through a light yellow filter, while reds and greens stay close to their natural grey. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir 1965' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir HDR (Canon C300)**: Soft, low-contrast black-and-white: blacks strongly lifted to grey, highlights compressed, bright overall, detail kept everywhere. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir HDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir LDR (Canon C300)**: Punchy high-contrast black-and-white: deep blacks, bright whites, skin rendered bright and blue skies dark, like shooting B&W through a yellow-orange filter. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir LDR' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Canon C300)**: Soft grey French New Wave black-and-white: lifted blacks and compressed whites, low contrast, greens and skies rendered dark and reds brighter. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir Nouvelle' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (Canon C300)**: Same soft, lifted-black Nouvelle B&W tone but with a stronger red-filter response: skin and reds brighter, greens and skies darker. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir Nouvelle Red' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Canon C300)**: Dramatic black-and-white with an extreme red-filter response: reds, magenta and skin glow bright, saturated greens drop to near black and blue skies and cyan go dark grey; low-key overall, whites top out near 65%. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir Red Wave' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Canon C300)**: Dark, low-key black-and-white named after Kodak Tri-X: the whole tonal range is pulled down (mid grey near 35%, whites topping out near 65%) with gentle contrast; reds, orange and skin render lighter than greens and skies, like a mild orange filter (no grain added). Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Noir Tri-X' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Retro Brown (Canon C300)**: Strong red-brown, sepia-like cast that is heaviest in the shadows and fades toward near-neutral highlights; skies drain to grey and saturated blues crush to near-black, giving a faded 1970s retro feel. Made for Canon Log footage from a Canon C300: a camera-profile input LUT plus the SpeedLooks 'Retro Brown' creative look in one Lumetri Color.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/GoPro/3200 Kelvin

- **SL Blue Cold (GoPro 3200k)**: Bright, airy cool grade: midtones lifted, cyan-teal cast in the shadows and lower mids, near-neutral highlights, greens and skin noticeably muted (pale, wintry skin). Made for GoPro Protune (flat) footage at 3200K (tungsten): the 3200K profile LUT expects warm/orange input and on its own pushes a neutral grey strongly blue (mid grey -> R 0.26, G 0.34, B 0.40); it is chained with the SpeedLooks 'Blue Cold' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (GoPro 3200k)**: Day-for-night: the image is pulled down hard (whites land near 50%) and washed in deep blue, skin goes grey-blue and colours are suppressed except blues, so daylight footage reads as moonlit night. Made for GoPro Protune (flat) footage at 3200K (tungsten): the 3200K profile LUT expects warm/orange input and on its own pushes a neutral grey strongly blue (mid grey -> R 0.26, G 0.34, B 0.40); it is chained with the SpeedLooks 'Blue Day4Nite' creative look in one Lumetri Color.
- **SL Blue Ice (GoPro 3200k)**: Icy cyan-blue grade: brighter mids with a blue-cyan cast, cool shadows and skies pushed to saturated cyan, while greens and reds keep their hue. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Blue Intense (GoPro 3200k)**: Strong teal-blue grade with extra contrast: deeper shadows, a blue-green cast in the mids and saturated cyan skies. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Blue Moon (GoPro 3200k)**: Moderate cool grade: slightly brighter mids, a mild blue-green cast and saturated cyan skies over near-neutral shadows. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Blue Steel (GoPro 3200k)**: Steely, bright, slightly cool grade with muted saturation: lifted mids, early highlight roll-off and grey-blue tones. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Clean, near-neutral grade with extra saturation and brighter mids; the HDR curve lifts the blacks slightly and keeps the most shadow and highlight detail of the three Clean Punch variants. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Clean, neutral grade that is bright and hard: mids lifted strongly, highlights clip early and saturation is boosted (the most contrasty Clean Punch). This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Clean, neutral grade with boosted saturation and a middle tone curve, between the gentle HDR and the hard LDR variants. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Heat (GoPro 3200k)**: Hot orange-gold grade: crushed blacks, bright mids with a strong warm cast and very saturated warm skin, while blue skies drain to a pale grey-blue and greens turn olive-yellow. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Orange (GoPro 3200k)**: Bright warm gold grade: lifted warm mids, reds pushed toward orange, clean white highlights and skies lightened to a pale blue. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Bright golden pastel grade: lifted mids, pale golden skin and highlights and pale cyan skies, with gentler shadows than Gold Rush LDR. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Golden warm grade with more contrast than the HDR version: deeper shadows, bright golden mids and pale cyan skies. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Sport (GoPro 3200k)**: Punchy sports grade: crushed blacks, strong saturation and slightly warm highlights over a near-neutral midtone balance. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (GoPro 3200k)**: Tobacco-amber grade: a warm brown-yellow cast in the mids, slightly lifted blacks, muted skies and olive greens. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Gold Western (GoPro 3200k)**: Dusty western grade: a strong warm sepia-gold cast, lifted blacks, desaturated skies and yellow-olive greens. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Neutral Start (GoPro 3200k)**: Neutral normalising grade: turns the flat camera image into a standard-contrast, fully saturated Rec.709 picture with no creative colour cast. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.
- **SL Noir 1965 (GoPro 3200k)**: Black and white with a bright, contrasty 1960s film curve: highlights clip early, skin and blue skies render light grey and reds go dark. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir HDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Soft, bright black and white with lifted shadows and low contrast, keeping the most tonal detail of the Noir set. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir LDR (GoPro 3200k)**: Hard black and white: crushed blacks and whites that clip early, giving a graphic high-contrast monochrome. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (GoPro 3200k)**: French New Wave black and white with a natural, moderate curve: soft highlight roll-off and slightly darker skies and greens. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (GoPro 3200k)**: Noir Nouvelle black and white with a mild red-filter channel mix: reds and skin render slightly lighter and blue skies slightly darker than in plain Noir Nouvelle (a subtle difference). This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (GoPro 3200k)**: Strong red-filter black and white: blue skies and greens go very dark, skin and reds stay bright, and highlights are capped below pure white. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (GoPro 3200k)**: Black and white in the style of Kodak Tri-X film: dense, slightly dark mids, deep blacks and soft highlights (no grain is added). This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light; because the output is monochrome its cooling input LUT only changes the grey mix, but on daylight or already-balanced footage it darkens and crushes the shadows.
- **SL Retro Brown (GoPro 3200k)**: Retro brown grade: a strong red-brown cast in the blacks and shadows, warm mids, clean near-neutral highlights, muted grey-blue skies and olive greens. This variant is for flat GoPro Protune footage shot under tungsten (3200K) light: its input LUT strongly cools the image first, so daylight or already-balanced footage turns heavily blue.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/GoPro/5800 Kelvin

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Cameras/Nikon D800

- **SL Blue Cold (Nikon D800)**: Cool, airy grade: lifts the midtones well above neutral, rolls highlights off early, shifts skies toward cyan and mutes greens and skin. This variant is for Nikon D800 flat picture-control footage; measured on Rec.709 test colours its input LUT behaves almost like the SpeedLooks Rec.709 profile (slightly brighter highlights, a little less saturation), so on ordinary footage the look lands close to its intended strength.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (Nikon D800)**: Day-for-night: darkens the image by roughly a stop, pushes a strong blue moonlight cast into mids and highlights and drains saturation from skin. This variant is for Nikon D800 flat picture-control footage; measured on Rec.709 test colours its input LUT behaves almost like the SpeedLooks Rec.709 profile (slightly brighter highlights, a little less saturation), so on ordinary footage the look lands close to its intended strength.
- **SL Blue Ice (Nikon D800)**: Icy cyan grade: slightly lifted cyan-tinted blacks, cyan-teal shadows and mids, clean near-neutral white highlights and muted skin. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Blue Intense (Nikon D800)**: Strong teal-cyan grade, darker and denser than Blue Ice: deep cyan mids, solid blacks and clearly desaturated skin while blues stay rich. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Blue Moon (Nikon D800)**: Soft cool 'moonlit' grade: gentle cyan-green cast, slightly lifted blacks, clean highlights and gently muted colour; subtler than Blue Ice or Blue Intense. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Blue Steel (Nikon D800)**: Steel-blue split grade: cyan-teal shadows over a near-black, green-tinted floor, bright mids, near-neutral to slightly warm highlights, pale washed-out skies and deep blues pushed towards cyan. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Nikon D800)**: Clean, near-neutral 'punch' grade in its flattest variant: slightly warm, softer contrast with lifted blacks and gentle highlights, richer skin and foliage, slightly muted blues. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Nikon D800)**: Clean 'punch' grade in its most contrasty variant: brightest mids, near-clipping highlights, deeper blacks, much richer skin and greens, muted blues. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Nikon D800)**: Clean 'punch' grade at normal dynamic range: slightly warm, moderately brighter mids, firmer contrast, richer skin and foliage, slightly muted blues. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Heat (Nikon D800)**: Hot desert-gold grade: strong orange-yellow cast in the mids, deep blacks, heavily saturated warm skin, blue skies drained almost to grey and greens pushed to olive-yellow. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Orange (Nikon D800)**: Warm orange grade: warm mids with neutral highlights, saturated orange skin and orange-shifted reds; where blues survive (log input) it reads as teal-orange blockbuster. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Nikon D800)**: Golden grade, brighter and softer variant of Gold Rush: strongly lifted mids, golden skin and yellows over slightly cool blue-green shadows, neutral whites and washed-out skies. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Nikon D800)**: Golden grade, contrasty variant: warm gold skin and mids, firmer contrast and deeper shadows than the HDR variant, richer skin saturation. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Sport (Nikon D800)**: Mild warm, contrasty 'sport / commercial' grade: true blacks, near-neutral mids with a slight warm lift, slightly yellow highlights and a modest boost to skin and greens. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Nikon D800)**: Tobacco-brown amber grade: amber-brown mids, lifted warm blacks, muted blues and saturated warm skin; a faded vintage-warm feel. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Gold Western (Nikon D800)**: Dusty Western grade: strong yellow-brown cast, lifted warm blacks, blues drained to grey, greens pushed to yellow, warm saturated skin. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.

Meant as a starting point for further correction. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Nikon D800)**: Black-and-white with classic 1960s film character: punchy contrast, bright, nearly clipping highlights and a mild warm-filter response (reds and skin render lighter, deep blues much darker than a plain desaturate). Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Noir HDR (Nikon D800)**: Flat black-and-white: lifted grey blacks, soft contrast and gentle highlights, with a mild warm-filter response (reds, skin and yellows a little lighter, deep blues darker than a plain desaturate). Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Noir LDR (Nikon D800)**: Hard black-and-white: crushed shadows, very bright highlights and high contrast; punchy graphic monochrome in which reds and skin render lighter and skies darker than a plain desaturate. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Nikon D800)**: Soft French New Wave style black-and-white: low contrast, lifted blacks and dimmed whites; a grey, matte look with a mild red-filter bias (reds lighter, greens darker than a plain desaturate). Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.

- **SL Noir Red Wave (Nikon D800)**: Dramatic black-and-white with a strong red-filter response: reds and skin very bright, blue skies dark, foliage near black, highlights capped below full white. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Nikon D800)**: Black-and-white in the spirit of classic Tri-X reportage film: slightly dark mids, softly compressed whites and a red-filter-like response (reds clearly lighter, skin and yellows slightly lighter, greens and blues slightly darker than a plain desaturate). Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
- **SL Retro Brown (Nikon D800)**: Vintage brown grade: red-brown shadows, warm mids, near-neutral highlights, deepened crimson reds and drained blues (saturated blues crush to near-black), evoking 1970s print film. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for Nikon D800 video: a D800 camera-profile LUT converts the footage, then the look is applied.
