- **SL Noir Tri-X (RED - REDLOGFILM)**: Black-and-white in the spirit of classic Tri-X reportage film: dark mids, strongly compressed whites (diffuse white ~0.69) and a red-filter-like response (reds lighter, greens darker than a plain desaturate). Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for RED footage decoded as REDlogFilm: a RED log profile LUT normalises the log image, then the look is applied.
- **SL Retro Brown (RED - REDLOGFILM)**: Vintage brown grade: red-brown shadows, warm mids, slightly dimmed near-neutral whites (diffuse white ~0.81), deepened crimson reds and drained blues (saturated blues crush to near-black), evoking 1970s print film. Camera-specific SpeedLooks version for RED footage decoded as REDlogFilm: a RED log profile LUT normalises the log image, then the look is applied.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/SpeedLooks/Universal

- **SL Blue Cold (Universal)**: Cold cyan-blue grade: teal-cyan mids and blue shadows, true blacks, clean highlights; skin stays readable but turns cooler and reds lean orange. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 footage (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock and news feeds): a universal profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Blue Day4Nite (Universal)**: Day-for-night: darkens the image by roughly 1.5 stops, pushes neutrals and highlights blue and drains skin to near-grey, faking moonlight from daylight footage. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 footage (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock and news feeds): a universal profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Blue Ice (Universal)**: Icy cyan grade: cyan cast in shadows and mids, blues and skies pushed hard toward cyan, overall brightness kept; skin stays readable (saturation about 0.9x) but leans cool. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Blue Intense (Universal)**: Like Blue Ice but harder: much darker shadows (grey 0.18 -> about 0.11), mids slightly darker and a stronger cyan cast; skin loses a little saturation (about 0.85x) and darker skin turns dusky. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Blue Moon (Universal)**: Soft, cool grade: a gentle cyan cast with greenish-cyan shadows, contrast and skin saturation almost unchanged; the mildest of the blue SpeedLooks. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Blue Steel (Universal)**: Bright, steely grade: cool blue-grey mids, green-cyan shadows, mids lifted (grey 0.45 -> about 0.56) and highlights pushed toward clipping; reds and greens are muted and skin is brighter but less saturated (about 0.8x). Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Clean Punch HDR (Universal)**: Clean, neutral punch: tone almost unchanged (the gentlest of the three Clean Punch variants, for high-dynamic-range scenes that need no extra contrast), richer colour (skin about 1.2x, primaries clearly stronger) and a very slight cool-green cast in the mids. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Clean Punch LDR (Universal)**: The brightest, most contrasty Clean Punch: mids lifted strongly (grey 0.45 -> about 0.58, 0.80 -> 0.95) with richer colour (skin about 1.2x saturation and brighter); made for low-dynamic-range (flat, dull or slightly underexposed) shots. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Clean Punch NDR (Universal)**: The middle Clean Punch (normal dynamic range): neutral balance, a moderate contrast and brightness lift (grey 0.45 -> about 0.48, 0.80 -> 0.87) and richer colour (skin about 1.2x saturation). Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Heat (Universal)**: Hot amber grade: warm orange mids and highlights, deeply crushed shadows (grey 0.18 -> about 0.08), skin much warmer and richer (about 1.4x saturation), greens pushed to olive-yellow and blues muted. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Orange (Universal)**: Golden, contrasty grade with neutral greys: crushed shadows, brighter mids and highlights (grey 0.45 -> about 0.53, 0.80 -> 0.96), skin and warm tones pushed toward golden orange and richer (about 1.4x), greens toward olive, blues kept clean and saturated, so warm skin stands out against cool skies. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Rush HDR (Universal)**: Bright, punchy golden grade: crushed shadows, strongly lifted mids and highlights (grey 0.45 -> about 0.57, 0.80 -> 0.97), slightly cool-neutral greys, skin brighter and yellower, greens to olive and blues deepened; overall saturation about unchanged. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Rush LDR (Universal)**: Like Gold Rush HDR with deeper shadows (grey 0.18 -> about 0.08) and a little less brightening (0.45 -> about 0.51, 0.80 -> 0.96): neutral greys, richer warm skin (about 1.2x), greens to olive and blues deepened. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Sport (Universal)**: Contrasty, punchy sports grade: near-neutral (very slightly cool) balance, blacks crushed (everything below about 0.1 goes black), mids a little darker and primaries strongly saturated (skin about 1.2x). Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Tobacco (Universal)**: Warm brown-amber 'tobacco' grade: warm shadows, mids and highlights with contrast about unchanged (slightly denser shadows), skin richer (about 1.3x saturation) while blues and skies are muted. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Gold Western (Universal)**: Dusty yellow-orange western warmth: warm shadows, warm mids and highlights, skin warmer and richer (about 1.3x), greens shifted to yellow-olive and blue skies muted toward grey. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Neutral Start (Universal)**: Filmic baseline, not a creative look: mids drop by most of a stop (grey 0.45 -> about 0.36, 0.80 -> 0.72) for a denser image, with slightly cool shadows and rich, clean primaries (skin about 1.1x saturation, darker); meant as a starting point before your own grade. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir 1965 (Universal)**: Contrasty black-and-white with bright highlights (grey 0.80 -> about 0.92) and a yellow-filter response: blue barely counts, so skies and blue clothing render darker than a plain desaturate while greens and skin render light. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir HDR (Universal)**: Soft, bright, low-contrast black-and-white: shadows and mids lifted (grey 0.18 -> about 0.33, 0.45 -> 0.61) while true black stays black; blues render dark, reds lighter than a plain desaturate. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir LDR (Universal)**: Hard, high-contrast black-and-white: blacks crushed (grey 0.18 -> about 0.04) and highlights pushed to white (0.80 -> 0.99); blues render dark. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle (Universal)**: Black-and-white with an orange-filter-like mix: reds and skin render lighter and greens (foliage) darker than a plain desaturate, skies stay mid-grey; tone about neutral with slightly softer highlights (French New Wave feel). Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir Nouvelle Red (Universal)**: A stronger red-filter black-and-white than Noir Nouvelle: red makes up about half of the mix, so reds and skin render light, foliage dark and blue skies a little darker than a plain desaturate; tone about neutral. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir Red Wave (Universal)**: Extreme, infrared-like black-and-white built almost only from the red channel: reds and skin render light while foliage and blue skies go very dark; mids overall darker (grey 0.45 -> about 0.34). Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Noir Tri-X (Universal)**: Moody black-and-white in the spirit of Kodak Tri-X photojournalism: darker mids (grey 0.45 -> about 0.35, 0.80 -> 0.72) while pure whites stay white, and an even, film-like colour mix (reds and blues count more, greens less than in a plain desaturate); it adds no grain. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.
- **SL Retro Brown (Universal)**: Brown vintage grade: warm brown mids, red-brown shadows, blues almost removed (pure blue goes near-black, skies dark slate) and greens muted, while skin gets warmer and richer (about 1.2x); slightly more contrast. Universal SpeedLooks version for ordinary Rec.709 video (phones, DSLRs in a standard profile, stock, news and web clips): the SpeedLooks 'Universal' camera-profile LUT feeds the look.

### Presets/Lumetri Presets/Technical

- **Full to Legal Range, 10-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is FullToSMPTE10.cube, an extended-domain LUT that maps -0.073..1.095 (the 10-bit full-range codes read as legal range) onto 0..1. On a full-range clip that Premiere decoded as legal range it brings the crushed blacks and clipped whites back (only if the out-of-range values survived decoding); on correctly decoded footage it just squeezes 0..1 into 64/1023..940/1023, giving greyer blacks and dimmer whites. A fix, not a creative look.
- **Full to Legal Range, 12-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is FullToSMPTE12.cube, an extended-domain LUT that maps -0.073..1.096 (the 12-bit full-range codes read as legal range) onto 0..1. On a full-range clip that Premiere decoded as legal range it brings the crushed blacks and clipped whites back (only if the out-of-range values survived decoding); on correctly decoded footage it just squeezes 0..1 into 256/4095..3760/4095, giving greyer blacks and dimmer whites. A fix, not a creative look.
- **Full to Legal Range, 8-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is FullToSMPTE8.cube, an extended-domain LUT that maps -0.073..1.091 (the 8-bit full-range codes read as legal range) onto 0..1. On a full-range clip that Premiere decoded as legal range it brings the crushed blacks and clipped whites back (only if the out-of-range values survived decoding); on correctly decoded footage it just squeezes 0..1 into 16/255..235/255, giving greyer blacks and dimmer whites. A fix, not a creative look.
- **Legal to Full Range, 10-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is SMPTEToFull10.cube, which expands legal range to full range: 64/1023 goes to 0 and 940/1023 to 1.0 (about +16% contrast and saturation), and values outside legal range are pushed below 0 / above 1 (they clip on display and export). Washed-out, grey-black footage regains its punch; a fix for mis-flagged footage, not a creative look.
- **Legal to Full Range, 12-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is SMPTEToFull12.cube, which expands legal range to full range: 256/4095 goes to 0 and 3760/4095 to 1.0 (about +16% contrast and saturation), and values outside legal range are pushed below 0 / above 1 (they clip on display and export). Washed-out, grey-black footage regains its punch; a fix for mis-flagged footage, not a creative look.
- **Legal to Full Range, 8-bit**: Technical range fix: one Lumetri Color whose Input LUT is SMPTEToFull8.cube, which expands legal range to full range: 16/255 goes to 0 and 235/255 to 1.0 (about +16% contrast and saturation), and values outside legal range are pushed below 0 / above 1 (they clip on display and export). Washed-out, grey-black footage regains its punch; a fix for mis-flagged footage, not a creative look.

### Presets/PiPs/25% PiPs/25% LL

- **PiP 25% LL**: Static 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area): the clip is scaled to 25% and parked in the lower-left quarter of the frame, roughly centred in it (box spans x 287-767, y 677-947 px at 1920x1080: ~285 px in from the side, ~135 px from the bottom), with the track below showing around it.
- **PiP 25% LL Scale Down from Full**: The clip starts full-frame and shrinks to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner, revealing the track underneath; the shrink spans the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% LL Scale In**: Over the clip's first second the picture grows from nothing (Scale 0) to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner, then holds.
- **PiP 25% LL Scale Out**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner that shrinks to nothing (Scale 0) over the clip's last second.
- **PiP 25% LL Scale Up to Full**: The clip starts as a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner and grows to fill the frame; the growth spans the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% LL Spin In**: Over the clip's first second the picture spins two full turns (720 degrees) while growing from nothing to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner.
- **PiP 25% LL Spin Out**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-left corner that spins two full turns (720 degrees) while shrinking to nothing over the clip's last second.

### Presets/PiPs/25% PiPs/25% LR

- **PiP 25% LR**: Static 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area): the clip is scaled to 25% and parked in the lower-right quarter of the frame, roughly centred in it (box spans x 1160-1640, y 677-947 px at 1920x1080: ~285 px in from the side, ~135 px from the bottom), with the track below showing around it.

- **PiP 25% LR Scale Up to Full**: The clip starts as a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-right corner and grows to fill the frame; the growth spans the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% LR Spin In**: Over the clip's first second the picture spins two full turns (720 degrees) while growing from nothing to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the lower-right corner.

### Presets/PiPs/25% PiPs/25% Motion

- **PiP 25% LL to LR**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that travels from the lower-left corner to the lower-right corner of the frame; the move is spread across the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% LR to LL**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that travels from the lower-right corner to the lower-left corner of the frame; the move is spread across the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% UL to UR**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that travels from the upper-left corner to the upper-right corner of the frame; the move is spread across the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% UR to UL**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that travels from the upper-right corner to the upper-left corner of the frame; the move is spread across the whole clip.

### Presets/PiPs/25% PiPs/25% UL

- **PiP 25% UL**: Static 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area): the clip is scaled to 25% and parked in the upper-left quarter of the frame, roughly centred in it (box spans x 287-767, y 140-410 px at 1920x1080: ~285 px in from the side, ~135 px from the top), with the track below showing around it.
- **PiP 25% UL Scale Down from Full**: The clip starts full-frame and shrinks to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner, revealing the track underneath; the shrink spans the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% UL Scale In**: Over the clip's first second the picture grows from nothing (Scale 0) to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner, then holds.
- **PiP 25% UL Scale Out**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner that shrinks to nothing (Scale 0) over the clip's last second.
- **PiP 25% UL Scale Up to Full**: The clip starts as a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner and grows to fill the frame; the growth spans the whole clip.
- **PiP 25% UL Slide In Left**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that slides in from off-screen left and stops in the upper-left corner during the clip's first second.
- **PiP 25% UL Slide In Right**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that slides in from off-screen right and stops in the upper-left corner during the clip's first second.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin In**: Over the clip's first second the picture spins two full turns (720 degrees) while growing from nothing to a 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin In Left**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that cartwheels in from off-screen left (two full turns, 720 degrees) and lands in the upper-left corner during the clip's first second.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin In Right**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that cartwheels in from off-screen right (two full turns, 720 degrees) and lands in the upper-left corner during the clip's first second.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin Out**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) in the upper-left corner that spins two full turns (720 degrees) while shrinking to nothing over the clip's last second.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin Out Left**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that leaves the upper-left corner and cartwheels off-screen left (two full turns, 720 degrees) during the clip's last second.
- **PiP 25% UL Spin Out Right**: 25%-scale picture-in-picture (480x270 at 1080p: a quarter of the width, 1/16 of the frame area) that leaves the upper-left corner and cartwheels off-screen right (two full turns, 720 degrees) during the clip's last second.

### Presets/Solarizes

- **Solarize In** *(unusable)*: Legacy preset: keyframes the old Solarize effect's Threshold from 100 to 0 over the clip's first second (stored 254 -> 0 on a 0-255 scale). Going by the In/Out naming of the other factory presets (Fast Blur In runs strong -> clean), the clip most likely starts fully solarized (part-negative) and resolves to the normal image; not verified. The Solarize effect no longer exists in Premiere 2026.
- **Solarize Out** *(unusable)*: Legacy preset: keyframes the old Solarize effect's Threshold from 0 to 100 over the clip's last second (stored 0 -> 254 on a 0-255 scale). Going by the In/Out naming of the other factory presets (Fast Blur In runs strong -> clean), the clip most likely starts normal and ends fully solarized (part-negative); not verified. The Solarize effect no longer exists in Premiere 2026.

## Lumetri LUTs and looks


### Looks/Legacy

- **Blue Cold**: Cold SpeedLooks grade: cyan-blue shadows, slightly green-cyan mids, colour reduced (about 0.75x overall, skin about 0.85x and shifted toward yellow) and a strong brightening, because it is built for log input.
- **Blue Day4Nite**: Day-for-night: neutrals go deep blue and one to two stops darker; strong colours keep their saturation but skin collapses to grey-mauve (about 0.2x through the Rec.709 SL patch). Applied straight to Rec.709 it is less dark and skin stays orange.
- **Blue Ice**: Icy cyan-blue mids and shadows with slightly lifted blacks; blues pushed toward cyan, greens toward cyan-green, skin desaturated to about 0.75x.
- **Blue Intense**: Like Blue Ice but with deeper, crushed shadows and a slightly stronger cyan cast; skin drops to about 0.65x saturation.
- **Blue Moon**: Softer cool grade: gentle cyan mids, neutral shadows with slightly lifted warm blacks, overall saturation about kept, skin mildly desaturated (about 0.8x).
- **Blue Steel**: Steely look: near-neutral but very bright mids, green-cyan shadows and reduced colour (about 0.75x overall, skin about 0.85x).
- **Clean Punch HDR**: Clean, neutral punch with more saturation (about 1.15x overall, skin about 1.25x); the gentlest-brightening Clean Punch variant, with slightly lifted warm blacks and compressed highlights for high-dynamic-range scenes.
- **Clean Punch LDR**: Clean Punch variant with the strongest brightening (mid grey near 0.9 applied raw) and richer, warmer skin (about 1.35x saturation); meant for low-dynamic-range (flat, low-contrast) scenes.
- **Clean Punch NDR**: Middle Clean Punch variant: neutral balance, punchy contrast, saturation up about 1.2x (skin about 1.3x).
- **Gold Heat**: Hot amber grade: strong warm-orange mids, crushed blacks, greens pushed yellow and darker, blue skies muted to pale lavender-grey; skin much warmer and more saturated (about 1.6x).
- **Gold Orange**: Warm orange-and-blue separation: warm mids, reds and skin pushed to orange (skin about 1.4x saturation) while blues and skies stay clean blue.
- **Gold Rush HDR**: Faded golden look: overall saturation reduced (about 0.8x), reds pushed orange, greens teal, skin turned golden-yellow, very bright mids; the HDR variant has gentler contrast.
- **Gold Rush LDR**: Same faded golden look as Gold Rush HDR (about 0.85x overall saturation) with darker shadows and more contrast; skin warmer and a little richer (about 1.2x).
- **Gold Sport**: Contrasty sports grade: neutral balance, crushed blacks, punchy saturated primaries (strongest when applied raw) and slightly warmer skin.
- **Gold Tobacco**: Warm brown-amber 'tobacco' grade: brown mids, warm slightly lifted blacks, purple-grey skies and richer skin (about 1.4x saturation).
- **Gold Western**: Dusty yellow-orange western warmth: strong warm mids, warm lifted blacks, greens pushed yellow-olive, blues and skies muted to grey-lavender; skin much warmer (about 1.5x).
- **Neutral Start**: Near-neutral SpeedLooks base: through the Rec.709 SL patch it is almost transparent (mid grey 0.45 to 0.43, saturation about 1.1x); on log it is a clean conversion with modest brightening and slightly cool shadows.
- **Noir 1965**: Bright high-key black-and-white: greens render light, reds mid-grey, blues dark (like a yellow-green filter).
- **Noir HDR**: Soft, low-contrast black-and-white: shadows and mids lifted (a 0.2 dark grey renders about 0.4) while black itself stays near 0 (about 0.09 through the Rec.709 SL patch); greens and reds render light, blues darker.
- **Noir LDR**: Hard high-contrast black-and-white: crushed blacks, very bright mids, blues render dark.
- **Noir Nouvelle**: Black-and-white with a red-filter response: reds bright, greens and blues dark, so skies darken and skin glows.
- **Noir Nouvelle Red**: Stronger red-filter black-and-white than Noir Nouvelle: reds near 0.8, greens and blues pushed to about 0.3.
- **Noir Red Wave**: Extreme red-filter black-and-white: reds go almost white, greens and blues fall to about 0.26, an infrared-like look.
- **Noir Tri-X**: Black-and-white in the spirit of Kodak Tri-X photojournalism: moderate contrast and a clear red-filter response (reds light, greens and blues dark; close to Noir Nouvelle but a little darker). No grain is added.
- **Retro Brown**: Brown vintage grade: warm brown mids, red-brown shadows with slightly lifted blacks, blues almost removed and greens darkened; overall saturation reduced (about 0.8x) while skin stays rich.

### Lumetri/Film

- **fxTechnicolor(3strip)** *(unusable)*: Not a usable colour LUT on its own: it is the 3D texture behind SpeedGrade's 'Technicolor 3-strip' film shader (fxTechnicolor(3strip).irtp in the same folder). Read as a plain LUT it brightens about 1.25x and turns any pixel above ~77% in any channel black.

### LUTs/Creative

- **Fuji ETERNA 250D Fuji 3510 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Fuji Eterna 250D daylight negative printed on Fuji 3510). Measured on Rec.709 input: crushed blacks, neutral mids, warm highlights, skin saturation up about 25%, greens deepened toward blue-green.
- **Fuji ETERNA 250D Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Fuji Eterna 250D on Kodak 2395). Measured: lifted warm blacks (black about 0.13-0.15), neutral mids, saturation about unchanged: a matte, low-contrast print.
- **Fuji F125 Kodak 2393 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Fuji F-125 negative on Kodak 2393). Measured: crushed blacks, slightly darker warm mids, yellow-warm highlights, dark rich greens: a dense, contrasty print.
- **Fuji F125 Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Fuji F-125 negative on Kodak 2395). Measured: lifted warm blacks, neutral mids, low contrast: a matte filmic print.
- **Fuji REALA 500D Kodak 2393 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Fuji Reala 500D on Kodak 2393). Measured: crushed blacks, warm highlights, deeper blues and greens, mild saturation boost: natural but punchy.
- **Kodak 5205 Fuji 3510 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Kodak Vision3 250D on Fuji 3510). Measured: deep blacks with cool-cyan shadows, neutral mids, warm highlights; reds shift orange, greens teal, blues violet: a gentle teal-orange split.
- **Kodak 5218 Kodak 2383 (by Adobe)**: Classic film-print emulation (Kodak Vision2 500T on Kodak 2383). Measured: slightly warm blacks, faintly cyan mids and lowered saturation (skin about 0.8x): a restrained Hollywood print look.
- **Kodak 5218 Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Film-print emulation (Kodak Vision2 500T on Kodak 2395). Measured: lifted blacks, cool highlights and reduced saturation: a matte, cool faded print.
- **Monochrome Fuji ETERNA 250D Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Black-and-white version of the Fuji Eterna 250D / Kodak 2395 print emulation (a .look wrapping that colour LUT with Primary layers). Measured: matte B&W with lifted blacks (about 0.13), mid grey 0.45 to 0.51; greens and reds render lighter than blues.
- **Monochrome Kodak 5205 Fuji 3510 (by Adobe)**: Black-and-white version of the Kodak 5205 / Fuji 3510 print emulation (a .look wrapping that colour LUT with Primary layers). Measured: normal-contrast B&W with deep blacks, mid grey 0.45 to 0.50; reds render lighter and greens darker than a plain desaturation would make them.
- **Monochrome Kodak 5218 Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)**: Black-and-white version of the Kodak 5218 / Kodak 2395 print emulation (a .look wrapping that colour LUT with Primary layers). Measured: matte B&W with lifted blacks (about 0.12), mid grey 0.45 to 0.52; greens render light, reds and blues darker.

### LUTs/Legacy

- **FullToSMPTE10**: Range conversion for 10-bit sources: squeezes full-range values into legal/SMPTE range (0 becomes the 16/255-equivalent black, 1.0 the 235/255 white), so the picture gets flatter with greyer blacks; the extended domain keeps super-blacks and super-whites.
- **FullToSMPTE12**: Range conversion for 12-bit sources: squeezes full-range values into legal/SMPTE range (0 becomes the 16/255-equivalent black, 1.0 the 235/255 white), so the picture gets flatter with greyer blacks; the extended domain keeps super-blacks and super-whites.
- **FullToSMPTE8**: Range conversion for 8-bit sources: squeezes full-range values into legal/SMPTE range (0 becomes the 16/255-equivalent black, 1.0 the 235/255 white), so the picture gets flatter with greyer blacks; the extended domain keeps super-blacks and super-whites.
- **Linear_to_Log**: Encodes scene-linear data into a Cineon-style log curve: on normal footage the picture turns very flat, grey and nearly colourless (black becomes 0.09, mid grey 0.45 becomes 0.61, saturation about 0.1x).
- **Log_to_Display_Gamma**: Converts Cineon-style film-scan log to a viewable display image; measured mid grey drops (0.45 to 0.24) and highlights go far above 1.0 (0.85 to about 3.0), so it needs clipping or a highlight roll-off after it.
- **Panalog4_to_Linear**: Converts Panavision Panalog 4 (Genesis camera log) to scene-linear; output goes above 1.0 in highlights (0.85 to about 2.7) and looks dark and contrasty on a Rec.709 timeline unless a display transform follows.
- **SMPTEToFull10**: Range conversion for 10-bit sources: expands legal/SMPTE range to full range (16/255 black goes to 0, 235/255 white to 1.0, about +16% contrast and saturation); values outside go below 0 / above 1.
- **SMPTEToFull12**: Range conversion for 12-bit sources: expands legal/SMPTE range to full range (16/255 black goes to 0, 235/255 white to 1.0, about +16% contrast and saturation); values outside go below 0 / above 1.
- **SMPTEToFull8**: Range conversion for 8-bit sources: expands legal/SMPTE range to full range (16/255 black goes to 0, 235/255 white to 1.0, about +16% contrast and saturation); values outside go below 0 / above 1.
- **log_to_lin**: Classic Kodak/Cineon conversion of 10-bit log to 8-bit 'linear' video (reference white 685, black 95, film gamma 0.6), stored as an Autodesk/Discreet 1D .lut.

### LUTs/Technical

- **ALEXA_Default_LogC2Rec709**: ARRI's default LogC-to-Rec.709 conversion (alexalutconv, full in / full out): flat grey LogC footage becomes normal-contrast Rec.709 with restored saturation (measured mid grey 0.45 to 0.51, saturation about 1.4x).
- **AMIRA_Default_LogC2Rec709**: AMIRA default LogC-to-Rec.709 conversion; measured nearly the same tone curve as the ALEXA default with slightly gentler saturation on pure primaries.
- **ARRI_Universal_DCI**: ARRI LogC conversion to DCI (P3) output with a gentle curve: measured mid grey 0.45 to 0.46, slightly lifted blacks, modest saturation (about 1.1x).
- **ARRI_Universal_HD**: ARRI LogC conversion to HD video with a gentler curve than the camera defaults: measured mid grey 0.45 to 0.43, saturation about 1.3x.
- **AlexaV3_K1S1_LogC2Video_DCIP3_EE**: ARRI LogC-to-video conversion with ARRI's K1S1 tone map and DCI-P3 output primaries; on a Rec.709 timeline it looks slightly lifted and the colours are off.
- **AlexaV3_K1S1_LogC2Video_Rec709_EE**: ARRI LogC-to-Rec.709 with ARRI's standard K1S1 tone map: restores contrast and saturation (mid grey 0.45 to 0.51, saturation about 1.4x).
- **D-21_delogC_EI0200_B1**: De-log conversion for the ARRI D-21 camera at EI 200: LogC to video, measured mid grey 0.45 to 0.44, saturation about 1.45x.
- **Phantom_Rec709_Gamma**: 1D curve that applies the Rec.709 transfer function to linear-light data (verified: 0.1 becomes 0.29, 0.45 becomes 0.67), brightening linear Phantom high-speed footage into normal video gamma with no gamut change.

## Motion graphics templates


### (1) Text Boxes

- **1-Line Fill Text Box 1 - Down**: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); the box unrolls downward from a thin bar with the text dropping in, holds centred on Position, and collapses back to a line on exit. Catalog render (one line '00 SAMPLE'): box about 480 x 115 px centred on Position.
- **1-Line Fill Text Box 1 - Up** [pass]: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); the box opens upward from a thin bar with the text rising in, holds centred on Position, and collapses to a line on exit (the house tag template). Catalog render (one line '00 SAMPLE'): box about 480 x 115 px centred on Position.
- **1-Line Fill Text Box 2 - Left**: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); the box slides out of a vertical line at its left edge (text revealed from behind that edge), holds, then slides back into the line on exit. The left edge is anchored at about x 195 (10% of frame width, ~765 px left of Position) and the box grows rightward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 195-677, y 480-594 at default Position).
- **1-Line Fill Text Box 2 - Right**: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); the box slides out of a vertical line at its right edge (text revealed from behind that edge), holds, then slides back into the line on exit. The right edge is anchored at about x 1733 (90% of frame width, ~773 px right of Position) and the box grows leftward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 1251-1733, y 480-594 at default Position).
- **1-Line Fill Text Box 3 - Left** [pass]: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); typewriter build: the box grows with the text one character at a time, holds, then removes characters from the end on exit. The left edge is anchored at about x 195 (10% of frame width, ~765 px left of Position) and the box grows rightward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 195-677, y 480-594 at default Position).
- **1-Line Fill Text Box 3 - Right**: One-line label in a white filled box with near-black Arial Bold text (#141414 on #FFFFFF; stroke width 0); typewriter build: the box grows with the text one character at a time (letters appear from the last one leftward), holds, then removes characters from the first one on exit. The right edge is anchored at about x 1733 (90% of frame width, ~773 px right of Position) and the box grows leftward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 1251-1733, y 480-594 at default Position).
- **1-Line Outline Text Box 1 - Down**: One-line label in an orange outline box (#FF9500, stroke 7, fill opacity 0) with white Arial Bold text; the frame unrolls downward with the text dropping in, holds centred on Position, and collapses on exit. Catalog render (one line '00 SAMPLE'): box about 480 x 115 px centred on Position.
- **1-Line Outline Text Box 1 - Up**: One-line label in an orange outline box (#FF9500, stroke 7, fill opacity 0) with white Arial Bold text; the frame opens upward with the text rising in, holds centred on Position, and collapses on exit (house mg.tag outline=True). Catalog render (one line '00 SAMPLE'): box about 480 x 115 px centred on Position.

The left edge is anchored at about x 188 (10% of frame width, ~772 px left of Position) and the box grows rightward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 188-684, y 472-601 at default Position).
- **1-Line Outline Text Box 2 - Right**: One-line label in an orange outline box (#FF9500, stroke 7, fill opacity 0) with white Arial Bold text; the box slides out of a vertical line at its right edge (text revealed from behind that edge), holds, then slides back into the line on exit. The right edge is anchored at about x 1741 (90% of frame width, ~781 px right of Position) and the box grows leftward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 1244-1741, y 472-601 at default Position).

The left edge is anchored at about x 188 (10% of frame width, ~772 px left of Position) and the box grows rightward with text length (catalog render with a 9-character word: x 188-681, y 472-601 at default Position).

At default Position it sits left of centre and above middle (catalog render with one line '12 SAMPLE': box x 479-928, y 349-520).

### (2) Text Presets

- **Decode & Fade Characters Separately** [pass]: Characters appear one by one left to right; each new character fades in while counting through shifted letters (AE text-animator Character Offset, e.g. R shows as X) before landing on the real one, a decode / hacker reveal. The out removes characters from the end back to the start with the same scramble.
- **Fade** [pass]: Slow eased fade of the whole text block in and out (internal name 'Ease Fade Slow'); the In/Out groups also expose position, scale, rotation and anchor offsets, all neutral by default, so it can become a fade plus slide or zoom.
- **Fade & Position Characters [$Right]**: Characters fade in one after another (first letter first) while sliding about 150 px leftwards into place from the right; the out fades and slides them back to the right, last letter first.
- **Fade & Position Characters [Left]**: Characters fade in one after another while sliding about 150 px rightwards into place from the left; 'Reversed Order' is on by default, so the last letter arrives first and the word builds from its right end. The out mirrors it.
- **Fade & Scale & Tracking Characters** [pass]: Characters fade in while the text grows from small (In 'Scale' 20) with wide letter spacing that tightens into place, a classic cinematic title breathe; the out shrinks and fades it back.
- **Fade Characters Separately** [pass]: Characters fade in one after another (left to right stagger) and fade out in reverse (last character first); no movement.
- **Fade Characters Together** [pass]: A soft left-to-right gradient fade: characters fade with heavy overlap so the line arrives almost as one, but the left side leads (the preview shows 'MISTER' up while 'HORSE' is still dim). The out fades from the end back to the start. No movement.
- **Position & Mask Ease In [Bottom]** [pass]: The text block slides in from below through an invisible mask edge, so it appears to rise out of nothing (a clean wipe-reveal) with an ease; the out slides back behind the mask.
- **Position & Mask Ease In [Left]**: The text block slides in from the left through an invisible mask edge at its final position, so it emerges out of nothing (a clean wipe-reveal) with an ease; the out slides back behind the same edge.
- **Position & Mask Ease In [Right]**: The text block slides in from the right through an invisible mask edge at its final position, so it emerges out of nothing (a clean wipe-reveal) with an ease; the out slides back behind the same edge.
- **Position & Mask Ease In [Top]**: The text block slides in from above through an invisible mask edge, so it appears to rise out of nothing (a clean wipe-reveal) with an ease; the out slides back behind the mask.
- **Position & Rotate & Scale Characters Overshoot [$Bottom]**: Each character pops up from zero scale just below its place (100 px) while rotating about 120 degrees, landing with an elastic overshoot, staggered left to right; the out reverses it, last letter first.
- **Position & Rotate & Scale Characters Overshoot [Top]**: Each character pops in from zero scale just above its place (100 px) while rotating about -120 degrees, landing with an elastic overshoot, staggered left to right; the out reverses it, last letter first.
- **Position & Rotate Characters Overshoot [Bottom]**: Characters shoot in from below (about 600 px) with a 120-degree twist and land with an elastic overshoot bounce, staggered across the word; the out reverses it.
- **Position & Rotate Characters Overshoot [Top]**: Characters shoot in from above (about 600 px) with a 120-degree twist and land with an elastic overshoot bounce, staggered across the word; the out reverses it.
- **Position & Rotate Characters [Bottom]** [pass]: The line uncurls into place: characters swing up from far below (800 px) along an arc, each rotating up to 300 degrees and easing to rest one after another, like a ribbon unrolling; the out curls them back down, last letter first.
- **Position & Rotate Characters [Top]**: The line uncurls into place: characters swing down from far above (700 px) along an arc, each rotating up to -300 degrees and easing to rest one after another, like a ribbon unrolling; the out curls them back up, last letter first.
- **Position Characters Overshoot [Bottom]** [pass]: Characters rise in from below (about 650 px) and land with a springy overshoot bounce, tightly staggered so the line arcs as it arrives; no rotation; the out reverses it.
- **Position Characters Overshoot [Top]**: Characters drop in from above (about 650 px) and land with a springy overshoot bounce, tightly staggered so the line arcs as it arrives; no rotation; the out reverses it.
- **Position Characters [Left]**: Characters stream in horizontally from the left (starting about 1500 px away) and ease into place without a fade; 'Reversed Order' is on by default, so the rightmost letter lands first and the word builds leftwards. The out sends them back off to the left.
- **Position Characters [Right]**: Characters stream in horizontally from the right (starting about 1500 px away), first letter first, easing into place without a fade; the out sends them back off to the right.
- **Position Ease In [Bottom]**: The whole text block slides in from below (starting off-frame, about 1600 px away) with a strong ease-out and stops cleanly; the out slides it back off. No fade, no stagger.
- **Position Ease In [Left]** [pass]: The whole text block slides in from the left (starting off-frame, about 2000 px away) with a strong ease-out and stops cleanly; the out slides it back off. No fade, no stagger.
- **Position Ease In [Right]**: The whole text block slides in from the right (starting off-frame, about 2000 px away) with a strong ease-out and stops cleanly; the out slides it back off. No fade, no stagger.
- **Position Ease In [Top]**: The whole text block slides in from above (starting off-frame, about 1600 px away) with a strong ease-out and stops cleanly; the out slides it back off. No fade, no stagger.
- **Position Overshoot [Bottom]**: The whole text block flies in from below (starting off-frame, about 1600 px away) and settles with an elastic overshoot bounce; the out reverses it. No stagger.
- **Position Overshoot [Left]**: The whole text block flies in from the left (starting off-frame, about 2000 px away) and settles with an elastic overshoot bounce; the out reverses it. No stagger.
- **Position Overshoot [Right]**: The whole text block flies in from the right (starting off-frame, about 2000 px away) and settles with an elastic overshoot bounce; the out reverses it. No stagger.
- **Position Overshoot [Top]**: The whole text block flies in from above (starting off-frame, about 1600 px away) and settles with an elastic overshoot bounce; the out reverses it. No stagger.

### (5) Logos

- **Instagram**: Animated Instagram logo badge: by default a pink-to-orange Instagram-gradient rounded-square badge with the white camera glyph, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable, and the Instagram gradient can be switched off.
- **Threads**: Animated Threads logo badge: by default a black rounded-square badge with the white Threads '@' glyph, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.
- **TikTok**: Animated TikTok logo badge: by default a black circular badge with the white TikTok note glyph, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.
- **Twitch**: Animated Twitch logo badge: by default a purple (#9147FF) circular badge with the white Twitch chat-bubble glyph, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.
- **X**: Animated X logo badge: by default a black rounded-square badge with the white X glyph, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.
- **Youtube**: Animated YouTube logo badge: by default a red circular badge with the white YouTube logo (play triangle cut out), about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.
- **Youtube Shorts**: Animated YouTube Shorts logo badge: by default a white rounded-square badge with the red Shorts logo and its white play triangle, about 360 px at Scale 100; it scales up, holds for the clip length, and scales away. Badge colour, opacity, outline and corner rounding are adjustable.

### (5) Shape Elements

- **Arrow**: A clean flat right-pointing arrow (about 290 x 220 px at default) that pops on, holds for the clip length and pops off; tip, tail, thickness and corner rounding are adjustable, 'Rotation' aims it.
- **Arrow Custom Path**: A thin curved arrow (about 820 px wide arc at default) that draws on along its path from tail to head, holds, then retracts into the arrowhead; start, end and both curvature handles are movable.
- **Arrow Loop**: A flat right-pointing arrow (about 290 px long at default) that is on screen from the first frame and keeps nudging back and forth along its own direction to pull the eye; no build-on or build-off.
- **Circle Stroke Custom Duration [$Trim Path 1]**: A circle (ellipse) outline, 350 x 350 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path like a marker ringing something, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off.
- **Circle Stroke Custom Duration [Trim Path 2]**: A circle (ellipse) outline, 350 x 350 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path like a marker ringing something, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off.
- **Circle Stroke Custom Duration [Trim Path 3]**: A circle (ellipse) outline, 350 x 350 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path like a marker ringing something, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off.
- **Curves 2-Points**: One S-shaped curved path (about 1000 px wide at default) along which three coloured strokes of falling width (blue 20, red 15, orange 10 px) chase each other: they draw through from start to end and retract, a 1.3 s swoosh with no hold.
- **Glitter**: A single twinkle: two overlaid four-point star sparkles (one long-rayed, one smaller) spinning at different speeds that flash up and shrink away in about 1.5 s; about 480 px across at Scale 100.
- **Line Custom Duration [$Left To Right 1]** [pass]: A straight horizontal line (about 870 px at default) that draws on from left to right, holds for the clip length, then draws off.
- **Line Custom Duration [Left To Right 2]**: A straight horizontal line (about 870 px at default) that draws on from left to right, holds for the clip length, then draws off.
- **Line Custom Duration [Left To Right 3]**: A straight horizontal line (about 870 px at default) that draws on from left to right, holds for the clip length, then draws off.
- **Line Custom Duration [Left To Right 4]**: A straight horizontal line (about 870 px at default) that draws on from left to right, holds for the clip length, then draws off.
- **Line Custom Duration [Left To Right 5]**: A straight horizontal line (about 870 px at default) that draws on from left to right, holds for the clip length, then draws off.
- **Line Fixed Duration [$Easing 1]**: A straight horizontal line (up to about 870 px) that draws on and straight back off in one eased 1.1-1.5 s stroke, with no hold: a swipe, not an underline that stays.
- **Line Fixed Duration [Easing 2]**: A straight horizontal line (up to about 870 px) that draws on and straight back off in one eased 1.1-1.5 s stroke, with no hold: a swipe, not an underline that stays.
- **Line Fixed Duration [Easing 3]**: A straight horizontal line (up to about 870 px) that draws on and straight back off in one eased 1.1-1.5 s stroke, with no hold: a swipe, not an underline that stays.
- **Lines**: A full-frame scatter of thin vertical streaks (white, red, blue, yellow) that pop up from dots, stretch tall and shrink away in about 1.3 s, like a confetti or speed-streak burst covering most of the frame.
- **Particle Fireworks**: A small ring of outline confetti shapes (circles, squares, triangles, crosses, stars) in six colours that bursts outward from a point and fades in about 1.7 s, growing to about 500 px across.
- **Rectangle Frame** [pass]: Viewfinder-style corner brackets around a wide, short area: the four corner strokes grow on, hold for the clip length and retract. 'Stroke Length' (0-100, default 25) sets how far each bracket runs along the sides (near 100 should close the box; unverified).
- **Rectangle Stroke Custom Duration [$Trim Path 1]**: A rectangle outline, 800 x 150 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off; corners can be rounded.
- **Rectangle Stroke Custom Duration [Trim Path 2]**: A rectangle outline, 800 x 150 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off; corners can be rounded.
- **Rectangle Stroke Custom Duration [Trim Path 3]**: A rectangle outline, 800 x 150 px by default, that draws itself on with a trim path, holds closed for the clip length, then draws itself off; corners can be rounded.
- **Rectangle Stroke Fixed Duration [$Trim Path 1]**: A short stroke segment that races once around a 400 x 400 px square outline and vanishes in about 1.3 s; the full box is never shown, so it reads as a quick chase accent, not a highlight box.
- **Rectangle Stroke Fixed Duration [Trim Path 2]**: A single stroke segment (a light trail, never a closed box) runs one lap clockwise around an invisible rectangle and disappears, in a fixed 1.33 s. Trim Path 2 pattern (measured from the bundled preview): a dot sits at the bottom-right corner for ~0.25 s, then whips round with the longest tail of the three variants (about half the perimeter at its peak) and drains down the right side, gone by ~1.15 s. Transparent 1920x1080 overlay with a built-in slide whoosh; colour, size, stroke width, rounded corners and round caps exposed.
- **Rectangle Stroke Fixed Duration [Trim Path 3]**: A single stroke segment (a light trail, never a closed box) runs one lap clockwise around an invisible rectangle and disappears, in a fixed 1.33 s. Trim Path 3 pattern (measured from the bundled preview): a fast start from the bottom-right corner that reaches the top-right corner by ~0.55 s with a segment about one side long, then a slow ease-out tail that shrinks down the right side until ~1.25 s. Transparent 1920x1080 overlay with a built-in slide whoosh; colour, size, stroke width, rounded corners and round caps exposed.

- **Sports Scoreboard**: Full-screen results board: a Title/Subtitle header over the amber/black/white stripe background, then 1-3 match rows each showing two team names with their scores; Hide removes the background so the table floats over footage.
- **Sports Text Background**: Full-screen card: a Title/Subtitle header bar over the striped sports background with a large empty translucent dark panel for your own content.
- **Sports Transition**: Full-frame wipe (about 1.75 s) of amber, black and white diagonal slabs that sweep across to cover a cut and then clear; Flip mirrors the direction and Play Backwards reverses it.

### [AE] Video Gaming Package

- **Gaming Background Loop**: Looping full-screen blue tech background: a moving grid with extruded white and blue 3D blocks sliding along diagonals; speed, angle and block motion are adjustable.
- **Gaming Bullet List**: Full-screen list card: a Title/Subtitle header and up to five bulleted lines (Number of Matches 1-5 sets how many show) in a translucent dark-blue panel over the animated blue grid-and-blocks background; Hide Background floats the panel over footage.
- **Gaming Graphic Overlay**: Small corner tag: letter-spaced pale-blue text on a small dark-blue box between orange square brackets that animates on in the top-left corner; Align Right / Align Bottom move it.
- **Gaming Intro**: Full-screen open over the blue grid and 3D-block background: an orange bracket logo frames a small Title above a big Subtitle word (default 'LEAGUE' / 'PLAY'); Alternative Design switches the logo variant.
- **Gaming Logo Loop**: Looping full-screen hold of the bracket logo with Title/Subtitle over the animated blue grid and 3D-block background; Hide Background leaves only the logo over your footage.
- **Gaming Lower Third Left** [corrected_unverified]: Lower-left strap: a blue gradient bar with small 3D blocks and an orange rule, bold white Title (Bungee) with an orange Subtitle under it.
- **Gaming Lower Third Right**: Mirror of Gaming Lower Third Left: a blue gradient strap with 3D blocks and an orange rule at the lower right, white Title over an orange Subtitle.
- **Gaming Transition**: Full-frame wipe (about 2 s) of blue and white extruded 3D blocks sliding diagonally across the frame to cover a cut; Horizontal/Vertical Flip change the direction.

### Captions and Subtitles

- **Bold Broadcast Caption**: Subtitle-style text graphic: ONE text field holding two lines (line break) of white Bebas Neue (caps-only font), left-aligned at the lower left inside broadcast margins, smaller than the Web version; 0.1 s fade in/out.
- **Bold Web Caption**: Subtitle-style text graphic: one centred line of larger white Bebas Neue (caps-only font) near the bottom, sized for online video; 0.1 s fade in/out.
- **Classic Broadcast Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic in Roboto Slab: two separate text fields (one line each, same size, 70 px) of white serif text at the lower left with broadcast margins.
- **Classic Web Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic: one line of white Roboto Slab serif text centred near the bottom, sized for online video.
- **Film Broadcast Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic in Trajan Pro 3 (caps with small-cap lowercase): ONE text field holding two lines (line break) of white serif text, left-aligned at the lower left inside broadcast margins; 0.1 s fade in/out.
- **Film Web Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic: one line of white Trajan Pro 3 small caps centred near the bottom, sized for online video.
- **Modern Broadcast Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic in League Gothic: two separate text fields (one line each, same size, 90 px) of tall condensed white text at the lower left with broadcast margins.
- **Modern Web Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic: one line of tall condensed white League Gothic centred near the bottom, sized for online video.
- **Simple Broadcast Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic in Lato: two separate text fields (one line each, same size, 58 px) of plain white sans-serif text at the lower left with broadcast margins.
- **Simple Web Caption**: Subtitle-style graphic: one line of plain white Lato sans-serif text centred near the bottom, sized for online video.

### Credits

- **Angled Credits**: Movie-poster billing block: seven lines of dense white Trajan Pro 3 credits (company presents, film title, starring, music by, director of photography, story/screenplay) set small and centred at the bottom of frame; the lines pop in one after another (scale up + fade, de-blurring) over about 0.5 s and blur/fade out over the last second.
- **Bold Credits**: A static movie-poster billing block: seven centred rows of white Bebas Neue caps (large names, small connective words such as 'presents', 'music by') in the lower ~40% of frame (y about 0.6-0.93); the rows slide in one after another with motion blur, hold, then fade.
- **Classic Credits**: A centred billing block in white Roboto Slab Bold (one weight: names large, connective words small), seven rows from 'company presents' to 'directed by' in the lower third of frame (y about 0.7-0.92); rows fade in one after another, hold, fade out.
- **Film Credits**: A centred billing block in small white Lato Regular (names larger than connective words), seven rows in the lower third of frame (y about 0.7-0.9); it builds in through a pixel-block digital glitch over ~0.6 s, holds clean, then breaks up into blocks at the end.
- **Modern Credits**: A left-aligned billing block in cyan (about #1194AA) League Gothic condensed caps in the lower left of frame (x 0.08-0.94, y 0.57-0.91); the seven rows build in one after another, hold, then fade.

### Essential Graphics

- **Basic Lower Third**: Plain two-line lower third with no background: a large 'Your Name Here' line (Myriad Pro Regular) over a smaller bold second line (Myriad Pro Bold), white, left-aligned at the lower left; static, no animation.
- **Basic Title**: Single centred line of plain white Myriad Pro text ('Your Title Here') in the middle of frame with no background; static, no animation.

### Graphic Overlays

- **Angled Coming Up Next**: 'COMING UP NEXT' in italic Lato (light 'coming up', heavy 'NEXT') at the top left, building in through a blocky glitch and glitching out in the last ~0.2 s.
- **Angled Live Overlay**: A small letter-spaced white 'LIVE' in Lato near the top right of frame, with the Angled family's blocky glitch-in and short glitch-out.
- **Bold Coming Up**: 'COMING UP NEXT' in white Bebas Neue caps inside a thin underline/box, with 'NEXT' in an outlined/hatched style, top left; pops in and fades out.
- **Bold Live Overlay**: A small boxed 'LIVE' in white Bebas Neue caps with an outlined treatment at the top right of frame; pops in and fades out.
- **Bold Tap to Hear**: A boxed white Bebas Neue 'TAP FOR SOUND' prompt at the bottom centre of frame (x 0.36-0.64, y 0.77-0.87) that pops in, holds and fades: a call to unmute for muted-autoplay feeds.
- **Classic Coming Up Next**: A navy-blue gradient bar that wipes in from the left edge at the top of frame carrying 'Coming Up Next...' in white Roboto Slab; fades out at the end.
- **Classic Live Overlay**: A navy-blue gradient bar at the top right with 'LIVE' in light white Roboto Slab; wipes in, holds and fades.
- **Film Coming Up**: 'Coming up next...' in small-caps Trajan Pro at the top left, revealed by a blue horizontal light streak and fading out slowly.
- **Film Live Overlay**: A small-caps Trajan 'LIVE' near the top right with the Film family's blue light-streak reveal and soft fade.
- **Modern Coming Up Next**: Cyan condensed League Gothic 'Coming Up Next' framed by square brackets at the top left; the brackets open out to reveal the text, and it fades at the end.
- **Modern Live Overlay**: A cyan '[LIVE]' in condensed League Gothic with square brackets at the top right; brackets open, hold, fade.

### house

- **HB Counter** [pass]: House big-number counter: a 260 px Bebas Neue Bold number (white, AE Drop Shadow) fades in over 0.15 s and counts with an ease-out from From to To between 0.1 s and 0.1 s + Count Seconds; a Montserrat Bold 44 px label with wide tracking (red #E0242B by default) fades in under it from 0.6 x Count Seconds, so it arrives while the count is settling. Transparent 1920x1080, horizontally centred (number at y 490, label at y 660), no out-animation, no audio.
- **HB Counter_v1_slider** *(obsolete)*: Earlier build of the house counter (same look and timing: big white Bebas number counting up, red label fading in under it) where From and To are slider controls published with the default 0-100 slider range (its own default To of 166 is already out of range), so Premiere clamps them and it cannot count past 100. Superseded by HB Counter, which takes From/To as text. -> HB Counter

- **Bold Lower Third Left** [untested: needs_setup]: A one-line Bebas Neue name super at the lower left: white caps between a thin rule above and a thin rule below (no box sides), all three words solid white in the 2026-10-13 render (no outlined word); scales up from its left edge with a slight overshoot (settles 0.52 s), then shrinks toward the left while fading.

- **Classic Lower Third One Line**: A navy-blue gradient bar from the left edge with a bold white Roboto Slab name ('Insert Name Here') at the lower left; wipes in, fades out.
- **Classic Lower Third Two Lines**: A navy-blue gradient bar with a bold white Roboto Slab name over a small light caps title line ('ADD TITLE HERE') at the lower left; wipes in, fades out.
- **Film Caption**: 'Image Caption Here' in Trajan Pro 3 (caps/small caps) at the bottom centre of frame (x 0.34-0.66, y 0.84-0.88), revealed by a blue light streak and fading slowly.
- **Film Lower Third Left**: A one-line Trajan Pro small-caps name at the lower left with the Film family's blue light-streak reveal and slow fade.
- **Film Lower Third Left Two Line**: A two-line Trajan Pro small-caps super (name over title) at the lower left, revealed by a blue horizontal light streak, holding, then fading.
- **Film Lower Third Right**: A right-aligned one-line Trajan Pro small-caps name at the lower right with blue light-streak reveal and slow fade.
- **Film Lower Third Right Two Line**: Right-aligned two-line Trajan Pro small-caps super (name over title) at the lower right, with blue light-streak reveal and slow fade.
- **Modern Image Caption**: Cyan League Gothic 'Image Caption Here' at the bottom centre of frame (x 0.39-0.61, y 0.85-0.91), revealed by square brackets that spread open and vanish; fades at the end.

### Slates

- **Angled Slate**: A full-frame technical slate: colour-bar background with a skewed-in yellow info card (Project, Client, Date, Version, Duration, Frame Rate, Interlacing, Resolution, Aspect Ratio, Bit Depth, Color Profile, Contact, Notes), a logo bubble and a running timecode readout.
- **Bold Slate**: Full-frame technical slate: SMPTE-style colour bars behind a dark translucent info panel in Bebas Neue caps (Project, Client, Date, Version, Duration, Frame Rate, Interlacing, Resolution, Aspect Ratio, Bit Depth, Colour Profile, Contact, Notes; the whole slate fades up from black), a blue 'LOGOTYPE' circle placeholder top-left and a running timecode top-right.
- **Classic Slate**: Full-frame technical slate: colour bars behind a white info panel set in Roboto Slab (serif) listing project, client, date, version, duration, frame rate, interlacing, resolution, aspect ratio, bit depth, colour profile, contact and notes (the panel animates in over the first ~0.5 s), with a logotype placeholder and running timecode.
- **Film Slate**: Full-frame technical slate: colour bars behind a pale grey translucent panel in Trajan small caps (cinema-poster serif) with project/client/tech fields, a large logotype placeholder and running timecode.
- **Modern Slate**: Full-frame technical slate: colour bars behind a bright cyan info panel in League Gothic condensed type with project/client/tech fields, a small logotype placeholder and running timecode.

- **Share**: Social call-to-action: a teal/cyan bold-italic 'Share' pops up from tiny in the frame centre while a ring of grey dashes bursts outward, then the word holds and blurs out over the last ~0.4 s; transparent background.

### Starter Pack/(4) Social Media/(2) Buttons

- **Mouse Cursor**: A standalone cursor that travels along a curved path from a start point to an end point and clicks: Cursor dropdown 1 = Mouse (arrow, default), 2 = Pointer (hand); Click (checkbox, on) toggles the click; Curvature Start/End Point bend the path; Show Guide (off) draws the path for positioning; fill and stroke colours are editable.
- **Youtube - Subscribe 1**: A standalone light-grey "Subscribe" pill with dark text that a hand cursor clicks: it flashes pink-red (Button Fill Click #FE4874) and settles dark grey (Button Fill Clicked #373737) with white "Subscribed" and a bell icon. Optional stroke; Button Width/Height/Round Corners resize it.
- **Youtube - Subscribe 2**: A standalone light-grey "Subscribe" pill that a hand cursor clicks into a dark-grey "Subscribed" (Button Fill Clicked #373737) - no pink flash and no bell (the simplest of the four). Optional stroke; Button Width/Height/Round Corners resize it.
- **Youtube - Subscribe 3**: A light-grey "Subscribe" button inside its own white rounded container pill (Container fill, stroke, Width/Height/Round Corners); a hand cursor clicks it, it flashes pink-red and settles dark grey with white "Subscribed" and a bell icon.
- **Youtube - Subscribe 4**: A white container holding a thumbs-up on the left, a light-grey "Subscribe" button in the middle and a bell on the right; the cursor clicks the like, then Subscribe (flash, then dark "Subscribed"), then the bell, and each icon turns from "Icons" (#CBCBCB) to "Icons Clicked" (#373737). Like Correction / Notification Correction slide the icons sideways.

### Starter Pack/(4) Social Media/(3) Elements

- **Icon with Notification Counter**: A white bell icon that swings while a red badge (Container #EB4E60) pops onto its top-right corner showing a count (Text, default "1"), like an app notification; badge colour, stroke, roundness, width and scale are editable, and the bell can be swapped for any Font Awesome glyph.
- **Notification Counter 1**: An Instagram-style activity bubble: a pink-red (#EB4E60) rounded box with a tail pops up holding a white heart and a count (Text, default "26"); icon, container, roundness, tail (on/off, position, scale) and stroke are adjustable, and the heart can be swapped for any Font Awesome glyph.

### Starter Pack/(4) Social Media/(4) Icons

- **(1) Heart 1**: An animated heart: a white outline (Icon colour, Stroke Width default 20) draws on, then fills and pops in red (Icon Active, default #F41616). "Design : Activation Animation" (checkbox, on) toggles the fill/pop; "Transform : Flip Horizontal" (checkbox) mirrors it.
- **(11) Bell 3**: A single-colour flat notification bell icon that rotates/pops in, holds and exits; only Transform (position, scale, rotation, skew), a Flip Horizontal checkbox and one colour ("Color", default white) are exposed.
- **(2) Like 3**: A single-colour flat thumbs-up icon that rotates/pops in, holds and exits; only Transform (position, scale, rotation), a Flip Horizontal checkbox and one colour ("Color", default white) are exposed.
- **(3) Dislike 3**: A single-colour flat thumbs-down icon that rotates/pops in, holds and exits; only Transform (position, scale, rotation), a Flip Horizontal checkbox and one colour ("Color", default white) are exposed.

### Starter Pack/(4) Social Media/(5) Logos

- **Custom**: A blank logo badge for your own platform icon: a red (#FF0808) circle that pops in and out holding a white Font Awesome 5 Brands glyph typed into the text field "Icon" (default U+F425, the Font Awesome flag). Type another FA5 Brands codepoint to make an Instagram, WhatsApp, Telegram or LinkedIn badge; icon/shape colours and opacity, optional stroke and Round Corners (under Design; 360 = circle) are editable.
- **Discord**: An animated Discord logo badge: a Discord-blurple (#5865F3) rounded square (Round Corners 80) holding the white Discord glyph that pops in and out (about 1.7 s); icon/shape colours and opacity, optional stroke and Round Corners (under Design) are editable.
- **Facebook**: An animated Facebook logo badge: a Facebook-blue (#0866FF) circle (Round Corners 360) holding the white Facebook glyph that pops in and out (about 1.7 s); icon/shape colours and opacity, optional stroke and Round Corners (under Design) are editable.

### Text Presets

- **Scale From Center Ease In** [pass]: One-line text title (default white Arial 'MISTER HORSE', centred) that grows from 0% at its centre with an eased scale-up and shrinks back to 0% on exit; optional stroke and an optional slow continuous scale drift.

### Titles

- **Angled Presents**: Single word 'PRESENTS' in light-grey, thin wide-tracked Lato Thin that builds in from glitchy horizontal slices, holds and slices out at the end; it sits BELOW centre (about y 690-750 of 1080, centred horizontally); transparent background.
- **Angled Title**: One-line title 'YOUR TITLE HERE' mixing Lato Thin, Heavy and Regular weights (the thin words are slanted); a white light-streak bar sweeps in and the title assembles from glitchy horizontal slices, holds centred (about 70% of frame width), then slices out; transparent background.
- **Bold Presents**: Small 'PRESENTS' in Bebas Neue caps between thin rules above and below; it scales up from a dot, holds centred (about 220 px wide on 1920) and shrinks away in the last ~0.4 s; transparent background.
- **Bold Title**: Bebas Neue 'Your Title Here' (renders all caps) inside a white outline box that pops/scales in, with a smaller 'EPISODE' line appearing underneath; holds centred, then scales down out.
- **Classic Logo Presents**: Placeholder logo of overlapping translucent blue circles marked 'LOGOTYPE' that blooms in from a blur, with small serif 'presents' fading in beneath; blurs out at the end.
- **Classic Title** [untested: needs_setup]: Serif 'Your Title Here' with a smaller 'Episode Name' line over soft dark-blue bokeh circles that drift and fade in; calm, understated title card.
- **Film Presents**: Tiny, wide-tracked Trajan 'PRESENTS' (source text 'presents'; Trajan renders caps) that fades in, holds centred and blurs/fades out at the end; classic cinema credit style on a transparent background.
- **Film Title**: Blue anamorphic lens-flare streak expands across the centre, then Trajan 'YOUR TITLE HERE' and 'EPISODE NAME' resolve from blue to white with a slow push, and fade out.
- **Modern Presents**: Small cyan 'PRESENTS' in League Gothic framed by square brackets that open out to reveal it; it sits BELOW centre (about y 760-840 of 1080, centred horizontally), holds, and shrinks/fades out in the last ~0.4 s; transparent background.
- **Modern Title**: Cyan 'Your Title Here' in League Gothic between square brackets that open out from the centre, with a small WHITE tag holding cyan 'EPISODE' below; holds centred, then the whole graphic shrinks and fades out in the last ~0.4 s; transparent background.

## Tools, features and scripting surfaces


### ae_scripting

- **3D: model layers, parametric meshes, cameras**: Imported 3D models are ThreeDModelLayer (24.4+); parametric meshes can be created by script from 26.3; camera/light layers via addCamera/addLight.
- **AVLayer switches (blend mode, 3D, adjustment, motion blur, quality)**: Timeline switches and modes on footage/solid/text/shape layers.
- **Add layers (footage, solid, null, shape, camera, light)**: LayerCollection methods that create each layer type.
- **Allow Scripts To Write Files And Access Network (preference)**: Security preference that gates File writes, Socket/network access and similar from scripts; off by default.
- **Animation presets (.ffx) via applyPreset**: Apply any .ffx preset (text animators, transitions, effect stacks) from a script.
- **Application control: scheduled tasks and restart**: Run a JavaScript string after a delay (once or repeatedly) inside AE, and restart AE from a script (app.restart, added 25.4).
- **Apply an effect by match name and set its parameters**: Add any installed effect to a layer and set its controls; the core of PVE effect specs.
- **Box text layout (auto-fit, vertical alignment, insets)**: Paragraph (box) text controls added in 24.6: box auto-fit height, vertical alignment in the box, inset spacing, overflow check.
- **BridgeTalk inter-application messaging (AE -> Premiere)**: ExtendScript's BridgeTalk sends a script string from one Adobe app to another; AdobeBridge uses it because Premiere has no -r flag.
- **Composition settings and actions**: CompItem attributes for size, duration, frame rate, background, work area, motion blur, renderer, plus duplicate/open.
- **Dropdown Menu Control items (isDropdownEffect, setPropertyParameters)**: Script the item list of a Dropdown Menu Control and read dropdown labels.
- **Error handling and uncatchable failures**: Script errors raise exceptions or modal dialogs; app.beginSuppressDialogs()/endSuppressDialogs() suppress script error dialogs, app.onError names a callback for errors (useful during renders), app.saveProjectOnCrash=false skips the save-on-crash dialog, and app.exitCode reports a status to a -r/-s caller.
- **Essential Properties and the Properties panel**: Precomp layers carry 'Essential Properties' (the parent comp's EGP controls) under match name 'ADBE Layer Overrides'; the Properties panel (Window > Properties) shows them and common layer controls in the UI.
- **Expression controls (Slider, Checkbox, Color, Point, Angle, Layer, Dropdown)**: Effects whose only job is to hold a value for expressions or rigs; the backbone of scripted templates.
- **Expressions from scripts**: Write, enable and check expressions on any expressible property.
- **ExtendScript language and runtime**: AE scripts are ExtendScript (.jsx): JavaScript at ECMA-262 3rd edition (ES3) plus E4X, File/Folder, ScriptUI, Socket and the $ debug object.
- **Fonts API (app.fonts, usedFonts, replaceFont)**: Query installed fonts, detect missing/substituted fonts, and replace fonts project-wide (24.0+).
- **Footage interpretation (FootageSource: frame rate, loop, alpha)**: Script the Interpret Footage settings of an imported item: conform frame rate, loop count, alpha mode, fields.
- **Guides and rulers**: Add, move and remove comp/layer guides (e.g. title-safe and 9:16 safe-zone guides).
- **Import footage and image sequences**: Import files (video, audio, stills, PSD/AI as comps, image sequences) into the project.
- **Indic and complex-script text (composer engine)**: Devanagari and other complex scripts need the Universal Type Engine composer (the default for new text layers since 22.1.1); TextDocument exposes composerEngine, digitSet, direction and kerning type.
- **Installed effects list (app.effects)**: Read-only array of every effect AE has: displayName, matchName, category, version. The authoritative existence check before adding an effect.
- **Keyframe interpolation and easing**: Control linear/bezier/hold interpolation, temporal ease (speed + influence), spatial tangents, roving and keyframe labels.
- **Layer and composition markers**: Create layer or comp markers with comments, durations, labels, chapters and cue-point parameters.
- **Layer geometry helpers (sourceRectAtTime, point mapping)**: Measure a text/shape layer's real bounds at a time, and map points between layer and comp space.
- **Layer parameter render stage (AE 26.5)**: Choose whether a layer parameter (e.g. Displacement Map's map layer) reads the source, masks only, or all effects.
- **Layer styles (Drop Shadow, Stroke, etc.)**: Photoshop-style layer styles have match names (e.g. 'dropShadow/enabled') but adding them by script is not documented.
- **Layer timing, order and copies**: Set when a layer starts/ends, its stretch, stacking order, and duplicate or copy it to another comp.
- **Masks and the Shape object**: Add masks and set their path, mode, feather and expansion from a script.
- **Media Replacement in MOGRTs (18.0+)**: Let Premiere editors swap an image/video inside a .mogrt by adding the layer itself to the Essential Graphics panel.
- **Memory, cache and rendering switches**: Purge caches, cap memory, switch rendering off (Caps Lock equivalent) or set Multi-Frame Rendering for the next render.
- **Menu command fallback (app.executeCommand)**: Runs any menu command by numeric ID; the only route to features with no scripting API.
- **Motion Graphics template (.mogrt) export**: Expose properties in the Essential Graphics panel and export the comp as a .mogrt for Premiere.
- **Parenting**: Parent a layer to another so it inherits transforms.
- **Per-character / per-paragraph styling (CharacterRange, 24.3+)**: Style part of a text layer (one word in a different colour, a different font for a phrase).
- **Pre-compose layers**: Move a set of layers into a new comp and replace them with a single precomp layer.
- **Preferences and Settings objects**: Read and write AE preference-file keys (app.preferences) and script settings (app.settings), persisted between sessions.
- **Project create / open / save / close**: Create, open, save and close .aep projects from a script.
- **Project housekeeping (reduce, consolidate, fix expressions)**: Script equivalents of File > Dependencies: reduce project, remove unused footage, consolidate, and auto-fix expressions after renames.
- **Project items and stable IDs: comps, folders, lookup**: Create comps and folders, find items by index, ID or selection, and find layers by their persistent ID (Layer.id / Project.layerByID, 22.0+).
- **Project settings (depth, color, expression engine, GPU)**: Read/write Project Settings: bit depth, working space, linear blending, expression engine, GPU renderer, time display.
- **Property tree navigation (Property / PropertyGroup)**: Every layer setting is a Property inside nested PropertyGroups, reachable by display name, match name or index.
- **Queue in Adobe Media Encoder**: Send queued render-queue items to Adobe Media Encoder and optionally start encoding.
- **Render Queue and Output Modules**: Queue comps, apply render-settings and output-module templates, set the output file, and render in-app.
- **Replace footage / layer source**: Swap what a footage item or a layer points at.
