- **Run a script from the command line (AfterFX.exe -r / -s)**: Send a .jsx file (-r) or a script string (-s) to the already-running After Effects instance from a shell or another program.
- **Scene Edit Detection (22.3+)**: Find cuts inside a video layer and optionally add markers, split the layer, or split and pre-compose each shot.
- **Setting values and keyframes**: Set static values or keyframes on any property.
- **Shape layer contents (groups, paths, fill, stroke, trim, repeater)**: Build shape graphics by adding vector items under the shape layer's Contents group.
- **Shell commands from a script (system.callSystem)**: Run an OS command line from ExtendScript and get its text output.
- **Text animators and selectors by script**: Build text animators (opacity, position, blur per character) with range selectors, plus variable-font axis animators (26.0).
- **Text layers: point and box (paragraph) text**: Create point text, box (paragraph) text and vertical text layers.
- **TextDocument styling (Source Text)**: Style text by reading Source Text's TextDocument, changing it, and setting it back.
- **Time remapping**: Enable time remap and keyframe source time for freeze frames, speed ramps and reverses.
- **Track mattes (setTrackMatte, AE 23.0+)**: Since AE 23.0 any layer can use any other layer as its track matte (not just the one above) by reference.
- **UI-only features (no scripting API)**: Several AI/analysis tools have no documented scripting entry point: Roto Brush propagation, Content-Aware Fill, 3D Camera Tracker / Warp Stabilizer analysis control, Track Mask / tracker analysis, the Graph Editor UI, the Properties panel UI, preference panes.
- **UXP vs ExtendScript status (2026)**: AE has no UXP scripting yet (Adobe plans a public UXP plugin beta by November 2026); ExtendScript stays the AE scripting API. Premiere has UXP since 25.6, and Adobe says ExtendScripts are not affected by the CEP-to-UXP transition.
- **Undo groups**: Wrap a script's edits into one Edit > Undo entry.
- **Viewer and selection control**: Open comps in the viewer, read/set selected layers and properties, and the active tool.
- **aerender command-line renderer**: aerender.exe renders comps or the whole render queue of a project from the command line, in a new headless AE instance or (with -reuse) the running one.
- **ExtendScript Toolkit (ESTK) -> VS Code ExtendScript Debugger** *(obsolete)*: The ExtendScript Toolkit is deprecated and unmaintained (and does not run on 64-bit-only macOS); Adobe's ExtendScript Debugger extension for VS Code is the official replacement.

### ae_tools

- **3D Camera Tracker**: Analyses footage (effect ADBE 3D Tracker) to solve the real camera move, then creates an AE 3D camera plus text/solid/null layers locked to the scene.
- **3D layers (Classic 3D)**: Any 2D layer can be made 3D (Z position, orientation, X/Y/Z rotation, material options) and viewed through cameras and lights; text can be per-character 3D.
- **3D model import (GLB/glTF, OBJ, FBX)**: Import 3D models as 3D model layers rendered by Advanced 3D (24.1+); embedded animations from imported models play since 25.0; cameras/lights can be extracted from glTF.
- **Adobe Media Encoder hand-off**: Sends AE comps (or Premiere sequences) to Adobe Media Encoder for encoding with AME presets (H.264, HEVC, social presets) while AE stays free.
- **Advanced 3D renderer**: GPU composition renderer for real 3D: imported 3D models, parametric meshes, PBR (Adobe Standard Material), Substance materials, environment lighting, shadows, depth of field (26.3), with high-quality antialiasing.
- **Animation presets (.ffx)**: Saved bundles of effects, keyframes and expressions (text animations, transitions, backgrounds) applied in one step from the Effects & Presets panel.
- **Cameras (including depth of field)**: Camera layers give perspective, moves and lens blur to 3D layers; one-node or two-node (point of interest); depth of field works in the Classic 3D renderer on 26.0 (measured), in Advanced 3D only from 26.3.
- **Classic 3D and Cinema 4D renderers (Cineware)**: The other two comp renderers: Classic 3D (fast, 2D layers in 3D space, effects allowed) and Cinema 4D (CPU, adds extrusion and bevels for text and shape layers). Cineware imports .c4d scenes. Cinema 4D (a Lite licence comes with AE) is an optional Creative Cloud add-on, not part of the AE install, and it is NOT installed on this machine.
- **Colour management: OCIO / ACES**: Project colour engine can be Adobe (ICC) or OCIO color managed (23.2+, ACES 1.2/1.3 configs or custom .ocio); 24.0 added OCIO effects (CDL/Look/Color Space/Display/File Transform); 26.5 upgrades to OCIO 2.5.1 with ACES 2.0 configs.
- **Common expressions library**: Proven one-liners an edit planner can paste into AE properties.
- **Content-Aware Fill**: Removes an object from video by filling a transparent hole (mask or alpha) with pixels from surrounding frames, then writes the fill as a rendered image-sequence layer.
- **Create Shapes from Text / Masks from Text / Shapes from Vector Layer**: Converts live text or Illustrator/PDF/EPS layers into shape layers (or masks) so outlines can be stroked, trimmed and deformed.
- **Data-driven animation (JSON, CSV, TSV, mgJSON)**: Import data files as footage and read them in expressions so charts, counters and text update from data.
- **Dynamic Link (After Effects comps in Premiere)**: Live link between Premiere and AE: replace Premiere clips with an AE comp, or import AE comps into Premiere; edits in AE update in Premiere without rendering.
- **Essential Graphics panel and MOGRT export**: Turns an AE composition into a Motion Graphics Template (.mogrt) with named controls that Premiere editors (and scripts) can change: text, colours, sliders, checkboxes, points, angles, dropdowns, fonts and replaceable media.
- **Essential Properties (Master Properties) and Media Replacement**: Properties added to a precomp's Essential Graphics panel show up on every instance of that precomp in a parent comp under 'Essential Properties', so each instance can carry different text/colours/media without duplicating the precomp.
- **Expression controls as rigs (Slider, Checkbox, Color, Point, Angle, Layer, Dropdown)**: Effects under Expression Controls (ADBE Slider Control, ADBE Checkbox Control, ADBE Color Control, ADBE Point Control, ADBE Point3D Control, ADBE Angle Control, ADBE Layer Control, ADBE Dropdown Control) that hold values other properties read by expression - the standard way to build one-knob rigs and MOGRT parameters.
- **Expressions (engine and scripting)**: JavaScript snippets on any property that compute its value every frame; the project's engine is JavaScript (default since 16.0) or Legacy ExtendScript.
- **Frame blending (Frame Mix / Pixel Motion)**: Interpolates new frames when footage is slowed, stretched or at a different frame rate: Frame Mix cross-dissolves, Pixel Motion uses optical flow.
- **Guides and rulers (percentage guides in 26.5)**: Non-rendering guides in comp/layer views; 26.5 adds percentage-based positions, pinning to bottom/right edges and per-guide colours.
- **Keyframe assistants and motion panels (Convert Audio to Keyframes, Sequence Layers, Motion Sketch, Smoother, Wiggler)**: Helpers that generate keyframes: Convert Audio to Keyframes (audio amplitude to slider keys), Sequence Layers (staggers layers with optional overlap), Time-Reverse Keyframes, Exponential Scale; panels Motion Sketch (record mouse path), Smoother, Wiggler.
- **Keyframe interpolation, easing and the Graph Editor**: Controls how values move between keyframes: Linear, Bezier (ease), Hold, auto/continuous bezier, roving spatial keys; the Graph Editor shapes speed and value curves.
- **Layer styles**: Photoshop-style layer styles (Drop Shadow, Inner Shadow, Outer Glow, Inner Glow, Bevel and Emboss, Satin, Color Overlay, Gradient Overlay, Stroke) applied per layer without effects.
- **Lights, environment light (HDRI) and shadows**: Light layers (Parallel, Spot, Point, Ambient, Environment) light 3D layers; Environment lights use an HDRI or any 2D layer for image-based lighting in Advanced 3D; 26.0 adds shadow casting for Spot and Parallel lights in Advanced 3D (helpx lists Spot, Parallel and Environment as shadow-casting, several at once).
- **Markers (composition and layer)**: Timed markers with comment, duration, label colour, chapter/URL and protected region; readable by expressions and scripts.
- **Mask tracking and Face Tracking**: Tracks a mask path through the shot (Position, Rotation, Scale, Skew, Perspective) or tracks a face (outline only, or detailed features that create a Face Track Points effect). AE 26.3 made the Mask Tracker up to 5x faster with timeline controls.
- **Masks and mask feathering**: Bezier/RotoBezier masks on any layer with modes (Add, Subtract, Intersect, Lighten, Darken, Difference), feather, variable feather (Mask Feather tool), expansion and opacity.
- **Merge Paths (shape operator)**: Combines the paths above it into one compound path with a boolean Mode: Merge, Add, Subtract, Intersect, Exclude Intersections.
- **Mocha AE (planar tracker)**: Boris FX planar tracker bundled with AE as the effect 'Mocha AE' (match name mochaAECC, category 'Boris FX Mocha'). Tracks flat surfaces through perspective and occlusion; exports corner pin / transform data or uses Mocha shapes as a matte.
- **Motion blur (layer and composition switches)**: Renders blur from AE-animated movement (transforms, masks, shapes, text, many effects) using a virtual shutter.
- **Object Matte tool (AI rotoscoping, AE 26.2+)**: Click or marquee-select a moving subject (person, animal, vehicle) and AE generates and tracks a matte through the shot. Replaced the Roto Brush tool in 26.2; older Roto Brush versions are chosen from a version selector in the Object Matte effect.
- **Offset Paths (shape operator)**: Grows or shrinks paths by Amount, with Line Join, Miter Limit, and Copies / Copy Offset to make concentric outlines.
- **Paint tools (Brush, Clone Stamp, Eraser)**: Non-destructive vector paint strokes drawn in the Layer panel: Brush paints colour, Clone Stamp copies pixels from a source layer/time, Eraser removes; each stroke is a timed, keyframable item under the layer's Paint effect (match name ADBE Paint, harvested under '_Obsolete' because the tools apply it).
- **Parametric meshes (3D primitives, AE 26.0+)**: Native 3D primitives drawn in AE - cube, sphere, cylinder, cone, torus, plane - with animatable shape and material properties (roughness, metalness, emissive); 26.2 adds displacement from height data.
- **Parenting, null objects and Create Nulls From Paths**: Parent layers to nulls to move groups together; the bundled Create Nulls From Paths panel links path vertices and nulls (Points Follow Nulls, Nulls Follow Points, Trace Path).
- **Performance: Multi-Frame Rendering, caching and preview**: Multi-Frame Rendering (22.0) renders several frames in parallel; Speculative Preview pre-renders while idle; the Composition Profiler shows per-layer render time; High Performance Preview Playback (25.2) manages the disk cache; 26.0 stores lossless compressed preview frames for longer previews.
- **Precompose (nesting) and Collapse Transformations**: Groups layers into a nested comp to control effect order, reuse, or treat many layers as one; Collapse Transformations lets a nested comp's vectors/3D stay sharp and interact with the parent.
- **Properties panel**: Contextual panel showing the important controls of the selected layer(s) - transform, text (character, paragraph, fill/stroke, animators, variable font axes) and shape properties - without twirling the timeline; redesigned in 25.0.
- **Puppet tools (Position, Advanced, Bend, Starch, Overlap pins)**: Deforms a layer with a mesh controlled by pins; applying a pin adds the Puppet effect (ADBE FreePin3) with Puppet Engine (1 Legacy, 2 Advanced = default) and Mesh Rotation Refinement.
- **Quick Apply and Proportional Scrubbing (AE 26.2+)**: Quick Apply is a search box that finds and runs any effect, animation preset or menu command; Proportional Scrubbing drags one property's hot-text across several selected layers and distributes the change by selection order (cascades in one drag).
- **Render Queue and output modules**: AE's built-in renderer: each queue item has Render Settings (quality, resolution, time span) and Output Modules (format, codec, alpha, audio, file path).
- **Repeater (shape operator)**: Makes N transformed copies of the shapes above it in the group: Copies, Offset, Composite (above/below) and a Transform (Anchor, Position, Scale, Rotation, Start/End Opacity) applied cumulatively per copy.
- **Responsive Design - Time (protected regions)**: Marks intro/outro regions of a comp or MOGRT that keep their timing when the comp or template duration is changed - the middle stretches, the animations at the ends do not.
- **Roto Brush (Next-Gen, version 3.0)**: AI rotoscoping: paint strokes on the subject on one frame and AE propagates a matte through the shot. Creates the 'Roto Brush & Refine Edge' effect (match name ADBE Samurai) whose Version dropdown 3 = Next-Gen (default), 1-2 = older models.
- **SVG import and Illustrator/SVG paste as shape layers**: 26.0 imports SVG as native editable shape layers (paths, fills, transforms) and converts Illustrator layers with gradients, strokes and transparency; 26.2 adds Import As Composition (editable layers) or Footage (rasterised); 26.3 lets Edit > Paste turn copied Illustrator/SVG content into shape layers.
- **Scene Edit Detection**: Finds the cuts inside a pre-edited clip and adds layer markers, splits the layer at each cut, or splits and precomposes each shot.
- **Shape layers (vector graphics)**: Resolution-independent vector layers made of groups, shapes (Rectangle, Ellipse, Polystar, Path), paint (Fill, Stroke, Gradient Fill, Gradient Stroke) and path operators, all animatable.
- **Slow-motion and synthetic blur effects: Timewarp, Pixel Motion Blur, CC Force Motion Blur**: Effect-based retiming and motion blur for footage (which the layer motion-blur switch cannot blur because its pixels do not move in AE).
- **Substance 3D materials (.sbsar, AE 26.0+)**: Import Substance 3D Designer materials (.sbsar), assign them to 3D models and meshes, and edit their exposed parameters inside AE; 1,300+ free materials in the Substance 3D community library. Beta (26.5): materials on 3D text/shapes and comp layers as material sources.
- **Text animators and the Range Selector**: Per-character/word/line animation on text layers: an Animator holds properties (position, scale, rotation, opacity, fill, tracking, blur, character offset...) and a Range Selector decides which characters are affected and by how much.
- **Text engine, Indic scripts and text scripting**: AE's Universal Type Engine (formerly 'South Asian and Middle Eastern', default since 22.1.1) shapes Devanagari, Urdu and other complex scripts; since 24.x most character/paragraph settings and fonts are scriptable through TextDocument and app.fonts.
- **Time remapping, freeze frames, reverse and time stretch**: Remaps which source frame plays at each comp time (speed ramps, holds, reverse) or uniformly stretches a layer's duration.
- **Track Motion / Stabilize Motion (point tracker)**: Classic 1-4 point tracker in the Tracker panel: tracks features frame by frame and applies the result to a target layer as Transform, corner pin or stabilisation.
- **Track mattes (any layer, 23.0+)**: Any layer can be another layer's track matte (Alpha, Alpha Inverted, Luma, Luma Inverted); the matte layer no longer has to sit directly above.
- **Trim Paths (shape operator)**: Shows only a portion of the paths above it: Start %, End %, Offset (degrees) and Trim Multiple Shapes (1 Simultaneously, 2 Individually). The standard way to draw lines on.
- **Variable font axis animation (AE 26.0+)**: Keyframe or drive by expression any axis of a variable font (weight 'wght', width 'wdth', slant 'slnt', italic 'ital', custom axes) through text animators, up to eight axes per layer; 26.3 adds a variable-font filter in the font list.
- **Warp Stabilizer**: Analyses and smooths or locks camera shake (effect ADBE SubspaceStabilizer; display name 'Warp Stabilizer' in the 26.0 harvest, 'Warp Stabilizer VFX' in older menus).
- **Wiggle Paths, Wiggle Transform and other path operators**: Procedural path modifiers: Wiggle Paths (jitters vertices), Wiggle Transform (randomises group transforms), Zig Zag, Pucker & Bloat, Round Corners, Twist.
- **Wiggly Selector and Expression Selector (text)**: Alternative text selectors: the Wiggly Selector randomly varies how strongly each character is selected over time; the Expression Selector computes the amount per character with an expression (textIndex, textTotal, selectorValue).
- **aerender (command-line renderer)**: aerender.exe renders a project's render queue or one comp headlessly; used by AdobeBridge vfx.py.
- **AI Assistant (agentic, beta)** *(beta)*: Natural-language assistant panel in the After Effects (beta) build: organises and renames project items, reads/writes/fixes expressions, and generates images and video with Adobe Firefly and partner models.

### audio_delivery

- **Broadcast audio track layouts (EBU R 123)**: Standard audio track allocations for programme files (EBU R 123, 48 kHz / 24-bit): 4a = 1-2 stereo, 3-4 encoded multichannel; 4b = 1-2 stereo mix, 3-4 stereo international sound or effects; 8a = 1-2 stereo, 3-8 5.1 (L R C LFE Ls Rs); 8b = 1-6 5.1, 7-8 stereo; 16a = 1-2 stereo mix, 3-8 5.1 mix, 9-10 stereo effects, 11-16 5.1 effects.
- **Documentary mix-level plan (dialogue, music, effects)**: A starting plan for mixing narration-led documentaries and explainers: dialogue sets the level, music sits under it, effects punctuate, and the whole mix lands on the delivery's loudness target and true-peak ceiling. Sourced targets: Netflix -27 LKFS dialogue-gated (+/-2 LU) with true peak at most -2 dBTP; EBU R 128 -23 LUFS, true peak at most -1 dBTP; ATSC A/85 -24 LKFS, true peak below -2 dBTP; web: see 'Delivery loudness targets'.
- **M&E (music and effects) mix**: The international version of the soundtrack: every sound except dialogue, fully filled so another language can be dubbed on top. Netflix: a fully filled music-and-effects submix with no dialogue, including ambience and foley fill, every non-dialogue sound of the main mix, at levels that mirror the final mix.
- **Scripted ducking from VO ranges**: Ducking without Premiere's UI-only Auto Ducking: take the VO clips' timeline ranges (or a voice-activity pass on the VO file), merge ranges separated by short pauses, and write the music clip's Volume 'Level' keyframes: down before each VO block, back up after it, only where the gap is long enough.
- **Stems and split-track export (dialogue, music, effects)**: Delivering the mix as separate stems (dialogue, music, effects, sometimes ambience) that add back up to the full mix, as 48 kHz / 24-bit uncompressed WAV. Premiere has no one-click stems export: stems are made by exporting audio-only passes with other tracks muted, by a multichannel master, or as an OMF for Pro Tools.

### audio_post

- **Adaptive Noise Reduction**: Older adaptive spectral noise reducer that learns changing noise; heavier to tune than DeNoise.
- **After Effects audio output and limits**: AE is not a mixing tool: legacy-quality audio effects (plus the 2026 Compressor/Gate/Distortion), no loudness meter, no ducking, no noise reduction. Render audio comes from the Output Module.
- **Amplify**: Gain boost/cut per channel; Auto Ducking writes its keyframes to this effect's 'Gain'.
- **Audio Gain dialog (Set / Adjust / Normalize peaks)**: Clip gain applied before the Volume effect: Set Gain to, Adjust Gain by, Normalize Max Peak to, Normalize All Peaks to (dB), with the measured Peak Amplitude shown.
- **Audio Levels (layer property)**: Every AE layer with audio has Audio > Audio Levels, a [left, right] pair in dB; keyframe it for fades and ducks inside a comp.
- **Audio Spectrum and Audio Waveform (visualizers)**: Generate effects that draw a frequency spectrum (ADBE AudSpect) or waveform (ADBE AudWave) from an audio layer, optionally along a mask path.
- **Audio Track Mixer (track effects, automation, submixes)**: Per-track faders, pan, mute/solo, insert effects and sends, automation modes (Off, Read, Write, Latch, Touch) and submix tracks; the Mix track is where a final limiter or Loudness Meter belongs.
- **Audio crossfade transitions (Constant Power, Constant Gain, Exponential Fade)**: The three installed audio transitions. Constant Power = smooth equal-power crossfade (default choice), Constant Gain = linear, can dip mid-fade, Exponential Fade = logarithmic and more gradual.
- **Audio meters, Dynamic Audio Waveforms and Global Mute**: Monitoring aids: Audio Meters (peak dBFS), waveforms that redraw with volume/keyframe/ducking changes (View > Dynamic Audio Waveforms) and live during trims, and muting of all/Source/Program playback: in the installed 26.0.2 via right-click on the Audio Meters (Mute All Audio / Mute Program Monitor); the menu-bar Global Mute button arrived in 26.2 (April 2026) and is not in 26.0.2.
- **Auto Ducking**: Essential Sound > Ducking on a Music or Ambience clip computes volume dips wherever the chosen 'Duck Against' types (Dialogue, Music, SFX, Ambience, untagged) play. Premiere adds an Amplify effect and writes the keyframes to its Gain param.
- **Auto Tag (audio category tagging)**: AI classifier that labels selected clips as Dialogue, Music, SFX or Ambience and adds clickable Essential Sound badges to the clips.
- **Automatic Click Remover**: Removes clicks, crackle and pops (designed for vinyl, works on some mouth clicks and digital ticks).
- **Bulk mute or bleep (censored word list)**: Transcript-driven censoring: add words to a Censored word list, filter them in the Text panel and apply a bleep tone or mute to one or all hits.
- **Clip volume (Volume effect 'Level')**: Every audio clip's intrinsic 'Volume' component ('Bypass', 'Level') sets clip level and carries fades and ducking keyframes. The main scriptable mixing control.
- **Compressor**: New (2026) AE audio compressor: Threshold (dB) -60..0 (default -16), Ratio (x:1) 0.4..30 (3), Knee (db) 0..30 (15), Attack (ms) 0..400 (6), Release (ms) 1..4000 (440), Auto Release (on), Makeup Gain (dB) -30..30, Output Limit (dB) -30..0 (-1).
- **Convert Audio to Keyframes**: Keyframe assistant that creates an 'Audio Amplitude' null with sliders 'Left Channel', 'Right Channel', 'Both Channels' keyed every frame from the comp's audio, for audio-reactive motion.
- **DeEsser**: Compresses only the sibilance band to tame harsh S/T sounds.
- **DeHummer**: Removes a hum frequency and its harmonics (power-line hum, lights, resonant tones).
- **DeNoise**: Adobe's modern broadband noise reducer (hiss, hum, fans, AC) with one Amount slider (0-100 %).
- **DeReverb**: Estimates and reduces room reverberation (0-100 %).
- **Delivery loudness targets (YouTube, Instagram, broadcast)**: Integrated loudness and true-peak targets for final mixes. YouTube: no official published spec; it turns loud uploads down toward about -14 LUFS and does not raise quiet ones (visible as 'content loudness' in Stats for nerds). Instagram/Facebook Reels: no published spec; community measurements point to about -14 LUFS. Ceiling -1 dBTP for lossy platforms (AES). Broadcast: EBU R128 -23 LUFS, ATSC A/85 -24 LKFS.
- **Distortion**: New (2026) AE distortion + bitcrusher: Type 1-6 (Soft Clip, Hard Clip, Saturation 1, Saturation 2, Tube, Fuzz), Drive 0..100 (25), Gain 0..300 (25), Mix 0..100 (100), Volume 0..100 (11), Resolution (bit) 1..24 (24), Downsample (x) 1..48 (1).
- **Dynamics**: Four-in-one dynamics strip: AutoGate, Compressor, Expander, Limiter, each with an Enabled switch.
- **Enhance Speech (Essential Sound)**: On-device AI that rebuilds a dialogue recording to sound like a close studio mic (removes noise, room and muddiness). Runs in the background with a progress bar; a Mix Amount slider blends enhanced and original.
- **Essential Sound Clarity (Dynamics, EQ presets, Vocal Enhancer)**: Dialogue > Clarity: Dynamics (Natural -> Focused compression/leveling), an EQ preset + Amount, and Vocal Enhancer (High Tone / Low Tone). The EQ presets are stored as 15-value arrays that match Graphic Equalizer (10 Bands) params exactly (Accuracy, Gain, Range, Reset, EQ Band 1-10, Is Processing), so they can be reproduced by script. Installed EQ presets: Background Voice, Intercom, Locked in the Trunk, Old Radio, On the Telephone, Outside of the Room, Podcast Voice, Subtle Boost (High Tone), Subtle Boost (Low Tone), That Public Radio Sound, Vocal Presence.
- **Essential Sound Creative Reverb**: Creative > Reverb adds a preset room with Amount and Clip Volume sliders. Presets are 13-value arrays that match Studio Reverb params in order (Low Frequency Cut, High Frequency Cut, Room Size, Width, Diffusion, Damping, Decay, Early Reflections, Early Reflections Delay, Dry Output Level, Wet Output Level, Is Processing, Number of Channels). Dialogue presets: Auditorium, Church, Large Reflective Room, Outside the Club, Small Dry Room, Thicken Voice, Warm Room, Warm Voice. SFX: Heavy Reverb, Light Reverb, Outside Reverb, Room Reverb. Ambience: Large Room Ambience, Outside Ambience, Room Ambience, Wind Effect.
- **Essential Sound Repair (Reduce Noise, Rumble, DeHum, DeEss, Reduce Reverb)**: Dialogue > Repair: five checkbox+slider (0-10) fixes. The installed (Config) file shows what each slider drives, which gives scriptable equivalents: Reduce Noise -> DeNoise 'Amount' 0..1; Reduce Reverb -> DeReverb 'Amount' 0..1 with 'Auto Gain' on; Reduce Rumble -> an FFT Filter high-pass curve (266 values); DeEss -> DeEsser params interpolated from 'Threshold' 0.875, 'Center Frequency' 0.191, 'Bandwidth' 0.257 (slider 0) to 0.5 / 0.261 / 0.527 (slider 10).
- **Essential Sound panel**: Task-based audio panel: tag each clip Dialogue, Music, SFX or Ambience and get type-specific one-slider tools. Sections per type (from the installed PremiereAdjustmentsModes.json): Dialogue = Enhance Speech, Loudness, Repair, Clarity, Creative (Reverb), Clip Volume; Music = Loudness, Duration (Remix), Ducking, Clip Volume; SFX = Loudness, Creative, Pan, Clip Volume; Ambience = Loudness, Creative, Stereo Width, Ducking, Clip Volume.
- **Essential Sound presets**: Factory one-click presets per audio type. Installed list (2026-10-03): Dialogue - Background Walla Walla, Balanced High Tone Voice, Balanced Low Tone Voice, Clean Up Noisy Dialogue, From Outside a Building, From the Radio, From the Telephone, From the Television, In a Large Room, In a Small Room, Make Close Up, Make Distant, Make Medium Shot, Over the Intercom, Podcast Voice. Music - Balanced Background Music, Duck against everything, Hard Vocal Ducking, Smooth Vocal Ducking, Remix to 30/60/90 Seconds. SFX - Explosion, From Outside, From the Left, From the Right, In a Large Room, Make Close Up, Make Distant, Make Medium Shot. Ambience - Focused, From Outside, Room Tone, Wide and Deep.
- **Export Loudness Normalization**: Export Settings / Media Encoder > Effects > Loudness Normalization: normalizes the rendered file to ATSC A/85, EBU R128 or ITU BS.1770-3 (only ITU allows a custom target), with optional true-peak limiter (look-ahead, release) and an XML loudness report.
- **Fill Left with Right**: Copies one channel into the other (Fill Left with Right / Fill Right with Left), for a mic recorded on one channel only.
- **Gate**: New (2026) AE noise gate: Threshold (dB) -60..0 (default -60), Attack (ms) 0..1000 (10), Hold (ms) 0..2500 (40), Release (ms) 5..4000 (220).
- **Generative Extend (audio)**: Firefly-powered extension of a clip's head or tail; for audio it can add up to 10 s of continuing ambience or room tone (video up to 2 s).
- **Generative Media Tool (sound effects)**: Generate sound effects (and video) from a text prompt inside the timeline, optionally guiding timing and intensity with your voice; results are added as editable clips.
- **Graphic Equalizer (10 Bands)**: Fixed-frequency 10-band (octave) EQ; the engine behind Essential Sound Clarity EQ presets, so its presets can be copied as numbers. 20- and 30-band versions also exist.
- **Hard Limiter**: Brickwall limiter with input boost and an optional true-peak mode; the last effect before export.
- **High-Low Pass**: Single high-pass or low-pass filter with Cutoff Frequency 1..20000 Hz (default 2000) and Dry/Wet Out.
- **Highpass**: Simple high-pass filter: removes everything below the Cutoff.
- **Loudness Auto-Match**: Essential Sound > Loudness > Auto-Match sets each clip to a per-type integrated loudness so cuts do not jump in level. Installed default targets (from (Config).essentialsound): Dialogue -23 LUFS, Music -25 LUFS, SFX -21 LUFS, Ambience -30 LUFS, untagged -24 LUFS (allowed range -42..-12 LUFS). The measured clip LUFS is shown under the button.
- **Loudness Meter (and Loudness Radar)**: TC Electronic ITU BS.1770 meter effect: Input (true-peak aware), Momentary (400 ms), Short-Term (3 s) and Integrated loudness, with presets for EBU R128, ATSC A/85 and online targets (YouTube, Spotify, Apple Podcasts, Netflix). Measures only, never changes audio. 'Loudness Radar' is the older TC radar-style meter, still installed.
- **Mastering**: All-in-one finisher: 3-band EQ, reverb, exciter (Retro/Tape/Tube), stereo widener, Loudness Maximizer, output gain.
- **Modify Audio Channels (clip channel mapping)**: Project-item audio channel mapping (Clip > Modify > Audio Channels): sets the clip channel format (Mono, Stereo, 5.1, Multichannel) and which source channel feeds each clip channel. The scriptable way to fix one-sided lav audio, pick the clean channel of a dual-mono recording, or turn a mono file into a stereo clip so stereo-only effects can be added.
- **Multiband Compressor**: Four-band compressor with crossover, per-band threshold/ratio/attack/release/gain and an output limiter (LMAX); a mastering tool.
- **Parametric EQ**: Three-band peaking EQ (Band 1/2/3 Enabled, Frequency 1..21609 Hz, Bandwidth 0.1..99, Boost/Cut -100..100) with a response graph.
- **Parametric Equalizer**: Five peaking bands plus low/high shelves and high-/low-pass filters with full frequency, Q and gain control.
- **Pitch Shifter**: Real-time pitch change (semitones, cents or ratio 0.5-2.0).
- **Premiere audio effect parameter scale (scripts)**: Through ExtendScript/QE every Premiere audio-effect parameter reads and writes as a normalized 0..1 number, not the dB/ms/% the UI shows. Linear params map as (ui - min) / (max - min). The intrinsic clip Volume 'Level' is the exception: linear amplitude where 0 dB = 10^(-15/20).
- **Premiere audio scripting surface**: What audio work in Premiere 26 can be automated from scripts, and what is UI-only. Scriptable: clip Volume Level (gain, fades, ducking keyframes), adding clip audio effects by name and setting their params, track mute, clip audio channel mapping (Modify > Audio Channels) on project items, export with a loudness-normalizing .epr. UI-only: everything in the Essential Sound panel (tagging, presets, Auto-Match, Auto Ducking, Enhance Speech, Repair/Clarity sliders, Remix), Audio Gain/Normalize dialog, Audio Track Mixer track effects, Loudness Meter readings, Separate Crosstalk, bleep/mute.
- **Remix tool and Essential Sound Duration**: AI retimes a music clip to a target duration by cutting and crossfading the middle at beat-compatible points, keeping the intro and ending. Usually within 1 s of the target (always within 5 s).
- **Reverb (AE)**: Simple algorithmic reverb: Reverb Time (ms) 0..5000 (100), Diffusion 0..100 (75), Decay 0..100 (25), Brightness 0..100 (10), Dry Out 0..400 (90), Wet Out 0..400 (10).
- **Separate Crosstalk**: Cloud AI that splits overlapping speakers in one clip into one track per speaker plus a background track; the original is disabled.
- **Single-band Compressor**: Classic compressor (threshold, ratio, attack, release, output gain); Adobe recommends it for voiceovers so the voice sits over music.
- **Studio Reverb**: Algorithmic (non-convolution, light CPU) reverb; Essential Sound Creative Reverb presets are Studio Reverb settings.
- **Tone**: Synthesizes up to five tones (Frequency 1-5 Hz, Level 0..100 default 20, Waveform options 1-5) on any layer, even a solid; original audio is ignored.
- **VO cleanup and mastering chain (recipe)**: Order of operations for documentary/explainer narration and reels, with the scriptable choice at each step.
- **Vocal Enhancer**: One-control voice-over polish: Male/Female modes cut sibilance, plosives and rumble and add radio-style compression; Music mode makes a bed sit under VO.
- **De-esser (AE beta)** *(beta)*: Sibilance reducer being tested in After Effects (beta): Threshold, Frequency (default 7000 Hz), Bandwidth (3000 Hz), Attack (1 ms), Release (50 ms), Sibilance Only.
- **Generate Music (beta)** *(beta)*: Firefly Music model generates instrumental background music for a selected timeline range from a prompt and tempo, with variations and optional looping; 48 kHz clips, no vocals.

### av_sync

- **Merge Clips (dual-system sound)**: Joins a camera clip and separately recorded audio into one merged clip in the Project panel, aligned by In points, Out points, Timecode or numbered Clip Markers, optionally dropping the camera's own audio.
- **Multi-camera source sequence: syncing the angles**: Premiere syncs the angles while building a multi-camera source sequence: by In Points, Out Points, Timecode (with sound and aux timecode variants), Clip Marker, or Audio (a chosen audio track).
- **Scripted sync fallback: offset by audio cross-correlation**: When Premiere's Merge Clips / Synchronize cannot be scripted, find each clip's offset outside the app: decode both audio tracks to mono PCM with ffmpeg, cross-correlate them (numpy FFT), take the lag of the peak as the offset, then place the second clip at that offset in the spec or timeline. Works for dual-system sound and for lining up multicam angles to a reference.
- **Synchronize clips in the timeline (Clip > Synchronize)**: Aligns selected clips on different tracks to each other by Clip Start, Clip End, Timecode, Clip Marker or an audio track chosen as the reference: the timeline way to line up a second camera or a recorder by waveform.

### camera_moves

- **Camera depth of field and rack focus**: A real rack focus in After Effects: 3D layers at different distances, a camera with Depth of Field on, and Focus Distance keyframed (or expression-linked) from the near subject to the far one; Aperture and Blur Level set how shallow it is.
- **Camera rigs: one-node vs two-node and a null rig for orbit, dolly and truck**: How to move an After Effects camera cleanly: a two-node camera aims at its Point of Interest, a one-node camera is aimed by rotation; parenting the camera to a 3D null turns orbit (rotate the null), dolly (move along the camera's Z) and truck/pedestal (move X/Y) into one keyframed property each.
- **Camera wiggle on the camera, not the layers**: Handheld or drift shake applied to the camera (through a parent null) so every 3D layer moves together with correct parallax, instead of wiggling each layer on its own.
- **Ken Burns move (stills pan and zoom)**: The documentary pan-and-zoom on a photo in either app: a slow scale change plus a pan, eased at both ends, kept inside the photo's real resolution.
- **Photo parallax with 3D layers and a camera**: The full-3D version of photo parallax: cut-out planes placed at different Z depths in front of one camera, each scaled up to cancel the perspective shrink, then a slow camera dolly or truck. Unlike the 2D two-plane recipe, perspective and occlusion are computed by AE.

### color_finishing

- **ASC CDL interchange**: ASC Color Decision List = per-channel Slope, Offset, Power plus Saturation, the industry exchange format for primary grades. Premiere has a numeric ASC CDL effect; AE applies .cc/.cdl files via OCIO CDL Transform.
- **After Effects colour management (Adobe/ICC engine)**: Project Settings > Color: Color Engine 'Adobe color managed' (default) uses ICC profiles: choose a Working Space (e.g. HDTV Rec. 709), Bit Depth (8/16/32 bpc), Linearize Working Space, Blend Colors Using 1.0 Gamma, Compensate for Scene-referred Profiles. Footage converts from its profile into the working space; output modules convert to an output profile.
- **Apply Color LUT (After Effects)**: AE effect (Utility > Apply Color LUT, match 'ADBE Apply Color LUT2') that applies a LUT file: .cube (incl. overrange IRIDAS), .3dl (incl. float/3DMESH, SCRATCH), cineSpace .csp and IRIDAS .look.
- **Bit depth (8/16/32 bpc) and linear blending**: AE project colour depth: 8 bpc (fast, bands), 16 bpc (smooth gradients), 32 bpc float (values above 1.0 for HDR, overbright glows, linear light). Linear blending makes blend modes, motion blur and opacity behave like light.
- **Black-and-white and tinted archival looks**: Monochrome or tinted treatment for archive sections, flashbacks and memorial segments: desaturate, rebuild contrast (B&W stocks were contrasty), optional sepia/duotone tint, grain.
- **Broadcast-safe / legal levels finishing**: Final safety stage for broadcast (and clean web) masters: keep luma within legal Rec.709 range (0-100 IRE = 16-235 8-bit / 64-940 10-bit) and chroma within the vectorscope's 75% targets. Tools: Video Limiter (Premiere and AE) and Broadcast Colors.
- **Bundled film-emulation LUTs and film shaders**: This install ships Adobe-made film emulations in Lumetri/LUTs/Creative: negative + print combinations such as 'Fuji ETERNA 250D Kodak 2395 (by Adobe)', 'Kodak 5218 Kodak 2383 (by Adobe)', 'Fuji F125 Kodak 2393 (by Adobe)' and Monochrome variants, plus legacy SpeedLooks (.look) and Lumetri/Film shaders (Technicolor 2/3-strip, BleachBypass .irtp) that the dropdowns do not expose.
- **Cinematic letterbox (2.39:1 bars)**: Black bars that crop a 16:9 frame to scope (2.39:1) for trailer/cinematic sections: on 1920x1080 the visible height is 803 px, i.e. 138 px (12.8%) bars top and bottom.
- **Clip colour override and Input LUT (Modify > Color)**: Per-clip colour interpretation: use the media's detected colour space, override it with another (camera log/gamut pairs such as ARRI LogC3/Wide Gamut3, Rec.709, Rec.2100 HLG/PQ...), apply an Input LUT, or tick Preserve RGB (no input-to-working conversion, for manual LUT grading). Adobe's Override Media Color Space list includes ACEScct, Apple Log, ARRI LogC3/LogC4, Canon Log/Log2/Log3, DJI D-Log, Fuji F-Log, Leica L-Log, Panasonic V-Log, RED Log3G10, Sony S-Log/S-Log2/S-Log3, P3-D65 HLG/PQ, Rec.601/709/2020/2100 and sRGB.
- **Colour conversion effects: Color Space Transform, SDR Conform, Cineon Converter**: Effect-level conversions for when interpretation can't be changed (nests, graphics, mixed sources): Color Space Transform (match AE.ADBE Color Space Transform: source 'SCS' and target 'TCS' colour-space popups 0..31, 'Tone Map Method' 0..3, 'Gain', 'Desaturation', 'Knee', 'GC Methods' 0..2 gamut compression), SDR Conform (HDR to SDR: Brightness 1..100 default 10, Contrast, Soft Knee) and Cineon Converter (log film scans).
- **Custom LUT folders (Creative / Technical)**: Premiere lists .cube files placed in the Common LUTs folders: LUTs\Creative appear in Lumetri Creative > Look, LUTs\Technical in Basic Correction > Input LUT, and LUTs\Input in the clip-level Modify > Color > Input LUT and Lumetri Settings > Source Clip > Input LUT (Premiere 24.0+). The per-user location also holds an Output folder. Folders are scanned at launch.
- **Export a grade as a LUT (.cube / .look)**: The Lumetri Color panel menu can export the current Lumetri instance's colour transform as a .cube file (Adobe's current page documents only Export .cube; Export .look appeared in older versions, check the menu), turning a hand-made curves/wheels/HSL grade into a reusable LUT.
- **Film grain (Add Grain, Match Grain, Noise, grain overlays)**: Grain hides compression banding, unifies mixed sources and sells a film look. AE: Add Grain (film-stock presets, detailed Tweaking/Color/Application controls), Match Grain (copies grain from a source layer), Remove Grain. Premiere 26 has no Add Grain: use Noise at low amounts, or a scanned grain clip on an upper track in Overlay/Soft Light.
- **Film-emulation look recipe**: A film look is built from: correction to neutral, a print-emulation LUT at partial intensity (contrast curve and colour crossover), slightly lifted/matte blacks, warm highlight roll-off, grain, and gentle vignette; optional letterbox for cinema framing.
- **Finishing: sharpening and noise reduction**: Final-stage detail control: light sharpening after scaling for web delivery (Lumetri Creative Sharpen, Unsharp Mask) and noise reduction before grading heavy noise (AE Remove Grain; Premiere's only harvested denoiser is VR De-Noise (match 'AE.Mettle SkyBox Denoise', built for 360 footage; on flat footage untested); Lumetri HSL Denoise only smooths the key).
- **Footage interpretation, output-module colour and display simulation (AE)**: Per-footage colour (Interpret Footage > Color: assign/override the media colour space, or Preserve RGB to skip conversion), per-output colour (Output Module Settings > Color: output profile/colour space), and viewer tools (View > Use Display Color Management; View > Simulate Output to preview an output space).
- **Full vs legal range fix (grey blacks / crushed blacks)**: Fix range mismatches common in screen recordings, OBS captures, forwarded phone clips and archive transfers: legal-range video read as full looks washed (grey blacks, dull whites); full-range video read as legal clips shadows and highlights.
- **HDR in After Effects (32 bpc, compander, highlight compression, Rec.2100 output)**: AE handles HDR in 32-bpc projects with an HDR working space; tools: HDR Compander (compress to 0-1 for 8/16-bpc effects and expand back), HDR Highlight Compression (rolls off values above 1.0), Rec.2100 PQ/HLG output colour space in the Output Module. AE reads CICP colour metadata (PNG, MOV, ICC profiles) on import and writes it on export (e.g. PNG Sequence with Output Color Space Rec.2100 PQ), plus optional HDR10 metadata in PNG Format Options (Adobe page, Mar 2026; the version that introduced it is not stated, so test on this 26.0 install).
- **HDR workflow and export in Premiere**: HDR in Premiere = an HDR working/output colour space (Rec.2100 HLG or PQ via Direct HDR (HLG) / Direct PQ (HDR) or an HDR output space), HDR-aware Lumetri (HDR checkbox), HDR scopes (0-10000 nits scale) and an HDR-capable export (10-bit HEVC/H.265 Main10 or ProRes) with HDR metadata.
- **Log / HDR source to SDR Rec.709 (tone-mapping strategy)**: Choosing how flat log, raw or HDR (HLG/PQ phone) clips become normal SDR: (a) Premiere colour management with Input Tone Mapping (default, best), (b) a technical LUT (fast, can clip), (c) per-clip conversion effects (SDR Conform, Color Space Transform), (d) in AE: interpret footage + working space, HDR Highlight Compression.
- **Lumetri Auto Color (Auto button + Intensity)**: Sensei-based one-click correction in Basic Correction: Auto analyses temperature, tint, exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows, whites, blacks and saturation and writes those sliders; the Intensity slider at the top of Basic Correction scales how strongly the auto result is applied.
- **Lumetri Basic Correction: white balance and tone**: First section of Lumetri Color: Input LUT, Auto + Intensity, White Balance eyedropper, Temperature, Tint, Saturation (Color group) and Exposure, Contrast, Highlights, Shadows, Whites, Blacks (Light group). It is the primary correction that makes shots neutral and matched before any look.
- **Lumetri Color (Premiere): effect, panel and scripting map**: Premiere's all-in-one grading effect (Effects > Video Effects > Color Correction > Lumetri Color, match 'AE.ADBE Lumetri', 130 harvested parameters) with six sections: Basic Correction, Creative, Curves, Color Wheels & Match, HSL Secondary, Vignette. The Lumetri Color panel (Window > Lumetri Color, Color workspace) edits the selected clip's instance; each section can be stacked as several Lumetri instances.
- **Lumetri Color Wheels & Match (Comparison View, Apply Match)**: Three wheels (Shadows, Midtones, Highlights) each with a brightness slider for lift/gamma/gain-style balancing. In Premiere the section adds Comparison View and Apply Match, a Sensei colour match that copies light and colour from a reference frame onto the current clip as editable wheel/slider values, with Face Detection to prioritise skin.
- **Lumetri Color in After Effects (flat parameter list)**: The same Lumetri engine as an AE effect (match 'ADBE Lumetri', category Color Correction) plus a Lumetri Color panel and Lumetri Scopes in the Color workspace. In AE the parameters are a flat list (no groups) with repeated display names, which changes how scripts address them.
- **Lumetri Creative section: Looks, Faded Film, Vibrance, split tint**: Creative section: Look (creative LUT dropdown + Browse), Intensity of the look, then Adjustments: Faded Film, Sharpen, Vibrance, Saturation, Shadow Tint and Highlight Tint wheels, Tint Balance.
- **Lumetri Curves: RGB curves and hue/saturation curves**: Curves section: RGB Curves (master luma curve plus red, green, blue) and five Hue Saturation Curves: Hue vs Sat, Hue vs Hue, Hue vs Luma, Luma vs Sat, Sat vs Sat, each with an eyedropper selector.
- **Lumetri HDR mode (High Dynamic Range checkbox)**: Lumetri's HDR switch enables HDR-specific controls for grading in HDR working spaces: HDR White (100..1000 nits reference in Basic and in Color Wheels), HDR Specular (-150..150, controls specular highlights) and HDR Range (100..10000) for the curves.
- **Lumetri HSL Secondary: key one colour and correct it**: Isolates a colour range by Hue/Saturation/Lightness (Key: Set color, Add color, Remove color eyedroppers and H/S/L range sliders), cleans the matte (Refine: Denoise, Blur) and corrects only that range (Correction: wheel, Temperature, Tint, Contrast, Sharpen, Saturation). Show Mask / Invert Mask help judge the key.
- **Lumetri Scopes (Premiere)**: Built-in, resizable video scopes: Vectorscope YUV and Vectorscope HLS, Histogram, Parade (RGB, YUV, RGB-White, YUV-White) and Waveform (RGB, Luma, YC, YC no Chroma), with a bit-depth/scale menu (8-bit, 10-bit, float, or HDR with a 0-10000 nits scale) and a colour-space setting.
- **Lumetri Scopes and Info panel in After Effects**: AE's Lumetri Scopes panel offers vectorscopes, histograms, parade and waveform designed for broadcast work, measuring in Rec. 601, Rec. 709 and Rec. 2020; the Info panel reads pixel values under the cursor.
- **Lumetri Vignette**: Centred edge darkening/brightening built into Lumetri: Amount (negative darkens, positive lightens), Midpoint (how far in it reaches), Roundness (rectangular to round), Feather (edge softness).
- **Lumetri presets (.prfpset) and AE grade presets (.ffx)**: Saved effect settings. Premiere ships Lumetri Presets (Effects panel > Presets > Lumetri Presets: Cinematic, Filmstocks, Monochrome, SpeedLooks (Cameras/..., Universal), Technical (Legal/Full range 8/10/12-bit)) and lets you save any Lumetri instance (Effect Controls > right-click > Save Preset). AE saves effect stacks as animation presets (.ffx).
- **OCIO / ACES colour management in After Effects**: Color Engine 'OCIO color managed' runs OpenColorIO transforms with a config file, a Working Color Space and a Display Color Space/view, enabling ACES workflows. This install (AE 26.0) bundles configs 'ACES 1.2', 'ACES 1.3 CG v1.0' and 'ACES 1.3 Studio v1.0' (Support Files/OpenColorIO-Configs); AE 26.5 upgraded to OCIO 2.5.1 with ACES 2.0 CG v4.0 / Studio v4.0 configs (not on this machine).
- **OCIO transform effects (CDL, Color Space, Display, File, Look)**: AE colour-correction effects that run OpenColorIO per layer: OCIO File Transform (applies a LUT or CDL file), OCIO Color Space Transform (input/output space menus), OCIO Display Transform (input space, display device, view transform, inverse), OCIO Look Transform (config looks), OCIO CDL Transform (.ccc/.cc/.cdl file with a CCCID list). Adobe: they do NOT require the project to use the OCIO engine.
- **Premiere colour management (project / sequence / clip)**: Since the Premiere 25 colour-management overhaul (beta Sept 2024, released 2025) every clip is converted from its media colour space into the sequence's working colour space, effects run there, and the sequence is converted to an output colour space for monitoring and export. Log and raw media are auto-detected and tone-mapped; three Wide Gamut presets give a wide working space (Adobe's pages do not name it; reports of an ACES-based space are unverified). Disable Color Management (or per clip Preserve RGB) gives the old manual LUT workflow.
- **Premiere colour scripting surface (ExtendScript + UXP)**: What a script can actually reach for colour in Premiere 26: effect parameters (Lumetri and colour effects), sequence working colour space, clip colour space read/override, input LUT IDs, linear compositing and max bit depth. ExtendScript is not affected by the CEP-to-UXP transition (Adobe, Sept 2026).
- **Project colour settings: auto-detect log/raw, colour-space-aware effects**: Project-level switches: 'Color Manage Auto Detected Log and Raw Media' (renamed from Auto Detect Log Video Color Space; auto-converts recognised log/raw into the working space), 'Enable Color Space Aware Effects' (effects compute correctly in any working space; when off, every effect assumes Rec.709 as in older versions; it is switched off for projects imported from older versions) and 'Default graphics white' (100 nits for SDR programs; sequences can override). Colour-space-aware effects (Adobe list): Lumetri, Black and White, Tint, Extract, Color Pass, Color Replace, Track Matte Key, Cross Dissolve, Film Dissolve, Dip to Black.
- **Recipe: cinematic trailer grade**: High-impact trailer look: deep but not crushed blacks, controlled highlights, complementary split tone, reduced overall saturation with protected skin, vignette, grain, optional 2.39:1 letterbox and flash frames.
- **Recipe: documentary archival footage**: Make archive (tape, film scans, old news, low-res web clips) sit well next to modern interviews: fix range and exposure, remove casts, decide between 'clean and seamless' or a deliberate archive look (B&W/sepia/period saturation), unify with grain, keep original aspect ratio.
- **Recipe: log or HLG phone/drone footage to a Rec.709 edit**: Normalising footage that arrives flat (log) or too bright/washed (HLG/HDR) into an SDR Rec.709 sequence before creative grading.
- **Recipe: news and political explainer (clean, honest, legal)**: Natural, consistent, credible look: neutral white balance, faithful skin, moderate contrast, no stylised looks on evidence footage, legal levels for broadcast.
- **Recipe: social reel punch (mobile-first)**: Phone-screen-friendly grade for Instagram/YouTube Shorts: clean whites, open shadows, extra vibrance rather than saturation, light sharpening, no heavy fade, grain or vignette that compression will smear.
- **Render at Maximum Bit Depth, Max Render Quality, Composite in Linear Color**: Sequence/export quality switches: Maximum Bit Depth renders effects in 32-bit float (fewer banding artefacts in skies, gradients, vignettes and heavy grades, required for 10-bit HDR export); Maximum Render Quality improves scaling; Composite in Linear Color changes how opacity/blend modes mix.
- **Scripted LUT selection (index route)**: How an automated build chooses a LUT: Lumetri 'Look' and 'Input LUT' are integer popups over the installed LUT list, so a script can only pick by index, and indices depend on what is installed.
- **Sequence colour presets: Direct Rec.709, Direct HDR, Wide Gamut**: Sequence 'Color setup' presets (Adobe, Jan 2026): Direct 709 (SDR) (default; Adobe also labels it Direct Rec. 709 (SDR) / Broadcast 709 (SDR): Rec.709 working and output, SDR passes through, log/raw tone-mapped and gamut-compressed on input); Direct HDR (HLG) and Direct PQ (HDR) (Rec.2020 HLG / PQ working and output); Wide Gamut (Tone Mapped) (keeps maximum log/raw/HDR data, output tone mapping, can slightly darken already-graded SDR media); Wide Gamut (Minimal Tone Mapping) (tone-maps only the upper highlights, better for mixed graded SDR); Wide Gamut (No Tone Mapping) (straight conversion, clips above Output Peak Luminance); Disable Color Management (manual LUT workflow). Advanced controls: Working/Output Color Space, input and output Tone Mapping, Gamut Compression, Graphics White.
- **Shot matching and flicker tools**: Making consecutive shots match: Premiere Color Match (Sensei, UI), manual matching with scopes; AE Auto Color / Auto Levels / Auto Contrast (automatic per-frame balancing), Color Stabilizer (match_name 'ADBE Deflicker': removes brightness/colour flicker against a reference frame).
- **Teal-and-orange / split-tone with scriptable effects**: Complementary split tone: cool (teal) shadows, warm (orange) highlights and skin. Lumetri's wheels and split-tone tints do it in the UI but are not scriptable; settable alternatives are AE Color Balance and Premiere ASC CDL.
- **Technical vs creative LUTs (order of operations)**: A technical LUT converts an encoding to a display standard (camera log/gamut to Rec.709, legal/full range conversions) and goes FIRST; a creative LUT is a look applied to already-normal Rec.709 images and goes AFTER correction, usually at reduced intensity.
- **Tone mapping and gamut compression options (Premiere colour management)**: How Premiere squeezes log/raw/HDR into a smaller range: Tone Mapping algorithms Hue Preservation (default; Highlight Saturation 0.5 and Knee), By Channel (smooth, slightly darker, desaturates bright saturated highlights) and Max RGB (smooth, slightly darker, keeps more highlight saturation); Gamut Compression Luminance Preserving (default) or Saturation Preserving. Each can run on input (per clip, before effects) or on output (whole sequence, after effects).
- **Where to put the grade: per clip, adjustment layer, master clip**: Three placements: per-clip effects (shot correction), an adjustment layer over a range (shared look: LUT, grain, vignette, limiter), and Premiere master-clip effects (applied in the Source Monitor/Project panel to every instance of a source clip).
- **Premiere Color Mode (beta) and the Lumetri panel retirement (27.0)** *(beta)*: A dedicated colour environment shown in the Premiere 26.2 public beta (April 2026) and slated for Premiere 27.0: Color Monitor, Clip Grid, Color Controls with Adjustments, Styles (film emulations, presets) and Modules, Color Shift (Sat/Hue/Lum Shift on auto-detected hues), clip/group/sequence grading, new scopes and a Color Management panel; runs in 32-bit float. In 27.0 the Lumetri panel is retired; the Lumetri Color effect remains and existing Lumetri grades open unchanged.

### compositing

- **Compositing finish order: key, despill, edge, grade-match**: The order that makes a green-screen composite sit in its plate: pull the key, clean the matte, suppress spill, then match black level, white level, colour cast, softness and grain to the background, and only then add light wrap. Most bad composites skip the match, not the key.
- **Light wrap (built from layers)**: After Effects has no light-wrap effect: it is built by letting a blurred copy of the background bleed a few pixels into the foreground's edge, so the subject picks up the plate's light. Built from a blurred background copy, two Set Matte passes and a Screen blend.

### credits

- **Rolling credits and crawls in Premiere**: Premiere 2026 makes credit rolls in the Properties panel: tick Roll on a graphic, set Preroll, Postroll, Ease in and Ease out, and choose whether the text starts and ends off screen. Only vertical rolls exist: the Legacy Titler (which had crawls) was removed in Premiere 23.0, so horizontal crawls need keyframed Position or After Effects.
- **Scrolling credits and crawls in After Effects**: End rolls and horizontal crawls built in After Effects: a box-text (or several text) layer moved by linear Position keys or a Position expression at a whole number of pixels per frame, which keeps the motion smooth.

### delivery

- **Delivery spec: YouTube upload (16:9, HD and 4K)**: What to export for YouTube, per YouTube's own recommended upload settings: MP4, H.264 High profile, progressive, closed GOP of half the frame rate, 4:2:0, BT.709, AAC-LC 48 kHz; SDR bitrates 8 Mbps (1080p 24/25/30), 12 Mbps (1080p 48/50/60), 35-45 Mbps (2160p 24/25/30), 53-68 Mbps (2160p 48/50/60); stereo audio 384 kbps.
- **Delivery spec: alpha overlays (ProRes 4444, PNG sequence)**: How to deliver graphics with transparency (lower thirds, tickers, logos, keyed elements) for compositing in another edit: ProRes 4444 or 4444 XQ .mov (the only ProRes flavours that carry alpha) or a PNG sequence (RGB + alpha, lossless). H.264 and HEVC .mp4 deliveries have no usable alpha.
- **Delivery spec: broadcast masters (ProRes 422 HQ, XDCAM HD422 MXF OP1a)**: The two common HD broadcast file masters: Apple ProRes 422 HQ in .mov (1920x1080 target 184 Mb/s at 25p, 220 Mb/s at 29.97p) and XDCAM HD422 (MPEG-2 4:2:2P@HL, 50 Mb/s CBR) in MXF OP1a, with 24-bit 48 kHz PCM audio. The broadcaster's spec sheet always wins: field order, track layout, loudness, slate and timecode come from it.
- **Delivery spec: mezzanine and archive masters**: What to keep after delivery: a high-quality mezzanine (ProRes 422 HQ, or 4444 / 4444 XQ for graphics-heavy or keyed work) from which every platform file can be re-made, plus the project, its collected media and the audio stems. Apple's 1920x1080 targets at 29.97: 422 HQ 220, 4444 330, 4444 XQ 495 Mb/s (25p: 184, 275, 413).
- **Delivery spec: vertical Reels, Shorts and TikTok (9:16)**: What to export for vertical platforms: 9:16, progressive, fixed frame rate, square pixels, H.264 MP4 (or MOV), stereo AAC. Meta's Reels ads guide recommends 1440x2560, up to 15 min and 4 GB, AAC 128 kbps or more, and keeping 14% top, 35% bottom and 6% each side free of text and logos. TikTok's ad pages ask for 540x960 or larger; YouTube has no published Shorts encode or safe-zone spec.
- **Export encoding choices: VBR 2-pass vs CBR, maximum depth and quality, field order**: The export switches that matter beyond the preset: bitrate mode (CBR faster, VBR better quality per size, 2-pass more efficient than 1-pass), Render at Maximum Depth (renders at the format's highest bit depth, usually 32-bit float), Use Maximum Render Quality (helps when scaling to a different frame size), and Field Order (progressive vs interlaced fields).

### delivery_color

- **Colour tags on export: Rec.709 colr/nclc and the QuickTime gamma shift**: Why an H.264 export can look washed out or brighter on a Mac (QuickTime, Safari, some phone apps) but fine in Premiere: the file is tagged BT.709 (QuickTime 'colr' atom 'nclc' 1-1-1 / H.264 VUI 1-1-1) and macOS displays it at about gamma 1.96, Windows about 2.2, while Premiere's viewer works at broadcast 2.4. Adobe's fix is awareness and a viewer setting, not a different export.

### documents

- **Document treatments (screenshots, paper, redaction)**: How to show documents, posts and articles on screen so they read and stay honest: capture sharp, frame on a neutral 'desk', push in on the line the VO quotes, mark it (highlighter, underline, circle), and redact in the pixels, not with an overlay, when the information must not survive.

### editing_grammar

- **Closing gaps (Ripple Delete, Close Gap)**: Removing empty space left by deletes and lifts: Ripple Delete on a gap or a clip closes it; Sequence > Close Gap closes every gap on the targeted tracks.
- **Edit grammar on After Effects layers (trim, slip, ripple by script)**: After Effects has no edit tools beyond layer trims, so edit grammar is three numbers per layer: startTime (where the source's first frame sits), inPoint and outPoint. Slip = move startTime only; ripple = shift the later layers; split audio = a separate audio layer with its own in point.
- **J-cut and L-cut (split edits)**: A split edit: the sound of the next shot starts before its picture (J-cut) or the sound of the outgoing shot carries on under the next picture (L-cut). The everyday grammar of interviews and documentaries: the cut feels smooth because sound and picture do not change on the same frame.
- **Lift and Extract (remove a range)**: Removing a marked range from the sequence: Lift (;) leaves a gap, Extract (') closes it by pulling the later clips back (a ripple delete of the range).
- **Match frame and reverse match frame**: Match Frame (F) opens the timeline shot's source clip at the same frame in the Source Monitor; Reverse Match Frame (Shift+R) finds where the Source Monitor's frame is used in the sequence.
- **Replace edit (Replace With Clip)**: Swapping the media of a shot already in the timeline while keeping its position, length and effects: the replace edit, used to try alternative takes or b-roll without rebuilding effects and keyframes.
- **Three-point and four-point edits (insert and overwrite)**: Placing a shot by marks: set three of source In, source Out, sequence In and sequence Out and the fourth follows; Insert (,) pushes later clips along, Overwrite (.) replaces what is there. With all four set, Premiere asks how to fit the clip (one choice changes its speed: fit to fill).
- **Trims: ripple, roll, slip and slide**: The four trims: Ripple Edit (B) changes one clip's length and moves everything after it; Rolling Edit (N) moves the cut between two clips without changing the sequence length; Slip (Y) changes which part of a clip is used without moving it; Slide (U) moves a clip along the track and trims its neighbours to keep the length.

### expressions

- **Index-based stagger and seeded randomness**: Duplicate a layer and let each copy offset itself by its layer index (stagger) or pick a stable random value (seedRandom with timeless): cascades, scattered particles and varied-but-repeatable motion without keyframing each copy.
- **Live lines between layers: createPath() and points()**: A shape path that is rebuilt every frame from layer positions: a connector between two nulls, a callout leader from a label to a point on a map, or a polyline through several points, with Trim Paths still working on it.
- **Marker-driven animation (expressions keyed to markers)**: Fire an animation at every comp or layer marker: the expression finds the latest marker at or before the current time and animates from it, so moving a marker (a beat, a VO cue) moves the animation with no keyframes.
- **Space transforms: toComp, fromComp, toWorld, fromWorld**: Convert points between a layer's own space, the comp's 2D space and 3D world space: pin a 2D label to a point on a 3D layer, attach things to parented or scaled layers, or measure distances for focus pulls.
- **Text style expressions (sourceText.style)**: Drive font size, fill colour, font and the text itself from expressions (AE 17.0+), per character from 25.0: data-driven captions, a word coloured by a checkbox, size that adapts to a slider.
- **clamp(), linear() and ease() remaps**: Keep a driven value inside limits (clamp) and remap one range onto another with linear or eased interpolation: the glue of every rig (a slider driving opacity, scale from audio amplitude, a counter that must stop at 100).
- **framesToTime() and timeToFrames()**: Frame-exact maths in expressions: offset by whole frames regardless of comp rate, step animation to frames, or print a frame counter.
- **lookAt() and pointing layers at targets**: Orient a 3D layer toward a target point (lookAt), or rotate a 2D layer toward one with atan2: arrows that point at a moving pin, a camera-facing card, a needle that tracks a value.
- **sampleImage() colour pickup**: Read the average colour of an area of a layer at a given time: tint a graphic to the footage under it, drive a light wrap or glow colour from the plate, or detect brightness for auto-contrast text.

### frame_rates

- **Mixed frame rates and drop-frame timecode (23.976, 29.97, 59.94 next to 25)**: Sources that do not match the sequence rate: keep the sequence at the delivery rate, conform clips on purpose (Interpret Footage for slow motion, frame blending or optical flow for real conversions), and know that 29.97 and 59.94 material counts time in drop-frame timecode.
- **Variable frame rate (VFR) phone and screen recordings**: Phone cameras and screen recorders often write variable frame rate video (the frame interval changes during the clip). Editors expect constant rates, so VFR shows up as audio drifting out of sync, stutter or wrong durations. Premiere flags it ('Variable Frame Rate Detected' in the Properties panel) and Adobe staff advise transcoding to a constant rate before editing.

### free_plugins

- **Adobe CEP Samples - PProPanel (official reference panel)**: Adobe's MIT-licensed sample CEP panel for Premiere Pro whose ExtendScript (PProPanel.jsx) demonstrates most of the Premiere scripting API (import, sequences, markers, export via AME, metadata, QE calls). Motion Bro even ships a copy of PProPanel.jsx inside its extension here.
- **Adobe Exchange / Creative Cloud desktop plugin marketplace**: Adobe's official marketplace (exchange.adobe.com, also inside Creative Cloud desktop) for free and paid UXP, CEP and C++ plug-ins for AE and Premiere (e.g. Motion Bro, WebM).
- **Adobe Stock free Motion Graphics templates**: Adobe Stock includes a set of free motion graphics templates (.mogrt titles, lower thirds, transitions, overlays) that Creative Cloud users can license at no cost and use from Premiere's Essential Graphics / Graphics Templates browser.
- **Bodymovin / Lottie exporter**: Free AE extension (MIT, Airbnb/Hernan Torrisi) that exports AE animations to Lottie JSON for web/iOS/Android playback (lottie-web player).
- **Bolt CEP / Bolt UXP (Hyper Brew, open source)**: MIT-licensed boilerplates for building Adobe panels with Vite + TypeScript + React/Svelte/Vue: Bolt CEP (CEP + ExtendScript, last push Jun 2026) and Bolt UXP (UXP for Photoshop, InDesign, Premiere Pro, with Illustrator and After Effects listed as Beta; last push Sep 2026), with hot reload and packaging.
- **Boris FX Mocha AE (bundled free with After Effects)**: Planar tracker from Boris FX that ships free inside After Effects 2026 as the effect 'Mocha AE' (match name mochaAECC, category 'Boris FX Mocha', harvested plug-in version 12.2.1). Tracks planes through motion and exports corner-pin / transform data and mattes back to AE.
- **CEP-to-UXP transition timeline (affects every CEP panel)**: Adobe's Sept 2026 announcement: CEP will be retired at the end of 2029. Premiere: UXP GA + Hybrid plugins now; stops accepting new CEP Marketplace submissions Dec 2027; CEP disabled by default Dec 2028. After Effects: UXP public beta Nov 2026; stop new CEP submissions and CEP disabled by default Dec 2028. Media Encoder: UXP public beta live now; same Dec 2028 dates. Illustrator: UXP public beta spring 2027; Dec 2028 dates. From Dec 2029 CEP is no longer included in new versions. 'ExtendScripts are not affected by this transition.'
- **Camera-manufacturer technical LUTs (DJI official and others)**: Free official log-to-Rec.709 conversion LUTs (.cube) from camera makers, e.g. DJI's LUT download page listing D-Log / D-Log M to Rec.709 LUTs for Mavic, Air, Mini, Avata, Osmo Pocket/Action and 360 cameras.
- **Duik Angela (RxLaboratory, free/open source)**: The standard free rigging and animation toolkit for After Effects (IK, bones, controllers, constraints, walk/run cycles, automation like wiggle/swing/blink, camera controls, scripting/expression tools). Licence GNU GPL-v3; current version 17.1.21 (released 18 Feb 2026); 17.2.0 alpha in pre-release.
- **Ease and Wizz (aescripts, name your own price)**: ScriptUI panel that applies easing expressions (expo, quint, back, elastic, bounce, etc.) between keyframes, including overshoot/spring/bounce without extra keyframes.
- **ExtendScript Debugger for VS Code (Adobe)**: Free official VS Code extension for running and debugging ExtendScript (.jsx) in After Effects, Premiere Pro and other ExtendScript hosts: breakpoints, conditional breakpoints, watch, call stack, multiple hosts. Current Marketplace version 2.1.0 (8 Aug 2025; previous 2.0.2, Jun 2022).
- **FXAA (Plugin Everything, free)**: Free effect implementing NVIDIA's Fast Approximate Anti-Aliasing to smooth jagged/stair-stepped edges on low-res logos, pixel-art, 3D renders or upscaled stills, cheaper than supersampling.
- **Film Impact effects and transitions (built into Premiere 25.5+)**: Adobe acquired Film Impact; Premiere Pro 25.5 (launched 10 Sept 2025, IBC) ships its 90+ transitions, effects and animations inside Premiere 'all included in your plan', and the separate Film Impact subscription was wound down. In the Premiere 2026 harvest 29 video effects have match names AE.Impact_*_FX (e.g. Light Leaks, Camera Shake, RGB Split, Volumetric Rays, Long Shadow, Logo Cutout, Rounded Crop, Wonder Glow, Echo Glow, Edge Glow, Glint, Bokeh Blur, Focus Blur, Compound Blur, 3D Rotate, Auto Align, Clone, Channel Mix, Wiggle, Grow, Shrink, Spin, Move, Spacer, Stroke, Vignette, Mosaic, Gaussian Blur, Directional Blur).
- **Free Motion Bro Presets bundle**: Motion Bro's free bundle: 8 packages, about 1,400 transition presets, graphics, videos and sound effects for Premiere and After Effects (Premiere and AE options are separate downloads). Licence tiers on the official page: Personal use - Free ($0), Personal use - Donate ($4), Commercial use ($17).
- **Free third-party log-conversion LUT packs**: Free .cube packs that convert many log profiles to Rec.709, e.g. Bounce Color 'True Log Conversion LUTs' (Sony S-Log2/3, Canon C-Log/2/3, Panasonic V-Log, DJI D-Log/D-Log M, Nikon, Fujifilm, RED, ARRI) and Alister Chapman's free S-Log3 LUTs (also D-Log M variants).
- **G'MIC plug-ins for After Effects / Premiere (reduxFX, open source)**: Tobias Fleischer's bridge that exposes the G'MIC image-processing library as native Adobe effect plug-ins (make_plugins.bat generates '1000+' individual Adobe plug-ins per the README: artistic, stylise, denoise, deformations), dual-licensed CeCILL-C / CeCILL v2; also builds separate OpenFX and frei0r versions.
- **Installing and running free ExtendScript / ScriptUI scripts in After Effects**: How free open-source .jsx tools (GitHub ScriptUI panels, rd: scripts, Duik) are installed in AE 2026: dockable panels go in Support Files/Scripts/ScriptUI Panels (appear under the Window menu), plain scripts in Support Files/Scripts (File > Scripts). Bundled here: ScriptUI Panels = Create Nulls From Paths.jsx, VR Comp Editor.jsx (plus Video Copilot's FX Console.jsx); Scripts = Smart Import.jsx, Sort Layers by In Point.jsx, Scale Composition.jsx, Find and Replace Text.jsx, Update Legacy Expressions.jsx, UpgradeLegacyBlurs.jsx and others.
- **Loading unsigned / open-source CEP panels (PlayerDebugMode)**: Unsigned CEP panels (GitHub projects, Pymiere Link dev builds, Adobe's PProPanel sample, your own Bolt CEP builds) only load when CEP debug mode is on for the CSXS version the app uses. AE 2026 and Premiere 2026 here both ship CEPHtmlEngine 12.0.1, i.e. CEP 12 -> registry key CSXS.12. This machine has HKCU\Software\Adobe\CSXS.10, CSXS.11 and CSXS.12 keys, all with PlayerDebugMode unset.
- **Mister Horse Animation Composer 4 (free Starter Pack)**: Free AE panel from Mister Horse: a browser of drag-and-drop animation presets, transitions, titles, social-media elements, graphic components and sound effects, plus free tools Keyframe Wingman (easing), Keyframe Actions, Anchor Point Mover and Transition Shifter. Installed here: CEP extension com.misterhorse.AnimationComposer v4.1.1 and Starter Pack product 3300 (569 .mhitemdata items, 60 .wav, previews as .webm).
- **Mister Horse Animation Composer for Premiere (formerly Premiere Composer, free)**: Free Premiere panel from Mister Horse (renamed from 'Premiere Composer' to 'Animation Composer' on 10 Jun 2026) with transitions, text boxes, text presets, sound effects and (2026) film/VHS/light-leak items. Installed here as CEP com.misterhorse.PremiereComposer v1.6.11 with Starter Pack product 3283: 135 .mogrt, 38 .wav SFX, 28 .mhpctransition, 14 .aegraphic.
- **Mixkit free video templates (Envato)**: Envato's free library of editable templates for Premiere Pro and After Effects (titles, lower thirds, transitions, logo reveals, intros, social/YouTube elements) plus free stock video, music and SFX, each under Mixkit's own per-type free licence (Video Templates Free License).
- **Motion Bro (free panel)**: Free preset-manager extension for AE and Premiere that previews and applies packs of transitions, text animations, graphics and SFX. Installed here: CEP extension MotionBro3 v4.5.0 in C:/Program Files (x86)/Common Files/Adobe/CEP/extensions/MotionBro3 (manifest hosts PPRO 15.0+, AEFT 18.0+; UPIA /list all shows it Enabled for After Effects 26.0 and Premiere Pro 26.0). Packs live in Documents/Motion Bro (here: 'Basic Transitions for After Effects by videolancer' = 137 .aep + 3 .ffx).
- **Motion Tools Pro (Motion Design School)**: AE extension: a customisable command panel with 30+ scripts (Add Null, AutoCrop, Ease Keyframes, Easings Manager, GIF converter, anchor tools, physics widget), an expressions library/editor and up to four panels.
- **Native OpenColorIO (OCIO) effects in After Effects 2026**: After Effects 2026 ships OCIO colour-pipeline effects natively, so no third-party OpenColorIO plug-in is needed: ADBE OCIO Color Space Transform, ADBE OCIO Display Transform, ADBE OCIO FILE Transform, ADBE OCIO Look Transform and ADBE OCIO CDL Transform (all present in the PVE AE harvest).
- **OpenFX (OFX) plug-ins in After Effects / Premiere - not supported**: After Effects and Premiere Pro are not OpenFX hosts: they use Adobe's own effect SDK, and no official OFX bridge exists. OFX plug-ins (Resolve/Nuke/Natron/Vegas builds) cannot be loaded; a vendor must ship an Adobe-format build (e.g. G'MIC ships separate Adobe and OFX builds).
- **OpenTimelineIO + FCP7 XML adapter**: Academy Software Foundation's Apache-2.0 timeline interchange library (OTIO v0.18.1, Nov 2025) with a separate otio-fcp-adapter (Apache-2.0) providing the 'fcp_xml' adapter that reads/writes Final Cut Pro 7 XML -- the format Premiere imports and exports.
- **Premiere UXP plugins API (GA) and official samples**: UXP is Premiere's modern plug-in platform: initial public beta in Premiere (Beta) 25.2 (4 Dec 2024), official release (GA) in Premiere 25.6, UXP Hybrid plugins (UXP + native C++ libraries) officially supported from Premiere 26.2. Official Apache-2.0 samples: AdobeDocs/uxp-premiere-pro-samples. This machine runs Premiere 26.0.2: UXP plugins yes, Hybrid plugins not yet.
- **Premiere native log colour management (free alternative to conversion LUTs)**: Premiere's colour management can convert supported log/raw camera formats straight to SDR/HDR without LUTs (per an Adobe help page seen only as a search snippet: ARRI LogC3/LogC4, Canon Log/Log2/Log3, Leica L-Log, Panasonic V-Log, Sony S-Log; unknown media defaults to Rec.709). The exact 2026 list (e.g. Apple Log, RED, Fujifilm, Nikon, DJI) was not verified.
- **Premiere third-party audio plug-ins (VST/VST3/AU) - Audio Plug-in Manager**: Premiere hosts third-party audio effects in VST 2.4, VST3 and (macOS) AU formats, so free VSTs (loudness meters, EQs, noise removal) can be used as clip or track effects.
- **ReaPlugs VST suite (Cockos, free)**: Free Cockos VST plug-ins from REAPER: ReaEQ (unlimited-band EQ), ReaComp, ReaXcomp (multiband), ReaGate, ReaFIR (FFT EQ/dynamics and noise-profile subtraction), ReaDelay, ReaJS. Latest ReaPlugs VST v2.36 (2 Jan 2016), 32/64-bit Windows.
- **TDR Nova (Tokyo Dawn Labs, free)**: Free parallel dynamic equaliser: four dynamic bands plus HP/LP filters, each band with its own dynamics, usable as static EQ, dynamic EQ, de-esser, multiband or wideband compressor. VST2/VST3/AAX on Windows 64-bit.
- **Types-for-Adobe (TypeScript definitions)**: MIT TypeScript declaration files for the ExtendScript DOMs of After Effects, Premiere, Audition, Illustrator, InDesign, Photoshop and ScriptUI (docsforadobe, last push May 2026).
- **UPIA - Unified Plugin Installer Agent (Adobe command-line installer)**: Adobe's command-line plug-in installer bundled with Creative Cloud desktop (5.5+) that installs/removes extension packages (.zxp; .ccx/.xdx per Adobe help, unverified here); replaces ExManCmd. Present on this machine (UPIA 8.5.0.13) at C:\Program Files\Common Files\Adobe\Adobe Desktop Common\RemoteComponents\UPI\UnifiedPluginInstallerAgent\UnifiedPluginInstallerAgent.exe.
- **UXP Developer Tool (UDT)**: Free Adobe desktop app for loading, reloading and debugging in-development UXP plugins (Premiere now; After Effects UXP public beta announced for Nov 2026).
- **Video Copilot Color Vibrance (free)**: Free Video Copilot colorize plug-in that maps an intense, hot colour wash onto footage and can output mattes/alpha from the colour selection.
- **Video Copilot FX Console (free)**: Free Video Copilot workflow plug-in: a pop-up search box (default Ctrl+Space) that applies any effect or animation preset by typing its name, plus FX shortcuts/overrides and a snapshot tool that exports the comp viewer as PNG into a gallery. Installed here: Support Files/Plug-ins/VideoCopilot/FXConsole.aex in After Effects 2026.
- **Video Copilot Orb (free)**: Free Video Copilot 3D sphere plug-in: renders photoreal planets/globes and spheres with a physically based shader, texture mapping, environment reflection maps, motion blur and UV controls inside AE's 3D camera.
- **Video Copilot Saber (free)**: Free Video Copilot effect plug-in that draws energy beams, lightsabers, neon outlines, lasers, portals, electricity and haze, with realistic glow falloff, built-in distortion, 50 presets and the ability to trace text layers or masks.
- **Voukoder (classic, free) / Voukoder Pro (now paid)**: FFmpeg-based encoder plug-in for Premiere/Media Encoder and After Effects (x264/x265, NVENC/AMD, VP9 and more). Classic Voukoder is officially end-of-life ('No further development or bug fixes will be done'); its successor Voukoder Pro (2026) is commercial: 69 EUR perpetual or 6 EUR/month subscription (subscription includes future versions).
- **WebM plug-in for Premiere / Media Encoder / AE (fnord)**: Free WebM import/export plug-in by Brendan Bolles (fnord) adding VP8/VP9 video and Vorbis/Opus audio to Premiere Pro, Media Encoder and After Effects export settings; distributed on Adobe Exchange; source moved from GitHub to GitLab.
- **Youlean Loudness Meter 2 (free version)**: Free loudness meter plug-in/app (VST2, VST3, AU, AAX; Windows and macOS) measuring integrated, short-term and momentary LUFS, true peak, loudness range, with broadcast presets (ITU-R BS.1770-4, EBU R128, ATSC A/85, OP-59, ARIB, AGCOM) and streaming presets (YouTube, Spotify, Netflix, etc.).
- **aescripts ZXP/UXP Installer (free)**: Free GUI installer from aescripts for any .zxp (CEP) or UXP extension for AE, Premiere, Photoshop, Illustrator, Audition and Media Encoder, with a fallback 'custom install' when Adobe's installer fails.
