- **auto-editor (open source silence/jump-cut tool)**: Public-domain (Unlicense) CLI (v31.7.2, released 3 Oct 2026) that analyses audio/motion and cuts dead air; with --export premiere it writes an FCP7 XML timeline that Premiere imports as an editable sequence.
- **fnord 3D Channel tools: Cryptomatte, EXtractoR, IDentifier (bundled)**: Free compositing utilities originally by fnord software (ProEXR author) that ship with After Effects 2026 in the '3D Channel' category: Cryptomatte (pick ID mattes from Cryptomatte EXR renders), EXtractoR (pull any channel out of a multichannel EXR) and IDentifier (object/material ID mattes).
- **nexrender (open-source AE render automation)**: MIT-licensed Node.js tool for data-driven After Effects rendering: a JSON job names a template .aep and composition, swaps assets (image/video/audio/data/script/static) and runs aerender, then post-render actions (e.g. @nexrender/action-encode, @nexrender/action-copy). Latest release v1.63.4 (3 Jun 2026); repo active.
- **pymiere (Python for Premiere Pro, open source)**: GPL-3.0 Python library that controls Premiere Pro through a small CEP extension ('Pymiere Link'), exposing Premiere's ExtendScript DOM as Python objects (pymiere.objects.app.project ...).
- **redefinery rd: scripts (Jeff Almasol, free)**: Classic bundle of ~50 free ExtendScript utilities by Jeff Almasol (e.g. rd_RenderLayers, rd_Duplink) for batch rendering layers, duplicating with links, renaming, etc.

### infographics

- **Gradient fills on shapes without scripting the gradient stops**: Shape-layer Gradient Fill colours ('ADBE Vector Grad Colors') are a custom property that scripts cannot set, so gradient bars and backgrounds need a workaround: a solid with the Gradient Ramp (or 4-Color Gradient) effect matted by the shape, or a saved .ffx that already carries the gradient.
- **Shape-layer charts from data (technique)**: How PVE's chart recipes turn numbers into shape layers: one value = one shape whose geometry is computed from the data (bar height, line vertex, ring trim percentage, wedge angle), grown with scale from a baseline origin or drawn on with Trim Paths, labelled with counter text. Data can be typed into the scene or read from a JSON/CSV footage item by expression.

### layout

- **Broadcast safe areas (action and title safe)**: The safe areas for HD broadcast as numbers: SMPTE ST 2046-1 puts Safe Action at 93% and Safe Title at 90% of the frame's width and height (1920x1080: 1786x1004 and 1728x972; 1280x720: 1190x670 and 1152x648). EBU R 95 says the same as margins: 3.5% (action) and 5% (graphics) on every side. Netflix recommends these values.
- **Multi-box layout geometry (margins, gaps, scale)**: The arithmetic behind two-way, three-up and picture-in-picture layouts: box width from frame width, margins and gaps; scale = box width / frame width for same-aspect sources; centre positions; and how those become After Effects Transform positions (source pixels) and Premiere ones (frame fractions) for bridge-built clips.
- **Platform UI safe zones (1080x1920 Reels, Shorts, TikTok)**: Where the app's own interface covers a vertical video: Meta asks Reels ads to leave 14% at the top, 35% at the bottom and 6% on each side free of text and logos (1080x1920: about 269, 672 and 65 px). TikTok publishes templates rather than numbers, and Google publishes no Shorts safe zone; a vendor estimate for Shorts is 288 px top, 672 bottom, 48 left, 192 right.
- **Premiere Safe Margins and After Effects safe guides (their defaults)**: Both apps draw safe-area guides, but their defaults are the old SD values: Premiere's standard action and title safe margins are 10% and 20%, After Effects' Grids & Guides default to Action-Safe 10% and Title-Safe 20% (and center-cut 32.5% / 40%). Read as total insets, those are 90% / 80% boxes, not the 93% / 90% of SMPTE ST 2046-1.
- **Text placement around faces (layout rule)**: The house rule as a layout procedure: never put on-screen text over a face. Decide where faces are in each shot before placing text, and move the text band (or reframe) instead of covering a face.

### media_management

- **Collect a project for hand-off (AE Collect Files, Premiere Project Manager)**: Packing a project with its media so someone else can open it: After Effects File > Dependencies > Collect Files (sources for all / all comps / selected comps / queued comps / none, plus a report) and Premiere's Project Manager (collect or transcode). Fonts are never collected: AE's report only lists them.
- **Missing fonts (After Effects Resolve Fonts, Adobe Fonts, templates)**: When a project or template uses fonts the machine lacks: After Effects shows a Resolve Fonts dialog on open, can activate Adobe Fonts, and swaps its default font in for anything it cannot get, which silently changes the design. Scripts should check fonts before rendering.
- **Relink offline media (Link Media)**: Bringing back media that moved: Premiere's Link Media dialog (with Locate) on open or from the Project panel, and the scripted route that walks the project, finds offline items and points them at new paths.

### motion_paths

- **Auto-Orient: Orient Along Path**: Makes a layer rotate to face the direction it is moving along its motion path: a plane, car or arrow following a route on a map points the right way automatically.
- **Paste a mask or shape path into Position (motion path)**: Turns a drawn line into motion: copy a mask or shape Path and paste it into Position, and the layer travels along it, with roving keyframes for an even speed.
- **Separate Dimensions (X and Y position curves)**: Splits Position into X Position and Y Position (and Z) so each gets its own keyframes and easing: arcs, bounces and news-graphic moves where horizontal and vertical motion need different timing.
- **Speed graph vs value graph (shaping eases)**: How to shape motion in the Graph Editor: the value graph shows the property's value over time, the speed graph its rate of change; spatial properties (Position) open on the speed graph, others on the value graph. Ease strength is keyframe velocity plus influence.

### pr_scripting

- **Audio clip Volume and fades**: Audio clips carry an intrinsic 'Volume' component whose 'Level' param sets gain and takes keyframes; fades and ducking dips are keyframes on it.
- **Auto Reframe sequence: Sequence.autoReframeSequence**: Generates a new, subject-tracked sequence at another aspect ratio (Premiere 14.0+).
- **CEP panels (HTML panels calling ExtendScript)**: The older extension system: an HTML/Node panel inside Premiere that runs ExtendScript with CSInterface.evalScript. Being retired in favour of UXP.
- **Captions from SRT: Sequence.createCaptionTrack**: Creates a caption track on a sequence from an imported caption file (.srt etc.).
- **Clip speed and reverse (Speed/Duration)**: Changing a timeline clip's speed or reversing it has NO supported scripting setter in ExtendScript or UXP; both only read speed.
- **Colour parameters: setColorValue / getColorValue**: Sets colour controls (Tint colours, Fill, Stroke, Glow colour, .mogrt colours).
- **Dynamic Link: project.importAEComps / importAllAEComps**: Links After Effects compositions into Premiere as live project items (edit in AE, Premiere updates). This is how AdobeBridge brings AE titles and heavy VFX shots into the Premiere cut.
- **Effect parameters: Component and ComponentParam.setValue**: Every clip's effects (Motion, Opacity, Volume, then applied effects in order) are Components; their controls are ComponentParams you read and set by display name.
- **Effect presets (.prfpset) from a script**: Saved Premiere effect presets (and the bins under Effects > Presets) cannot be applied by ExtendScript, QE or UXP. Presets do not appear in getVideoEffectList().
- **ExtendScript DOM (official Premiere scripting API)**: Premiere's documented ES3 JavaScript object model rooted at `app`: projects, bins, sequences, tracks, clips, effect parameters, keyframes, markers and export. Synchronous (blocks the UI while it runs). Frozen since Premiere 23.0 but still working in 26.x; it is the surface AdobeBridge (pr_build.jsx) drives.
- **FCP 7 XML import as an alternate edit path**: An externally generated Final Cut Pro 7 XML (xmeml) imported into Premiere can carry things the scripting API cannot set: constant speed changes, nested sequences, dissolves/wipes, markers, track layout.
- **Interchange export: FCP XML, AAF, OMF, project subset**: Exports the project or a sequence to Final Cut Pro 7 XML, AAF, OMF, or a new .prproj holding one sequence; opens FCP XML as a project.
- **Intrinsic Motion and Opacity components**: Every video clip carries 'Motion' (Position, Scale, Scale Width, Uniform Scale, Rotation, Anchor Point, Anti-flicker Filter, Crop Left/Top/Right/Bottom) and 'Opacity' (Opacity, Blend Mode), all scriptable.
- **Keyframes: setTimeVarying, addKey, setValueAtKey, setInterpolationTypeAtKey**: Animates any keyframable ComponentParam (Scale push-ins, Position drifts, blur reveals, volume fades).
- **Lumetri Color from a script (incl. Look and LUT selection)**: Lumetri Color can be added by QE and most of its numeric controls set; the built-in Creative 'Look' and Basic 'Input LUT' are numeric INDEX controls, while custom .cube files sit in opaque string params that a script cannot set.
- **Markers: sequence and clip markers**: Creates, colours, types and reads markers on sequences and project items.
- **Media Encoder queue: app.encoder**: Queues sequences, project items or files to Adobe Media Encoder and starts the batch, so Premiere stays free while renders run.
- **Media import: project.importFiles and bins**: Imports media files (video, audio, stills, image sequences, .srt) into a bin (.mogrt files go through importMGT instead).
- **Metadata: XMP and project metadata**: Reads and writes XMP and Premiere project-panel metadata on project items; adds custom metadata fields.
- **Motion Graphics Templates: importMGT and getMGTComponent**: Places a .mogrt on a track and edits its exposed parameters (text, font, size, colours, sliders, checkboxes, Duration).
- **Nested sequences (no direct Nest call)**: There is no 'Nest' method in ExtendScript, QE (known) or UXP. Nesting is done by placing one sequence's project item inside another, or by createSubsequence plus replacement.
- **Placing clips: overwriteClip / insertClip with source in/out**: Puts a project item on a track at a time, using the source range set on the project item.
- **Premiere scripting gap list (what no surface can do on 26.0.2)**: Editing operations with no supported scripting path in ExtendScript, QE (known) or UXP, and the workaround PVE planners should use for each.
- **Project Manager: collect or transcode a project for hand-off (app.projectManager)**: Scripted version of File > Project Manager: copies (or transcodes) a project's used media plus a trimmed project into a destination folder, optionally rendering Dynamic-Linked AE comps to clips.
- **Project lifecycle: newProject, openDocument, save, saveAs, closeDocument**: Create, open, save and close .prproj files from a script.
- **QE DOM (undocumented): overview**: The 'Quality Engineering' DOM Adobe uses for internal testing. It is the only ExtendScript way to ADD video/audio effects and transitions, add tracks and razor clips. Undocumented, unsupported, but working on 26.0.2 for what AdobeBridge uses.
- **QE: add audio effects (getAudioEffectList, getAudioEffectByName, addAudioEffect)**: Lists all 53 audio effects on this install and applies one to an audio clip.
- **QE: add tracks (QESequence.addTracks)**: Adds video and audio tracks to the active sequence (the official DOM cannot).
- **QE: add transitions (getVideoTransitionList, getVideoTransitionByName, addTransition)**: Lists 154 video transitions (plus 3 audio: Constant Power, Constant Gain, Exponential Fade) and applies one at a clip's head or tail with duration, alignment and single-sided control.
- **QE: add video effects (getVideoEffectList, getVideoEffectByName, addVideoEffect)**: Lists all 143 video effects on this install and applies one to a clip by display name.
- **QE: razor (cut clips at a time)**: Cuts every clip on a QE track at a timecode, like the Razor tool.
- **QE: synthetic media, playback and frame export**: QE can create synthetic items (transparent video, bars and tone, universal counter) and drive the player; frame-export helpers are reported but unverified.
- **QE: unsupported clip methods (setSpeed, setReverse, removeEffects, slip, slide, roll, moveToTrack)**: Reflection of a QE track item lists more methods: addAudioEffect, addTransition, addVideoEffect, canDoMulticam, getClipPanComponent, getComponentAt, getProjectItem, move, moveToTrack, remove, removeEffects, rippleDelete, roll, setAntiAliasQuality, setBorderColor, setBorderWidth, setEndPercent, setEndPosition, setFrameBlend, setMulticam, setName, setReverse, setScaleToFrameSize, setSpeed, setStartPercent, setStartPosition, setSwitchSources, setTimeInterpolationType, slide, slip.
- **Render inside Premiere: Sequence.exportAsMediaDirect**: Renders a sequence to a file with an .epr export preset, synchronously, without Media Encoder's queue.
- **Script delivery to Premiere: BridgeTalk relay from After Effects**: Premiere has no simple, documented command-line flag for running a script (the scripting guide says command-line execution needs extra configuration, varies by platform and is not recommended; Adobe recommends CEP panels). AdobeBridge runs a JSX in After Effects with `AfterFX.exe -r <absolute path>`, and that JSX forwards the Premiere script over BridgeTalk. Premiere reports back through files.
- **Scriptable slow motion and speed ramps: Timewarp effect**: Premiere 26 ships the AE Timewarp effect (match name AE.ADBE Timewarp). It retimes footage INSIDE the clip with a keyframable 'Speed' and optical-flow interpolation, and it can be applied and keyed from a script, so it is the practical scripted speed tool.
- **Selection, linking and Scene Edit Detection**: Selects timeline clips from a script, links/unlinks A/V, and runs Premiere's cut detection on the selection.
- **Sequence creation and settings**: Creates sequences and sets frame size and frame rate.
- **Sequence housekeeping: playhead, in/out, work area, clone, delete**: Controls the playhead (CTI), sequence in/out and work area, and duplicates or deletes sequences.
- **Source-clip edits: subclips, frame-rate interpretation, scale to frame**: Edits the master clip in the bin: subclips, overriding the interpreted frame rate or pixel aspect, scale-to-frame, colour labels, proxies, relinking.
- **Time Remapping (speed ramps on the clip)**: Premiere's Time Remapping (rubber-band speed ramps) is not exposed to any documented scripting surface.
- **Time values and ticks**: Premiere times are Time objects; 1 second = 254016000000 ticks. Some calls take seconds, some ticks strings, some timecode strings.
- **Track state: mute, lock, targeting**: Mutes, locks and targets tracks; reads track names and clip/transition collections.
- **TrackItem timing and state: start, end, inPoint, outPoint, move, remove, disabled**: Reads and edits a placed clip's timeline position, trim, enabled state and name.
- **UXP Premiere API: overview and versions**: Adobe's modern JavaScript (ES6+, async) plugin API for Premiere: `const ppro = require('premierepro')`. Public beta in 25.2 (Dec 2024), official in 25.6, the only surface getting new features (26.2 hybrid C++ plugins, 26.3 and 26.5 additions).
- **UXP plugin hosting and external control (UDT, Developer Mode, WebSocket, hybrid plugins)**: UXP code runs only as a loaded plugin; there is no command-line or file-menu way to run a UXP script. External tools drive it by having the plugin open a WebSocket to a local server.
- **UXP: ProjectConverter (OpenTimelineIO, FCP XML, AAF export)**: Newer UXP builds export a sequence to OpenTimelineIO, FCP XML or AAF.
- **UXP: SequenceEditor (insert, overwrite, clone, remove, .mogrt)**: Timeline editing in UXP: insert or overwrite project items at a TickTime on track indices, duplicate track items, remove (with ripple), insert .mogrts.
- **UXP: effects add/remove (VideoFilterFactory, AudioFilterFactory, ComponentChain)**: Supported (non-QE) way to add video effects by MATCH NAME, add audio effects by display name, insert at an index, and REMOVE effects.
- **UXP: export (EncoderManager, Exporter.exportSequenceFrame)**: UXP renders sequences immediately or via Media Encoder, encodes files or clips, and exports single frames as images.
- **UXP: parameters and keyframes (ComponentParam, Keyframe)**: UXP sets values and keyframes through Keyframe objects and Actions.
- **UXP: project, sequence and clip utilities**: UXP project/sequence/source-clip calls: create sequences from media or presets, clone, subsequence, settings, input LUT and frame-rate overrides on clips, markers, work area, scene-edit detection, object masks.
- **UXP: track items, tracks and selection**: UXP reads track items and changes their timing, name and enabled state through Actions; speed and adjustment-layer status are read-only.
- **UXP: transactions and Actions (lockedAccess, executeTransaction)**: UXP changes the project only through Action objects added to a CompoundAction inside project.executeTransaction, which must run inside project.lockedAccess. One transaction = one undo step.
- **UXP: transcripts and caption tracks**: UXP can transcribe a clip with Premiere's Speech-to-Text, export/import transcript JSON and attach external transcripts; caption tracks can be read, muted and (26.3+) renamed, but there is no call that creates captions from a transcript.
- **UXP: video transitions (TransitionFactory, AddTransitionOptions)**: Supported way to add or remove a video transition at a clip's start or end with duration, alignment and single-sided options.

### pr_tools

- **2026 effect and transition additions (26.2-26.5)**: Later 2026 builds add: Channel Blur, Gradient and Noise effects plus 3D Spinback and Slide transitions (26.2); Shape Dissolve transition and Magnify lens effect (26.3); new Lens Flare, updated Twirl and Mosaic softness (26.5).
- **Adjustment layers**: A transparent clip whose effects apply to every track below it for its duration; one place to grade, blur, shake or add grain to a whole section.
- **Adobe Media Encoder queue, presets and batch**: Background encoder shared by Premiere and After Effects: queue many sequences/comps, render while editing, custom .epr presets, watch folders.
- **Auto Color (Lumetri)**: One-click AI correction of exposure, white balance and contrast, written into the Lumetri Basic Correction sliders with an Intensity control.
- **Auto Reframe (sequence command and effect)**: AI subject tracking that pans/scales footage to keep the action in frame when changing aspect ratio (16:9 -> 9:16, 1:1, 4:5). Works as a whole-sequence command that builds a new sequence, or as the 'Auto Reframe' effect on a single clip.
- **Auto-ducking (music under dialogue)**: Essential Sound writes volume keyframes on Music (or other) clips so they dip whenever dialogue/SFX/etc. is present.
- **Bleep or mute censored words**: Transcript-driven bulk censoring: keep a censored-words list, then mute every occurrence or cut in a bleep (or other sound) on a new track.
- **Captions (caption tracks, styles, export)**: Caption tracks built from a transcript or imported SRT/SCC/etc., styled as text and exported either as a sidecar file or burned into the picture.
- **Color Match (Comparison View)**: Matches the current shot to a reference frame by writing Lumetri colour-wheel and saturation values, optionally weighting skin tones (Face Detection).
- **Color management (log/raw auto-detect, tone mapping)**: Since 25.0 Premiere colour-manages media: it can auto-detect log and raw footage, convert it to the sequence working space, and tone-map HDR/wide-gamut sources to SDR Rec.709 with gamut compression.
- **Content Credentials (C2PA)**: Provenance metadata attached to exports; generative-AI usage (Generative Extend, Generative Media) is recorded so viewers/platforms can see AI was used.
- **Dynamic Link: After Effects comps in Premiere**: Live link between an AE composition and a Premiere clip: no intermediate render; edits in AE update in Premiere. 'Replace With After Effects Composition' sends selected clips to a new linked comp with the sequence's settings.
- **Enhance Speech**: AI dialogue cleanup that removes background noise/room and makes speech sound studio-recorded, with a Mix Amount to blend back the original.
- **Essential Sound panel and audio auto-tagging**: Task-based audio panel: each clip is typed Dialogue, Music, SFX or Ambience (AI auto-tagging adds clickable badges), and each type exposes simple controls (Loudness, Repair, Clarity, Enhance Speech, Ducking, Remix, Reverb, Pan).
- **Export mode and Quick Export**: Premiere's export page: destinations (Media File, social platforms), preset, format, range, Effects section (Lumetri Look/LUT, SDR Conform, Image/Name/Timecode overlays, Time Tuner, Video Limiter, Loudness Normalization), Captions (sidecar or burn-in); Quick Export is the one-click version.
- **Film Impact effects and transitions pack (90+)**: Adobe bought Film Impact and built its 90+ GPU-accelerated effects, transitions and text animations into Premiere 25.5 (Sept 2025): glitches, VHS damage, light leaks, blurs, motion/zoom/spin transitions, typewriter and text animators.
- **Firefly app and Firefly Boards integration**: Send images/video/concepts from Firefly and Firefly Boards straight into a Premiere project; since 26.3 a Boards selection can become a ready sequence (selection order = edit order).
- **Frame Hold / freeze frame**: Freezes a frame: from the playhead to the clip end (Add Frame Hold), as an inserted still segment (Insert Frame Hold Segment), or for a part of a clip via a 0% Time Remapping segment.
- **Frame.io V4 and Adobe Stock panels**: In-app panels: Frame.io V4 for uploading versions and reading time-coded review comments; Adobe Stock for searching and licensing stock video/audio/images without leaving Premiere.
- **Generative Extend**: Firefly-generated extra frames at the head or tail of a clip (and extra room tone/ambience on audio) to cover a short shot, hold a reaction, or fit a transition.
- **Generative Media tool (text-to-video and SFX in the timeline)**: Select a gap or range on a track, describe a shot or sound effect, and Premiere generates editable video or SFX clips in place through a floating 'generative task bar'; video can use Firefly or partner models (helpx names Google Veo, Kling and Luma) with reference frames sampled from the sequence.
- **Loudness: Auto-Match, Loudness Radar/Meter and export normalization**: Three ways to hit a loudness target: Essential Sound Loudness > Auto-Match per clip type, Loudness Radar / Loudness Meter audio effects for measuring, and Loudness Normalization in the export settings for the final file.
- **Lumetri Scopes**: Waveform (RGB/Luma/YC), RGB Parade, Vectorscope (YUV, HLS) and Histogram for judging exposure, skin tone and legal levels.
- **Markers**: Timeline/clip markers (Comment, Chapter, Segmentation, Web Link) with names, comments, colours and durations (range markers); used for beats, review notes and YouTube chapters.
- **Media Intelligence and the Search panel**: On-device analysis of imported footage (objects, places, camera angles, dialogue, metadata) so clips can be found with natural-language search; 26.0 added search by sound description and by similar visuals.
- **Morph Cut**: A video transition that hides a jump cut inside one talking-head shot by face-tracking and optical-flow morphing between the two frames, so a removed 'umm' or sentence looks like continuous speech.
- **Motion Graphics Templates (.mogrt) and Graphics Templates panel**: Reusable animated titles/lower-thirds/end cards authored in After Effects (or Premiere) with exposed parameters (text, colours, checkboxes, sliders) that are edited per use in Premiere. The proven way for AdobeBridge to put designed, animated text on a Premiere timeline.
- **Multi-camera editing**: Syncs several angles into a multicam source sequence and lets the editor cut between angles live during playback.
- **Nesting (nested sequences)**: Wraps selected timeline clips into a sequence that sits as one clip, so effects, speed or transforms can be applied to the group.
- **Object Mask (AI subject masking)**: Hover and click a person or object and Premiere builds an AI mask and tracks it through the shot (on-device). Use it as an opacity mask or to limit any effect.
- **Paper Edit**: Builds a new sequence from transcript selections: highlight the lines to keep across one or more interviews, preview, and create a sequence with the matching video/audio in edit order.
- **Properties panel**: Context-sensitive panel (since 25.0) that shows the most-used controls for whatever is selected: transform, crop, speed and opacity for video; type, loudness and Enhance Speech for audio; font/fill/stroke/background for text and captions; parameters of MOGRTs. Can edit several clips at once.
- **Proxies and ingest**: Low-resolution stand-ins attached to full-res media for smooth editing; one button switches back to full quality for export.
- **Remix (music retiming)**: AI retiming of a music track to a target duration by cutting or looping musical sections at beat-matched points with automatic crossfades.
- **Scene Edit Detection**: Finds the original cuts inside a flattened video file (a downloaded news clip, a finished trailer, an old programme) and re-creates them as edit points, subclips or markers.
- **Scripting surface: ExtendScript + QE DOM (what AdobeBridge uses)**: Premiere's classic JavaScript API (app.project, Sequence, Track, TrackItem, ProjectItem, Component, ComponentParam, app.encoder) plus the undocumented QE DOM (app.enableQE(); qe.project...) that AdobeBridge uses to add effects and transitions. Adobe froze the ExtendScript API at 23.0: no Premiere feature added after 2022 (Object Mask, Generative Extend, media intelligence, text-based editing, Remix, Enhance Speech, Properties panel) has an ExtendScript call.
- **Scripting surface: UXP Premiere API**: The modern plugin API (Premiere DOM classes: Project, Sequence, SequenceEditor, VideoClipTrackItem, ComponentParam, VideoFilterFactory, TransitionFactory, Transcript, EncoderManager, Markers, SequenceUtils, ObjectMaskUtils...). Most classes exist since 25.6; a few were added in 26.2/26.3/26.5. Edits are built as Actions and committed with project.executeTransaction / lockedAccess. It is the only scripted route to transcription (Transcript.transcribeClipProjectItem).
- **Sequence Index panel**: Searchable, sortable spreadsheet view of every clip in the open timeline, with filters for offline media, flash frames (shown in red), transitions, effects, label colour and media type, and CSV export of the filtered rows.
- **Sequences and sequence settings (incl. vertical)**: Creating timelines with the right frame size, frame rate and audio settings: from a preset, from a clip, or by editing Sequence Settings (e.g. 1080x1920 for reels, 1080x1080 for square).
- **Shape masks with tracking (redesigned in 26.0)**: Rectangle, Ellipse and Pen masks on Opacity or on any effect, with feather/expansion/opacity/invert and automatic tracking. 26.0 rewrote them: tracking up to 20x faster, rounded corners, numeric control, add/subtract/intersect blend modes and a 3D perspective tracking mode.
- **Single-word captions**: Native caption mode that shows one word at a time (social-reel style) with customizable styling and export.
- **Speech to Text (transcription)**: On-device transcription of clips/sequences into a word-timed transcript in the Text panel; the base for captions, text-based editing, Paper Edit, censored-word bleeping and media-intelligence dialogue search.
- **Speed/Duration, Rate Stretch and Optical Flow**: Constant speed change of a clip (slow motion, fast motion, reverse) with a choice of frame interpolation.
- **Text-based editing (pauses and filler words)**: Edit the timeline by editing the transcript: select text to insert/overwrite into a sequence, or delete words, pauses and filler words ('um', 'uh') with an automatic ripple.
- **Time Remapping (speed ramps)**: Variable speed inside one clip using speed keyframes on the clip's rubber band, with smooth Bezier ramps between speeds.
- **Track mattes (Track Matte Key)**: Uses the alpha or luminance of a clip on a higher track as the visibility matte of the clip it is applied to (text-filled video, shape reveals, luma wipes).
- **Transition handles, fade handles and Multi Transitions**: On-clip handles in the timeline: audio clips get interactive fade handles, and since 26.0 video clips get transition handles that apply the default video transition (Cross Dissolve unless changed) and set its length by dragging. Multi Transitions applies or adjusts transitions across many selected clips/edits at once.
- **Translate Captions**: AI translation of an existing caption track (generated or imported, e.g. SRT) into other languages; each language lands on its own caption track. Released in Premiere Pro 25.2 (April 2025).
- **Type tool, graphics layers and text/track styles**: Native titles: the Type tool (T) creates a graphic clip with text/shape layers styled in the Properties panel; Text Styles and Track Styles keep fonts/colours consistent.
- **Premiere AI Assistant (beta)** *(hidden)*: Chat panel in the Premiere public beta that carries out project chores from plain-language requests: create/organise bins, rename and label clips, generate transcripts, detect slates, add markers, build stringouts and rough assemblies on a timeline.

### safety

- **Photosensitive flash rule (Harding / ITU-R BT.1702) and pve check**: The photosensitive epilepsy rule every delivery must meet: no more than 3 flashes in any one second where the flashing covers more than a quarter of the screen (ITU-R BT.1702-3, Ofcom). A flash is a pair of opposing brightness changes of 20 cd/m2 or more where the darker image is below 160 cd/m2; any change to or from saturated red counts; regular stripe patterns are covered too. pve.check_spec and check_scenes enforce the 3-per-second limit.

### text_animators

- **Per-character 3D text (Enable Per-character 3D)**: The text layer switch that gives every character its own Z position and X/Y rotation inside a 3D layer, so Animator X Rotation, Y Rotation and Position Z act per character, and characters can face the camera.
- **Text More Options: Anchor Point Grouping, Grouping Alignment, Fill & Stroke, Inter-Character Blending**: The text layer's More Options group: what each character/word/line scales and rotates around (Anchor Point Grouping + Grouping Alignment), how fill and stroke stack across characters (Fill & Stroke), how overlapping characters blend (Inter-Character Blending), and Variable Font Spacing.
- **Text animator properties: the full set with match names**: Every property an After Effects text Animator can hold, with its match name: transform (Anchor Point, Position, Scale, Skew, Skew Axis, X/Y/Z Rotation, Opacity), Fill and Stroke (Color, Hue, Saturation, Brightness, Opacity, Stroke Width), spacing (Tracking Type and Amount, Line Anchor, Line Spacing), characters (Character Value, Offset, Alignment, Range) and Blur.
- **Text on a path (Path Options)**: Makes a text layer follow a mask path: Path (which mask), Reverse Path, Perpendicular To Path, Force Alignment, First Margin and Last Margin. Animating First Margin slides the text along the path.
- **Text selectors: Range, Wiggly and Expression (match names and Advanced options)**: What decides which characters an Animator affects: the Range Selector (Start, End, Offset in percent or index, plus Advanced: Units, Based On, Mode, Amount, Shape, Smoothness, Ease High, Ease Low, Randomize Order, Random Seed), the Wiggly Selector (random per-character amounts over time) and the Expression Selector (an Amount expression per character).

## Recipes (verified looks built from the cards)


### archive_looks

- **Aged photo print** [weak; AE pass]: A still archive photograph treated as an old print: faintly warm grey monochrome, faded milky blacks, dimmed whites, slight softness, fine grain and only slightly darker edges.
- **Analogue broadcast tape** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: 1980s-90s TV news on analogue tape: 4:3 pillarbox, soft horizontal detail, colour bleeding sideways, boosted reds, lifted blacks and faint chroma noise; clean, not damaged.
- **Archive blend-in** [pass; AE pass]: A subtle match pass that lets clean modern, stock or AI-generated shots sit between real archive: slightly muted and faded, a touch soft, light grain and a faint vignette.
- **Black-and-white newsreel** [weak; AE pass]: 1930s-50s newsreel: punchy black-and-white with heavy moving grain, gate weave, exposure flicker and dark corners.
- **CRT television screen** [pass; AE pass]: Footage shown on an old tube TV: bulged barrel picture with black rounded corners, phosphor bloom, colour fringes, soft lines, noise and darkened edges; After Effects adds curved scanlines.
- **CRT vertical roll** [pass; AE pass]: The broadcast slips its vertical hold: in half a second the picture rolls one full frame upward with vertical smear, a brightness surge and static, landing back on the same frame.
- **Colour to archive era shift** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Animated time jump: over about 1.2 s the colour drains into sepia while contrast, grain, fade, weave and vignette build up, taking a present-day shot into the past.
- **Damaged VHS tape** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Worn consumer VHS: smeared oversaturated colour bleeding sideways, soft picture, crawling horizontal line jitter, colour fringes and heavy chroma noise.
- **Faded colour print** [pass; AE pass]: 1970s-80s colour print that has aged: low contrast, milky lifted blacks, dimmed whites, colour drained and drifting magenta-red as the cyan dye fades, with fine grain.
- **Film burn reel-end** [pass; AE pass]: The reel runs out: the last second of the shot overexposes and burns through like melting celluloid into the next shot or to light.
- **Grungy film reel** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Worn projection print: muted warm colour with lifted blacks, moving grain, gate weave, random exposure lifts and a soft vignette; the After Effects version adds drifting vertical scratches.
- **Projector beam** [pass; AE pass]: Film as projected in a dark hall: warm lamp colour, hot centre falling off to dark edges, halation bloom on highlights, soft rays, gate weave and lamp flicker.
- **Sepia film** [pass; AE pass]: Moving footage turned into warm brown-and-cream monochrome film with contrast, grain, light gate weave, gentle flicker and darkened edges.
- **Silent-era film** [pass; AE pass]: 1910s-20s silent film: stepped undercranked motion, high-contrast monochrome dyed amber, heavy grain, strong weave and flicker inside a round iris vignette.
- **Super 8 home movie** [pass; AE pass]: Amateur 8 mm home movie: warm, slightly oversaturated colour, soft focus, coarse grain, 18 fps stutter, loose weave and a rounded vignette.
- **TV power-off** [AE untested: needs_setup]: Old CRT switch-off: the picture squashes into a bright horizontal line, collapses to a dot and goes black (and the reverse for switch-on).
- **VHS tracking burst** [pass; AE pass]: A 0.4 s tape tracking error on a hit: rows tear sideways, the picture jumps vertically, colours split and static spikes, then it snaps back to the clean tape picture.

### audio_mix

- **Music ducks under VO (scripted, from VO ranges)** [corrected_unverified]: The music bed sits at full level in the gaps and drops 12 dB under every VO line, with short ramps, built from the VO clip times instead of Premiere's UI-only Auto Ducking.

### cleanup

- **Archive and phone-call audio restoration** [AE untested: audio_only]: Cleans old broadcast, vinyl-era or phone-recorded speech: clicks and crackle repaired, hiss reduced, and the band limited to what the source really carries.
- **Archive dust and speck cleanup** [pass]: Removes white/black dust specks and small dirt from scanned film and old photos with a small median-style despeckle.
- **Burned-in ticker and band crop-out** [pass; AE pass]: Removes a burnt-in news ticker, lower third or subtitle band along one edge by scaling about the opposite edge's centre.
- **Corner logo crop-zoom removal** [pass; AE pass]: Pushes a corner watermark or channel bug out of frame by scaling the clip about the OPPOSITE corner, so the other three edges stay put.
- **Field interview noise rescue** [AE untested: audio_only]: Rescues a noisy location interview or piece-to-camera: heavier steady-noise removal, rumble cut, the gaps between phrases gated down, level evened and peaks caught.
- **Frame-rate judder conform** [broken; AE broken]: Meant to blend in-between frames when 24/25 fps footage sits in a 30 fps edit (or vice versa) so pans stop stuttering. Render-checked, it changes nothing: neither at 25 fps into 25 fps nor on a 30 fps variable-frame-rate phone clip in a 25 fps sequence (frame-to-frame change identical to the plain clip in both apps, the clip's repeated frames kept).
- **Green screen key** [pass; AE pass]: Keys a green (or blue) screen presenter cleanly over a background clip on the track below, with spill removed and screen edges matted off. On footage with no green screen the key removes nothing and only the garbage-matte trim shows (edges go black where no background track sits below, render-checked).
- **Handheld shake stabilize** [pass; AE untested: needs_analysis]: Smooths jittery handheld or phone footage while keeping the camera move, cropping slightly to hide the moving edges.
- **Interlace combing and line twitter fix** [untested: needs_setup; AE untested: needs_setup]: A tiny vertical-only blur that hides interlace combing on old TV recordings and the twitter of thin horizontal lines in tickers and documents. On clean progressive footage it leaves no visible change (render-checked at Softness 1.5).
- **Interview jump-cut smoothing** [AE pass]: Hides a jump cut inside one locked-off talking-head shot so the speaker seems to talk continuously after an 'um' or a cut sentence is removed.
- **Loudness master for social and broadcast** [AE untested: audio_only]: Final level stage: the mix lands on the delivery loudness (about -14 LUFS for YouTube/Instagram, -23/-24 for TV) with a true-peak-safe ceiling.
- **Low-light noise cleanup** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Smooths sensor noise and chroma speckle in dark rally, night or indoor phone footage, then restores a little edge crispness.
- **Low-res archive upscale cleanup** [weak; AE weak]: Makes 360p-720p archive or web clips hold up in a 1080p edit: mild denoise before the cover-fit scale-up and gentle sharpening to counter the softness. On a file that is already 1080p (soft but pre-upscaled) only the faint denoise shows: the Radius 0.8 sharpen changes nothing there (render-checked), so use Web delivery sharpen on such files.
- **Mains hum removal (50 Hz)** [AE untested: audio_only]: Removes the steady electrical hum (Indian 50 Hz mains and its harmonics) from lights, cameras or ground loops under speech.
- **Narration VO polish chain** [AE untested: audio_only]: Studio-style voice-over from a home or office recording: hiss and room removed, rumble cut, warmer and clearer, evened out and peak-safe.
- **Phone pan jello fix** [pass; AE pass]: Straightens the leaning verticals and wobble that phone and CMOS cameras produce on fast pans. On footage with no skew it leaves the picture unchanged (render-checked), so it has no visible look of its own.
- **Talking-head skin smoothing** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Gentle beauty pass that softens skin texture and compression blotches on interviews while edges stay defined.
- **Timelapse and archive deflicker** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Averages each frame with its neighbours to iron out exposure flicker in locked-off timelapses and flickery film/archive transfers.
- **Web delivery sharpen** [weak; AE weak]: Light, halo-free edge sharpening for 1080p footage that still holds fine detail; on very soft upscaled sources (360p/480p blown up to 1080p) Radius 2 is narrower than the blurred edges, so the gain is only a few percent and barely visible.
- **Wire, boom and scratch line removal** [pass]: Paints out one thin straight line (a cable, boom pole, wire or vertical film scratch) by pulling in neighbouring pixels.

### compositing

- **Green-screen finish: key, despill, edge, then grade-match** [pass; AE pass]: The full order for a real green-screen plate: key the screen, clean and choke the matte edge, remove the green spill, then match the foreground's colour and level to the background plate (light wrap and grain last).

### credits

- **News ticker crawl (looping)** [corrected_unverified]: A red ticker band along the bottom with a white BREAKING label box and headlines crawling right to left in an endless seamless loop, rendered with alpha for overlay.
- **Rolling end credits (AE scroll)** [pass]: A centred credit list scrolls up the screen at a constant whole-pixel speed, from below the frame until the last line has left the top.

### documents

- **Paper drop-in** [pass]: A document drops into frame from above onto a dark desk, lands with a slight tilt and settles, with a soft contact shadow.
- **Redaction bars wipe on** [pass]: Black bars wipe across sensitive lines of a document one by one, the classic redaction look.
- **Screenshot zoom with highlighter** [pass]: A screenshot of an article, post or document sits on a dark desk; the frame pushes in on one line and a yellow marker stroke sweeps across it.

### grades

- **Cinematic teal-orange** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Blockbuster split tone: base cooled, contrast up, shadows pushed teal and highlights and skin pushed warm orange, as a numeric primary grade (Lumetri Temperature + ASC CDL + per-channel Levels in Premiere, Color Balance in After Effects).
- **Clean newsroom** [pass; AE pass]: Neutral, honest broadcast correction: a little contrast, recovered highlights, opened shadows and near-natural colour, so mixed press, phone and studio clips cut together without looking graded.
- **Cold tense** [weak; AE pass]: Suspense grade: drains the warm cast to a cold, muted grey with blue-teal blacks, slightly under-exposed, deeper shadows and a firm vignette; on warm tungsten footage it reads cold-neutral, not a strong blue-cyan cast.
- **Crisis red edge** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Alarm grade for breaking or crisis beats: cool, muted, contrasty picture with a red glow pressing in from the frame edges.
- **Day-for-night** [pass; AE pass]: Fake night from daylight: about 1.5 stops under, strong blue moonlight cast, sky and highlights pulled down hard, colour reduced and heavy dark edges.
- **Desaturated war** [pass; AE pass]: Bleach-bypass style conflict grade: colour drained to about 40%, hard contrast, a faint olive-green cast, gritty sharpening, a dark vignette and mono grain.
- **Documentary interview soft cinematic** [pass; AE pass]: Gentle cinematic interview look: slightly warm, highlights rolled off, shadows opened, a hint of faded blacks and a soft vignette that pulls the eye to the speaker.
- **Golden hope** [pass; AE pass]: Clean, warm, vivid optimism: golden temperature, a touch of exposure, crisp contrast and richer colour without any fade.
- **Newsroom broadcast-safe finish** [pass; AE pass]: Last step for TV delivery: pull hot whites down a touch in Lumetri, then clamp anything above the legal level with Broadcast Colors (reduce luminance, PAL).
- **Noir high-contrast** [pass; AE pass]: Hard black-and-white: full desaturation, steep contrast with crushed-but-not-dead blacks and bright whites, crisp detail, a heavy vignette and fine mono grain.
- **Promise-to-reality swing** [pass; AE pass]: Animated grade change on one shot: warm, saturated 'promise' swings to a cold, muted, harder 'reality' over about a second.
- **Punchy social** [pass; AE pass]: Clean, vivid feed-ready grade: more contrast and vibrance, held highlights, a little sharpening, with skin kept natural.
- **Saffron brand warm grade** [pass; AE pass]: Brand-tinted warmth: golden-saffron temperature and richer colour, with a soft saffron glow creeping in from the frame edges (Premiere).
- **Saffron colour splash** [weak; AE corrected_unverified]: Drains the frame to grey and keeps only the tones closest to bright saffron #FF9933; on the dim test plate that is just a few orange specks (flower petals), so sample the real flag colour into Color before relying on the flag staying in colour.
- **Warm nostalgic** [pass; AE pass]: Memory look: warm amber cast, softened contrast, faded milky blacks, lowered saturation, a soft vignette and fine mono grain.

### impacts

- **Bass drop slam** [pass; AE pass]: The hero hit: the frame slams in zoomed, flashes bright and shakes, then settles to normal in about half a second.
- **Chromatic pulse** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A soft radial colour-fringe breath with a 4% scale bump on each beat, like the lens flexing on the kick.
- **Crash zoom cut** [pass; AE pass]: The cut rushes through a radial zoom blur and the incoming shot lands slightly zoomed and settles back.
- **Echo trail hit** [pass; AE pass]: Moving subjects smear into glowing ghost trails for a beat on the hit, then snap back to a single clean image.
- **Exposure flash hit** [pass; AE pass]: The frame blows out toward white on the hit and recovers in about a quarter second, like a camera flash or blast.
- **Glitch burst** [pass; AE pass]: A third-of-a-second digital corruption: block tearing, colour-channel smear and noise that resolves to clean picture.
- **Glitch cut** [pass; AE pass]: A digital-tear transition: slice displacement and colour fringing mix the two shots and resolve on the incoming one.
- **Graphic jolt** [pass; AE pass]: A title, card or sticker shudders hard for a beat, then settles, while the footage under it stays still.
- **Impact shake hit** [pass; AE pass]: A short, violent handheld jolt on a boom or slam that decays to a locked frame in about half a second.
- **Light burst flash** [weak; AE corrected_unverified]: Highlights bloom into a warm burst of light on the hit and fade back over about half a second.
- **Negative flash** [pass; AE pass]: Three frames (0.12 s at 25 fps) of full photo-negative on the hit, then straight back to normal.
- **Paparazzi strobe** [pass; AE pass]: A short burst of one-frame white camera flashes (two, half a second apart) for a press-scrum or red-carpet feel.
- **RGB split hit** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: The colour channels tear apart sideways for a few frames on the hit and snap back together.
- **Shockwave thump** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: An impact point bulges out and the whole frame flexes like a pressure wave passing through, then settles.
- **Snap punch-in** [pass; AE pass]: A two-to-three-frame snap zoom to a tighter framing on the hit word, with real motion blur, then hold.
- **Speed ramp slam** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: The shot runs fast into the hit, slams into slow motion on it, then ramps back to real time.
- **Stutter step** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Motion drops to a choppy 8 fps for half a second on the hit, like dropped frames, then runs smooth again.
- **Whip smear hit** [pass; AE pass]: A sideways motion smear with colour fringing and a small kick of the frame, as if the camera flinched on the hit.
- **White flash cut** [pass; AE pass]: A press-camera white-out on the cut: the outgoing shot blows to white and the incoming one lands bright and settles.

### infographics

- **Bar chart grow (shape layers)** [pass]: Five vertical bars grow from a baseline one after another, each with a value label that rides the bar top and counts up to its value.
- **Donut chart segments** [pass]: Three coloured ring segments draw on in turn around a grey track, with the first segment's percentage counting up in the centre.
- **Highlighter and underline on a key line** [pass]: A marker-yellow highlight sweeps behind a line of text, then a red underline draws under it: the 'this is the sentence that matters' beat.
- **Line chart draw-on** [pass]: A data line draws itself across a gridded plot with Trim Paths while a dot pops on at each data point as the line reaches it, ending on a counter for the last value.
- **Percentage ring with counter** [pass]: A ring fills clockwise to a percentage while the number counts up in its centre and a caption fades in.
- **Pie chart slices pop** [pass]: Filled pie wedges computed from the data pop in one after another from the centre, each with its percentage outside the rim.
- **Progress bar fill** [pass]: A horizontal bar fills from the left to a percentage with a counter below it.
- **Timeline bar with milestones** [pass]: A line draws left to right and a milestone dot, year and event label pop on each time the line reaches them.

### layouts

- **Blur fill: 16:9 into 9:16** [pass; AE pass]: Blur fill for vertical video: a landscape shot sits full-width in a 9:16 frame over a scaled-up, blurred, darkened copy of itself (the blur fill) instead of black bars.
- **Blur fill: portrait or 4:3 into 16:9 (pillarbox fill)** [pass; AE pass]: Blur fill for portrait and 4:3 sources: the photo or archive clip sits at full height in a 16:9 frame and a blurred, darkened copy of it fills the side bars (pillarbox blur fill).
- **Presenter plus screenshot picture-in-picture** [pass; AE pass]: A document or screenshot fills the left of the frame at full height while the presenter sits in a lower-right box, both with soft shadows over a blurred fill.
- **Three-up row** [pass; AE pass]: Three 16:9 boxes in a row over a dark backplate: three voices, three places or three examples at once.
- **Two-way (double box) split** [pass; AE pass]: Two 16:9 boxes side by side over a dark blurred backplate, the news 'two-way' for an anchor and a correspondent or two sides of a debate.

### moves

- **2.5D photo parallax (two planes)** [pass]: A photo split into a clean background plate and a cut-out subject: the subject drifts and grows faster than the background, which reads as depth. Built as an After Effects VFX scene, no 3D camera needed.
- **Eased slow push-in** [pass; AE pass]: A gentle 10% push over 6 s that starts and lands softly: the ease is built from five Transform scale keys sampled on a smoothstep curve, because the bridge writes linear keyframes.
- **Focus pull in (blur to sharp)** [pass; AE pass]: The shot opens out of focus and sharpens in 0.8 s, a 2D stand-in for a lens focus pull on the first beat of a scene.
- **Ken Burns pan and zoom on a still** [pass; AE pass]: A documentary still drifts: a slow 15% push-in while the frame tilts down the photo, built from the clip zoom ramp plus Transform position keyframes so both apps get the same move.

### stylized

- **Blueprint** [pass; AE pass]: Technical-drawing look: the image becomes pale edge lines on blueprint navy, with a measuring grid in After Effects.
- **Blur-to-reveal** [pass; AE pass]: Shot opens defocused and desaturated, then pulls into focus and full colour on the key word.
- **Bodycam / dashcam POV** [pass; AE pass]: Chest-camera look: strong fisheye barrel with black corners, walking shake, hard contrast with clipped highlights, sensor noise and a corner timecode.
- **CCTV security camera** [pass; AE pass]: Surveillance-camera look: washed, nearly colourless, contrasty picture at a choppy 12 fps, soft with video noise, slight barrel lens and a corner timecode with no box.
- **Colour splash** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Everything goes grey except one chosen colour (a flag, a party colour, a red file), with a small contrast push.
- **Comic ink (cel shade)** [pass; AE pass]: Comic-book treatment: flat shading bands, ink outlines and boosted colour. After Effects does it natively with Cartoon; Premiere's Posterize + Find Edges version renders as washed pastel bands with thin magenta/green contour lines, a soft screen-print look rather than dark ink.
- **Courtroom pencil sketch** [pass; AE pass]: Grey pencil lines on warm paper with a faint trace of the photo's shading, like a courtroom or reconstruction sketch.
- **Drone camera HUD** [pass; AE pass]: Aerial-camera viewfinder: a slightly muted, controlled grade with a running corner timecode; After Effects adds the thin 6x6 telemetry grid and centre reticle ring, which Premiere has no effect for (add a frame/bracket MOGRT on the track above).
- **Duotone brand** [pass; AE pass]: Two-colour graphic treatment: shadows mapped to a deep brand colour and highlights to an accent colour, after a contrast push.
- **Engraved gold relief** [pass; AE pass]: Raised-relief emboss toned gold-brown, like a coin, medal, stamp or plaque.
- **Face pixelation (anonymise)** [pass; AE untested: needs_second_layer]: Coarse square pixelation confined to a tracked box (face, number plate, document) on a duplicate of the clip, the rest of the frame untouched.
- **Newsprint photo** [pass; AE pass]: Old newspaper reproduction: greyscale with grey-ink blacks and dull paper whites, gritty sharpening and paper grain, tinted to newsprint cream.
- **Night vision** [pass; AE pass]: Green image-intensifier look: green-tinted luminance, lifted blooming highlights, heavy grain, slight softness and a dark goggle vignette.
- **Painted brush strokes** [pass; AE weak]: Painterly repaint of the frame with visible diagonal daubs and slightly richer colour.
- **Paper cut-out collage** [pass; AE pass]: Cut-out paper look for a subject with transparency: posterized flat colour, a ragged white paper rim, a soft drop shadow and stop-motion frame rate.
- **Photocopy stencil** [pass; AE pass]: Hard two-tone threshold like a photocopied protest poster or spray stencil: ink black on paper white with copier speckle.
- **Pixel-resolve reveal** [pass; AE pass]: Shot starts as a few giant colour blocks and resolves in doubling steps to full resolution, like an image loading.
- **Pop-art poster** [pass; AE pass]: Flat screen-print colour bands with punchy saturation: the clip is softened first so Posterize gives large clean shapes instead of noisy speckle.
- **Soft face blur (oval)** [pass; AE untested: needs_second_layer]: Heavy blur inside a soft-edged patch on a duplicate of the clip: a gentler anonymising look than pixelation (a feathered box in Premiere, a soft oval in After Effects).
- **Thermal false colour** [pass; AE pass]: Fake thermal-camera look: brightness mapped from deep purple shadows to hot mustard-yellow highlights, soft and slightly grainy like a low-resolution sensor; After Effects' Tritone adds a red-orange middle stop that Premiere's two-colour Tint cannot.

### text_motion

- **3D swing-in title** [pass; AE pass]: The title swings in on its vertical axis like a door or a card from 80 degrees while pushing in from depth, then settles flat with a soft shadow.
- **Blur focus-pull title** [pass; AE pass]: The text comes up out of a heavy blur and fade into crisp focus over about half a second: a calm, premium reveal for the whole block.
- **Bullet whip-on** [pass; AE pass]: The line rushes in sideways with a hard horizontal motion smear and snaps into place in about a quarter second.
- **Cinematic tracking breathe** [corrected_unverified; AE pass]: Wide-spaced letters fade in and tighten into place while the title grows slightly out of a soft focus, under a warm bloom that flares and settles: the classic trailer / documentary title breathe.
- **Decode scramble reveal** [corrected_unverified; AE pass]: Characters arrive left to right while cycling through wrong letters before locking in, a hacker / data-decode reveal with a light bloom and an RGB fringe that settles as the word resolves.
- **Documentary chapter card** [pass; AE pass]: Full-screen chapter break: the picture dips to black into a darkened blurred plate, a year rolls to the chapter's date over 'CHAPTER 01', a rule draws on and the chapter title rises out of a line in the brand colour.
- **Glitch title hit** [pass; AE pass]: A title that bursts on with a few frames of blocky digital corruption and RGB tearing, then sits clean.
- **God-ray title** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Warm volumetric light rays stream out of the title's letters, building from nothing as it holds, for epic trailer and anniversary titles.
- **Gold light-sweep reveal** [untested: needs_setup; AE corrected_unverified]: A logo or title wipes on behind a bright warm-gold light beam riding the edge, then keeps a soft bloom: a premium shine pass.
- **Mask rise reveal** [pass; AE pass]: A text block rises out of an invisible line with a strong ease, as if pushed up from behind a cut, over a thin rule that draws on beneath it; the sober documentary default.
- **Neon flicker-on** [weak; AE pass]: A neon-sign title that stutters on with a few quick blinks, then holds lit inside a coloured glow halo.
- **News lower-third tag** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A short name, place or date label in a solid brand-colour box that opens upward from a thin bar, holds and collapses on exit: the house tag.
- **Overshoot word slam** [corrected_unverified; AE pass]: One big display word whose letters spring up from below and land with an elastic overshoot, finished with a soft bloom and slightly worn edges, landing on a beat.
- **Quote card** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A spoken or written quote fades on character by character in large white type over a darkened, blurred plate inside a thin frame, with a rule and a small grey attribution arriving after it.
- **Stat counter hit** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A huge number counts up with an ease-out and lands on a boom, its label fading in underneath and a rule drawing on, while the picture punches on the landing.
- **Typewriter type-on** [pass; AE pass]: Text types on one character at a time, left to right, with no fade, like a typewriter or terminal.
- **Word-stagger pop captions** [untested: needs_setup; AE pass]: A short caption whose words pop on one after another with a small elastic overshoot, for social-reel captions and list beats.

### transitions

- **Blur-to-focus reveal** [pass; AE pass]: The new shot dissolves in heavily defocused and pulls into sharp focus, a gentle reveal of the scene or subject.
- **Channel switch (TV power off/on)** [AE pass]: The outgoing picture collapses like an old CRT switching off (to a bright line, then a dot, then black) and the next shot powers on in reverse.
- **Chapter change: dip to black**: A slow eased fade down to black and back up, with a low sub drop in the black, marking the end of one chapter and the start of the next.
- **Dip to brand colour** [pass; AE pass]: The picture washes into a solid brand colour and the next shot emerges from it, with the colour set exactly (Premiere's Dip to Color transition is stuck on grey-lavender).
- **Era change: colour drains to black-and-white** [pass; AE pass]: The last second of the present-day shot drains to greyscale, then a film dissolve carries it into black-and-white archive footage, so the jump in era reads as one move.
- **Flash cut** [AE pass]: A hard white flash frame bridges two shots: the outgoing blows to white and the incoming lands overexposed and settles in a few frames.
- **Glitch cut (two-sided AE tear)** [AE untested: needs_setup]: A digital glitch tears across the cut: horizontal slice displacement, scanline tearing and RGB fringing mix the two shots and resolve to the new one.
- **Impact shake cut** [AE untested: needs_setup]: Both shots shake violently with motion-blurred jolts around the cut, as if hit by an impact or tremor, and settle on the new shot.
- **Iris reveal**: The incoming shot opens from the centre of the frame in an expanding hard-edged circle, the classic iris reveal.
- **Jump-cut punch-in** [pass; AE pass]: A jump cut inside a talking-head shot is hidden by punching the second half in by 15 %, so it reads as a deliberate camera change.
- **Memory flashback (soft white)** [pass; AE pass]: The picture fades up into white and the incoming shot emerges from the white soft and out of focus, sharpening as the memory settles.
- **Push-through match cut** [pass; AE pass]: Both shots keep the same slow push-in across the cut and a 6-frame film dissolve blends the matched shapes, so a graphic match cut (eye to moon, wheel to clock) reads as one continuous move.
- **Rewind to the past (tape)** [AE corrected_unverified]: Analog VHS tracking damage tears across the cut, as if a tape is rewound to an older recording: tracking-noise bands, horizontal tearing, vertical jumps and washed-out colour.
- **Time skip flicker** [AE untested: needs_setup]: An unstable projector-style flicker jumps between the two shots for half a second and settles on the new one, signalling that time has passed.
- **Trailer blackout hit**: A very short eased dip through black on a heavy impact sound, the trailer-style blackout between beats.
- **Vertical swipe whip (reel)** [pass; AE untested: needs_setup]: A vertical whip for 9:16 reels: the outgoing shot scrolls up out of frame under vertical motion blur and the next one arrives from below, like a fast feed swipe, identical in both apps.
- **Warm light leak** [AE pass]: Warm orange-red light-leak streaks with a slight chroma split sweep across the frame and wash out the cut, a film-camera feel.
- **Whip pan** [AE untested: needs_setup]: A fast horizontal whip: the outgoing shot is flung off to the left under heavy motion blur and the incoming shot arrives from the right, as if the camera swung.
- **Zoom punch cut** [AE untested: needs_setup]: The outgoing shot rushes forward into a radial zoom blur and the incoming shot snaps out of the same blur, a fast punch through the centre of the frame.

### vfx_overlays

- **Bokeh dream background** [pass; AE pass]: The shot racks out of focus into lens bokeh discs and dips slightly darker so text or a cut-out on top stands out.
- **Chart / shape pulse glow** [weak; AE untested: needs_setup]: A graphic element with transparency (map state, chart bar, icon, key number) pulses with a coloured glow to pull the eye; in Premiere the Edge Glow is only a faint tint on the brightest pixels (render-checked on footage, not yet on an alpha graphic), so the pulse is subtle, while the After Effects version glows from the alpha edge.
- **Drifting fog / smoke haze** [pass; AE pass]: A slow grey fractal fog drifts across the shot at low opacity while the grade lifts the blacks, so the scene reads as smoky or misty.
- **Film burn cut** [pass; AE untested: needs_setup]: The outgoing shot overexposes and warms toward orange-white as a film burn takes it, then the burn clears onto the next shot.
- **Firelight glow flicker** [weak; AE pass]: A warm orange grade with an amber spotlight pool whose intensity flickers irregularly and amber-bloomed highlights, giving a torch- or fire-lit feel; in the Premiere render the pool landed upper left rather than low in frame, so check where it falls.
- **Floating dust motes** [corrected_unverified]: Sparse soft specks of dust drift slowly through the frame, catching light, for an old-room or sunbeam atmosphere.
- **Glint sparkle on highlights** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Star-shaped glints twinkle on the brightest highlights (metal, jewellery, water, lights) for a festive or premium sparkle.
- **God rays from above** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: Warm volumetric light rays stream down from a source above the frame through the bright areas and slowly drift.
- **Heat haze shimmer** [pass; AE pass]: Low-amplitude turbulent displacement makes the whole frame ripple like air over hot tarmac, with a slight warm push.
- **Lens flare sweep** [pass; AE pass]: A classic lens flare fades up and travels left to right across the top of frame, like the sun catching the lens.
- **Light leak wash over a cut** [pass; AE pass]: Film light-leak streaks wash across the cut and keep glowing off the incoming shot before fading. Premiere's Light Leaks effect alone renders cool cyan-blue streaks; the warmth comes from the transition (After Effects carries a warm light-ray bloom instead).
- **Lightning strike flash** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A branching white-blue bolt hits the frame while the exposure double-flashes and decays, like a close lightning strike.
- **Map / chart region spotlight pulse** [pass; AE pass]: Everything except one region of a map or chart dims and softens while a warm light pool on the region pulses.
- **Map location pulse ring** [corrected_unverified]: A solid dot marks a place on a map while a thin ring expands from it and fades, repeating like a radar ping.
- **Rainstorm** [pass]: Fast slanted rain streaks over a darker, cooler, desaturated grade for a stormy monsoon feel.
- **Rising embers** [corrected_unverified]: Small glowing orange-yellow sparks drift upward from the bottom of frame and fade out, with a soft glow on them.
- **Route / connector line draw** [pass; AE pass]: A glowing line draws itself from one point to another (city to city, cause to effect). Premiere draws a thin white-core line with the plug-in's blue-violet glow and no end dot; After Effects lands an amber dot at the end.
- **Snowfall** [pass]: Procedural snow falls over the shot in two depth layers (fine distant flakes plus a few large near flakes) under a cool grade.
- **Soft highlight bloom** [pass; AE pass]: Highlights bloom into a soft warm glow and contrast eases off, giving a dreamy, nostalgic or heroic sheen.
- **Title disintegrates to dust** [pass; AE corrected_unverified]: A title breaks into fine grains that warp and blow sideways until nothing is left, like text turning to dust.

## Known gaps (completeness critic)


### Resolved

- 2026-10-04: Premiere builder second-copy bug FIXED in pr_build.jsx (all effects added first, nth spec -> nth same-name component); proven with two Strobe Lights carrying their own values.
- 2026-10-04: the 28 recipes whose values were fixed after a failed render were re-rendered with the fixed builder and rechecked (verified.recheck).
- 2026-10-04: Strobe flash rate now checked by pve.check_spec (Random Strobe Probablity acts per frame; > 3 flashes/s flagged unless Blend With Original >= 90); Neon flicker-on capped at 12%.
- 2026-10-04: verdict wording clarified in PVE.md: [wrong] = card text rewritten from the render, [broken] often a test limit (GPU-less VR renders, unfinished analysis).
- 2026-10-14: AdobeBridge read After Effects' AB_WARN / AB_ERROR notes with an ASCII-only pattern, so a note carrying AE's curly quotes was dropped (an AB_ERROR then did not fail the build). Fixed: adobe_bridge.ae_notes (printable ASCII or well-formed UTF-8, used by build(), vfx.render_scenes, make_house_mogrts.py and pve/live_tests.py), with offline tests.
- 2026-10-14: pve check no longer flags values against a harvested range whose minimum is above its maximum (the uint32 probe artefact on Track Matte Key 'Matte:' and other track pickers).

### Gaps

- Editing grammar (Premiere): no card or recipe covers J/L (split) cuts, ripple/roll/slip/slide trims, three-point and replace edits, lift/extract, match frame or closing gaps. 'j-cut', 'split edit', 'roll edit' and 'three-point' get zero hits. Cutting interviews is mostly this, and the scripted route (TrackItem start/end/inPoint on unlinked audio vs video, plus the QE slip/roll methods marked unsupported) is never written up as a technique. **Covered 2026-10-13: J-cut and L-cut (split edits); Trims: ripple, roll, slip and slide; Three-point and four-point edits (insert and overwrite); Replace edit (Replace With Clip); Lift and Extract (remove a range); Match frame and reverse match frame; Closing gaps (Ripple Delete, Close Gap); Edit grammar on After Effects layers (trim, slip, ripple by script)**
- Audio/video sync: nothing on Merge Clips, Synchronize by audio waveform, dual-system sound or syncing multicam angles ('merge clips', 'synchroniz' and 'dual-system' get zero hits). The Multi-camera card is UI-only and gives no scripted fallback, such as finding the offset by cross-correlation in Python/ffmpeg and placing clips at that offset. **Covered 2026-10-13: Scripted sync fallback: offset by audio cross-correlation; Merge Clips (dual-system sound); Synchronize clips in the timeline (Clip > Synchronize); Multi-camera source sequence: syncing the angles**
- Camera-move recipes: there is no 'moves' recipe family. Missing are Ken Burns pan/zoom on stills (mentioned only inside the PR Keyframes tool card and 'Aged photo print'), 2.5D photo parallax (cut-out planes plus a camera), camera orbit/dolly on a null rig, rack focus with DOF, a slow push with ease, and camera wiggle on a camera instead of on a layer. The Cameras tool card is a summary: it has no scripting for Position/Point of Interest or one-node vs two-node (autoOrient), and no rig. The only 3D move recipe is '3D swing-in title'. **Covered 2026-10-13: Camera rigs: one-node vs two-node and a null rig for orbit, dolly and truck; Camera depth of field and rack focus; Photo parallax with 3D layers and a camera; Ken Burns move (stills pan and zoom); Camera wiggle on the camera, not the layers; Ken Burns pan and zoom on a still; Eased slow push-in; Focus pull in (blur to sharp); 2.5D photo parallax (two planes)**
- Motion paths: Auto-Orient / Orient Along Path, pasting a mask path into Position, Separate Dimensions and Speed vs Value graph shaping have no tool coverage ('auto-orient' 0, 'separate dimensions' 0, 'speed graph' 0 in tool/recipe cards). These are needed for map routes (a plane or arrow following a line) and for news-graphic eases. **Covered 2026-10-13: Auto-Orient: Orient Along Path; Paste a mask or shape path into Position (motion path); Separate Dimensions (X and Y position curves); Speed graph vs value graph (shaping eases)**
- Text animator catalogue: 'Text animators and the Range Selector' lists only some properties. Missing are the full property set with match names (Anchor Point, Skew/Skew Axis, Line Anchor, Line Spacing, Character Value/Range/Alignment, Stroke Width, Fill Hue/Saturation/Brightness, Tracking Type, Enable Per-character 3D) and the More Options group (Anchor Point Grouping, Grouping Alignment, Inter-Character Blending). The Based On / Shape / Mode / Units index orders are marked unverified. Text on a path (Path Options: Reverse Path, Perpendicular To Path, Force Alignment, First/Last Margin) has no tool card at all; only presets mention it. **Covered 2026-10-13: Text animator properties: the full set with match names; Text selectors: Range, Wiggly and Expression (match names and Advanced options); Text More Options: Anchor Point Grouping, Grouping Alignment, Fill & Stroke, Inter-Character Blending; Text on a path (Path Options); Per-character 3D text (Enable Per-character 3D)**
- Expressions missing from 'Common expressions library': marker-driven animation (thisComp.marker / marker.nearestKey(time) to fire ins and outs on beats or VO cues), createPath()/points() for live lines between nulls (map connectors, callout leaders), clamp(), lookAt(), fromComp/toWorld, text-style expressions (text.sourceText.style.setFontSize / setFillColor), sampleImage() for colour pickup, framesToTime/timeToFrames, and index-based stagger (index*delay with seedRandom). Each gets zero hits in tool cards. **Covered 2026-10-13: Marker-driven animation (expressions keyed to markers); Live lines between layers: createPath() and points(); clamp(), linear() and ease() remaps; lookAt() and pointing layers at targets; Space transforms: toComp, fromComp, toWorld, fromWorld; Text style expressions (sourceText.style); sampleImage() colour pickup; framesToTime() and timeToFrames(); Index-based stagger and seeded randomness**
- Infographics and data viz: no recipe builds a bar chart, line chart, pie or donut, percentage ring, progress bar, timeline bar or highlighter underline from shape layers. Those exist only as AE presets. Charts are a news-explainer staple. The 'Data-driven animation (JSON, CSV)' card exists but no recipe connects it to a chart. Gradient Fill stops cannot be set by script and no workaround is given (Gradient Ramp/Fill effect, or a template .ffx). **Covered 2026-10-13: Shape-layer charts from data (technique); Gradient fills on shapes without scripting the gradient stops; Bar chart grow (shape layers); Line chart draw-on; Donut chart segments; Pie chart slices pop; Percentage ring with counter; Progress bar fill; Timeline bar with milestones; Highlighter and underline on a key line**
- Rolling credits, end crawls and news tickers: Premiere's Essential Graphics Roll/Crawl, AE scroll setups and a looping ticker band are not covered. 'ticker' hits are about cropping out a burned-in ticker; 'rolling credits' and 'roll/crawl' get zero. **Covered 2026-10-13: Rolling credits and crawls in Premiere; Scrolling credits and crawls in After Effects; Rolling end credits (AE scroll); News ticker crawl (looping)**
- Safe areas as numbers: no card gives broadcast action/title safe (93% / 90% of the frame), Premiere Safe Margins, or platform UI safe zones for 1080x1920 Reels/Shorts/TikTok (top and bottom UI bands, right-side button column). 'Guides and rulers' only says to use guides, and the text-off-faces house rule has no matching layout card. **Covered 2026-10-13: Broadcast safe areas (action and title safe); Premiere Safe Margins and After Effects safe guides (their defaults); Platform UI safe zones (1080x1920 Reels, Shorts, TikTok); Text placement around faces (layout rule)**
- Delivery and export specs: no card holds concrete encode settings per destination: H.264/HEVC profile and level, VBR 1-pass vs 2-pass, target/max bitrate, AAC 48 kHz bitrate, frame-rate and field-order settings, broadcast masters (ProRes 422 HQ, XDCAM HD422 MXF OP1a), mezzanine and archive masters, or alpha overlays (ProRes 4444 / PNG sequence). The tool cards name the mechanisms (.epr, output module templates, AME) but not what to choose. 'vbr', 'aac', '59.94' and 'field order' get zero hits. **Covered 2026-10-13: Delivery spec: YouTube upload (16:9, HD and 4K); Delivery spec: vertical Reels, Shorts and TikTok (9:16); Delivery spec: broadcast masters (ProRes 422 HQ, XDCAM HD422 MXF OP1a); Delivery spec: mezzanine and archive masters; Delivery spec: alpha overlays (ProRes 4444, PNG sequence); Export encoding choices: VBR 2-pass vs CBR, maximum depth and quality, field order**
- Audio deliverables: stems and split-track export (dialogue/music/effects), M&E mixes, and broadcast channel layouts are missing ('m&e' 0). There is also no documentary mix-level plan (dialogue peak range, music bed under VO vs in the clear, SFX level, a bus structure) beyond the ducking and loudness-target cards. **Covered 2026-10-13: Stems and split-track export (dialogue, music, effects); M&E (music and effects) mix; Broadcast audio track layouts (EBU R 123); Documentary mix-level plan (dialogue, music, effects)**
- Scripted ducking: Auto Ducking is UI-only, and the scripted fallback appears only as one sentence inside the Auto Ducking card. No recipe turns VO clip ranges into music Volume 'Level' keyframes (dB under VO, ramp length, how long a gap must be before the music recovers), even though pr_build already writes Volume keys. **Covered 2026-10-13: Scripted ducking from VO ranges; Music ducks under VO (scripted, from VO ranges)**
- Variable frame rate and mixed rates: phone and screen recordings with VFR (audio drift, conform by transcoding), drop-frame timecode, and 23.976/29.97/59.94 next to 25 fps are almost uncovered: 'variable frame rate' 1 effect-card hit, '59.94' 0, 'drop frame' 1. 'Frame-rate judder conform' was tested only on a 25-in-25 clip. **Covered 2026-10-13: Variable frame rate (VFR) phone and screen recordings; Mixed frame rates and drop-frame timecode (23.976, 29.97, 59.94 next to 25)**
- Colour tags on export: nothing covers the washed-out or darker look of H.264 exports in QuickTime/Safari/Instagram (Rec.709 colr/nclc tags, gamma 2.4 vs 1.96 display interpretation, Premiere 2026 export colour options). 'quicktime', 'colr' and 'nclc' get zero hits. **Covered 2026-10-13: Colour tags on export: Rec.709 colr/nclc and the QuickTime gamma shift**
- Reframing with blur fill: no recipe fills 4:3 archive into 16:9, or 16:9 into 9:16, with a scaled, blurred, darkened copy behind the shot ('blur fill' 0, 'pillarbox' 2). This is one of the most common doc/reel layouts. **Covered 2026-10-13: Blur fill: 16:9 into 9:16; Blur fill: portrait or 4:3 into 16:9 (pillarbox fill)**
- Multi-box layouts: no recipe for a news two-way or double box, a three-up, or a presenter-plus-screenshot picture-in-picture with consistent margins, borders and drop shadows. PiP hits are effects and presets, not a layout. **Covered 2026-10-13: Multi-box layout geometry (margins, gaps, scale); Two-way (double box) split; Three-up row; Presenter plus screenshot picture-in-picture**
- Explainer document treatments: no recipe for zooming into a screenshot of a tweet or article with a highlighter or marker stroke on the key line, a paper or document drop-in, or a redaction bar. 'highlighter' and 'tweet' get zero hits in tool/recipe cards. **Covered 2026-10-13: Document treatments (screenshots, paper, redaction); Screenshot zoom with highlighter; Paper drop-in; Redaction bars wipe on; Highlighter and underline on a key line**
- Compositing finish: light wrap and edge integration after a key have one passing mention ('Bit depth' card). There is no despill-then-wrap-then-grade-match recipe for real green-screen plates. **Covered 2026-10-13: Compositing finish order: key, despill, edge, grade-match; Light wrap (built from layers); Green-screen finish: key, despill, edge, then grade-match**
- Flash/strobe safety as a rule: recipes mention 'under 3 flashes per second', but no card states the threshold (Harding / ITU-R BT.1702: no more than 3 flashes in any 1 s, saturated red flashes count) and pve.check_spec does not enforce it. The PR Strobe Light card's own example breaks it (see weak_cards). **Covered 2026-10-13: Photosensitive flash rule (Harding / ITU-R BT.1702) and pve check**
- Media management: relinking offline media (1 hit), missing fonts in .mogrt and AE projects ('font substitution' 0; Adobe Fonts auto-activation), and collecting a project for hand-off with fonts are only partly covered. **Covered 2026-10-13: Relink offline media (Link Media); Missing fonts (After Effects Resolve Fonts, Adobe Fonts, templates); Collect a project for hand-off (AE Collect Files, Premiere Project Manager)**

### Weak cards

- pr_effect 'Strobe Light' (contradictory, safety): its built-in paparazzi example (Duration 0.29 / Period 0.33 / Random Strobe Probablity 50) claims about 1.5 flashes/s and lists 20-50 as typical. Recipe 'Paparazzi strobe' measured the probability acting PER FRAME (50 gave about 12 flashes/s, 14 of 24 frames white), and the AE card also says per frame. Following the card breaks the 3-per-second limit. It also claims a flash lasts 'Period minus Duration', which contradicts Adobe's documented meaning (Duration = flash length) and the AE card; the luma scan may have counted the un-strobed gaps as the flashes. Duration = Period 'drawing nothing' also fits the documented meaning: a constant strobe has no flicker to detect.
- ae_effect 'Strobe Light' (status unusable): the same plug-in (AE.ADBE Strobe) flashes in Premiere (PR card pass; the newsreel recipes rendered 13 of 25 frames white with it).
